# 04 — Build Plan

**Product:** shop system for agro-vet and farm-supply shops (name to be decided)
**First shop:** MPC — Mabatini Pumba Center, Usa River, Arusha, Tanzania
**Version:** 1.170 · 21 Nov 2026 · Portion 4 of the planning pack, updated in portion 5
**Built from:** `01-product-spec.md` v1.0, `02-domain-model.md` v1.1, `03-architecture.md` v1.0 — now v1.1, v1.2 and v1.1, with Appendix A applied
**Status:** living document. Everything here is decided unless marked *(default)* — our best guess, safe to change at a stage review. Small open items are in the parking lot (§20). The changes this plan made to the other docs are listed in Appendix A (applied). BP7 and BP16 are ADRs in `docs/adr/`; the working rules for Claude Code are in `CLAUDE.md`.

**Changes in v1.170** (21 Nov 2026, ATT-13 built): the ATT-13 card says what it built, in the plan Jack approved, its ten commits after the card's as the card named them. Building it found the card's story one time wrong: the shop closes at 18:40, so a Toka at 18:30 is "left early" and the day would still wait; Juma leaves at 18:45, as the card now says. 02 §12 and 03 §5.8 follow (02 v1.55, 03 v1.94). Marked **(v1.170)**.

**Changes in v1.169** (21 Nov 2026, ATT-13): `/start-task ATT-13` found it a row, so its card is written (BP2), as Jack approved its screens: the laptop offers no *Ingia kazini* after a Toka and says *Umeshatoka leo. Mwambie meneja.*; from the day under a worker's calendar, a manager or the owner crosses out a wrong tap (*Batilisha*) and adds a forgotten one (*Ongeza Ingia au Toka*), each with a reason, in ATT-6's sheet; the crossed-out tap stays, struck through (A2). One choice by Jack: a fix keeps a decided day's decision, and the day says its taps changed (A). A tap entered in the office is an `AttendanceEvent` with no laptop, its maker `created_by` and a reason, its time trusted (`entered`), and no photo (`entered`): 02 §12 follows when it is built. The row's notes move into the card. Marked **(v1.169)**.

**Changes in v1.67** (13 Nov 2026, SYNC-12's review): Jack chose to fix every finding in this card, which roughly doubles it. The rescue import checks each record as the server checks its envelope, so a file edited by hand can't stop the loading laptop's pushes. A record another laptop sent first still counts in the sending laptop's numbers, so a dying laptop back to life raises no `device_gap`. The push gains an optional `carried_from` (where a rescued record was made, and who loaded the file), kept on the answer log's line (`sync` migration 0003): the server checks the record's time against the laptop that made it, not the one that loaded it, and flags a loader without `device.manage` (03 v1.51). A second review, its findings fixed too: by Jack's choice, a rescued clock-in always waits for a manager, as its time came from a file anyone could edit (02 v1.25); a laptop loading its own file sends the records as its own; the import refuses what the server refuses; a twin of a carried record counts its number too; a suspended loader is flagged; a set-aside carried record keeps where it was made; and a carried record's maker is checked on the side it was made on. The parking lot gains item 24. A note for SALE-4: the rescue file carries no pending effects, as a clock-in has none; a sale's go in the file's next format. Marked **(v1.67)**.

**Changes in v1.66** (13 Nov 2026, SYNC-12): Jack's choice, while planning SYNC-12: *Toka* with unsent records shows a note and still logs out, instead of a warning that stops the logout. Logging out loses nothing: the laptop sends its own records with its key, whoever is logged in (SYNC-8, SYNC-16), so a stop would guard nothing and teach staff to tap past warnings. In the plan he approved: the export and import live on a new counter page, *Kompyuta hii*; anyone logged in may export, as a dying laptop can't wait for the manager, and only someone with `device.manage` may import, as an edited file could clock in a colleague who never came; set-aside records stay out of the file (parking lot 23 decides them). Marked **(v1.66)**.

**Changes in v1.65** (13 Nov 2026, SYNC-16): the SYNC-16 card says what it built and settled. Background Sync works on Chrome and Edge, the laptops too, not only Chrome on Android. Jack's choice: the hand test runs on the laptop's Edge before the PR, and on the shop phone with the live demo after the merge, ticked in the tick PR, as the phone runs a service worker only on https. The technical choices, in the plan Jack approved: a block the worker hears is a note in the shop's database, which the page takes; the worker reads the CSRF token with the Cookie Store and learns the shop from the sync's name; it only pushes. Marked **(v1.65)**.

**Changes in v1.64** (13 Nov 2026, SYNC-11): the SYNC-11 card says what it built and settled. The plan he approved: nine steps, one idea each, and one test fix. Notes for SALE-3, ATT-6 and SYNC-16. Marked **(v1.64)**.

**Changes in v1.63** (12 Nov 2026, SYNC-10): the SYNC-10 card says what it built and settled. Jack's choice: a local session lives in the tab's memory only, so a reload asks for the PIN again. And *Tuma sasa* sends even during the wait after a failed push. The wait itself moves to `meta`, so every tab sees it (03 v1.47); the *Tuma sasa* button goes on SYNC-11's screen. SYNC-9's *Done when* now names its own moments and the shared wait. From its review: SYNC-11's note names `askToSendNow()` and its offline state, SYNC-16 names its tests, and notes for SYNC-14 and the parking lot (item 22). Marked **(v1.62)**.

**Changes in v1.61** (12 Nov 2026, SYNC-8): the SYNC-8 card says what it built: the bootstrap names the laptop's last number, kept in `meta` (the larger of the server's and the laptop's own); `saveDocument` writes a document, its number, its outbox row and its pending effects in one Dexie transaction (`outbox.ts`); the push sends the outbox in number order and reads its four answers (`push.ts`), before the pull; the next push waits after a failure (`backoff.ts`); and a live list of the records set aside, whose screen Jack gave to SYNC-11 (03 v1.46). One change to the card, in the plan Jack approved: each test in the commit of the code it proves, as on SYNC-4, and five commits, one idea each. **Changes in v1.58** (11 Nov 2026, ATT-1): one choice by Jack: clocking in needs no key, on any of the shop's laptops. The ATT-1 card says what it built: the push's `sent_at`, the time of a record's predecessor handed to its saving code, `staffpay.AttendanceEvent`, the time check and the clock document (03 v1.43, 02 v1.23). After its review, every finding fixed, each in its own commit with its test; two more choices by Jack: a flag about two records that disagree names both makers, and rule 6's four parts may come in two cards, the server's first (ATT-1, then SYNC-11). **Changes in v1.57** (11 Nov 2026, SYNC-5): two choices by Jack. The card's `device_gap` flag "resolves itself": the system closes it, with a note of its own, once every number it was missing has arrived, and it is the one kind the system may close (02 v1.22). And a record made after its maker left is flagged as one made while suspended, since a person's keys stay given when they go. The plan Jack approved splits the card's three commits into one idea each. The SYNC-5 card says what it built: the push's report (`app_version`, `unsent_count`, `outages`), kept on the device; one flag per gap in a device's numbers, about the record that came after it; the late record closing it; `people.DeviceOutage`, a new ledger; and the maker's key and status checked again (03 v1.42, 02 v1.22). **Changes in v1.56** (10 Nov 2026, SYNC-4): the SYNC-4 card says what it built: `POST /api/sync/v1/push/`, up to 100 documents, each saved once, in the laptop's order, in a transaction of its own, and answered `accepted`, `duplicate` (with the first answer), `invalid` (with a flag) or `retry` (the batch stops there); the `CounterRecord` base; the list of document types; the answer log, `sync.PushedDocument`, a new ledger; a new flag kind, `invalid_document`; and a change block's last number, a document's `posted_seq` (03 v1.41, 02 v1.21). **Changes in v1.55** (10 Nov 2026, SYNC-3): the SYNC-3 card says what it built: `GET /api/sync/v1/pull/`, the changes after a device's cursor, each record as it is now, through the registry, a record that left the lists as `archive`, and on every page the next cursor, whether more changes wait, the server time and `min_app_version` (03 v1.40). And one more choice by Jack: the lists are read one after another, the cursor first, so the third *Done when* now says what that proves. Marked **(v1.53)**.

A reset link holds its key after the `#`, and the real server sends no email until OPS-5. AUTH-4, AUTH-6, UI-5 and OPS-5 get notes. Marked **(v1.30)**.

**Changes in v1.29** (3 Nov 2026, AUTH-3): three choices by Jack, made while planning AUTH-3. A first or forgotten password is set face to face: the owner types a temporary one, and the person chooses their own at the next login; an email link is only for people who have one; no SMS for now (ADR-0038, which supersedes ADR-0017; 02 v1.14, 03 v1.16). With that job, AUTH-3 would be more than twice its size (§3.3), so it moves to a new card, AUTH-6, right after it; until then, the superuser sets passwords in the admin. A person with current jobs at two shops is refused safely until a new task, AUTH-7, lets them choose, before a second real shop joins (§14). AUTH-3 checks a shop's status at every login, a device's included, as AUTH-2 left for it. UI-5 waits for AUTH-6, reads allauth's own error shape and sends a person with no email to the owner; PPL-4's staff screen sets a temporary password through AUTH-6; OPS-5 notes the real server's email. Marked **(v1.29)**.

**Changes in v1.28** (3 Nov 2026, AUTH-5): one choice by Jack, and what building AUTH-5 settled (03 v1.15). The device list shows a side's devices to whoever works on that side, a seller included, who changes none of them: the card's third *Done when* said a manager sees only the sides they hold `device.manage` on, which PPL-5's routes can't do for a show action yet, and a device's name and last call are no secret. On Render the demo reads the caller's address from Cloudflare's `CF-Connecting-IP`, Render's own advice, since two proxies stand in front there. OPS-6's deploy makes the throttles' cache tables. Built in five commits, each with its own tests, so each passes `make check`. The review's fourteen fixes are on the card: each limit in a table of its own, a start counted by the caller's network and keeping no address, only real starts and new tries of a code counting, and one answer for a taken id wherever it is taken (03 v1.15, 02 v1.13). AUTH-3's *Watch out* gains the cache's trap for allauth's login limits. Marked **(v1.28)**.

**Changes in v1.27** (30 Oct 2026, AUTH-2): four choices by Jack, made while planning AUTH-2 (02 v1.12, 03 v1.14). AUTH-2 was twice its size, so the manager's confirm, the throttles and the office device routes UI-3 needs, which no card built, move to a new card, AUTH-5, right after it; UI-3 waits for AUTH-5. A confirmed pairing moves its key's hash to the device, so the hash lives in one place, and the login finds a key's shop through one named door that reads only the shop's id; the device is then read through the guard. The throttles count in PostgreSQL, in Django's database cache, shared by every worker (ADR-0023). AUTH-2 also settles the key's cookie (SameSite Strict, `/api/` only, sent again before browsers drop it after 400 days), CSRF on the start of a pairing, a start refused for a key that already opens a device, and versioned device routes. Built, the card names its tests and what it settled: a key is hidden from every error page, Django's own `set_cookie` included; a refused device keeps its cookie alive; the sweep calls every device route as a device of each shop and with no key. At AUTH-2's review, Jack chose a new ADR for the login door: ADR-0037 supersedes ADR-0022, naming the three ways past the shop guard. Marked **(v1.27)**.

**Changes in v1.26** (30 Oct 2026, AUTH-1): eight choices by Jack, made while planning AUTH-1 (02 v1.11, 03 v1.13). A new device belongs to no shop until a manager says yes, so it waits in a `PairingRequest` with no shop, a new exception to T1; the `Device` is made in the manager's shop when they confirm. The device gets its key when it asks, and the manager's yes switches it on, so the key never passes through the manager's phone. The key is stored as its SHA-256 hash. The per-shop counter keeps no edit history. Blocking and unblocking need a reason, written in the audit trail, and `device.manage` on the device's home side; unblocking keeps the key. `DeviceOutage` and the sync columns move to SYNC-5, `last_seen_at` to AUTH-2: nothing in AUTH-1 writes them. The card's commits follow. After AUTH-1's review, the card lists its fixes, one commit each: the key never shows in a printout; every new database rule has a test, as do a suspended manager, another shop's manager and a blank unblock reason; two pairings at the same moment are tested with two connections. AUTH-2 gets four notes: the path from a key to its device past the guard, a confirm sent twice, the key kept out of error pages, and the device showing its shop. Marked **(v1.26)**.

**Changes in v1.25** (30 Oct 2026, PPL-5): three choices by Jack, made while planning PPL-5. Every route names the permission each of its actions needs, and an action it doesn't name is refused, so no route is ever open by forgetting. A visit reads the person's permissions once, so a list costs the same few queries however many sides a shop has. A show action (a list, a read) needs only its side to be visible for now; one that needs a key of its own, such as buying prices (`cost.view`), debts (`debt.view`) or reports (`report.view`), is refused until the first card that shows rows behind one builds it: CAT-2 gets a note. The card's commit that gives the sweep's staff the Owner role moves up, before the hooks, so no test that passed before turns red on the way; the card's own tests come first and fail until the hooks pass them. Built, the card names its tests and what it settled: every read of a route keeps to the person's sides, its own @actions included; a change to a whole list needs its key on the whole shop; a create sent twice is answered as a read. After PPL-5's review, the card lists its fixes, one commit each: a create that doesn't ask for itself is asked on the whole shop; a method a route doesn't answer, OPTIONS included, is 405 again; a change to one row is checked before its action runs; routes add to their rows in `base_rows()`; a change always names a key of its own; a repeat runs the route's own checks. Marked **(v1.25)**.

**Changes in v1.24** (29 Oct 2026, PPL-3): one decision made while planning PPL-3: a new shop gets its role templates from a `people` service, `set_up_shop`, which makes the shop with `create_shop` and then copies them, in one transaction, because `shops` may not import `people` (ADR-0035, 03 v1.12). A shop made by `create_shop` alone has no roles, so nobody may act there: forgetting fails closed. The card's last commit is renamed to match. CORE-6's two side hooks move to a new card, PPL-5, right after PPL-3 (Jack): with them, PPL-3 would be twice its size (§3.3). AUTH-3 and AUTH-4 wait for PPL-5, so no login comes before the routes check permissions. Deliveries are weighed as they arrive, offline, by anyone with `stock.weigh` (Jack, 02 v1.10): STK-3 and STK-4 carry it. The card names the tests and the commits it built, and what it settled: the six templates as Jack chose them, and who may give a role (Jack); PPL-4, PPL-5, AUTH-1, AUTH-4, DATA-1, CAT-1 and CAT-2 get notes. After PPL-3's review, the card lists its fixes, one commit each: the giver check reads the role as stored; a person of a shop that isn't open is refused loudly; a new code reaches the shops that already have roles. PPL-4's row says what taking a role away needs, and CAT-1 asks Jack whether a product is checked on any of its sides or on every one. Marked **(v1.24)**.

**Changes in v1.23** (29 Oct 2026, CTRL-2): one decision by Jack, made while planning CTRL-2: office edits are recorded by a trigger of our own into one append-only table, `control.EditEvent`, not by django-pghistory, whose tables CORE-7's sweep refuses (ADR-0036, 03 v1.11). No device exists yet, so the history and the audit trail name the person, and AUTH-4 adds the device. CORE-7's sweep gets a list of the files allowed to run raw SQL, with one file. The card names the tests and the commits it built, and CTRL-5's row names both books. After CTRL-2's review, the card lists its fixes, one commit each: the trigger refuses an edit of another shop's row, keeps each edit's own time, and alone writes the history; services that pass their own `by` tell PostgreSQL too; every shop table people change keeps a history, payment accounts and stock locations included (Jack). CTRL-5, PPL-4, AUTH-1 and AUTH-4 get notes. Marked **(v1.23)**.

**Changes in v1.22** (29 Oct 2026, CTRL-1): three decisions by Jack, made while building CTRL-1 (02 v1.8): a flag may close straight from open, always with a note; every explanation is kept; and a person a flag names may explain it but never close it. CTRL-4 checks who may close one (`flag.review`). After CTRL-1's review, the card lists the tests and the fixes it built, one commit each, and CTRL-4's row gets two notes for its screen. So the card also builds the flag's notes and four actions, not two, and keeps its people as link rows: it is `M`, not `S`. Its services take Actors and rows, as CORE-6's do, not ids. The type codes of generic links are one list in `core`, empty until a card points at its own tables (03 v1.10). Marked **(v1.22)**.

**Changes in v1.21** (28–29 Oct 2026, CORE-7): the card names the tests and the commits it built. The guard's checks got a commit of their own, between the model sweep and the route sweep. Two reviews made the sweep stricter, one fix per commit. It refuses two routes with one name; looks for shop A's names and amounts as well as its ids, in any answer it can read, and reports one it can't (a PDF) unless the route has a leak test of its own; looks as shop A first, who must see its own rows; tries edits, voids (POST) and deletes as well as reads, each answered as for an id no shop has; follows pages up to a limit; and scans for raw SQL, hand-built queries and Django's base manager beside the two doors. Two things were settled in CORE-7: tests may use `all_shops` too, as CORE-2's own test of the admin's door does; and the two link tables Django makes for `User`'s admin permissions, which have no `shop` and CASCADE, sit on the sweep's list of infrastructure tables (Jack: they hold Django's admin data, never a shop's). The sweep is never loosened, nor edited, to let something pass: what grows is its data, in `backend/tests/conftest.py`, and its allowed lists, in `backend/tests/sweep_lists.py`. For later cards: a route over a new table gives the sweep's two shops a row of it, with values only shop A has; PPL-3 gives both shops' sweep staff all of their own shop; a route under `api/` that reads a shop's tables but is not an office route, such as pairing (AUTH-2), PIN login (AUTH-4) or sync (SYNC-2 on), is refused by the sweep until a reviewed change teaches the sweep to call it; SALE-2's lines keep their side through their sale, which the side check can't name yet. Background tasks live in each app's `tasks.py` (03 v1.9). Marked **(v1.21)**.

**Changes in v1.20** (28 Oct 2026, CORE-6): the card names the tests and the commits it built. It made three more commits than it listed: a taken shop code got its own, the base views and the creates were split in two, and this docs commit closes it. After the review, Jack chose to close the API docs in production: the page runs code from a CDN on our address; the demo and a laptop keep them open. What CORE-6 decided, for later cards: login names who acts and the shop, as an Actor in `request.auth` with a `shop_id` and a `staff_id`, and the base view opens that shop around the whole visit; office routes need a staff member, so a device alone is refused (AUTH-2, AUTH-3, AUTH-4). Two side hooks, `visible_sides()` and `check_side_permission()`, wait for PPL-3. A client-made id must be a UUIDv7. An id taken in another shop is refused as `id.taken`, revealing nothing (SYNC-4). The error shape still needs its place in the schema, and every error code a message (UI-2). Marked **(v1.20)**.

**Changes in v1.19** (28 Oct 2026, CORE-5): the card names a test for each *Done when* line and the runner's own checks. Its float check is a test, not CI config, so its commit is `test:`, not `ci:`. The card also names `split_into_units`, the helper behind E1's "26 bags + 35.5 kg". UI-1 gains a *Watch out* on the traps the shared vectors pin, which the TypeScript side must match. Marked **(v1.19)**.

**Changes in v1.18** (27 Oct 2026, CORE-4): CORE-4's `Setting` has no `set_by` column. Who set a value is the row's `created_by`, which every shop row has had since PPL-2 (Jack, while planning CORE-4; 02 v1.7). The two tolerances default to 0 until the owner agrees values (02 §20, item 2). The card names a test for its last *Done when* line and the extra tests its plan added, and splits its last commit in two: the dated rows, then the lookup. From CORE-4's review, for later cards: SHOP-3's row says what its write service checks and two choices it makes (backdated values; a side going back to the shop's value); CAT-2's row names the clash between one price row per date (03 §9) and a same-day fix. Marked **(v1.18)**.

**Changes in v1.17** (27 Oct 2026, CORE-3's tick): CORE-7's *Watch out* says which tables really lose the shop guard: an abstract base with a fresh `Meta`, or a table with managers of its own. A table that only writes a fresh `Meta` keeps it, as CORE-3's review showed and `core/models.py` now says. Marked **(v1.17)**.

**Changes in v1.16** (27 Oct 2026, CORE-3): the card builds an `AppendOnly` base model, not a mixin, as the plan Jack approved says; 03 §5.3 already calls it a base. From CORE-3's review, for the cards that build void tables: a *Watch out* on CORE-3 lists the two rules each void table adds itself (a complete stamp, in its own shop), how a void is written, and why the trigger's SQL never changes once on `main`; MONEY-1, CASH-2 and ATT-1 point to it. Marked **(v1.16)**.

**Changes in v1.15** (27 Oct 2026, SHOP-2): one decision by Jack, made at SHOP-2's review. The admin's *Add shop* page keeps saving a bare shop, with no sides; making it use `create_shop` is a later task, in the parking lot (§20, item 16). SHOP-2's *Watch out* records the answer it chose: `create_shop` takes no "who". From SHOP-2's review, for later cards: DATA-1 adds a `kind` argument to `create_shop`; CORE-6 also turns a taken shop code into a code of our own; CORE-7 sweeps many-to-many links and the tables Django makes for them; a cash drawer serves exactly one side, which MONEY-2 checks (§20, item 17). Marked **(v1.15)**.

**Changes in v1.14** (27 Oct 2026, PPL-2): two decisions by Jack, made while planning PPL-2. A staff member's photo is optional, and it arrives with PPL-4, which already waits for the file storage a photo needs (CTRL-3); with no photo, UI-4's staff picker shows the full name in large letters with the nickname under it (02 v1.4). PPL-2 builds no admin page: a staff admin page is its own task, in the parking lot (§20, item 15). From PPL-2's review, for later cards: `created_by` reaches SHOP-2's and CTRL-1's tables; PPL-3 also covers people who have left; AUTH-4 sets Argon2's cost on purpose; PPL-4 waits for CTRL-2, so a suspension's reason is stored. Marked **(v1.14)**.

**Changes in v1.13** (27 Oct 2026, CORE-2): the card's last *Done when* line names the admin's own manager, `all_shops`, beside `system_context()`. The card builds both, and both read every shop, so a line naming only one was not true. What the guard means for later cards is in their new *Watch out* lines: PPL-2 (writes and admin pages need a current shop; the test-only table follows `ShopScoped`), CORE-6 and SYNC-4 (an id already taken in another shop), CORE-7 (sweep the guard itself). Marked **(v1.13)**.

**Changes in v1.12** (27 Oct 2026, SHOP-1): CORE-7's allowed list names the `Shop`: it is the tenant, so it has no `shop`, and the sweep must pass with no change to the test (02 v1.3, 03 v1.6). Marked **(v1.12)**.

**Changes in v1.11** (26 Oct 2026, SPIKE-2): decisions by Jack, made while planning SPIKE-2. The card gains the *Learn* line its drill starts from, and a *Tests* line. Its page size, `size: 80mm auto`, is not valid CSS, so Chrome would drop it: the page measures the slip and sets the height itself. The card ships in two PRs, as SPIKE-1 did, because the demo deploys only from `main`. OWN-1 is answered: an Epson receipt printer on USB, with 80 mm paper. The QR code is left out of the spike, because the shop doesn't use QR codes yet; whether R2's slips carry one becomes parking-lot item 14. Marked **(v1.11)**.

**Changes in v1.10** (25 Oct 2026, SPIKE-1): SPIKE-1's findings (`docs/spikes/01-pwa-offline.md`) reach the cards that build on them. SYNC-13 starts from the update bar SPIKE-1 built, adds regular update checks, and gains a *Watch out* line; SYNC-12 and SYNC-6 gain one each. The parking lot gains the workers' iPhone question, for Jack to decide, and what SPIKE-1 left untested. Marked **(v1.10)**.

**Changes in v1.9** (25 Oct 2026, SPIKE-1): two decisions by Jack, made while planning SPIKE-1. The card ships in two PRs: the demo deploys only from `main`, so the device tests need the spike's code there first, and the second PR's deploy tests the update prompt. The card also gains the *Learn* line its drill starts from. Marked **(v1.9)**.

**Changes in v1.8** (23 Oct 2026, OPS-2): DATA-1 gains a *Watch out* line from PPL-1's review. Django's `/admin/login/` has no rate limit yet. The demo goes public with OPS-2, but it has no accounts, and the free plan has no shell to make one, so there is nothing to guess. DATA-1's seed makes the first account with a password, so the limit, or keeping that account out of the admin, comes with it. Marked **(v1.8)**.

**Changes in v1.7** (23 Oct 2026, OPS-1): the image job becomes the fourth required check on `main`, Jack's decision while planning OPS-1. From OPS-2 on, every merge to `main` deploys the demo, so an image that doesn't build or serve must block the merge, not break the demo after it. OPS-1's *Build* line and *Done when* say so. OPS-2 also gains a *Watch out* line from OPS-1's review: on the demo, Render's proxy is the way in, not Caddy, and the image's cache and compression settings are still open. Marked **(v1.7)**.

**Changes in v1.6** (23 Oct 2026, PPL-1): two decisions by Jack, made while planning PPL-1. PPL-1 asks an email only of the superuser, because most of the shop's people have a phone but no email, so AUTH-3 gains a *Decide first* line on logins and lost passwords without email. Each group gets its own web address on one server: parking lot item 11, to become an ADR with OWN-3. Marked **(v1.6)**.

**Changes in v1.5** (22 Oct 2026, CORE-1): PPL-1 gains a *Watch out* line on the admin's `id`: CORE-1 keeps the id editable on purpose, so DRF keeps the id a client sends (ADR-0007). Marked **(v1.5)**.

**Changes in v1.4** (22 Oct 2026, REPO-7): release-please runs with a token from the repo's own GitHub App, so its release PR runs CI like any other (03 v1.5). REPO-7's *Build* line says so, and OPS-6 gives the deploy its new reason for being a job of the release workflow. OPS-1's note on job names also lists `pr-title`, the third required check. Marked **(v1.4)**.

**Changes in v1.3** (21 Oct 2026, REPO-6): `control` sits on layer 0, beside `core` (ADR-0035, 03 v1.4), so REPO-6 lists three exceptions; CORE-2 and PPL-3 gain a *Watch out* line on what the layer contract refuses. After REPO-5, OPS-1 also adds a Dependabot entry for its `Dockerfile` and keeps its job name clear of the required checks. Marked **(v1.3)**.

**Changes in v1.2** (20 Oct 2026, REPO-4): OPS-5's Caddyfile also sends `/_allauth/` to Django, so password login works in production as it does locally (ADR-0017, 03 v1.3). Marked **(v1.2)**.

**Changes in v1.1** (18 Oct 2026, portion 5): Claude Code makes every commit, but only after Jack says yes, and every commit message carries a `Why:` line (BP11, §3.3–§3.5, REPO-5 — confirmed with Jack); a task starts with `/start-task` and ends with `/finish-task` (§3.3); a changed decision is a new ADR that supersedes the old one (§3.2, §19); REPO-1, REPO-3 and REPO-4 gain the Claude Code setup, the script names and the guard tests; Appendix A is applied. Each change is marked **(v1.1)**.

---

## 0. How to read this

- A **build plan** turns the spec, the domain model and the architecture into an ordered list of small tasks, so building becomes "do the next task".
- **Stage** = a group of tasks that ends in something you can see: a demo, a deploy or a go-live. **Epic** = one feature area, a parent issue on GitHub (e.g. `SYNC`). **Task** = the smallest piece of work: one issue, one branch, one pull request, one Claude Code session.
- **Task IDs** are an epic code plus a number (`CORE-2`, `SYNC-8`). Once the issues exist they never change, even when a task moves to another stage. The codes are listed in §4.
- **Sizes:** `S` ≈ 1–2 hours, `M` ≈ 2–4 hours. Nothing bigger — it is split first (BP3).
- **Modes:** *Jack-led* or *Claude-led* (BP10).
- **Order:** every stage lists its tasks in build order — take them top to bottom.
- **Detail:** stages 0–2 have full task cards (§5–§7). Stages 3–7 have one line per task; their cards are written at the review that ends the stage before (BP2). R2 and R3 are outlines.
- Tags **[R1] [R1b] [R2] [R3] [Later]** as before, plus **[R1+]**: the R1 follow-ups shipped in the weeks after the Drugs go-live (BP8). **✚** = added beyond the three docs; veto freely.

## 1. The plan on one page

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    S0["0 · Groundwork"] --> S1["1 · Foundation"] --> S2["2 · Offline engine"] --> S4["4 · Attendance<br/>built"]
    S4 --> S3["3 · Ready for real data"] --> L1["Attendance live<br/>R1b"]
    L1 --> S5["5 · Catalog +<br/>sale skeleton"] --> S6["6 · Drugs side live<br/>R1"] --> S7["7 · R1 follow-ups<br/>R1+"]
    S7 --> R2["R2 · Foods side"] --> R3["R3 · Customers"]
```

| Stage | Ends with | Milestone | Rough hours |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 Groundwork | CI green, the demo deploys itself, the two biggest unknowns answered | Foundation | 30–45 |
| 1 Foundation | Pair a device and log in with a PIN; shops can't see each other; ledgers refuse edits | Foundation | 65–90 |
| 2 Offline engine | A clock-in made offline reaches the server exactly once; anyone can try it on the demo | Foundation | 50–65 |
| 3 Ready for real data | Production server, tested backups and alerts — before any real data | Foundation | 25–35 |
| 4 Attendance live (R1b) | Every worker clocks in; the first month's pay is calculated | R1b · Attendance | 45–60 |
| 5 Catalog + sale skeleton | Products searchable offline; one sale from the device to the ledgers and back | R1 · Drugs | 40–55 |
| 6 Drugs side live (R1) | The Drugs side runs on it; SmartMauzo and the paper books stop | R1 · Drugs | 100–140 |
| 7 R1 follow-ups (R1+) | Returns, daily counts, shopping list, exports — the weeks after go-live | R1+ | 25–35 |

Stage 4's build cards run before stage 3: the server is rented only once attendance works on the demo, right before its go-live (BP14) **(v1.74)**.

Hours = task sizes plus up to 40% for learning, debugging and review: about **350–490 hours** to the Drugs go-live. They are replaced by real numbers after the first ten tasks (§19).

**Rough calendar.** Building starts in early November 2026, after portions 5 and 6, at about 8 hours a day, 5 days a week (Jack, 18 Oct). *Ready* means the part works on the real server; the shop starts using it when the owner agrees (BP7). Attendance starts on the 1st of a month (§16). The offline demo — the part worth showing recruiters — is ready at the end of stage 2.

| Hours a week | Offline demo | Attendance ready | Drugs side ready |
|---|---|---|---|
| **40** *(8 h × 5 days)* | **early December 2026** | **mid-December 2026 — in use from January** | **late January 2027** |
| 30 | mid-December 2026 | end of December 2026 — in use from January | mid-February 2027 |
| 20 | end of December 2026 | end of January 2027 — in use from February | early April 2027 |

The Foods side (R2) isn't sized yet; at 40 hours a week it is ready around March–April 2027. Eight hours a day next to shop work is a lot, so the real hours are checked after the first ten tasks and the dates move with them (§19).

## 2. Key decisions

BP7 and BP16 are ADR-0033 and ADR-0034 (BP5 is covered by ADR-0012, D12's ADR); the others are working rules in `CLAUDE.md` and in this plan **(v1.1)**.

**BP1 — Stages end in something you can see.**
Stages 0–3 build the base once. From stage 4, each stage ends in a go-live or a demo, then a short review.
*Why:* progress you can show to the owner and to recruiters, and a fixed point to stop and re-plan.

**BP2 — Near work in detail, far work in outline.** ✚
Stages 0–2 have full cards now. Later stages have one line per task; their cards are written at the review that ends the stage before.
*Why:* "many things will change while building" — cards for month five would be stale by then.

**BP3 — One task = one branch = one PR = one Claude Code session, and it fits one sitting.**
`S` ≈ 1–2 h, `M` ≈ 2–4 h; anything bigger is split before it starts.
*Why:* a task fits an evening after the shop closes, and small PRs are easy to review and to learn from.

**BP4 — Vertical slices after the foundation.**
Each task goes through every layer it needs — model, service, API, screen, tests — and works when merged.
*Why:* nothing half-built waits for months; every merge can be shown.

**BP5 — The custom `User` comes before any `migrate`, in stage 0.** ✚
It lands before the demo's first deploy, because that deploy runs migrations.
*Why:* Django can't swap its user model after the first migration (02 D12). *Cost:* very high if missed.

**BP6 — The clock-in is the first offline document.** ✚
The sync engine is proven end to end with a clock-in (SYNC-11); the sale then reuses it (SALE-4).
*Why:* it is the smallest real document — one table, no money, no stock — so the hardest part (sync) is learned on simple ground. This holds whichever release goes first.

**BP7 — One website with everything, built one part after another: attendance first.** ✚
The website holds every part — attendance, the Drugs side and the Foods side — in one repo, at one address (confirmed with Jack). The parts are built in this order: attendance (R1b), then the Drugs side (R1), then the Foods side (R2). Each part is switched on in settings; the shop starts using it once the owner agrees, at the review before its go-live *(default: as soon as it is ready)*.
*Why attendance first:* it is the smallest real feature; every worker on both sides uses it daily; it fixes problem #2 (spec §4); and it tests the devices, PINs, offline storage and power cuts on real shop days before any money depends on them — while the Drugs product data is prepared.
*Cost:* the Drugs side (problem #1) is ready a few weeks later than if it were built first; the product name, the web address and the PDPC registration are needed sooner (§15).
*If the shop prefers to start several parts on the same day:* the build order stays the same; only the go-live dates in §16 move.

**BP8 — R1 goes live in two steps.** ✚ *(default)*
**R1** is what replaces SmartMauzo and the paper books on the Drugs side. **R1+** — partial returns, daily counts, reorder levels and the shopping list, WhatsApp receipts, exports, debt aging, debt adjustments, merging duplicate customers and the Lipa Namba check — follows in the weeks after go-live, with normal deploys (§12).
*Why:* real use sooner; none of R1+ is needed on day one (a full return is a cancel). If the printer spike fails, WhatsApp receipts move into R1.

**BP9 — Tests first where the answer is known.**
Calculations, posting, rules and sync are written test-first; screens get tests alongside. The worked examples (02 §18) and the attendance table (spec §8.1) become named tests, and E1–E5 also become shared test vectors (03 §8).
*Why:* the docs already hold the expected numbers.

**BP10 — Jack-led and Claude-led tasks.** ✚
The first task of each new kind is *Jack-led*: a short drill on the idea first (the card's **Learn** line), then Claude writes the tests and explains, Jack writes the main code, and Claude reviews. The rest are *Claude-led*: Claude Code writes; Jack reviews and explains it back in the PR. About one task in four is Jack-led.
*Why:* job interviews ask you to explain and write code yourself, not only to ship it; it also matches "drill, then apply the same day".

**BP11 — GitHub flow, squash merge, conventional commits.**
Short branches from `main`. The PR title is a conventional commit, checked in CI. Squash-merge puts one commit per task on `main`; the small commits stay visible inside each PR. release-please ✚ writes the changelog and the version tags from those titles (REPO-7): `v0.x` until the first go-live, `v1.0.0` at it.
**Every commit is approved by Jack** (confirmed with Jack) **(v1.1)**: Claude Code makes the commits in both modes, but before each one it says plainly what was done and why, and it commits only after Jack says yes. Every commit message carries a `Why:` line with a strong reason (§3.5); a `commit-msg` hook refuses a message without one (REPO-5).
*Why:* a history anyone can read, releases with no manual bookkeeping, and every step of the history understood and approved by Jack.

**BP12 — This file is the plan; GitHub tracks the status.**
Issues are created by script for the current and the next stage only.
*Why:* 150 issues created today would go stale and bury the next task.

**BP13 — Data preparation and baselines start now.** ✚
The product, customer, debt and staff sheets (Appendix B) are fixed today, so the shop can fill them in while the code is built. Baselines (spec §12) are measured before each go-live — starting with this month's month-end pay.
*Why:* entering hundreds of products with selling units often takes longer than building the import, and the "before" numbers vanish at go-live.

**BP14 — The production server is rented just before the first go-live.** ✚
Stage 3 always sits right before it: after stage 4's build cards, before ATT-7 and LIVE-1 **(v1.74)**.
*Why:* no rent for an idle server; the demo covers everything before that, and the owner sees attendance working on it before paying **(v1.74)**.

**BP15 — The demo opens without pairing.** ✚
On the demo only, *Jaribu demo* registers the visitor's browser as a device and shows the demo staff with their PINs (OPS-3).
*Why:* a recruiter will not pair a device, and the offline counter is the part worth showing.

**BP16 — No open-source licence yet.** ✚ *(default)*
The repo is public, with all rights reserved and one line in the README saying so.
*Why:* it is the only reversible choice — code released under an open licence stays open forever, and the product may be sold to other shops later. Revisit before R3 or a first outside contributor.

## 3. How every task runs

### 3.1 The task card

```markdown
### CORE-2 · Tenancy guard that fails closed
`M` · Jack-led · after SHOP-1 · branch `feat/core-2-tenancy-guard`
**Learn:** what you will understand afterwards (the drill, for Jack-led tasks).
**Build:** what to make, in a few lines.
**Done when:**
- [ ] checks anyone can verify
**Tests:** `test_names_to_write_first`
**Commits:** `test(core): …` → `feat(core): …`
```

- **after** = tasks that must be merged first. **needs** = an answer from the owner or the shop (§15).
- **Done when** is the task's acceptance test: when every box is ticked, it is done.
- **Commits** is a suggested order of small commits, one tested step each.

### 3.2 Ready and done

**Ready** — a task can start when the tasks it comes after are merged, its card is clear, any owner answer it needs is in (or a default is agreed), and it fits one sitting.

**Done** — a task is done when:
1. Every *Done when* box is ticked.
2. Its tests pass in CI; worked examples have their named tests.
3. Every file has a header comment, and comments explain *why* — generously, for learning.
4. Logic sits only in services and calculations; import-linter passes; services and calculations keep at least 90% test coverage (03 §11).
5. Migrations have been read line by line; `makemigrations --check` is clean.
6. The API schema and the TypeScript types are regenerated, with no difference.
7. New screen text is in `sw.json` and `en.json`; Jack has checked the Swahili.
8. The demo seed covers the feature, if it needs data.
9. If a decision changed: a new ADR that supersedes the old one, plus the matching change to 02, 03 or this plan, in a `docs:` commit (ADR-0000) **(v1.1)**.
10. The PR is squash-merged with green CI, the card is ticked in this file, and the hours are noted in the PR.

### 3.3 A Claude Code session, step by step

1. Take the next *Ready* card on the board and move it to *In progress*.
2. Create the branch (§3.4, steps 1–2).
3. Start Claude Code — it opens in **plan mode** — and type `/start-task CORE-2`. It reads CLAUDE.md and the card, checks the card is *Ready*, and plans small steps, each ending in a commit with its reason. Nothing is written yet **(v1.1)**.
4. Read the plan, ask *why* until every step makes sense, then approve. A *Jack-led* task starts with a 15-minute drill on the idea; then Claude writes the tests, Jack writes the main code, and Claude reviews.
5. Build step by step — tests first where the card says so. After each green step, Claude Code says plainly what was done and why, shows the commit message, and commits only after Jack says **yes** **(v1.1)**.
6. Type `/finish-task`: it runs `make check` (everything CI runs), goes through the Done list, asks the `reviewer` subagent for a fresh review, helps write **What I learned** in 3–5 lines, and drafts the PR **(v1.1)**.
7. Push, open the PR and fill in the template.
8. When CI is green: squash-merge, tick the card here, note the hours, `/clear`.

If a task runs past twice its size: stop, write what blocks it in the issue, then split it or bring it to a chat.

### 3.4 The git routine

```bash
# 1. Start from the latest main
git switch main
git pull --ff-only

# 2. One branch per task, named on its card
git switch -c feat/core-2-tenancy-guard

# 3. Commit each small, tested step. Claude Code does this after your "yes";
#    by hand it looks like this (the second -m is the reason, §3.5):
git status                 # what changed?
git add -p                 # pick the pieces that belong together (or: git add <file>)
git commit -m "test(core): a query without a shop raises" \
           -m "Why: forgetting the shop must fail loudly, not leak another shop's rows (ADR-0022)."

# 4. Run every check CI runs
make check

# 5. Push the branch and open the pull request (the template opens in the browser)
git push -u origin feat/core-2-tenancy-guard
gh pr create --web

# 6. If main moved while you worked, bring its changes in (no force-push needed)
git fetch origin
git merge origin/main

# 7. CI is green: squash-merge, delete the branch, update main
gh pr merge --squash --delete-branch
git switch main
git pull --ff-only
```

### 3.5 Commit messages

`type(scope): what it does, in the present tense` — then a blank line and a `Why:` line **(v1.1)**.

- **Types:** `feat` (new behaviour), `fix`, `test`, `refactor` (same behaviour, better code), `docs`, `chore` (tooling), `ci`, `build`, `perf`.
- **Scope:** the app or area — `core`, `people`, `sync`, `sales`, `frontend`, `deploy`…
- **Why:** one or two sentences on why this change is needed now — the rule, test or *Done when* item it serves — not a repeat of the subject. Every commit has one (confirmed with Jack), and a `commit-msg` hook refuses a commit without it (REPO-5). No strong reason means the change belongs in another commit **(v1.1)**.
- **Subject examples:** `feat(people): custom User with phone and email` · `test(sync): same batch twice posts once` · `fix(money): expected cash ignored cash change`.
- The PR title uses the same format: it becomes the one commit on `main`, and release-please reads it.

## 4. GitHub setup

Done in portion 6.

- **Milestones:** `Foundation` (stages 0–3) · `R1b · Attendance` · `R1 · Drugs` · `R1+` · `R2 · Foods` · `R3 · Customers`.
- **Epics:** one parent issue per code below, with its tasks as sub-issues. R2 adds its own epics (§13) at the stage 7 review.

| Code | Epic | Code | Epic |
|---|---|---|---|
| `REPO` | Repository, tooling, CI | `ATT` | Attendance |
| `SPIKE` | Questions answered by a quick experiment | `WAGE` | Pay runs and payslips |
| `CORE` | Base models, tenancy, triggers, settings, numbers | `CAT` | Products and prices |
| `SHOP` | Shops, sides, locations, accounts, settings screen | `STK` | Stock |
| `PPL` | Users, staff, roles | `SALE` | Selling |
| `AUTH` | Devices, PIN and password logins | `MONEY` | Payments |
| `CTRL` | Flags, audit, attachments | `CASH` | Drawers and cash-up |
| `UI` | App foundations and shared screens | `DEBT` | Customers and credit |
| `SYNC` | The offline engine | `RPT` | Reports and exports |
| `DATA` | Imports and the demo seed | `OPS` | Images, servers, deploys, backups, monitoring |
| `LIVE` | Going live (mostly not code) | `FOLIO` | Portfolio |

- **Issue title:** `CORE-2 · Tenancy guard that fails closed`; the body is the card. A task issue template matches the card.
- **Labels:** `size:S` `size:M` · `mode:jack-led` `mode:claude-led` · `type:feat|fix|chore|docs|spike|ops` · `needs-owner` (waiting for an answer) · `owner` (the shop and owner track, §15) · `blocked`.
- **Project board:** Backlog → Ready → In progress → In review → Done, with the fields *Stage*, *Size*, *Mode* and *Hours*.
- **PR template:** task ID and `Closes #…`; what and why; how it was tested; screenshots; *What I learned*; the Done checklist; hours.
- **Protection:** `main` changes only through PRs with green CI, for admins too; squash-merge only; no force-push; secret scanning and push protection on; Dependabot weekly.
- **Issues are created by a script** using `gh`, from the cards of the current and the next stage (BP12); the owner track (§15) becomes issues labelled `owner`.

## 5. Stage 0 — Groundwork

**Goal:** an empty but real repo — CI green, the demo deploying itself, and the two biggest unknowns (offline on the shop's own devices, and printing) answered in the first weeks.
**Done when:** a PR runs CI green; merging deploys the demo; both spike reports are in `docs/spikes/`.
**You'll learn:** uv and a Django project layout; Next.js as a static app; Docker Compose and a reverse proxy; GitHub Actions; container images; service workers; printing with CSS.
**Needs:** OWN-1 (printer details), OWN-2 (which shop devices).
**Order:** top to bottom. SPIKE-2 and FOLIO-1 can move anywhere after OPS-2.

### REPO-1 · Repository and GitHub setup
Done in **portion 6**, in chat — not with Claude Code.
**Done when:**
- [x] A public GitHub repo with the layout of 03 §4, docs 01–04, the ADRs, `CLAUDE.md` and `.claude/` (unpacked from the portion 5 bundles), a README stub and `.editorconfig` **(v1.1)**.
- [x] `.gitignore` covers `.env` and `.env.*` (but not `.env.example`), `private/`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, `.claude/settings.local.json`, `__pycache__/`, `node_modules/`, `.next/` and `frontend/out/` **(v1.1)**.
- [x] Templates, labels, milestones and the issues for stages 0 and 1 exist (§4).
- [x] `main` is protected; secret scanning and push protection are on.

### REPO-2 · Backend skeleton
`M` · Claude-led · after REPO-1 · branch `chore/repo-2-backend-skeleton`
**Learn:** a uv project; a Django project with settings split by environment; how pytest-django finds them.
**Build:** `backend/` with Python 3.14, Django 6.1 and DRF through uv; `config/settings/{base,dev,test,prod}.py` reading environment variables; the 13 empty apps of 03 §5.1 under `apps/`, each with a header saying what it owns; `GET /api/health/` → `{"status": "ok"}`; ruff, mypy + django-stubs, pytest + pytest-django.
**⚠ Don't run `migrate` on any database until PPL-1 is merged** (BP5).
**Done when:**
- [x] `uv run pytest`, `ruff check` and `mypy` pass; `manage.py check` is clean.
- [x] Every app says in one line what it owns.
**Tests:** `test_health_returns_ok`
**Commits:** `chore(backend): uv project with Django 6.1` → `chore(backend): settings per environment` → `chore(backend): empty domain apps` → `chore(backend): ruff, mypy and pytest` → `feat(core): health endpoint`

### REPO-3 · Frontend skeleton
`M` · Claude-led · after REPO-1 · branch `chore/repo-3-frontend-skeleton`
**Learn:** Next.js used as a static app (`output: 'export'`) and why the counter needs that (03 AD2).
**Build:** `frontend/` with pnpm, Next.js 16 (static export, `images.unoptimized`), React 19, TypeScript strict, Tailwind v4, shadcn/ui, ESLint, Prettier, Vitest; a *Karibu* page; the scripts `dev`, `build`, `test`, `lint` and `typecheck` (`.claude/rules/frontend.md`) **(v1.1)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] `pnpm build` writes plain files to `out/` that open with no Node server.
- [x] Lint, type check and one Vitest test pass.
**Commits:** `chore(frontend): next.js static export` → `chore(frontend): tailwind and shadcn` → `chore(frontend): lint, format and vitest`

### REPO-4 · Local development like production
`M` · Claude-led · after REPO-2, REPO-3 · branch `chore/repo-4-local-dev`
**Learn:** Docker Compose; a reverse proxy; why one address matters for cookies.
**Build:** `compose.dev.yml` with PostgreSQL 18 and Caddy on `localhost:8080` (`/api/` and `/_allauth/` to Django on your machine, everything else to the Next.js dev server); a `Makefile` (`dev`, `test`, `check`, `fmt`, `migrate` — `check` also runs the guard's tests: `python3 -m unittest discover -s .claude/hooks` **(v1.1)**); `.env.example` with every setting; "Run it locally" in the README.
**Watch out:** Caddy runs in Docker while Django and Next.js run on your machine, so Caddy reaches them through `host.docker.internal` (on Linux, add `extra_hosts: ["host.docker.internal:host-gateway"]`). Still no `migrate` until PPL-1.
**Done when:**
- [x] From a fresh clone, following the README, `localhost:8080` shows *Karibu* and `localhost:8080/api/health/` answers from the same address.
- [x] Django connects to the PostgreSQL in Compose.
- [x] `make check` runs the guard's tests too **(v1.1)**.
**Commits:** `chore(dev): postgres and caddy in compose` → `chore(dev): make commands` → `docs: run it locally`

### REPO-5 · CI and branch protection
`M` · Claude-led · after REPO-4 · branch `ci/repo-5-ci`
**Learn:** GitHub Actions jobs, service containers and caching; why `main` is protected even when you work alone.
**Build:** `ci.yml` — a backend job (uv, ruff, mypy, `makemigrations --check`, pytest with a coverage report on a PostgreSQL 18 service) and a frontend job (pnpm, lint, types, Vitest, `pnpm build`); pre-commit hooks (ruff, Prettier) and a `commit-msg` hook that refuses a message without a conventional subject and a `Why:` line (§3.5) **(v1.1)**; Dependabot weekly for uv, pnpm, Actions and Docker; branch protection (§4), with no bypass for admins.
**Done when:**
- [x] A PR shows two green checks; a lint error turns it red.
- [x] A direct push to `main` is refused — your own included.
- [x] A commit without a `Why:` line is refused on Jack's machine **(v1.1)**.
**Commits:** `ci: backend checks` → `ci: frontend checks` → `ci: require both checks on main` → `chore: pre-commit hooks` → `chore: commit message check` → `ci: dependabot`

### REPO-6 · Module boundaries in CI
`S` · Jack-led · after REPO-5 · branch `ci/repo-6-import-linter`
**Learn:** a layered architecture, turned into a test.
**Build:** import-linter contracts for the layers in 03 §5.1, with the allowed exceptions (`control` → `core`, `inventory` → `money`, `services` → `sales`); every app may import `control`, which imports only `core` (ADR-0035) **(v1.3)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] CI runs the contracts; a test commit where `core` imports `sales` goes red, then is removed.
**Commits:** `ci(backend): layer contracts`

### REPO-7 · Changelog and version tags
`S` · Claude-led · after REPO-5 · branch `ci/repo-7-release-please`
**Learn:** semantic versioning; how a changelog is written from commit messages (BP11).
**Build:** a CI check that PR titles follow the commit format (§3.5); release-please reading the squash-merged titles, starting at `v0.1.0`. It runs with a token from the repo's own GitHub App, so the release PR gets its required checks **(v1.4)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] A PR titled "update stuff" fails the title check.
- [x] After a `feat:` merge, release-please opens a release PR; merging it writes `CHANGELOG.md` and tags `v0.1.0`.
**Commits:** `ci: check pull request titles` → `ci: require the title check on main` → `ci: release-please` → `docs: release-please runs with an app token` → `docs: correct what release-please reads from a pull request` → `test: more pull request title shapes`

### CORE-1 · Base model classes
`S` · Jack-led · after REPO-2 · branch `feat/core-1-base-models`
**Learn:** abstract models; UUIDv7 and why time-ordered IDs keep indexes fast.
**Build:** in `core`: `UUIDModel` (primary key from `uuid.uuid7()`) and `TimeStamped` (`created_at`). Abstract only: no tables.
**Done when:**
- [x] A model built on them gets version-7 ids that sort by creation time.
- [x] `makemigrations --check` finds nothing to do.
**Tests:** `test_ids_are_uuid_version_7` · `test_ids_sort_by_creation_time` · `test_serializers_keep_the_id_a_client_sends`
**Commits:** `test(core): uuid7 primary keys` → `feat(core): abstract base models` → `fix(core): keep the id a client sends` → `docs(core): true comments on the base models`

### PPL-1 · Custom User — the first migration
`M` · Jack-led · after CORE-1, REPO-4 · branch `feat/ppl-1-custom-user`
**Learn:** why the user model must be swapped before the first migration; user managers without usernames; phone numbers in international form.
**Build:** `people.User` (02 §5): UUIDv7 id, `full_name`, `phone` (optional, unique, stored as `+255…`), `email` (optional, unique whatever the letter case), password optional (PIN-only staff have none), `language`; no username; a manager with `create_user` and `create_superuser` (by email); `AUTH_USER_MODEL = "people.User"`; the admin; a phone normaliser in `core` (the `phonenumbers` library ✚, default region TZ).
**Watch out:** list `id` in the admin's `readonly_fields`. CORE-1 keeps the id editable on purpose: with `editable=False`, DRF would drop the id a client sends (ADR-0007). An id changed in the admin would save a copy of the row **(v1.5)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] On an empty database, `make migrate` creates `people.User` before `admin`, and `createsuperuser` works by email.
- [x] `0712 345 678` is stored as `+255712345678`.
- [x] Two users can't share a phone, or an email in any letter case; a user can exist without a password.
**Tests:** `test_phone_stored_as_plus255` · `test_email_unique_ignoring_case` · `test_user_without_password` · `test_superuser_needs_email`
**Commits:** `test(people): user rules` → `feat(core): phone normaliser` → `feat(people): custom User model` → `feat(people): user manager and admin`

### OPS-1 · The backend image
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-1, REPO-5 · branch `build/ops-1-backend-image`
**Learn:** Docker images and multi-stage builds; why containers shouldn't run as root.
**Build:** a multi-stage `Dockerfile`: build the frontend, copy `out/` in, Django serves it with WhiteNoise (the demo's setup, 03 §13); gunicorn; a non-root user; CI builds the image on every PR (03 §12). Add a `docker` entry to `.github/dependabot.yml` for the new `Dockerfile` (REPO-5 watches only the compose images), and name the image job none of `backend`, `frontend` and `pr-title`: those are the required checks **(v1.3, v1.4)**. Then make the image job the fourth required check (`.github/rulesets/main.json`, applied with `protect`): every merge will deploy the demo (OPS-2), so a broken image must block the merge **(v1.7)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] `docker run` of the image serves *Karibu* and `/api/health/` from one address.
- [x] The image builds in CI on every PR.
- [x] A PR whose image check is red can't merge **(v1.7)**.
**Commits:** `build: backend image with the static app` → `ci: build the image on pull requests` → `ci: dependabot watches the backend image` → `ci: require the image check on main` **(v1.7)**

### OPS-2 · The demo deploys itself
`M` · Claude-led · after OPS-1 · branch `ci/ops-2-demo-deploy`
**Learn:** container registries; deploy hooks; smoke tests.
**Build:** `deploy-demo.yml`: merge to `main` → push the image to GitHub Container Registry → Render deploy hook → smoke test `/api/health/`; a free Render web service with free PostgreSQL (fake data only); migrations run when the service starts; `docs/runbooks/demo.md`, including re-creating the free database every 30 days (03 AD15).
**Watch out:** on the demo, Render's proxy is the way in, not Caddy. `prod.py` trusts `X-Forwarded-Proto` because "Caddy is the one way in": check that Render's proxy also overwrites that header, and make the comment true. The image serves every file with a 60-second cache and no compression; long caching for the hashed `_next/static/` files (`WHITENOISE_IMMUTABLE_FILE_TEST`) and compressed admin files are still open **(v1.7)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] A merge to `main` updates the demo with no manual step; a failing smoke test turns the workflow red.
- [x] The demo shows *Karibu* over HTTPS.
**Commits:** `ci: deploy the demo on merge` → `docs: demo runbook and link`

### SPIKE-1 · Offline on the real shop devices
`M` · Jack-led · after OPS-2 · needs OWN-2 · branch `spike/spike-1-pwa-offline`, then `spike/spike-1-results` **(v1.9)**
**Learn:** what makes a website installable as an app; how a service worker keeps the app's files and gets a new version; storage inside the browser, and why the browser may delete it **(v1.9)**.
**Question:** does the static app install, open with no internet and keep its data on the shop's own laptop and phone?
**Build:** manifest and icons; the Serwist service worker built after `next build` over `out/`; a throwaway `/spike` page that writes and reads a Dexie row and shows the result of `navigator.storage.persist()`; a visible version number.
**Watch out:** the demo deploys only from `main` (OPS-2), so the device tests need the spike's code merged first. The card ships in two PRs: the first brings the Serwist setup and the `/spike` page to the demo; after the device tests, the second removes the page and adds the report, and its deploy is the new deploy the update box needs. The shop's devices change, so the spike tests the laptops and the phone the shop uses at the time, and the report names them (OWN-2) **(v1.9)**. Jack tested one laptop and the phone; what was left untested is in the parking lot (§20) **(v1.10)**.
**Done when** (from the demo address, on the shop laptop and one Android phone):
- [x] It installs as an app and opens in airplane mode.
- [x] A Dexie row survives closing the browser and restarting the device.
- [x] Persistent storage is granted, or the reason it isn't is written down.
- [x] A new deploy shows an update prompt and switches.
- [x] Results, device models and Chrome versions are in `docs/spikes/01-pwa-offline.md`. The Serwist setup stays; the `/spike` page is removed.
**Commits:** the first PR: `docs: spike-1 card gets a learn line and two pull requests` → `feat(frontend): manifest and icons` → `feat(frontend): serwist service worker` → `feat(frontend): update prompt` → `feat(frontend): visible app version` → `feat(frontend): spike page for offline storage`; the second PR: `chore(frontend): remove the spike page` → `docs(spikes): offline results` **(v1.9)**

### SPIKE-2 · A slip from Chrome on the shop printer
`S` · Jack-led · after OPS-2 · needs OWN-1 · branch `spike/spike-2-printing`, then `spike/spike-2-results` **(v1.11)**
**Learn:** CSS for paper: print styles, `@page` and sizes in millimetres; how Chrome sends a page to the printer's driver; printing with no print window (kiosk printing) **(v1.11)**.
**Question:** can Chrome print a readable slip on the shop's printer in one click?
**Build:** a print page for 80 mm paper (OWN-1: an Epson receipt printer on USB) with Swahili text and a bold total, opened from the demo address on the printer laptop; normal printing first, then Chrome kiosk printing (`--kiosk-printing`) **(v1.11)**.
**Watch out:** CSS can't size a page "as long as the slip": `size: 80mm auto` is not valid CSS, so Chrome drops the whole line. The page measures the slip and sets `@page { size: 80mm <height>; margin: 0 }` itself; whether the driver's own roll-paper size works better is part of the answer. The demo deploys only from `main` (OPS-2), so the card ships in two PRs, as SPIKE-1 did: the first brings the page to the demo; after the printer test, the second removes it and adds the report. The shop's printer and laptop can change, so the report names the ones tested. The QR code is left out: the shop doesn't use one yet (§20, item 14) **(v1.11)**. From UI-7 **(v1.41)**: the slip now prints in Source Sans 3, so the report names the font too. If a real slip prints too thin, look at the font first.
**Done when:**
- [ ] A readable slip prints in one click.
- [ ] Model, driver, paper width, working margins and a photo are in `docs/spikes/02-printing.md`.
- [ ] If it fails, the fallback is written down (WhatsApp receipts move into R1; a fix is needed before R2's slips).
**Tests:** `the slip's words are in Swahili` · `the total is bold` · `the button prints` · `the page is as tall as the slip` **(v1.11)**
**Commits:** the first PR: `docs: spike-2 card gets a learn line and a valid page size` → `test(frontend): what a printed slip shows` → `feat(frontend): slip print page`; the second PR: `chore(frontend): remove the slip print page` → `docs(spikes): printing results` **(v1.11)**

### FOLIO-1 · README, first version
`S` · Claude-led · after OPS-2 · branch `docs/folio-1-readme`
**Done when:**
- [x] The README states the problem in three lines, the status ("in development"), the stack, links to the docs, the demo link and the CI badge.
- [x] It holds no real shop data.
**Commits:** `docs: readme first version` → `docs: readme runs migrate now that ppl-1 is merged` → `docs: readme matches the repo today` → `docs: readme in shorter sentences, with terms explained`

## 6. Stage 1 — Foundation

**Goal:** the backbone every feature stands on — shops and sides, tenancy that fails closed, append-only tables, dated settings, exact numbers, staff and permissions, devices and logins.
**Done when:** the Playwright test *pair a device → PIN login → idle lock → logout* passes in CI (UI-6); the tenancy sweep and the trigger tests are green.
**You'll learn:** abstract models and migrations; multi-tenancy; PostgreSQL triggers; decimals and rounding; DRF conventions; permissions; cookies, sessions and CSRF; django-allauth; hashing; end-to-end tests.
**Order:** top to bottom. CORE-5 can move anywhere — a good break from the database.

### SHOP-1 · The Shop
`S` · Claude-led · after PPL-1 · branch `feat/shop-1-shop`
**Build:** `shops.Shop` (02 §4): name, code, timezone (Africa/Dar_es_Salaam), currency (TZS), default language (sw), `kind` (live, demo, practice), `practice_of`, `status`; the admin.
**Done when:**
- [x] `kind` and `status` accept only their values (database check constraints); the code is unique.
**Tests:** `test_shop_kind_must_be_known` · `test_shop_code_unique`
**Commits:** `test(shops): shop rules` → `feat(shops): Shop model`

### CORE-2 · Tenancy guard that fails closed
`M` · Jack-led · after SHOP-1 · branch `feat/core-2-tenancy-guard`
**Learn:** context variables; custom model managers; why "fail closed" beats "remember to filter" (03 §5.4).
**Build:** a `current_shop` context variable with `shop_context(shop)` and `system_context()`; the `ShopScoped` abstract base (UUIDv7 id, `shop`, `created_at`; `created_by` comes in PPL-2, empty for rows the system makes) whose default manager filters by the current shop and raises when none is set; a separate unscoped manager for the superuser admin.
**Watch out:** `core` is layer 0, so the layer contract refuses any import of `shops` from it, also in tests and in `if TYPE_CHECKING:` blocks (REPO-6, ADR-0035). Give the tests their shops through fixtures in `backend/conftest.py`, which is outside `apps`, and decide here whether type-only imports should count (`exclude_type_checking_imports` in `backend/pyproject.toml`) **(v1.3)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] A scoped query with no current shop raises `NoShopSelected`.
- [x] Inside `shop_context(A)` only A's rows come back.
- [x] Reading across shops works only inside `system_context()` or through the admin's own `all_shops` manager; both are easy to search for in the code **(v1.13)**.
**Tests:** `test_query_without_shop_raises` · `test_shop_context_filters_rows` · `test_system_context_reads_all_shops`
**Commits:** `test(core): tenancy guard` → `feat(core): current shop context` → `feat(core): ShopScoped base and manager`

### PPL-2 · Staff members and PINs
`M` · Jack-led · after CORE-2 · branch `feat/ppl-2-staff`
**Learn:** a membership versus a user (02 D12); hashing with a server secret (a "pepper"); Argon2.
**Build:** `StaffMember` (02 §5): user, display name, job title, `started_on`, `left_on`, `status` (active ⇄ suspended → left), PIN hash, `pin_changed_at` (the optional photo comes with PPL-4 **(v1.14)**); `created_by` added to `ShopScoped` now that staff exist; services `add_staff`, `set_pin` (4 digits; no 0000, 1234 or four of a kind; Argon2 + server pepper), `suspend`, `unsuspend`, `mark_left`.
**Watch out:** `StaffMember` is the first real `ShopScoped` table (CORE-2). Only `save()` works with no current shop: `create()`, `get_or_create()`, `update()` and `full_clean()` read through the guarded `objects`, so services and tests run inside `shop_context(shop)`. The guard doesn't stop a row being written for another shop, so the services check it (T1). `created_by` on `ShopScoped` also changes the test-only `probe.Note` table: make its next migration with the test settings, or `make check` stays red **(v1.13)**. No admin page here: a staff admin page is its own task (§20, item 15) **(v1.14)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] One user can be staff in two shops.
- [x] A weak PIN is refused; a stored PIN can't be checked without the server secret.
- [x] A person who has left can't be given a PIN.
**Tests:** `test_one_user_staff_in_two_shops` · `test_weak_pin_refused` · `test_pin_check_needs_pepper` · `test_left_staff_cannot_get_pin`
**Commits:** `test(people): staff rules` → `feat(people): StaffMember model` → `feat(people): PIN hashing with pepper` → `feat(core): created_by on shop records`

### SHOP-2 · Sides, stock locations and cash drawers
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-2 · branch `feat/shop-2-sides`
**Build:** `Side` (name, one-letter code unique per shop, `sale_flow`, `is_live` off by default), `StockLocation` (one hidden default per side), `PaymentAccount` (kind, name, provider, number, sides, status); service `create_shop(name, code, sides)` → the shop, its sides, one default location and one cash drawer per side, in one transaction.
**Watch out:** `ShopScoped` now carries `created_by`, a link to `people.StaffMember` (PPL-2), so these tables' migrations depend on `people`'s. `shops` is layer 1 and may not import `people`, not even as a type (`exclude_type_checking_imports` is off): `create_shop` sets `created_by_id`, or a `people` service does the write, as PPL-3 already asks for the roles **(v1.14)**. Settled in SHOP-2: `create_shop` takes no "who". A brand-new shop has no staff yet, so every row it makes has `created_by` empty, like any row the system makes (02 §1) **(v1.15)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] `create_shop` with sides D and F makes 2 sides, 2 hidden locations and 2 cash drawers — or nothing at all if any step fails.
- [x] Side codes are unique within a shop; a new side starts switched off.
**Tests:** `test_create_shop_makes_location_and_drawer_per_side` · `test_create_shop_all_or_nothing` · `test_side_code_unique_within_shop` · `test_side_starts_switched_off`
**Commits:** `test(shops): create_shop` → `feat(shops): Side, StockLocation and PaymentAccount` → `feat(shops): create_shop service`

### CORE-3 · Append-only tables, enforced by PostgreSQL
`M` · Jack-led · after CORE-2 · branch `feat/core-3-append-only`
**Learn:** database triggers; why the database is the last line of defence (03 AD7, 02 T2).
**Build:** a migration operation that adds a trigger refusing UPDATE and DELETE on a table; a variant that allows filling the void stamp (`voided_at`, `voided_by`, `void_reason`, `void_approval`) once and nothing else; an `AppendOnly` base model, built on `ShopScoped`, that refuses the same in Python with a clear message. A base, not a mixin: a mixin can be listed in the wrong place, and then it never runs or drops the shop guard **(v1.16)**.
**Watch out** (for the cards that build void tables: MONEY-1, CASH-2, ATT-1) **(v1.16)**: `VoidStampTrigger` names the stamp's columns, so a void table uses exactly `voided_at`, `voided_by`, `void_reason` and `void_approval`. The trigger only checks that `voided_at` is filled once and that nothing else changes, so each table adds two rules of its own: a check constraint that a filled stamp is complete (a reason, and who: T3), and a check that `voided_by` and `void_approval` belong to the row's shop (T1). The void service fills the stamp with one guarded `update()` (`filter(pk=…, voided_at__isnull=True)`), never `save()`, which `AppendOnly` refuses. If it updates 0 rows, the row was already voided (a retry, or someone else first), so it returns the stored result (03 §5.5). The trigger's SQL is frozen once on `main`: a change is a new rule (`core_void_stamp_only_v2`) with new migrations. A later migration that must update ledger rows lifts the trigger on purpose, in a reviewed step.
**Done when:**
- [x] On a test table, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` fail in the database itself, not only in Django.
- [x] On a void table, the stamp can be filled once; a second change fails; changing any other column fails.
**Tests:** `test_append_only_update_fails_in_database` · `test_append_only_delete_fails` · `test_void_stamp_set_once` · `test_void_cannot_change_other_columns`
**Commits:** `test(core): append-only rules` → `feat(core): append-only trigger operation` → `feat(core): void-stamp variant` → `feat(core): append-only base model` **(v1.16)**

### CORE-4 · Dated settings
`M` · Claude-led · after CORE-3, SHOP-2 · branch `feat/core-4-settings`
**Build:** the settings registry in `core` (key, type, default, scope, description — 02 D13); `shops.Setting` rows (key, value, `effective_from`, optional side, note), insert-only; who set a value is the row's `created_by`, which every shop row has had since PPL-2, so there is no `set_by` column **(v1.18)**; `get_setting(key, shop, side=None, on=date)`; the keys R1 and R1b need — e.g. `attendance.on_time_until` 07:35, `attendance.late_fine` 1,000, `money.rounding_step` 1, `cash.tolerance` 0, `count.tolerance_pct` 0, `expiry.alert_days` 90, `stock.block_negative` off, `session.idle_minutes` 15. The two tolerances are 0 *(default)* until the owner agrees values (02 §20, item 2): every difference is flagged, and a new row changes them with no deploy **(v1.18)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] With no rows, the default comes back; the latest row on or before the date wins; a side row beats a shop row; tomorrow's row is ignored today.
- [x] A value of the wrong type is refused; rows can't be edited.
**Tests:** `test_default_when_no_rows` · `test_latest_row_on_or_before_date_wins` · `test_side_row_beats_shop_row` · `test_future_row_ignored_until_its_day` · `test_setting_type_checked` · `test_setting_rows_cannot_be_edited` · `test_same_day_fix_wins` · `test_setting_key_checked` · `test_get_setting_refuses_wrong_arguments` · `test_every_default_is_a_valid_value` · `test_value_kinds_read_and_write_text` **(v1.18)**
**Commits:** `docs: created_by records who set a setting or a price` → `test(core): setting rules` → `feat(core): settings registry` → `feat(shops): dated Setting rows` → `feat(shops): get_setting reads the rule in force` **(v1.18)**

### CORE-5 · Exact money and quantities
`M` · Jack-led · after REPO-2 · branch `feat/core-5-numbers`
**Learn:** why floats lose shillings; half-up rounding; one source of truth for two languages (03 §8).
**Build:** `core/numbers.py`: money (2 places) and quantity (3 places) helpers on `Decimal`; `round_money(amount, step)`, half-up; `split_into_units(quantity, factors)`, stock as whole selling units, biggest first, plus the rest in the base unit **(v1.19)**; `shared/test-vectors/` files with inputs and expected outputs as strings (`rounding.json`, `units.json` — E1's numbers among them); a pytest runner that loads every vector file.
**Done when:**
- [x] Every vector passes.
- [x] E1's 1,335.5 kg shows as 26 bags of 50 kg + 35.5 kg.
- [x] A lint rule or a test stops `float` from appearing in calculation code.
**Tests:** `test_vectors_rounding` · `test_vectors_units` · `test_e1_shown_as_bags_and_kg` · `test_no_floats_in_calculation_code` · `test_every_vector_file_is_run` · `test_bare_numbers_refused` **(v1.19)**
**Commits:** `test(core): rounding and unit vectors` → `feat(core): money and quantity helpers` → `test: no floats in calculation code` → `docs: core-5 card names the tests it built` **(v1.19)**

### CORE-6 · API conventions
`M` · Claude-led · after SHOP-2 · branch `feat/core-6-api`
**Build:** domain errors with codes and one exception handler (`{"code", "message", "fields", "params"}` — 03 §5.10); base views (tenancy and a per-side permission hook); idempotent creates (the client sends the UUID); the `Editable` base with `version` (stale → `409 version_conflict`); cursor pagination; drf-spectacular at `/api/schema/` and `/api/docs/` (public on the demo and on a laptop, closed in production **(v1.20)**).
**Watch out:** the guard checks the shop when a read is built (CORE-2). So the base view sets the current shop after DRF has authenticated the visit, around the whole view, and builds its rows in `get_queryset()`: `queryset = Side.objects.all()` in a class body raises as the module loads. DRF's unique check on `id` reads through the guarded manager, so it sees the current shop only: an id already taken in another shop passes it and fails at the insert (`IntegrityError`, a 500). Request code may not read across shops, so decide here how that failure becomes a clean refusal that reveals nothing (03 §5.4) **(v1.13)**. A shop code that is already taken still comes back with Django's generic `unique` code, not one of ours: `Shop.code`'s `unique=True` (SHOP-1) gives no way to name it. Give it a `shop.code_taken` code with the others **(v1.15)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] The Side endpoints show every behaviour above in tests.
- [x] The schema builds with no warnings.
**Tests:** `test_error_shape` · `test_shop_code_taken_has_its_own_code` · `test_side_list_shows_only_the_current_shops_sides` · `test_another_shops_side_is_not_found` · `test_side_routes_need_a_shop_login` · `test_shop_closed_after_the_visit` · `test_side_list_is_paged_with_a_cursor` · `test_side_permission_hook_checks_each_side` · `test_create_twice_with_same_uuid_makes_one_row` · `test_two_copies_at_the_same_moment_make_one_row` · `test_uuid_of_another_shop_refused_revealing_nothing` · `test_stale_version_gets_409` · `test_device_alone_cannot_act_on_office_routes` · `test_list_shows_only_the_sides_a_person_may_see` · `test_another_shops_side_cannot_be_renamed` · `test_create_needs_a_uuid7` · `test_crash_answers_in_the_error_shape_and_is_logged` · `test_docs_hidden_in_production` · `test_docs_public_on_the_demo` · `test_schema_builds_without_warnings` **(v1.20)**
**Commits:** `feat(core): error codes and handler` → `feat(shops): a taken shop code gets its own error code` → `feat(core): base views open the visit's shop` → `feat(core): idempotent creates` → `feat(core): version conflicts` → `feat(core): openapi schema` → `docs: core-6 card names the tests it built` **(v1.20)**

### CORE-7 · The tenancy sweep
`M` · Claude-led · after CORE-6 · branch `test/core-7-tenancy-sweep`
**Build:** one test module that finds every model and every list and detail route of our apps by itself: every model has `shop` (outside an allowed list: `User`, `Unit`, `Shop` **(v1.12)** and the infrastructure tables); every foreign key uses `PROTECT` (T3); every route called as shop B returns none of shop A's rows (03 §5.4).
**Watch out:** sweep the guard itself too (CORE-2). Every `ShopScoped` table's default manager and its `objects` must be a `ShopScopedManager`: an abstract base that writes a fresh `Meta` (its tables get `all_shops`), or a table that declares managers of its own (its own `objects`, or any manager beside a fresh `Meta`), drops the guard with no error. A table whose only change is a fresh `Meta` keeps it: Django takes the name from its first parent (CORE-3's review) **(v1.17)**. Also check that `all_shops` appears only in `admin.py` files, and `system_context(` only in jobs, management commands and the seed; today only a search finds them **(v1.13)**. Tests may use `system_context(` too: `test_create_shop_all_or_nothing` (SHOP-2) counts every shop's rows after a failed `create_shop`. A many-to-many field, such as `PaymentAccount.sides` (SHOP-2), has no `on_delete` of its own: skip it, and check its link table's foreign keys instead. List the models with `get_models(include_auto_created=True)`, so a link table Django makes by itself, with no `shop` and with CASCADE, turns CI red **(v1.15)**. Two more checks from CORE-6 **(v1.20)**: every table's primary-key rule keeps PostgreSQL's name, `<table>_pkey`, which the create relies on to tell a taken id from other clashes; and every route over a table that links a side names that link in `side_field` (None for a table of the whole shop). Settled in CORE-7 **(v1.21)**: tests may use `all_shops` too, as CORE-2's `test_admin_manager_reads_all_shops` tests the admin's door itself. The two link tables Django makes for `User`'s admin permissions (`people_user_groups`, `people_user_user_permissions`) have no `shop` and CASCADE; they sit on the sweep's list of infrastructure tables, because they hold Django's admin data, never a shop's: their CASCADE only tidies that data, as when an admin group is deleted (Jack, 28 Oct 2026). The sweep is never loosened, nor edited, to let a table or a route pass. What grows instead: the two shops it sends its visitors between, filled in `backend/tests/conftest.py`, which gets a row of each new route's table and the query parameters a route may need (`sweep_queries`); and the tables, routes and files allowed to break a rule, listed in `backend/tests/sweep_lists.py`, each with its reason. Teaching the sweep something new, such as calling the sync routes with a device's key, is a new check, in a reviewed change. Every route under `api/` must be an office route, or sit on that file's short list of routes that read no shop's table (health, schema, docs), so none escapes the sweep.
