After the first review, the route sweep also refuses two routes with one name, finds shop A's names and amounts as well as its ids, tries each edit a route takes, and stops after 20 pages; and the scan also finds Django's base manager, which only tests may use. After the second: it looks as shop A first, who must see its own rows through each route; sends POST too (a void, and a create sent an id of A's); wants each of A's rows answered exactly as an id no shop has; reads a download sent in pieces, and a CSV cell by cell; reports an answer it can't read, such as a PDF, unless `OWN_LEAK_TESTS` names the route's own leak test; needs a key only shop A has; and the scan also finds raw SQL and hand-built queries, which only the files in `HAND_BUILT_QUERIES` may build.
**Done when:**
- [x] A new model without `shop`, a foreign key that cascades, or a route that leaks turns CI red — with no change to the test.
**Tests:** `test_every_model_has_shop` · `test_every_foreign_key_protects` · `test_every_primary_key_keeps_its_postgres_name` · `test_every_shop_table_keeps_the_guard` · `test_all_shops_only_in_admin` · `test_system_context_only_in_system_code` · `test_base_manager_only_in_tests` · `test_raw_sql_only_in_migrations_and_system_code` · `test_hand_built_queries_only_where_listed` · `test_no_route_leaks_across_shops` · `test_every_side_link_is_named_on_its_route` · `test_every_api_route_is_swept` · `test_every_route_left_to_its_own_test_has_one`; and the checks' own: `test_sweep_lists_every_kind_of_table` · `test_shop_check_finds_tables_without_shop` · `test_link_check_finds_links_that_act_on_delete` · `test_primary_key_check_finds_a_key_with_another_name` · `test_primary_key_check_skips_database_views_only` · `test_guard_check_finds_tables_that_lose_the_guard` · `test_door_check_finds_every_use` · `test_call_check_finds_every_call` · `test_call_check_skips_types_and_plain_names` · `test_door_check_skips_comments_strings_and_the_definition` · `test_doors_are_allowed_only_in_their_places` · `test_door_check_reads_our_code` · `test_route_sweep_finds_the_side_routes` · `test_marks_leave_out_choice_words_and_zero_amounts` · `test_sweep_reads_shop_a_rows_past_every_manager` · `test_route_check_finds_a_route_that_leaks` · `test_route_check_needs_rows_to_look_for` · `test_route_check_tells_every_route_apart` · `test_route_check_finds_a_leak_that_shows_no_ids` · `test_route_check_reads_answers_that_are_not_json` · `test_route_check_reads_json_written_with_escape_codes` · `test_route_check_reads_a_csv_cell_by_cell` · `test_route_check_reads_a_download_sent_in_pieces` · `test_route_check_reports_an_answer_it_cannot_read` · `test_own_leak_test_check_finds_stale_names` · `test_route_check_sends_the_parameters_a_route_needs` · `test_route_check_needs_shop_a_to_see_its_own_rows` · `test_route_check_reports_a_list_it_cannot_look_in` · `test_route_check_tries_edits_of_shop_a_rows` · `test_route_check_sends_a_delete_last` · `test_a_crash_on_a_shared_key_turns_the_sweep_red` · `test_route_check_takes_an_edit_that_checks_what_was_sent_first` · `test_route_check_sends_posts_too` · `test_route_check_lets_a_shared_key_answer_with_shop_b_row` · `test_route_check_needs_a_key_only_shop_a_has` · `test_route_check_reads_every_page_and_stops` · `test_side_check_finds_routes_that_misname_the_side` · `test_side_check_sees_routes_that_only_make_rows` · `test_api_route_check_finds_a_route_off_the_base` **(v1.21)**
**Commits:** `test: model sweep` → `test: guard sweep` → `test: route sweep` → `docs: core-7 card names the tests it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the core-7 card and its notes follow the review`; then a second review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the core-7 card follows the second review` **(v1.21)**

### CTRL-1 · Flags
`M` · Claude-led · after CORE-2 · branch `feat/ctrl-1-flags` **(v1.22)**
**Build:** `control.Flag` (02 §13): kind from a code list, subject (type code + UUID), expected, actual, difference, people, side, status (open → explained → resolved | dismissed), notes; `raise_flag()` and `resolve_flag()` services. Also **(v1.22)**: the type codes, one list in `core` (`apps/core/links.py`, 03 §5.1), with `link_type_of(row)` and `load_link(code, id)`, which loads through the shop guard; the people as link rows (`FlagPerson`); the notes as append-only rows (`FlagNote`: who, when, the step and the words); and `explain_flag()` and `dismiss_flag()` beside the two services. After the review **(v1.22)**: each step names a person, never a device alone, and takes the phone's id for its note, so a step sent twice is written once; the people a flag names are append-only too; and a trigger of the flag table's own (`apps/control/operations.py`) keeps a flag's facts as raised and lets its status move forward only, in the database too.
**Watch out:** `Flag` is `ShopScoped`, so it carries `created_by`, a link to `people.StaffMember` (PPL-2), and its migration depends on `people`'s. `control` is layer 0 and may not import `people`, not even as a type **(v1.14)**; how its services take staff members instead was settled in CTRL-1, below **(v1.22)**. Settled in CTRL-1 **(v1.22)**: since CORE-6, who acts is an `Actor` (`apps/core/tenancy.py`), so the services take the people as Actors too, and the subject and the side as rows: each T1 check compares `shop_id`s, with no query. A generic link has no foreign key, so `raise_flag` checks that the subject is a saved row of the flag's shop. `FlagPerson` links to `StaffMember` twice (`staff`, and `created_by` from `ShopScoped`), so `Flag.people` names its `through_fields`. The list of type codes starts empty: no table a flag is about exists yet. A card that raises flags about its rows adds their code, and a code, once stored, never changes.
**Done when:**
- [x] Unknown kinds are refused; the status only moves forward; a flag's subject loads from its type code and UUID.
- [x] A flag can close straight from open, always with a note; every explanation is kept, and none can be edited (Jack, 29 Oct 2026; 02 v1.8) **(v1.22)**.
- [x] A person a flag names can explain it but never close it (Jack, at CTRL-1's review) **(v1.22)**.
**Tests:** `test_raise_flag_records_subject_and_people` · `test_flag_status_only_moves_forward` · `test_flag_closes_without_explanation` · `test_named_person_cannot_close_their_own_flag` · `test_every_explanation_is_kept` · `test_unknown_flag_kind_refused` · `test_flag_numbers_come_in_pairs` · `test_flag_subject_loads_from_its_type_code_and_uuid` · `test_flag_links_stay_in_one_shop` · `test_nightly_job_raises_each_shops_flags` · `test_flag_note_required` · `test_every_step_names_its_person` · `test_a_retried_step_is_written_once` · `test_a_step_checks_only_what_it_changes` · `test_flag_notes_cannot_be_edited` · `test_flag_people_cannot_be_changed` · `test_database_refuses_what_the_services_check_first`; the flag table's trigger: `test_flag_status_moves_forward_only_in_database` · `test_flag_facts_never_change_in_database` · `test_flag_never_deleted_in_database` · `test_flag_trigger_removed_when_migrated_back` · `test_flag_trigger_sql_never_changes_once_on_main`; and the type codes': `test_row_named_by_its_type_code` · `test_link_loads_its_row_inside_its_shop` · `test_table_without_type_code_refused` · `test_type_code_must_name_a_shop_table` · `test_every_type_code_fits_where_it_is_stored` · `test_type_code_check_finds_bad_codes` **(v1.22)**
**Commits:** `docs: flags close with a note and keep every explanation` → `feat(core): rows named by a type code and a UUID` → `test(control): flag rules` → `feat(control): Flag, its people and its notes` → `feat(control): raise, explain, resolve and dismiss a flag`, then the review's fixes, one commit each (two of them a docs commit and its fix, for Jack's own-flag rule), and `docs: the ctrl-1 card and its notes follow the review` **(v1.22)**

### CTRL-2 · Audit trail
`M` · Claude-led · after CTRL-1, PPL-2 · branch `feat/ctrl-2-audit`
**Build:** `control.AuditEvent` (append-only) and `audit(...)`; staff status changes audited. Office edits are recorded by a trigger of our own, `control_record_edit()`, into one append-only table, `control.EditEvent`: who, when, which row, and the columns that changed, before and after. The person comes from the visit, which tells PostgreSQL who acts (`apps/core/acting.py`) **(v1.23)**.
**Watch out** **(v1.23)**: Settled in CTRL-2 (Jack, 29 Oct 2026; ADR-0036): not django-pghistory, as this card first said. By default its tables, one per tracked table, are not built on `ShopScoped`, link with `DO_NOTHING` and have no guard, and its context table has no shop: CORE-7's sweep refuses them all, rightly. No device exists yet (AUTH-1), and a visit knows its device only from AUTH-4: the history and the audit trail name the person, and AUTH-8 adds the device **(v1.33)**. `approval` waits for the Approval table. The visit tells PostgreSQL who acts with `set_config`, which is raw SQL: CORE-7's sweep gets a list of the files allowed to run it, `RAW_SQL` in `backend/tests/sweep_lists.py`, with one file and its reason. The trigger makes ids with `uuidv7()`, so PostgreSQL 18 is needed (03 §3); its SQL is frozen once on `main`, as core's is. A PIN hash is never copied into the history. After the review **(v1.23)**: the visit also tells PostgreSQL its shop, and the trigger refuses an edit of another shop's row (T1); a guard refuses any history row the trigger didn't write; each row keeps its edit's own time (`clock_timestamp()`); `RecordEdits` checks its skip list against the table; services that pass their own `by` (`editing()`, the staff actions) tell PostgreSQL too, so the history agrees with `updated_by` and the audit line. Every shop table that is not a ledger keeps a history unless `NO_EDIT_HISTORY` lists it with its reason: payment accounts and stock locations keep one (Jack, at CTRL-2's review); flags (their notes) and account–side links (never edited) don't. A column named as a secret (`*_hash`, `*_key`, `*secret*`, `*password*`, `*token*`) must be on its table's skip list.
**Done when:**
- [x] Suspending a staff member writes one audit event with the reason; so do bringing one back and marking one as left **(v1.23)**.
- [x] Editing a staff member, or renaming a side through its route, leaves a history row naming the person; the device joins in AUTH-8 **(v1.23, v1.33)**.
- [x] Audit rows and history rows can't be edited or deleted, in Django and in PostgreSQL **(v1.23)**.
- [x] Every shop table people change records its edits, payment accounts and stock locations included: a new one that doesn't, or a secret column it would copy, turns CI red **(v1.23)**.
**Tests:** the audit events: `test_audit_records_who_what_and_why` · `test_suspend_writes_audit_event` · `test_unsuspend_and_leave_write_audit_events` · `test_refused_action_writes_no_audit_event` · `test_audit_needs_a_reason` · `test_audit_stays_in_one_shop` · `test_audit_needs_a_transaction` · `test_unknown_audit_action_refused` · `test_database_refuses_bad_audit_rows` · `test_audit_rows_append_only`; who acts: `test_acting_as_puts_back_who_acted_before` · `test_office_visit_tells_the_database_who_acts`; the history: `test_edit_recorded_with_person_and_changes` · `test_office_edit_recorded_with_person` · `test_staff_edit_recorded_with_person` · `test_pin_hash_never_copied_to_history` · `test_edit_outside_a_visit_names_nobody` · `test_edit_that_changes_nothing_writes_no_history` · `test_history_rows_append_only` · `test_edit_trigger_removed_when_migrated_back` · `test_record_edits_checks_its_names` · `test_edit_trigger_sql_never_changes_once_on_main` · `test_every_office_table_records_its_edits` · `test_history_check_finds_tables_that_lose_it`; and the review's: `test_only_the_trigger_writes_history` · `test_edit_of_another_shops_row_refused` · `test_each_edit_keeps_its_own_time` · `test_record_edits_checks_the_table_it_is_put_on` · `test_payment_account_number_change_recorded` · `test_history_check_finds_a_secret_that_would_be_copied` · `test_acting_as_leaves_a_failed_transaction_to_its_rollback` · `test_acting_as_leaves_a_broken_atomic_block_to_its_rollback` · `test_acting_as_costs_two_queries` · `test_audit_refuses_floats` · `test_service_edit_names_its_by_outside_a_visit` · `test_staff_action_names_its_by_outside_a_visit` **(v1.23)**
**Commits:** `docs: office edits are recorded by our own trigger` → `test(control): audit rules` → `feat(control): AuditEvent and audit()` → `feat(people): staff status changes write audit events` → `feat(core): each office visit tells the database who acts` → `feat(control): edit history written by the database` → `feat: staff members and sides keep their edit history` → `docs: the ctrl-2 card names the tests it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the ctrl-2 card and its notes follow the review` **(v1.23)**

### PPL-3 · Roles and permissions per side
`M` · Jack-led · after PPL-2, SHOP-2 · branch `feat/ppl-3-permissions`
**Learn:** role-based permissions, scoped by side (02 §5, rule X1).
**Build:** the permission codes in `core`; `Role`, `RoleAssignment` (role + side, or all sides) and `PermissionGrant`; the role templates (Owner, Manager, Seller, Order writer, Cashier, Worker) kept in code and copied into each new shop (Appendix A) by `set_up_shop`, a `people` service that makes the shop with `create_shop` **(v1.24)**; `effective_permissions(staff, side)` and `require(staff, permission, side)`. Also **(v1.24)**: `copy_role_templates(shop)`; `assign_role` and `grant_permission`, which only someone with `staff.manage` on that side may use, and only to give what they hold there themselves (Jack); a new code, `stock.weigh`, on every template (02 v1.10).
**Watch out:** `create_shop` lives in `shops` (layer 1), which may not import `people` (layer 2): the layer contract refuses it (REPO-6, ADR-0035). Decide here how a new shop gets its roles, for example a `people` service that calls `create_shop` and then copies the templates **(v1.3)**. Settled in PPL-3 **(v1.24)**: `set_up_shop(name, code, sides)` in `people` makes the shop with `create_shop`, then copies the templates, in one transaction. A shop made by `create_shop` alone has no roles, so nobody may act there: forgetting fails closed. CORE-6's two side hooks, and the sweep's staff, moved to a card of their own, PPL-5 (Jack, while planning PPL-3) **(v1.24)**. Also settled in PPL-3 **(v1.24)**: the six templates hold what Jack chose for MPC (`apps/core/permissions.py`): the Owner every code; the Manager all but the owner's four (`drawer.approve`, `pay.approve`, `settings.manage`, `staff.manage`); the Seller, Order writer, Cashier and Worker as spec §6, with credit and returns for the Seller, and products and prices for the Order writer; weighing for all. A role's codes are one PostgreSQL array (`django.contrib.postgres` is now an installed app), checked by the database against the list, so a new code comes with a migration. Asked about the whole shop (`side=None`), only roles and grants on every side count. `effective_permissions` reads the person's status in its own query, so a copy loaded before a suspension holds nothing. After the review **(v1.24)**: the giver check reads the role as stored, locked, never the copy handed in; asking about a person of a shop that isn't open fails loudly (`ShopMismatch`); a role's name is trimmed; and a card that adds a code also adds it, in a data migration, to the Owner and Manager roles of every shop that already has its roles (03 §5.8).
**Done when:**
- [x] A Worker can't `sale.bargain`; a Manager on all sides has the Manager permissions on both.
- [x] An extra grant on one side works only there.
- [x] A suspended member, or one who has left, has no permissions **(v1.14)**.
**Tests:** the card's rules: `test_worker_cannot_bargain` · `test_all_sides_role_covers_both_sides` · `test_grant_limited_to_its_side` · `test_suspended_member_has_no_permissions` · `test_require_refuses_with_permission_denied` · `test_whole_shop_needs_all_sides` · `test_member_brought_back_holds_their_roles_again` · `test_side_of_another_shop_refused`; the codes and templates: `test_every_code_fits_where_it_is_stored` · `test_every_template_names_known_codes` · `test_owner_template_holds_every_code`; the tables: `test_role_permissions_must_be_known` · `test_one_role_per_template_per_shop` · `test_role_given_once_per_side` · `test_extra_permission_known_and_given_once`; giving: `test_shop_gets_its_own_copy_of_each_template` · `test_templates_copied_once_per_shop` · `test_role_assignment_stays_in_one_shop` · `test_left_staff_gets_no_role` · `test_role_given_keeps_who_gave_it` · `test_giving_a_role_needs_staff_manage` · `test_manager_cannot_make_himself_owner`; a new shop: `test_new_shop_gets_the_six_roles` · `test_set_up_shop_all_or_nothing` · `test_shops_roles_are_copies`; and the review's: `test_giver_check_reads_the_role_as_stored` · `test_suspended_or_departed_giver_gives_nothing` · `test_database_refuses_a_role_that_breaks_a_rule` · `test_person_of_a_shop_not_open_refused` · `test_everyone_may_weigh_what_arrives` · `test_manager_holds_all_but_the_owners_four` · `test_role_name_trimmed` **(v1.24)**
**Commits:** `docs: a people service gives each new shop its roles` → `docs: office routes check permissions in a card of their own` → `test(people): permission rules` → `docs: deliveries are weighed as they arrive, by anyone, offline` → `feat(core): permission codes` → `feat(people): roles, assignments and grants` → `feat(people): give a person a role or an extra permission` → `feat(people): effective permissions per side` → `feat(people): nobody gives more than they hold` → `feat(people): a new shop gets its role templates` → `docs: the ppl-3 card names the tests it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the ppl-3 card and its notes follow the review` **(v1.24)**

### PPL-5 · Office routes check permissions per side
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-3 · branch `feat/ppl-5-route-permissions` **(v1.24)**
**Build:** CORE-6's two side hooks read PPL-3's permissions. `visible_sides(request)` gives the sides the person acting holds any permission on, or None when one of their roles or grants covers all sides. `check_side_permission(request, side_id)` asks for the permission the route names for the action, on the row's side, or on the whole shop when `side_id` is None; a read needs only the side to be visible. Each route names the permission each action needs; the side routes ask for `settings.manage` to add or rename a side. `core` may not import `people` (ADR-0035), so the person in `request.auth` answers for their own permissions, as the `Actor` already answers for its shop (`apps/core/tenancy.py`). Also, as Jack chose while planning PPL-5 **(v1.25)**: each route names, in one list, the permission each of its actions needs (`action_permissions`), and an action missing from it is refused with a server error, never let through; a test walks every route and finds it first. A visit reads the person's permissions once and keeps them on the view, which lives for that visit only. A show action needs only its side to be visible; one that names a key of its own is refused for now.
**Watch out:** CORE-6's base view (`backend/apps/core/api/base.py`) has two side hooks, which allow everything until this card fills them in (from PPL-3, which held these notes until v1.24) **(v1.20)**. `visible_sides(request)` narrows every read, so a list leaves the other sides out and their rows are "not found". `check_side_permission(request, side_id)` asks about the action (`self.action`) for each row handed out one at a time: read, edited, made (with the new row's side, None for the whole shop), and a create's repeat as a read. A route names where its rows keep their side in `side_field`. A suspended member, or one who has left, gets nothing from either. CORE-7's sweep calls every route as `staff_a` and `staff_b`, the two shops' staff members, who have no role: once the hooks refuse a person with no permission, give each all of their own shop in the sweep's shops (`backend/tests/conftest.py`), or the sweep's look as shop A, who must see its own rows, and its check that shop B sees its own sides, fail **(v1.21)**. The other tests that call routes as `staff_client_a` or `staff_client_b` (`backend/conftest.py`) need a person who holds the route's permission too. A row of the whole shop (`side_field` None) has no side to hide it, so a person who sees no side at all, such as one suspended, must see none of those rows either. From PPL-3 **(v1.24)**: the hooks ask `effective_permissions(staff, side)` (`apps/people/permissions.py`) through the person in `request.auth`. `shop_a` and `shop_b` (`backend/conftest.py`) are bare Shop rows with no roles, so the fixtures copy them in (`copy_role_templates`) and give their staff the Owner role on every side (`assign_role`, `by=None`). Settled while planning PPL-5 **(v1.25)**: the permissions are kept on the view, never on the person in `request.auth`. Tests send the same `StaffMember` object to visit after visit, and a login may too, so a copy kept on it would outlive a suspension. The rule for what a person holds on a side (02 §5) moves into one calculation in `core`, which PPL-3's `effective_permissions` uses too, so it is written once. Show actions with a key of their own wait for the first card that shows rows behind one: buying prices (`cost.view`, CAT-2), debts (`debt.view`, DEBT-7), reports (`report.view`). That card decides how a list keeps to the sides where the key is held, and whether a row without it is "not found" or refused. Settled while building PPL-5 **(v1.25)**: the sides a person sees are kept to in `get_queryset()`, where every read of a route starts, not in DRF's `filter_queryset()`, so a route's own @action that reads rows directly, as an export may, shows a person only their sides too. A change sent to a whole list, which names no row, needs its key on the whole shop; an action on one row finds it with `get_object()`, which checks that row's side. A create sent twice gets its row back as a read, without asking for the create's key again. OPTIONS, which DRF answers for every route by itself (`metadata`), needs no name. `StaffMember.held_permissions()` imports `apps/people/permissions.py` inside the method: that file imports the models, so at the top each would wait for the other. After the review **(v1.25)**: a change to one row is found and checked in `initial()`, before its action runs, so an @action that finds its row another way is still checked; a create other than CreateOnceMixin's, which asks for itself, is asked on the whole shop; a method a route doesn't answer gets 405, OPTIONS too, since it would describe a route's forms to anyone; a route adds to its rows in `base_rows()`, and a test refuses one that writes over `get_queryset()`; a change named `SEES_THE_SIDE` is refused, the mirror of the show-action rule; a repeated create goes through the route's own row checks, only the side check treating it as a read.
**Done when:**
- [x] A person sees only the sides they hold a permission on; another side's row is "not found".
- [x] An action without the permission its route names is refused (403 `permission_denied`) and writes nothing.
- [x] A suspended member, or one who has left, gets nothing from any office route.
- [x] The tenancy sweep passes with its two staff holding all of their own shop, with no change to its checks.
- [x] Every action of every route names its permission; an action that doesn't is refused, and CI turns red **(v1.25)**.
- [x] A visit reads the person's permissions once: a list of five sides costs the same queries as a list of two **(v1.25)**.
- [x] A show action that names a key of its own is refused until a card builds it **(v1.25)**.
**Tests:** the routes (`backend/tests/test_route_permissions.py`): `test_list_shows_only_sides_with_a_permission` · `test_action_needs_its_permission` · `test_side_routes_name_their_permissions` · `test_suspended_member_gets_nothing_from_routes` · `test_who_sees_no_side_sees_no_whole_shop_rows` · `test_every_read_of_a_route_keeps_to_the_persons_sides` · `test_change_to_a_whole_list_needs_its_key_on_every_side` · `test_every_route_names_a_permission_for_each_action` · `test_permission_check_finds_what_breaks_its_rules` · `test_unnamed_action_refused` · `test_show_action_with_a_key_stopped_for_now` · `test_permissions_read_once_per_visit`; and the review's: `test_a_create_that_does_not_ask_needs_its_key_on_every_side` · `test_methods_a_route_does_not_answer_get_405` · `test_a_change_to_one_row_is_checked_before_it_runs` · `test_a_route_adds_to_its_rows_without_losing_the_side_check` · `test_no_route_writes_over_get_queryset` · `test_rows_check_finds_a_route_that_writes_over_get_queryset` · `test_change_with_no_key_of_its_own_refused` · `test_a_create_sent_twice_keeps_the_routes_own_checks` · `test_a_key_on_one_side_opens_only_that_side` · `test_a_create_sent_twice_is_answered_as_a_read`; what a person holds (`apps/core/tests/test_held_permissions.py`): `test_a_side_adds_the_every_side_codes` · `test_whole_shop_counts_only_every_side_codes` · `test_a_code_on_every_side_shows_every_side` · `test_sides_seen_are_those_with_a_code` · `test_a_side_with_no_codes_is_not_seen` · `test_nothing_held_sees_no_side`; and the person who answers for them: `test_staff_member_answers_for_their_permissions` **(v1.25)**
**Commits:** `docs: the ppl-5 card names each action's permission and reads them once per visit` → `test(core): routes check permissions` → `test: sweep staff hold all of their own shop` → `feat(core): the person acting answers for their permissions` → `feat(core): side hooks read the permissions` → `docs: the ppl-5 card names the tests it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, `docs: comments say what the hooks do now`, and `docs: the ppl-5 card and its notes follow the review`. The sweep's staff get their roles before the hooks, not after: with the hooks first, every route test would fail until the last commit **(v1.25)**

### AUTH-1 · Devices and pairing
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-3 · branch `feat/auth-1-devices`
**Build:** `core.ShopSequence` (named per-shop counters, locked while used); `people.Device` and `people.PairingRequest` (02 §5) **(v1.26)**; services `start_pairing()` (a waiting pairing request, with no shop, an 8-character code valid 10 minutes, with no look-alike characters such as 0/O or 1/I, and the device's key, given to it at once and stored as its hash **(v1.26)**), `confirm_pairing(code, name, home_side, by)` (needs `device.manage`; makes the Device in the manager's shop, with the device code from `ShopSequence` and the key's hash, which switches the key on **(v1.26)**), `block` and `unblock` (a reason and an audit line each **(v1.26)**).
**Done when:**
- [x] A code works once and expires after 10 minutes.
- [x] Device codes are never reused, even after a block and a new pairing.
- [x] Only `device.manage` can confirm; the key is stored hashed.
- [x] Blocking and unblocking need a reason and write an audit line (Jack, 30 Oct 2026) **(v1.26)**.
**Watch out** **(v1.23)**: CTRL-2's history check covers every shop table that is not a ledger, so `people.Device`'s migration lists `RecordEdits`, with the key's hash column on its skip list: a column named like a secret (`*_hash`, `*_key`…) left off it turns CI red (`backend/tests/test_edit_history.py`). From PPL-3 **(v1.24)**: `confirm_pairing` checks `require(by, Permission.DEVICE_MANAGE, home_side)` (`apps/people/permissions.py`), which refuses with Django's `PermissionDenied`. Settled while planning AUTH-1 (Jack, 30 Oct 2026) **(v1.26)**: a new device belongs to no shop until a manager says yes, so it waits in `PairingRequest`, which has no shop and sits on the sweep's `NO_SHOP` list (`backend/tests/sweep_lists.py`); the `Device` is born `active` in the manager's shop, so it is never `pending` (02 v1.11). The key is made in `start_pairing`, because the device asks there: the manager's phone never holds it, and the server never keeps it in plain text. It is 32 random bytes, stored as its SHA-256 hash, not with Argon2: nobody can guess it, and AUTH-2 finds the device by its hash on every request. `ShopSequence` keeps no edit history (`NO_EDIT_HISTORY`): only the system moves it, each number is kept on the row that took it, and from SYNC-1 it moves on every sale. Blocking and unblocking need `device.manage` on the device's home side; unblocking keeps the key, so a device that comes back can still send what it holds. `DeviceOutage` and the sync columns (`last_synced_at`, `last_device_seq`, `unsent_count`, `app_version`) move to SYNC-5, and `last_seen_at` to AUTH-2: nothing here writes them. Who confirmed a device is its `created_by`, so it has no `registered_by` (as CORE-4's `Setting` has no `set_by`). Settled while building AUTH-1 **(v1.26)**: every pairing code ever given stays taken (unique), so `start_pairing` draws again on a clash, and stops loudly after five. A code is stored as typed, not hashed: it lives 10 minutes and works once, and a code this short could be guessed offline even from a hash. `confirm_pairing` asks for the permission before it reads the code, so someone without it learns nothing about codes. A device's name is unique in its shop, in any letter case, blocked devices included. The key's hash has a unique index of its own, not led by `shop`: a request that brings a key names no shop yet. A block goes through `editing()` with the version it has just read, never one a screen sent: it must not be refused because someone renamed the device a moment ago. New error codes for UI-2's messages: `pairing.code_unknown`, `pairing.code_used`, `pairing.code_expired`, `device.name_taken`, `device.not_active`, `device.not_blocked`, `device.reason_required`. After the review **(v1.26)**: the key never shows in a printout (`NewPairing.key` has `repr=False`, and `start_pairing` is marked `@sensitive_variables("key")` for Django's error pages), and a pairing request prints as its id, never its waiting code. Tests that need two transactions at once use `backend/tests/concurrency.py`, which asks `pg_locks` who waits: inside one transaction PostgreSQL shows `pg_stat_activity` as at the first look, like a photo, so a connection that starts waiting later never shows there. The review's other ideas were left for later (Jack): triggers that let a counter only go up and keep a confirmed request as it is, a device's own `device.other_shop` code, and one dependency Django added to `control`'s 0004.
**Tests:** the counters (`apps/core/tests/test_sequences.py`): `test_counter_starts_at_one_and_counts_up` · `test_each_shop_counts_alone` · `test_rolled_back_number_is_given_again` · `test_unknown_counter_name_refused` · `test_counter_of_another_shop_refused` · `test_counter_needs_a_transaction` · `test_counter_row_locked_until_commit`; pairing (`apps/people/tests/test_pairing.py`): `test_start_pairing_belongs_to_no_shop` · `test_pairing_code_has_no_look_alikes` · `test_pairing_code_clash_draws_again` · `test_device_key_stored_hashed` · `test_device_made_in_the_confirmers_shop` · `test_pairing_code_single_use` · `test_pairing_code_expires` · `test_unknown_code_refused` · `test_typed_code_is_cleaned` · `test_confirm_needs_device_manage` · `test_confirm_checks_permission_before_the_code` · `test_home_side_of_another_shop_refused` · `test_device_names_unique_in_a_shop`; blocking (`apps/people/tests/test_blocking.py`): `test_block_writes_audit_with_reason` · `test_block_needs_a_reason` · `test_only_an_active_device_is_blocked` · `test_unblock_keeps_the_key` · `test_block_needs_device_manage` · `test_device_code_never_reused`; and the review's: `test_device_key_never_printed` · `test_manager_who_cannot_act_cannot_confirm` · `test_two_managers_confirm_one_code_at_once` · `test_manager_who_cannot_act_cannot_block` · `test_manager_of_another_shop_cannot_block` · `test_first_numbers_at_once_make_one_counter` · `test_database_keeps_one_counter_per_name`, and the database's own rules (`apps/people/tests/test_device_rules.py`): `test_database_refuses_a_device_that_breaks_a_rule` · `test_database_refuses_a_device_that_repeats_another` · `test_database_refuses_a_request_that_breaks_a_rule` · `test_database_refuses_a_request_confirmed_without_a_time` · `test_database_refuses_a_request_that_repeats_another` **(v1.26)**
**Commits:** `docs: a pairing request belongs to no shop until a manager confirms it` → `feat(core): per-shop counters` → `test(people): pairing rules` → `feat(people): Device and PairingRequest` → `feat(people): pairing services` → `feat(people): block and unblock a device` → `docs: the auth-1 card names the tests it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the auth-1 card and its notes follow the review` **(v1.26)**

### AUTH-2 · Device key login
`M` · Claude-led · after AUTH-1, CORE-6 · branch `feat/auth-2-device-key`
**Build:** the device key as an HttpOnly, Secure cookie, `device_key`: SameSite Strict, sent only to `/api/`, kept 400 days and sent again as the device is seen **(v1.27)**; a DRF authentication class that reads it, returns the device as `request.auth`, an Actor with its `shop_id` and a `staff_id` of None, since a device is not a person (CORE-6's office routes refuse it; the sync routes open its shop) **(v1.20)**, and enforces CSRF; a blocked device gets `401 device.blocked`; the Device's `last_seen_at`, moved here from AUTH-1 **(v1.26)**. Also **(v1.27)**: `DeviceView`, the base of every route a device calls with its key, which opens the device's shop for the whole visit; the two routes a device calls before and after a manager's yes, `POST /api/devices/v1/pairing/` (start a pairing: sets the key's cookie) and `GET /api/devices/v1/me/` (the device's shop, name, code and home side); and the sweep taught to call every device route as a device of each shop. The manager's confirm, the throttles and the office device routes move to AUTH-5 (Jack).
**Done when:**
- [x] Requests from a paired device carry its shop; a blocked device is refused on its next request.
- [x] A request without the CSRF token is refused, a start included; the throttle moves to AUTH-5 **(v1.27)**.
- [x] A key's hash lives in one place: on its waiting request, then on its device **(v1.27)**.
- [x] A device that already holds a working key can't be given another **(v1.27)**.
- [x] `last_seen_at` is written at most every 5 minutes and never fills the edit history **(v1.27)**.
- [x] A device route that shows shop A's rows to shop B's device turns CI red, with no change to the existing checks **(v1.27)**.
**Watch out** **(v1.20)**: DRF answers 401 only when the first login class names itself in `authenticate_header()`; without it, a refused login is a 403 (CORE-6's `test_error_shape`). For `401 device.blocked`, give the device key's class one. CORE-7's sweep refuses a route under `api/` that is not an office route (`test_every_api_route_is_swept`), because it can't call it as another shop. Only a route that reads no shop's table may sit on the sweep's `NOT_SHOP_ROUTES` (`backend/tests/sweep_lists.py`), with its reason. A pairing route that a device calls before it has a key reads a shop's tables while it acts for no shop yet: decide here how the sweep calls it, and teach the sweep in a reviewed change. PIN login (AUTH-4) and the sync routes (SYNC-2 on) read a shop's tables with a device's key: teach the sweep to call them as a device of shop B **(v1.21)**. From AUTH-1 **(v1.26)**: the device gets its key from `start_pairing`, before any shop is known, and keeps it; the key is found by its SHA-256 hash (`key_hash`, unique), on the `PairingRequest` while it waits (no shop) and on the `Device` once confirmed. Decide here how the login class finds the device's shop without a door the sweep refuses (`system_context()` is for system jobs only). Write `last_seen_at` without filling the edit history at every request: a column changed alone and on the skip list writes no history row (`RecordEdits`, `apps/control/operations.py`). From AUTH-1's review **(v1.26)**: a confirmed `PairingRequest` keeps its key's hash and names its device, so key → request → device is a path past the shop guard. Decide whether the login finds a device through it, as the one door, or whether `confirm_pairing` empties the request's hash, a column then empty only once confirmed. A confirm sent twice, by a double tap or after a lost answer, is refused as `pairing.code_used` though the device was paired: let the route take the new device's id from the phone (a UUIDv7, CORE-6's `CreateOnceMixin`), so a repeat is answered as a read. The key passes through the pairing route: keep it out of error pages there too, as `start_pairing` does (`@sensitive_variables`). Once paired, the device shows its shop's name, so a code claimed by the wrong shop's manager is seen at once. Settled while planning AUTH-2 (Jack, 30 Oct 2026) **(v1.27)**: the card was twice its size, so the manager's confirm, with the note above on a confirm sent twice, the throttles, and the office device routes UI-3 needs move to AUTH-5. `confirm_pairing` moves the key's hash from the request to the device: one fact in one place (02 v1.7), so key → request → device is no path past the guard, and a request's hash is empty exactly once it is confirmed. The login finds a key's shop through one door, `shop_of_device_key` (`apps/people/login_doors.py`), which reads only a device's `shop_id`, by the key's hash, in that hash's own index; its file is on the sweep's `HAND_BUILT_QUERIES`, with its reason. The device itself is then read through the guard, inside that shop. A key that opens no device, or still waits for a manager, is no login (401 `not_authenticated`). The start is CSRF-checked too, and refuses a key that already opens a device (`pairing.already_paired`): otherwise another website could make a shop laptop ask again, and the new cookie would wipe its working key for good. A blocked device is still seen, so a stolen phone that keeps calling shows when. `last_seen_at` moves at most every 5 minutes, by `.update()`, so the row's `version` never moves and a rename screen is never refused for it; the device's history skips it, through a new migration step that puts the frozen trigger back with a longer skip list. Chrome keeps a cookie 400 days at most, so the cookie is sent again whenever `last_seen_at` moves. The cookie is named `device_key`: Django's error pages hide a cookie whose name holds `KEY`. Device routes are versioned, as sync's are: devices are the slowest clients to update. New error codes for UI-2: `device.blocked`, `pairing.already_paired`. Settled while building AUTH-2 **(v1.27)**: the start's key is hidden from error pages by `set_key_cookie` (`apps/people/api/authentication.py`), marked `@sensitive_variables()` with no names: Django hides a variable in every frame below a marked one, by name, and its own `set_cookie` holds the key as `value`, so a mark by name would have let a crash there print it. The login's object for each visit notes whether it saw the device (`seen`), and `DeviceView` reads it from `request.authenticators` as the answer leaves, to send the cookie again; a refused device, a blocked one included, still has its cookie sent again, so its key outlives a long block. A CSRF refusal keeps DRF's own code, `permission_denied`, as a session's does. The door's one query reads `shop_id` alone, which `test_key_door_reads_only_a_shop_id` pins. The test fixture `give_role` skips a role already given, so the sweep's devices and the staff clients can both give the Owner role. The sweep's probe app has three device routes (honest, leaky, blind) to prove the device check. The database refuses a confirmed request that keeps a hash, and a waiting one with none (`pairing.key_misplaced`). The device routes' operations keep their version in their names (`devices_v1_me_retrieve`), so a later `v2` gets names of its own. After the review **(v1.27)**: the whole login is marked `@sensitive_variables()`, so neither the key nor its hash shows in an error report, in its frame or below it (Django hides marked variables only with DEBUG off; cookies whose name holds KEY always); only an active device logs in, asked as "not active", so a status a later card adds fails closed; a probe device route that reads through the guard proves each visit opens its device's shop, and closes it; the sweep reads a ViewSet's methods from its router, reports a device route it can send nothing to, and wants a leak test of its own, named in `DEVICE_ROUTES_WITHOUT_A_LOOK`, for one with no GET; `start_pairing(replacing=...)` takes the key the cookie holds now, locks its waiting request as `confirm_pairing` does, and retires its code, or refuses a key that already opens a device, a rule that moved from the view into the service; `ChangeRecordEdits` refuses a migration that isn't atomic; 0007's SQL is tested both ways; ADR-0037 supersedes ADR-0022 (Jack). Left for later: `ChangeRecordEdits` trusts its `was` list rather than reading the trigger's own arguments; `HAND_BUILT_QUERIES` names `login_doors.py` whole, so AUTH-3's door brings its own test that it reads a shop's id alone; a suspended shop's devices still log in, since nothing checks a shop's status yet: the card that adds that check covers the device login too.
**Tests:** `test_pairing_throttled` moves to AUTH-5 **(v1.27)**. The login (`apps/people/tests/test_device_login.py`): `test_device_key_sets_current_shop` · `test_blocked_device_refused` · `test_device_requests_need_csrf` · `test_start_pairing_needs_csrf` · `test_unknown_key_opens_nothing` · `test_waiting_key_opens_nothing` · `test_device_key_opens_no_office_route`; its parts (`test_device_authentication.py`): `test_key_door_reads_only_a_shop_id` · `test_login_finds_the_device_in_its_own_shop` · `test_device_is_an_actor_of_its_shop` · `test_blocked_device_still_seen` · `test_device_key_cookie_hidden_on_error_pages`; the two routes and the cookie (`test_pairing_routes.py`): `test_start_pairing_sets_the_key_cookie` · `test_key_cookie_secure_in_production` · `test_paired_device_cannot_start_again` · `test_blocked_device_cannot_start_again` · `test_waiting_device_may_ask_again` · `test_start_pairing_keeps_the_key_out_of_error_pages` · `test_cookie_sent_again_when_seen` · `test_cookie_not_sent_again_while_fresh` · `test_blocked_device_keeps_its_cookie` · `test_paired_device_learns_its_shop_and_code` · `test_me_hands_out_the_csrf_cookie`; the key's one place (`test_pairing.py`, `test_device_rules.py`): `test_device_key_stored_hashed` (changed) · `test_confirm_moves_the_key_to_the_device` · `test_refused_confirm_keeps_the_key_waiting` · `test_database_refuses_a_confirmed_request_that_keeps_its_key` · `test_database_refuses_a_waiting_request_without_a_key`; last seen (`test_device_seen.py`): `test_last_seen_written_at_most_every_five_minutes` · `test_last_seen_writes_no_history` · `test_seen_device_keeps_its_version`; the history's skip list (`apps/control/tests/test_edit_history.py`): `test_change_record_edits_skips_the_new_column` · `test_change_record_edits_puts_the_old_list_back` · `test_change_record_edits_checks_its_names`; and the sweep (`backend/tests/test_tenancy_sweep.py`): `test_device_route_sweep_finds_the_device_routes` · `test_device_route_check_finds_a_leak` · `test_no_device_route_leaks_across_shops` **(v1.27)**; and the review's: `test_login_keeps_the_key_out_of_error_reports` · `test_device_visit_opens_its_own_shop` · `test_device_visit_closes_its_shop` · `test_every_device_route_without_a_look_has_its_test` · `test_asking_again_retires_the_old_code` · `test_asking_again_with_a_working_key_refused` · `test_asking_again_while_a_manager_confirms` · `test_a_manager_confirms_while_the_device_asks_again` · `test_change_record_edits_needs_an_atomic_migration` · `test_migration_0007_moves_the_hashes_both_ways` **(v1.27)**
**Commits:** `docs: a device's key moves to it when confirmed, and its login finds the shop by one door` → `test(people): device key login rules` → `feat(people): a confirmed pairing moves its key to the device` → `feat(control): a table's edit history can skip one more column` → `feat(people): a device records when it was last seen` → `feat(people): device key authentication` → `test: the sweep calls device routes as a device of each shop` → `feat(people): a new device asks to pair and gets its key cookie` → `feat(people): a paired device learns its shop` → `feat(people): the key cookie is sent again before browsers drop it` → `docs: the auth-2 card names the tests it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, `docs: ADR-0037 supersedes ADR-0022, naming the doors past the shop guard`, and `docs: the auth-2 card and its notes follow the review`. The sweep is taught before the first device route, so no commit leaves it red **(v1.27)**

### AUTH-5 · Office device routes
`M` · Claude-led · after AUTH-2, PPL-5 · branch `feat/auth-5-device-routes` **(v1.27)**
**Build:** the office routes for a shop's devices, `/api/v1/people/devices/`, on CORE-6's `ShopViewSet` with `side_field = "home_side"`: the manager's confirm as a create, with the new device's id made on the phone (a UUIDv7, `CreateOnceMixin`); the device list, with `last_seen_at`; rename, through a new `rename_device` service and `editing()`; block and unblock, each with a reason, calling AUTH-1's services. Throttles on pairing: the start, per address, and the confirm, per person, counted in Django's database cache (`DatabaseCache`), in PostgreSQL (Jack, 30 Oct 2026; ADR-0023).
**Watch out:** From AUTH-2 **(v1.27)**: a confirm sent twice, by a double tap or after a lost answer, is refused as `pairing.code_used` though the device was paired. `confirm_pairing` takes the id the phone sent, so `CreateOnceMixin` finds the device and answers the repeat as a read. Each action names its permission (`action_permissions`, PPL-5): `device.manage` on the device's home side for every change; the list only sees the side. A device's row never shows its key's hash. The database cache needs its table: the deploy runs `manage.py createcachetable` (OPS), as Django does by itself for the test database; a test that counts a throttle starts from an empty cache. DRF finds who asks by the address in `X-Forwarded-For` only when `NUM_PROXIES` says how many proxies stand in front (Caddy, or Render's proxy on the demo); without it, a phone could send any address it likes and never be slowed down. Every device of a shop shares the shop's one public address, so the start's limit counts the whole shop: a few devices an hour is plenty. A device's own calls to `me` are not throttled. CORE-7's sweep needs a device row in each sweep shop (`backend/tests/conftest.py`): AUTH-2's `sweep_device_clients` pairs them. allauth's login limits (AUTH-3) count in the same cache. Settled while building AUTH-5 **(v1.28)**: the list shows a side's devices to whoever works on that side, a seller included, who changes none of them (`SEES_THE_SIDE`; Jack, 2 Nov 2026). The confirm takes the code as `pairing_code`: a device's `code` is its number in receipt numbers, which the answer shows. The home side comes through `SideField` (`apps/shops/api/fields.py`), which keeps to the visit's shop and the sides the person sees, so a hidden side is `does_not_exist`, never a 403 that tells it exists. Block and unblock are actions of their own (`devices/{id}/block/`, `unblock/`), each naming its form for the schema: `RouteSchema` reads every POST of a route as its create. A blank reason reaches the service and comes back as `device.reason_required`. The confirm's limit counts per User, so one person working at two shops has one count, and only the create: the list, a rename or a block are never slowed down. `NUM_PROXIES` comes from the environment, 1 (Caddy). On the demo, Render puts Cloudflare and its own load balancer in front, so the start's limit reads Cloudflare's `CF-Connecting-IP` instead (`CLIENT_IP_HEADER`, `render.yaml`), Render's own advice; a request without it counts with the proxy's address, so it is slowed down sooner, never let through. The cache keeps up to 10,000 counts *(default)*. `createcachetable` runs in `start-demo.sh` and `make migrate`; on the real server, OPS-6's deploy. The sweep's two shops pair their devices before `sweep_shops` is ready (`sweep_sides` → `sweep_device_keys`), so the office sweep finds a device in each. After the review **(v1.28)**: a start counts by the caller's network, an IPv6 address by its `/64`, where a phone takes new addresses at will, and an IPv4 address written as IPv6 as itself; the counts keep a fingerprint of it, made with the server's secret key, never the address. Each limit counts in a cache table of its own (`throttle_pairing_start`, `throttle_pairing_confirm`), 10,000 rows each: a full table loses a third of its rows by their keys' ABC order, so in one shared table a flood of starts cleared the managers' counts. Only a start the shop really sent counts, a POST that passed its CSRF check, which `DeviceView` now makes in `check_permissions()`, before DRF counts: before, a web page's hidden requests used up the shop's starts. Only a new try of a code counts (`DeviceViewSet.get_throttles()`): a confirm sent again gets its device, never "wait", and a send with no code is not counted. A taken id gets one answer, the same for a hidden side's row and another shop's: `CreateOnceMixin` leaves full_clean's "unique" on the id out, as if unseen (`apps/core/api/base.py`). The device's status keeps its own name in the schema, `DeviceStatusEnum`. Every test that uses the database starts with empty caches (`backend/conftest.py`): a committing test's counts outlive Django's clean-up, and pytest-django runs those tests last. Two honest limits, written in `apps/people/api/throttles.py`: two requests at the same instant on two workers can both pass on one count; and refused tries count only while requests don't each run in one transaction (`ATOMIC_REQUESTS`).
**Done when:**
- [x] A confirm sent twice makes one device, and the second answer is the first's.
- [x] Repeated starts from one address, and a manager's repeated confirms, are slowed down (429 `throttled`, with `wait`).
- [x] The device list shows the devices of the sides a person sees, a seller's included, and no others; only `device.manage` on a device's home side renames, blocks or unblocks it (Jack, 2 Nov 2026) **(v1.28)**.
**Tests:** `test_pairing_throttled` · `test_confirm_sent_twice_makes_one_device` · `test_device_routes_name_their_permissions` · `test_device_list_keeps_to_the_persons_sides`. Built **(v1.28)**: the confirm with the phone's id (`test_pairing.py`): `test_confirm_keeps_the_phones_id`; the rename (`test_rename_device.py`): `test_rename_device_needs_device_manage_on_its_side` · `test_rename_device_refuses_a_stale_version` · `test_rename_device_refuses_a_name_taken`; the routes (`test_device_routes.py`): `test_manager_confirms_a_waiting_device` · `test_confirm_sent_twice_at_once_makes_one_device` · `test_confirm_needs_device_manage_on_the_home_side` · `test_confirm_on_a_side_not_seen_finds_no_side` · `test_confirm_refuses_another_shops_side` · `test_seller_sees_the_list` · `test_device_rows_never_show_the_key_hash` · `test_manager_renames_a_device` · `test_manager_blocks_and_unblocks_with_a_reason` · `test_block_needs_a_reason` · `test_seller_cannot_rename_block_or_unblock` · `test_other_sides_manager_cannot_change_the_device`; the limits (`test_pairing_throttles.py`): `test_confirm_throttled_per_person` · `test_start_throttle_reads_the_address_behind_the_proxy` · `test_start_throttle_reads_cloudflares_header_on_the_demo` · `test_only_pairing_is_throttled`; and the review's **(v1.28)**: `test_start_throttle_counts_an_ipv6_network_as_one` · `test_start_throttle_counts_an_ipv4_address_written_as_ipv6_as_itself` · `test_caller_network` · `test_start_counts_keep_no_address` · `test_a_flood_of_starts_never_clears_a_managers_count` · `test_refused_requests_never_use_up_the_shops_starts` · `test_a_confirm_sent_again_is_never_slowed_down` · `test_a_send_with_no_code_does_not_count` · `test_start_without_cloudflares_header_counts_with_the_proxys_address` · `test_a_taken_id_is_refused_alike_wherever_it_is_taken` · `test_a_manager_of_one_side_changes_only_its_devices` · `test_a_manager_who_may_not_work_gets_nothing` · `test_a_fields_choices_keep_a_name_of_their_own` (`apps/core/tests/test_api_docs.py`) · `test_a_committing_test_leaves_a_count_behind` · `test_the_next_test_finds_no_count` (`backend/tests/test_caches.py`)
**Commits:** `test(people): device route rules` → `feat(people): confirm a pairing with the phone's id` → `feat(people): office device routes` → `feat(people): pairing throttles in the database cache`. Built **(v1.28)**, each commit with its own tests: `feat(people): confirm a pairing with the phone's id` → `feat(people): rename a device` → `feat(people): managers confirm and list devices` → `feat(people): managers rename, block and unblock devices` → `feat(people): slow pairing down, counting in the database cache` → `docs: record auth-5's device list and pairing limits`; then the review's fourteen, one per finding, each with its own test (`fix(people)`, `fix(core)`, `test(people)`, `test`, `docs`) **(v1.28)**

### AUTH-3 · Passwords for the owner and managers
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-1, CORE-6, PPL-5 **(v1.24)** · branch `feat/auth-3-allauth`
**Check first:** the installed django-allauth supports login by phone with no username (03 AD3).
**Decide first:** PPL-1 asks an email only of the superuser: most of the shop's people have a phone but no email (Jack, 23 Oct). Before building, choose how the owner and managers log in and get back a lost password without email (an SMS code, a first password the owner sets face to face, or the owner registering their phone as a device, then a PIN), and record it as a new ADR that supersedes 02 §5's email rule and ADR-0017's resets by email **(v1.6)**. Decided (Jack, 3 Nov 2026) **(v1.29)**: the owner sets a first or forgotten password face to face, and the person chooses their own at the next login; an email link is only for people who have one; no SMS for now (ADR-0038). Not the owner's phone paired as a device, then a PIN: a paired device keeps an offline copy of the shop's prices, costs and debts, which a personal phone shouldn't hold. Setting a temporary password is its own card, AUTH-6: with it, this card would be more than twice its size. Until then, the superuser sets passwords in the admin. How the shop is chosen: a person with one current job works at that job's shop; a person with current jobs at two shops is refused (`staff.choose_shop`) until AUTH-7 lets them choose, before a second real shop joins. Nobody works at two shops today.
**Watch out** **(v1.20)**: CORE-6's office routes need the staff member acting in `request.auth`, with its `shop_id` and `staff_id` (a StaffMember has both). allauth's session alone leaves it empty, so those routes answer 403 `not_authenticated`. A login must name the staff member it acts as, and one person can work at two shops (02 D12): decide how the shop is chosen. Check on every visit, not only at login, that the staff member is still active and the shop too: a suspension takes effect at once (ADR-0017). From AUTH-5 **(v1.28)**: allauth's login limits count in the `default` cache, a table in PostgreSQL (CACHES). A full database cache table loses a third of its rows by their keys' ABC order, not their age, so a flood of failed logins from many addresses could clear other callers' counts in it, a guesser's own included: decide whether the login limits get a table of their own, as each pairing limit has, and test a flood. Count by the caller's network, never the raw address, as `caller_network` does (`apps/people/api/throttles.py`): an IPv6 phone takes new addresses at will. Settled while building **(v1.30)**: allauth 65.21.1, with no extras: the `headless` extra brings `pyjwt` and `cryptography` for mobile-app tokens, which we don't use. Only the routes the app uses are mounted (`apps/people/api/password_urls.py`): allauth's own list has no setting to leave one out, and would let a person change their own email or phone past the owner. The change of one's own password waits for AUTH-6. A login takes a phone only as `+255…`: allauth's own field refuses `0712…` before it asks us. No phone in `ACCOUNT_SIGNUP_FIELDS`, so allauth's stage that checks a phone by SMS never starts, and nothing reaches the hooks that would send one. allauth keyed its count of wrong passwords on the email or username, so every phone login shared one count, and five wrong passwords for anyone's phone locked out every phone user; our adapter keys it on the login name, whichever it is (`_get_login_attempts_cache_key`, a private method, which `test_wrong_passwords_count_per_phone` pins). allauth counts only in the `default` cache, with no setting to change it, so `default` is the login limits' own table, `throttle_login`, 100,000 rows; nothing else may count there; a database made earlier keeps an empty `django_cache`. The counts keep fingerprints made with the server's secret key: of the caller's network (`caller_fingerprint`, shared with pairing) and of the login name, as allauth would have kept the address itself, and a plain hash of a phone, which can be worked back by trying every number. Only a pairing flood can clear login counts in a shared table, as allauth's keys sort first, so the flood test goes that way. A reset lifts the person's count, by phone and by email (`set_password`): allauth's own clean-up looks only in its table of addresses, which we leave empty. A login's door: `shops_of_person`, which reads the shops' ids of a person's current jobs, two at most. The person's job counts as current when suspended, so the login can say why it refuses. New error codes for UI-2: `staff.suspended`, `shop.suspended`, `staff.choose_shop`, and allauth's own, in its shape (UI-5). Two tests marked a person as leaving on the UTC date, which from midnight to 3 a.m. in Tanzania is before they started; they now use the start date. Honest limits: a stranger who knows a person's phone can keep them locked out, by five wrong passwords every five minutes; a flood of about 2,000 networks at once could clear a guesser's own count; a reset request's count keeps a plain hash of the email, emails strangers typed included, until its row is read again or pruned (OPS-7), as allauth's route offers no hook, so a backup could show who was asked about **(v1.31)**; a person with an email and a phone has a count of wrong passwords for each, so ten tries, not five **(v1.31)**; the reset email's text is allauth's own, in English. Settled by the review **(v1.31)**: allauth's default reset key copied the email into its own table of addresses, which its login and reset trust, so an old mailbox kept working links and a reused address opened its old owner's account: we use Django's key, and the table stays empty and off the admin. Two password checkers hashed a wrong password twice and a right one once, which timing at the admin's unlimited login could show: allauth's checker alone now, which reads the admin's form too. A person's session on the demo's admin ends once at that deploy, as a session names its checker. Passwords were PBKDF2, though 03 said Argon2: now Argon2 at 19 MiB, 2 passes, 1 lane, OWASP's minimum, to fit the demo's 512 MB and two workers (`apps/people/hashers.py`), and an older hash moves at its next login. Reset emails: 3 an hour per address (`ACCOUNT_RATE_LIMITS`), as allauth's default allows 7,200 a day; a link lasts an hour (`PASSWORD_RESET_TIMEOUT`), not 3 days. The rules of who may act live in `apps/people/selectors.py`, and the login classes only translate them. The API docs routes read no login: a suspended person's session got 403 on pages open to everyone.
**Build:** django-allauth headless, browser client: login by email or phone + password, logout, password reset by email for those who have one (printed to the console in dev), sign-up closed, login rate limits; sessions stored in PostgreSQL. The office routes' login reads the session as the person's staff member at their shop, through a login door that reads only a shop's id, and checks the person and the shop on every visit **(v1.29)**. A suspended shop's devices are refused too, as AUTH-2 left for the first card that checks a shop's status **(v1.29)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] A manager logs in by email or by phone and gets a session that DRF accepts.
- [x] A reset link from the console sets a new password; sign-up is refused; repeated wrong passwords are slowed down.
- [x] A suspended person, or a suspended shop, is stopped on the next request **(v1.29)**.
- [x] A person at two shops is refused safely until AUTH-7 lets them choose **(v1.29)**.
**Tests:** `test_login_by_email` · `test_login_by_phone` · `test_signup_closed` · `test_password_reset_by_email` · `test_login_rate_limited`. Built **(v1.30)**, each file with its own: the login (`apps/people/tests/test_password_login.py`): those, and `test_login_by_email_any_case` · `test_phone_login_needs_the_country_code` · `test_phone_login_sends_no_sms` · `test_wrong_passwords_count_per_phone` · `test_login_needs_csrf` · `test_logout_ends_session` · `test_password_too_like_the_person_is_refused`. ### AUTH-6 · The owner sets a password face to face
`M` · Claude-led · after AUTH-3, CTRL-2 · branch `feat/auth-6-temporary-password` **(v1.29)**
**Decide first:** who may set one: the owner only, or also a manager the owner gives `staff.manage`, under the giver rule (02 §5), so a manager can never set the owner's. Decided (Jack, 4 Nov 2026) **(v1.32)**: also someone the owner gives `staff.manage`, on the whole shop, for someone who holds no key they lack, and never for the owner. No new ADR: ADR-0038 says the owner sets it, this card left open whether the owner may hand the job over, and the key is the owner's own to hand over. The rule is in 02 §5.
**Build:** a "must change password" mark on `User`, in a migration; a service that sets a person's temporary password and the mark together, with an audit line (CTRL-2) that names who did it and never holds the password; an office route for it; while the mark is on, every office route answers `password.must_change`, and the person reaches only allauth's change of password, which clears the mark. The superuser resets the owner's in the admin, which sets the same mark (ADR-0038).
**Watch out:** the temporary password goes through the password checker like any other, and once sent it is never shown again: no answer, log or audit line holds it. Changing a password ends the person's other sessions (Django's session check), so a lost phone that was logged in is logged out. From AUTH-3 **(v1.30)**: mount allauth's `account/password/change` in `apps/people/api/password_urls.py`, which AUTH-3 left out, so a person can choose their own; add it to `test_only_the_routes_on_our_list_are_mounted`, and take it off `test_routes_not_on_our_list_do_not_exist` **(v1.31)**. A password allauth sets lifts the person's count of wrong passwords (`AccountAdapter.set_password`); a temporary one set by our own service doesn't go through it, so lift it there too, or a person who typed it wrong five times waits 5 minutes for the owner's new one. Settled while building **(v1.32)**: a password opens the whole person, so the key is asked on the whole shop, and a key on one side isn't enough. Added to the card, each with its test: the new password can't be the temporary one (`password.same_as_temporary`), by the change route or a reset link, or the owner would go on knowing it. A person who can open the admin goes to the superuser (`password.superuser_only`), as the admin reads every shop's rows. So does an owner, suspended or not, and between two owners too. The comparison reads what a person was given, whatever their status (`given_permissions`, `HeldPermissions.covers`): a suspended person holds nothing, so a manager would otherwise look stronger than them, and hold their login when they came back (`password.more_keys`). Nobody sets their own (`password.own`): a thief holding a logged-in phone would skip the current password. The change route always asks for the current password (`ChangeOwnPasswordView`), as allauth's own skips it for someone with no password, which AUTH-4's PIN sessions would reach. A reason is needed (`staff.reason_required`), as `audit()` asks for one (T3). A first password typed in the admin sets the mark too, as ADR-0038 says "first or forgotten"; disabling a password there takes it off. `lift_wrong_password_count` lifts only the person's own count, per login name, through allauth's internal helpers, as `ratelimit.clear` needs a request for its count per network, and a service has none; a test pins it. The route answers 204 with no body, and takes 128 characters at most, so a huge password can't reach the slow hash. Settled by the review (Jack, 4 Nov 2026): spaces around a password come off, in the service, the route and the admin's forms, as allauth's login takes them off what is typed, so one stored with them could never log in, and a test that read only the stored hash hid it; its tests now log in for real. A job at another shop isn't looked at: reading that shop's rows during a visit passes the guard, which ADR-0037 keeps for a login before any shop is open, and nothing is lost until AUTH-7, as a person at two shops is refused at every office route. Who may is asked before the hash, so a refused try pays for none, and again after it, with both people's rows locked in id order, so a giver suspended meanwhile sets nothing. The lock is `no_key`: a key given at the same moment needs a light lock on the giver's row at its commit, and a full lock here would deadlock with it. While a temporary password is open, a key its setter lacks waits (`staff.temporary_password_open`, read from the last audit line across the person's jobs at this shop, as someone who left and came back has a new job and the same password): the person chooses their own first, or someone holding that key sets a new one. A password typed in the admin writes no line, so it holds no key back by itself, and a reset there leaves an earlier setter's line counting, which fails safe. A person with no phone and no email is refused (`password.no_login_name`). The admin's own password page (`/admin/password_change/`) follows the rules of choosing one's own (`ChooseOwnPasswordForm`): spaces come off inside the field, before its "required" check, as its parent has no second check, so spaces alone would store an empty password and lock the person out. Every variable on a password's way is hidden from error reports, as the library at the bottom of the hash names it `secret`: the service, the route, the admin's forms, each allauth route (`_route`, `password_urls.py`), and the login's checker (`PasswordBackend`, `apps/people/accounts.py`), as Django's `authenticate()` hides only `credentials` and the nearest rule wins; for the same reason the own page writes `clean_old_password` out, with no call to Django's. A narrower rule of Django's own can still sit below our wrappers, such as the password checks on the admin's forms. Nothing sends these reports yet (no `ADMINS` or `LOGGING`), which OPS-10 changes. That new checker's name ends each person's session once, at its deploy. Choosing a password, by the change route, a link or the admin's own page, saves only the password and the mark, as a whole-row save could undo a switch-off made meanwhile. A failed lift of the count undoes the temporary password. `lift_wrong_password_count` lives in `apps/people/login_counts.py`. Not fixed: someone who can open the admin and also has a job can disable their own password there to take the mark off; they hold the whole admin anyway. Honest limits: a reset in the admin is kept in Django's admin log, not in the shop's audit trail, as a person belongs to no one shop; Django's `changepassword` command sets no mark and keeps spaces around the password, so use the admin; a temporary password has no time limit; a request sent twice writes two audit lines, and the person has one temporary password, the one typed; a setter who logs in as the person and chooses a new password themselves takes the mark off, and no system can tell who typed it (Jack, 4 Nov 2026), so before giving a big key the owner sets a fresh one (PPL-4 says so on its screen), and a PIN-only worker wouldn't even notice; the temporary one is refused as typed, so the same in other capitals passes, which also takes the mark off with a password the setter can guess, and once the mark is off a person may go back to it; an owner editing a role later isn't checked against an open temporary password until PPL-4 builds that edit.
**Done when:**
- [x] The owner sets a temporary password; the person logs in with it and must choose their own before any office screen opens.
- [x] Only someone allowed may set one, never for the owner; the audit line names who did it, and holds no password.

### AUTH-4 · PIN login and "me"
`M` · Jack-led · after AUTH-2, PPL-5 **(v1.24)** · branch `feat/auth-4-pin-login`
**Learn:** one session per person on a shared device; lockouts that double.
**Build:** `POST /api/auth/pin-login` (device key + staff + PIN → a session for that person on that device; idle lock from settings; five wrong PINs lock that person on that device for a minute, doubling each time, up to an hour **(v1.33)**); `POST /api/auth/logout`; `GET /api/auth/me` (user, staff, shop, device, home side, permissions per side).
**Watch out:** PINs are hashed with Django's `Argon2PasswordHasher` at its default cost, about 100 MiB of memory per check (`apps/people/pins.py`): set the cost on purpose for the demo's 512 MB and two workers, and re-hash old PINs when it changes (`must_update`). Passwords already have theirs, 19 MiB (`apps/people/hashers.py`, AUTH-3 **(v1.31)**), and PINs don't go through it. A PIN session follows the same rules of who may act: `job_of_person` and `check_shop_active` (`apps/people/selectors.py`), never a second copy **(v1.31)**. `pin_matches` answers at once for a person with no PIN but takes about 0.1 s otherwise, so the timing shows who has one; and a malformed hash raises. Refuse people who have left as well as suspended ones (PPL-2) **(v1.14)**. The PIN session's login class returns the staff member as `request.auth`, which names the visit's shop for CORE-6's base view; it checks on every visit that the member and the shop are still active, so a suspension stops the next request **(v1.20)**. From CTRL-2 **(v1.23)**: here a visit first knows its device, so the session keeps it; the two books learn it in AUTH-8 **(v1.33)**. Settled while planning (Jack, 4 Nov 2026) **(v1.33)**: a lock's wait stops growing at an hour, as someone typing wrong PINs on purpose could otherwise keep a coworker out for days, and a new PIN lifts the lock at once. From PPL-3 **(v1.24)**: `me` lists `effective_permissions` for each side, and for the whole shop (`side=None`). From AUTH-3 **(v1.30)**: `StaffSessionAuthentication` (`apps/people/api/staff_session.py`) reads any Django session as the person's one job, and checks the person and the shop on every visit, so a PIN session passes through it too. A PIN knows its device's shop, so the session names that shop and the person's job there, and a person with jobs at two shops isn't asked to choose at the counter; AUTH-7 still covers a password **(v1.33)**. From AUTH-6 **(v1.32)**: the mark closes the office routes in `job_of_person`, so a PIN session meets it too: someone on a temporary password logs in by PIN, and the office routes answer `password.must_change` until they choose their own, while the counter, on the device's key, goes on working. Decided (Jack, 4 Nov 2026) **(v1.33)**: yes, that is what it should say. The PIN works and the person can sell; the office routes stay closed until they choose their own; `me` stays open and says so, so the app can tell them why. The change route asks for the current password of everyone (`ChangeOwnPasswordView`), so a PIN session can't give a PIN-only worker a password. Settled while building **(v1.34)**: PINs use the same hasher as passwords (`ShopArgon2PasswordHasher`), 19 MiB. An old hash moves over at the person's next login, in one UPDATE of `pin_hash` alone: `pin_changed_at` stays, so devices keep their offline check, the history skips the column, and it happens only if the hash is still the one just checked, so a new PIN set meanwhile is kept. Each answer costs one full check: a person with no PIN is checked against a made-up hash (`_nobodys_hash`), and so is anyone the guard doesn't find, so neither the words nor the time tell them apart. A damaged hash matches nothing, writes one log line with no hash in it, and doesn't crash. Every variable on a PIN's way is hidden from error reports. The lock is `people.PinLock`, one row per person per device, on `NO_EDIT_HISTORY`: only the system moves it, and it names nobody. A try is counted before its check, holding the row's lock, so tries sent at once can't pass five, and the lock is dropped before the slow check. The row keeps `next_lock_minutes` itself, not a count of locks, so nothing can grow past the limit. A suspended person is neither counted nor checked. Routes end in a slash, as all of ours do. `pin-login` answers 204 with nothing; 400 `pin.wrong`, or `pin.locked` with `params.wait` in whole seconds, rounded up; 403 `staff.suspended`. Someone who left, a person switched off, another shop's person and a made-up id all get `pin.wrong`. 16 characters at most reach the service. A limit of 30 tries a minute per device *(default)*, in a cache table of its own (`pin_login`), as made-up people meet no lock. The route is on `DEVICE_ROUTES_WITHOUT_A_LOOK`. The session (`apps/people/api/pin_session.py`) is always a new one: the old row is deleted, even if it was the same person's. It names the shop, the job, the device and `pin_set_at`. `job_of_pin_session` (`apps/people/selectors.py`) checks on every visit, in this order: the device's key beside it (`session.wrong_device`, first, so a copied number learns nothing about the person), the shop, the device (`device.blocked`), the person and the PIN's time (no job: `not_authenticated`), the status, the mark. A new PIN ends its badges (Jack). The idle time is read at login, and a visit that passes pushes it forward, at most once a minute. `DEVICE_KEY_COOKIE` moved to `apps/people/device_keys.py`: DRF loads `staff_session.py` while DRF itself is starting, when `authentication.py`'s imports can't load yet. Logout is a plain `APIView` on DRF's own session login, so it works whatever has happened to the person or the device, with its CSRF check, and it is on `NOT_SHOP_ROUTES`. `me` is a `ShopViewSet` with one action, so the sweep calls it; its login, `MeSessionAuthentication`, skips only the mark. Both name their cookie to the schema by hand (`auth=[{"cookieAuth": []}]`), as drf-spectacular warns of two classes under one name, and its type hint for that argument is wrong. Honest limits: a coworker at the counter can try about 150 PINs a day on one name, and that person is locked out for most of the day, so they will notice; more if that person keeps logging in, as the right PIN starts the waits again. PINs stop coworkers using each other's names, not a determined thief (03 §5.9). Nobody is told of a lock yet. A badge can end up to a minute early, or half its idle time at a short one. A change of `session.idle_minutes` counts from the next login. Anyone at a device can learn that a person is suspended, with no PIN: the device already holds the status. After the review (Jack, 4 Nov 2026) **(v1.35)**: a badge never opens the Django admin, whoever's it is, as the admin reads every shop's rows and none of a badge's checks runs there (`_not_by_pin`, `apps/people/admin.py`); a password typed in the admin over a badge starts a clean session, as Django's login would keep the mark. allauth's `auth/session` treats a badge as nobody (`PasswordSessionView`, `apps/people/api/password_urls.py`), as it answers with the name and the email, and the device's cookie doesn't reach it; logging out through it still works. The idle time is pushed at half its length when that is under a minute, as at the 1-minute setting a badge in use always ended first. `check_pin` asks that the open shop is theirs (T1). Each visit of a PIN's session finds its shop through the device key's door, and it must be the shop the badge names, so rule 1 holds as written and a mismatch reads no shop's row. A damaged hash's log line gives the staff id. Who may set a PIN (Jack): the rule for a temporary password, through one check, `_check_may_act_as`, with codes of its own: `pin.own`, `pin.superuser_only`, `pin.more_keys`, and asked again under the locks after the hash; `by=None` is the system or the superuser, the only one who sets an owner's. More honest limits: the fifth wrong try answers `pin.locked` for a real person and stays `pin.wrong` for someone not found, so five tries can tell them apart, though another shop's person looks like a made-up one; the first no-PIN answer on each server worker costs a second hash, once; whoever sets a PIN knows it, and a PIN has no mark that makes the person choose their own, so PPL-4's screen should let the person type it themselves. After the second review (Jack, 4 Nov 2026) **(v1.36)**: to the admin's login page, and to allauth's reset route, a badge is nobody too, as each answers with whoever `request.user` is; only a POST with its CSRF token ends a badge at the admin, never a plain visit, which any link can cause. One more honest limit: a PIN login moves the person's `last_login`, which Django's reset key is made from, so a reset link already sent stops working; it fails safe, and the person asks for a new one. Left as they are, as nothing can reach or break them today: a badge with a field missing answers a server error, and only `start_pin_session` writes badges; the order of the checks in `job_of_pin_session`, and its `user=user`, aren't pinned by a test.
**Done when:**
- [x] A PIN works only on an active device of the same shop; a suspended person is refused.
- [x] The lockout doubles; logging out ends the session at once.
- [x] `me` lists the permissions per side.
