### AUTH-8 · The two books name the device
`M` · Claude-led · after AUTH-4, CTRL-2 · branch `feat/auth-8-device-in-the-books`
**Build:** `control.AuditEvent` and `control.EditEvent` get a `device` link (empty for a visit with no device, PROTECT), `audit()` reads the device from the visit's note **(v1.37; it first said: takes the device as it takes who)**, and `acting_as` (`apps/core/acting.py`) tells PostgreSQL the device too. The trigger that records an edit is frozen once on `main`, so it reads the device in a new function, `control_record_edit_v2()`, which every office table's trigger moves to in a reviewed migration; the history check's function name (`RECORD_EDIT_FUNCTION`, `backend/tests/test_edit_history.py`) moves with them (X3).
**Watch out** **(v1.33)**: split from AUTH-4 (Jack, 4 Nov 2026): together they were twice a card's size (§3.3). `control` is layer 0, so it names the device by string (`"people.Device"`), and its services take it as they take an Actor (ADR-0035) **(changed, v1.37: `audit()` takes no device, below)**. A visit knows its device from a PIN session or a device's key; a password on a person's own phone names none, and the link stays empty. From AUTH-4 **(v1.34)**: a PIN's session already names its device (`PIN_SESSION`, `apps/people/api/pin_session.py`), and `job_of_pin_session` has checked it by the time a route runs; `request.auth` stays the staff member. A device's own visit is `request.auth` itself (`DeviceView`). Settled while planning (Jack, 4 Nov 2026) **(v1.37)**: `audit()` takes no device. It reads the note the trigger reads (`current_device_id`, `apps/core/acting.py`), one small query per line, and one more when it names a device, as `full_clean` checks the link **(v1.38)**. Handed in by each service, as this card first said, a forgotten one would be silent, and the two books would disagree on one action. ADR-0035 still holds: `control` imports only `core`. Settled while building **(v1.37)**: `acting_as(actor, device=…)` writes the third setting, `mpc.device_id`, in its one query, so a visit still costs two. Left out, the device stays as told, as `editing()` and `_save` open an inner block for `by` mid-visit, and wiping it would lose the device on nearly every edit. `None` says "no device". The device stays only inside its own shop, a device of another shop is refused with no query, and `current_device_id` refuses a row of another shop than the visit's, as the trigger does (T1). `job_of_pin_session` hands back the device it already loaded, and the login object keeps it (`StaffSessionAuthentication.device`), where `ShopViewSet` reads it by name, as `core` may not import `people`. Old migrations keep `RecordEdits`, on the frozen rule; a new table lists `RecordEditsV2`; the 8 office tables moved with `MoveRecordEditsToV2` (`people/0011`, `shops/0008`), built on `ChangeRecordEdits` for its one transaction and its undo. `RAW_SQL`'s file stays, with its reason widened to `mpc.*`. Honest limit: a password names no device, even on a paired laptop whose key cookie is there, as a password's session never reads the key (`test_password_on_a_paired_laptop_names_no_device`). After the review (Jack, 5 Nov 2026) **(v1.38)**: a book line naming a device links to that device's row, and its link is checked at commit with a light lock. So two managers blocking each other's devices at once would deadlock, and one would get a server error. `editing()` and `_set_device_status` lock with `select_for_update(no_key=True)`: two edits still take turns. The same clash, older than this card, was found for two managers suspending each other, and Jack chose to fix it here (`_locked`). A visit says which device it has even when it has none, and a test plants a stale device to pin it. After the second review (Jack, 5 Nov 2026) **(v1.39)**: the lock change keeps every promise tested, on real PostgreSQL. A role takes the lighter lock too, as it had no reason not to. New tests fail if a lock in `editing()`, `_set_device_status` or `assign_role` is removed; the first thread tests only catch a lock too strong. Honest limit: a save that changes a plain unique column takes the full lock anyway (no service does); a device's rename doesn't count, as its name's rule is on `Lower("name")`, and rewriting that rule as plain columns would bring the clash back. Two managers suspending each other both end up suspended today, as `suspend` has no check of who may yet: not the rule (PPL-4).
**Done when:**
- [x] An edit made in a PIN session names the person and the device, in both books.
- [x] A visit with no device leaves the link empty.
- [x] Every office table's trigger runs the new function; the history check finds any left behind.
**Tests:** `test_edit_history_names_the_device` · `test_audit_line_names_the_device` · `test_visit_without_a_device_names_none`. Built too **(v1.38)**: `test_pin_session_names_the_person_and_the_device_in_both_books` · `test_password_on_a_paired_laptop_names_no_device` · `test_visit_with_no_device_clears_one_left_on_the_connection` · `test_visit_leaves_no_device_on_the_connection` · `test_inner_block_keeps_the_visits_device` · `test_outer_device_survives_a_failed_inner_block` · `test_audit_line_of_another_shop_never_names_the_device` · `test_move_to_v2_undone_puts_the_old_rule_back` · `test_the_move_to_v2_kept_each_skip_list` · `test_two_managers_blocking_each_others_devices_both_go_through` · `test_two_managers_suspending_each_other_both_go_through` · `test_two_edits_of_one_device_take_turns_the_second_is_refused` · `test_block_waits_for_a_rename_and_still_goes_through` · `test_two_owners_giving_each_other_one_role_take_turns` **(v1.39)**
**Commits:** `feat(control): the two books have a device link` → `feat(control): the edit trigger reads the device` → `feat(people): a visit tells postgres its device`. Built **(v1.37)**, each commit with its own tests: `feat(control): the two books have a device link` → `feat(core): acting_as tells postgres the device` → `feat(control): the edit trigger reads the device` → `feat(control): an audit line names the device` → `feat(people): a visit tells postgres its device` → `docs(plan): auth-8 names what it built`. From the review **(v1.38)**: `fix(core): a lock on a row no longer blocks rows that link to it` → `fix(people): two managers acting on each other no longer deadlock` → `test(people): a visit with no device clears a stale one` → `test(core): the device survives a failed step and leaves with the visit` → `docs(control): comments say what the review settled` → `docs(plan): auth-8 says what the review settled`. From the second review **(v1.39)**: `test(people): tests fail if an office edit's locks are removed` → `fix(people): a role takes the same lighter lock as other office rows` → `docs(people): comments name the lock each step really takes` → `docs(plan): auth-8 says what the second review settled`.

### UI-7 · The look: colours, fonts, shapes and icon
`M` · Jack-led · after REPO-3 **(v1.40)** · branch `feat/ui-7-the-look`
**Learn:** what a design token is and how Tailwind turns it into classes; why an offline app keeps its fonts inside itself; what contrast means.
**Build:** replace shadcn's placeholders in `globals.css` with the shop's own look (ADR-0039): the green godown and container, the blue at the godown's back, the white and faded-red Foods bags.

| Token | Colour | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| `--primary` | green `#15803D` | main buttons, top bar |
| `--secondary`, `--ring` | blue `#1E40AF` | links, what is selected, focus ring |
| `--background`, `--card` | white `#FFFFFF` | screens |
| `--destructive` | faded red `#AD5145` | warnings only |
| `--foreground` | `#17231B` | text |
| `--muted` / `--muted-foreground` | `#F3F7F4` / `#566559` | pale panels, quiet text |
| `--border` | `#D9E3DC` | lines |
| `--input` | `#7D8C83` **(v1.41)** | the edge of a box you type in |
| `--radius` | 8 px | corners |
| control heights | 52 px big, 44 px normal | buttons, inputs |

Fonts: Source Sans 3 by default and Atkinson Hyperlegible Next as the second, both Latin-only files in `src/fonts/` with their licence, loaded by `next/font/local`. `data-font="second"` on `<html>` changes the font; a lint rule refuses `next/font/google`. Buttons show the hand pointer. The dark colours are removed. `make-icons.mjs` draws the godown: a green square, a white building, a blue door, no letters.
**Watch out:** keep the `@custom-variant dark` line. Without it, shadcn's `dark:` classes follow the device's setting, and a phone in dark mode gets half-dark screens. Check that both font files have same-width digits (`tnum`), which is not yet confirmed for Atkinson; if one fails, stop and bring the choice to Jack. Downloading the two font files (about 29 KB and 34 KB) needs Jack's yes first. `/spike/slip` takes the new font, so print it once and compare with SPIKE-2. The faded red is a touch darker than the first try (`#B3574B`), which read at only 4.43:1 on a pale panel. Settled while planning (Jack, 5 Nov 2026) **(v1.41)**: `--input` is `#7D8C83`, not the pale line. A white box on a white screen shows only its edge, and the pale line read at 1.31:1, where WCAG asks 3:1 for the edge of a box. `tokens.test.ts` checks `--input` and `--ring` at 3:1; `--border` only decorates, so it isn't checked. Settled while building **(v1.41)**: the table leaves out seven tokens that shadcn's parts read. `--primary-foreground` and `--secondary-foreground` are white, `--card` and `--popover` are white with the normal text colour, and `--accent` is the pale panel with the normal text colour. The chart and sidebar tokens are gone, as no screen uses them; the card that needs them gives them the shop's colours. The page's default outline is `--ring` at full strength, as half strength reads at only 2.57:1. Digit widths, read from the files: Source Sans 3 is even as it stands (all 497 at weight 400; the file's own table says 472, as its default weight is 200), and it has no `tnum` feature, which it doesn't need. Atkinson runs from 407 to 648, and is all 632 with `tnum`. So `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` is on `<html>`, and no screen can forget it. Both were measured in Chrome at weights 400, 600 and 700. The files come from Fontsource at fixed versions, with their fingerprints in `fonts.test.ts`, so a changed file fails and says to measure again. Fontsource's Source Sans licence names Google Inc., and its Atkinson licence repeats its first line and names a file we don't ship, so each of our copies starts with the owner's notice from the font's own record, above the unchanged licence text. The README says the fonts stay under that licence (ADR-0034 still holds for our own code). The Source Sans licence reserves the name "Source", so we use the files as they come. Atkinson's Latin file lacks the modifier apostrophe (U+02BC); the plain `'` and `’` are in both. The Android icon draws the godown at 80%, as the preview's drawing reaches past the safe circle, and a test reads the file's pixels to keep it inside. Two checks wait for the real world. The demo deploys only from `main`, so the phone check comes after the merge, before the card is ticked: uninstall the old app first, as Chrome may keep an old icon for a while. The slip passed print preview, and its real print joins SPIKE-2's next shop visit. From the review (Jack, 5 Nov 2026) **(v1.41)**: `color-scheme: only light` on `:root` asks Chrome's Auto Dark Theme not to darken the page, as the contrast checks only hold for our own colours; the image check counts two fonts in the service worker's list, until UI-6's browser tests; and the *Toleo jipya* bar has `print:hidden`, as it could otherwise come out on a customer's receipt.
**Done when:**
- [x] No placeholder grey is left in `globals.css`.
- [x] Every text and background pair passes 4.5:1.
- [x] With the network off, both fonts still show, and no request goes to Google.
- [x] Money lines up in both fonts.
- [x] A button on a laptop shows the hand pointer.
- [x] Installed on a phone, the new icon isn't cut by Android's mask.
**Tests:** `tokens.test.ts` (contrast, no placeholder left) · `manifest.test.ts` (theme colour equals `--primary`; the icon files' pixels **(v1.41)**) · `fonts.test.ts` (the files, their fingerprints and licences, that the page receives both fonts, the font switch, same-width digits, the lint rule) **(v1.41)** · `pointer.test.ts` (the hand pointer's selector, on real elements) **(v1.41)** · `scripts/smoke_image.sh` (both fonts in the service worker's list) **(v1.41)**
**Commits:** `feat(frontend): the shop's colours and shapes` → `feat(frontend): two fonts inside the app` → `fix(frontend): buttons show the hand pointer` → `feat(frontend): the godown icon` → `docs(plan): ui-7 says what it built` **(v1.41)**. From the review **(v1.41)**: `test(frontend): a changed font file fails a test` → `test(frontend): the page must receive both fonts` → `test(frontend): the look can't drift from the card` → `fix(frontend): only what can be pressed shows the hand pointer` → `test(frontend): the startup screen stays the shop's green` → `fix(frontend): each font keeps its own licence and owner` → `fix(frontend): browsers leave the light look alone` → `test(deploy): the image check finds both fonts` → `fix(frontend): the update bar stays off the paper` → `chore(frontend): comments and rule order follow the new look` → `docs(plan): ui-7's review leaves notes for later cards`

### UI-8 · The pieces: button, input, label, card, alert
`M` · Claude-led · after UI-7 **(v1.40)** · branch `feat/ui-8-the-pieces`
**Build:** `pnpm shadcn add button input label card alert`, left untouched in `components/ui/`. One piece of ours in `components/design/` for each part, setting the look (ADR-0039): the button has kinds `main` (green), `second` (blue outline) and `danger` (faded-red outline), and sizes `big` (the default) and `normal`; a field ties its label and error to its input. `SideBand` is blue, or striped white and faded red. A lint rule lets only `components/design/` import `components/ui/`, so no screen can skip the look, and `.claude/rules/frontend.md` says so. A `/design` page shows every colour, both fonts and each piece in each state, with its text in `sw.json` and `en.json`.
**Watch out:** shadcn's parts need `@base-ui/react`, a new dependency, so Jack's yes comes first (rule 12). Read what the shadcn command changes after each add. From UI-7 **(v1.41)**: if a part fades its focus ring with `ring-ring/50`, ours sets it to full strength, as half strength reads at only 2.57:1 on white. On green, the blue ring reads at only 1.74:1, so anything you can focus on green uses a white ring (5.02:1). The danger button's hover tint stays at 5% or less, as the button can sit on a pale panel: `--destructive` on its own 5% tint reads at 4.52:1 there, and on 10% at 4.21:1 (10% only passes on white, at 4.55:1). The main button gets darker on hover and never fades: white on green faded to 90% reads at 4.17:1, and on green darkened to 88% at 6.10:1. `button.test.tsx` checks both limits. `h-control-big` and `h-control-normal` give the two heights. `data-font` changes the font only where `font-sans` is on the same element, as `<html>` works out the font and passes it down, so `/design`'s second-font sample needs both. `--input` is now a dark edge colour: if a part paints a switched-off box with it (shadcn may use `disabled:bg-input/50`, which would give a heavy grey), ours picks a paler colour. If `shadcn add` writes oklch greys or chart and sidebar tokens back into `globals.css`, `tokens.test.ts` fails: remove them. Settled while planning (Jack, 6 Nov 2026) **(v1.42)**: the side band is colour only, with no words. The side's name stays in words in the top bar (UI-1), so nobody has to tell colours apart, and no word sits on red and white stripes. So the band is hidden from screen readers and needs no contrast check. Its `look` is `blue` or `striped`, and a screen must always give it, as §20 item 19 is still open. The 5 Nov preview page no longer opens, so this card is the reference. Settled while building **(v1.42)**: `@base-ui/react` is pinned at 1.9.0 (Jack's yes); `shadcn add` changed no token and kept `cn` at its version. shadcn's parts import `cn` from the package itself, not from `src/lib/utils.ts`, and `cn` doesn't know our height names: `cn("h-8", "h-control-big")` keeps both, and CSS order decides. So our pieces write `h-(--control-big)`, a spelling it knows is a height. `components/ui/` stays as shadcn wrote it: Prettier skips it, as the commit hook would rewrite it, and its `README.md` is its header. The outline kinds start from shadcn's `outline`, not its `destructive`, which is a pink fill with a faint red focus ring; the blue outline keeps the 5% limit too, so the two match. Tailwind writes shadcn's `aria-invalid:ring-destructive/20` after `focus-visible:ring-ring`, so a focused box with an error would show only the pink ring: `aria-invalid:focus-visible:ring-ring` comes later and keeps the blue one (read in the built CSS). The lint rule is eslint-plugin-import's `no-restricted-paths`, which comes with Next's rule sets: it follows the file an import points to, so every spelling is caught, and it leaves the font ban alone. The alert has two kinds: `info`, a blue edge with `role="status"`, and `danger`, faded red with `role="alert"`. A field shows its label, hint and error above the box, as GOV.UK's design system does, so a phone's keyboard can't cover the error. `/design` shows switched-off and error samples, and hover, press and focus are tried live: Jack checked all of them, and the Swahili, on 6 Nov 2026. The contrast sums moved to `src/test/look.ts`, shared by `tokens.test.ts`, `pointer.test.ts` and the pieces' tests. From the review (Jack, 6 Nov 2026) **(v1.42)**: the lint lock first read only `.ts` and `.tsx`, so a `page.jsx` passed; it now takes Next's own list (`js, jsx, mjs, ts, tsx, mts, cts`), as Next loads the import plugin only for those, and a `.cjs` file can't be a page. The button's colour checks read every state the button has a colour for, whatever its name, refuse faded text and any fade but switching off, and keep each kind's own text colour; four tests feed them a broken class and expect them to fail. A button switched off but kept reachable by Tab (`focusableWhenDisabled`) has no `disabled` attribute, so shadcn's fade missed it: ours fades on Base UI's `data-disabled`. `role="status"` is read when its text changes, and a box that appears with its text may not be read at all; `role="alert"` is read when it appears. `[data-font="default"]` sets the default font back, so `/design`'s default sample stays true once a person picks the second. A field's hint is 16 px, and its `className` styles the whole field. The card title keeps `leading-snug`, which `text-lg` had dropped. A focused button with an error keeps the blue ring, as the box does. The card footer's text is checked on the pale panel. 03 §7.3 names the oldest browsers the look works in: Chrome and Edge 111, Safari 16.4. `guard.py` refuses edits to shadcn's files, and CLAUDE.md's rule 8 names them. Two commit subjects, "side band" and "design page", stay as this card named them: the squash merge keeps only the PR title.
**Done when:**

- [x] Tabbing through the page shows a focus ring on every piece.
- [x] A screen importing `components/ui/` fails lint.
**Tests:** `button.test.tsx` · `field.test.tsx` · `side-band.test.tsx` · `import-rule.test.ts`. Built too **(v1.42)**: `card.test.tsx` · `alert.test.tsx` · `src/app/design/page.test.tsx` (a swatch for every colour token, every word from the message files, every line in English too). From the review **(v1.42)**: the colour checks' own tests in `button.test.tsx` (`catch faded text on hover`, `catch a state they have no name for`, `catch a button faded by opacity`, `catch an outline whose text turns dark on hover`) · `test_shadcn_part_refused` (`.claude/hooks/test_guard.py`)
**Commits:** `build(frontend): shadcn's first components` → `feat(frontend): button in the shop's look` → `feat(frontend): field, card and alert` → `feat(frontend): side band` → `chore(frontend): screens import only our pieces` → `feat(frontend): design page` → `docs(plan): ui-8 says what it built` **(v1.42)**. ### UI-1 · App foundations: Swahili, numbers, layout
`M` · Claude-led · after REPO-3, CORE-5, UI-8 **(v1.40)** · branch `feat/ui-1-foundations`
**Build:** next-intl (Swahili by default, English switch) with `sw.json` and `en.json`; money and quantity formatting (`sw-TZ`, TZS without decimals, "26 mifuko + 35.5 kg"); big.js and a TypeScript `roundMoney` that pass the **same** `shared/test-vectors`; a layout with a counter area and an office area, under a green top bar with `SideBand` **(v1.40)**; the *Inahitaji mtandao* banner on office screens when offline, built on the alert piece **(v1.40)**. The English switch keeps its choice on this browser until UI-9 saves it on the person **(v1.40)**.
**Done when:**
- [x] The same vector files pass in pytest and in Vitest.
- [x] Switching to English changes every visible string.
- [x] Office pages show the banner offline.
**Watch out** **(v1.19)**: the vectors pin three traps, so the TypeScript side must give the same text as Python (`backend/apps/core/numbers.py`). A half goes away from zero: −2.5 → −3, while JavaScript's `Math.round(-2.5)` gives −2 (big.js `Big.roundHalfUp` is right). A zero answer is written `0.00` or `0.000`, never with a minus. The split cuts toward zero: −120 kg is −2 bags and −20 kg (big.js `Big.roundDown`), not `Math.floor`'s −3 bags and +30 kg. A factor of 1 is refused: the base unit's own selling unit (E1's Kilo) is the rest, so it is left out. The Vitest loader refuses a bare JSON number, as pytest's does, because `JSON.parse` reads it as a float. From UI-7 **(v1.41)**: on the green top bar, the blue focus ring reads at only 1.74:1, so anything you can focus there uses a white ring (5.02:1). From UI-8 **(v1.42)**: the side band carries no words, so the top bar writes the side's name. Our pieces read `--ring`, so the bar sets `--ring` to white for what sits on it, and no piece changes. None of the three button kinds can sit on the green (a green button vanishes, and the blue outline reads at 1.74:1), so what sits on the bar needs a kind of its own, built in `components/design/` with its sums in `button.test.tsx`. The offline banner is `Alert kind="info"` (`role="status"`), which a screen reader reads when its text changes, not when it appears: keep the banner's box on office screens, and give it its text when the network drops. Settled while building (Jack, 6 Nov 2026) **(v1.43)**: the words come through use-intl, next-intl's own core, pinned at 4.14.9: next-intl 4.14 brings a code compiler (`@swc/core`) and a file watcher, both with install scripts, for tools a static app never uses (ADR-0040 supersedes ADR-0032). big.js is pinned at 7.0.1 with `@types/big.js` 7.0.0, as it has no types of its own (Jack's yes for all three). Our copy of big.js, `Decimal` in `src/lib/decimal.ts`, is strict: a JavaScript number anywhere is refused, and it caught one while building. `splitIntoUnits` counts with big.js's exact `mod`, so no division is cut at 40 places. In Swahili, stock reads word first, "mifuko 26 + kilo 35.5", and in English number first, "26 mifuko + 35.5 kilo": each book holds its order (`stock.part`), and the unit words are the shop's own, so only the order changes. A part that is zero is left out, nothing at all is "kilo 0", and stock below zero reads "-(mifuko 2 + kilo 20)", as a minus on each part reads like a sum. `formatMoney` refuses cents rather than round them away. `TopBar` sets only the look: `ON_GREEN` paints the green and sets `--ring` to white for what sits on it. The button's fourth kind, `bar`, is the main button with a white edge (5.02:1 on the green). `LanguageButton` names the other language in that language, with `lang` on the word, and `AppFrame` puts both in each area's layout and gives the page its one `<main>`. Until UI-3, the bar names no shop or side and shows no band. On `/design` the band samples are real top bars, with a working language button, and the live banner sits under the alerts. The banner's box is hidden while online by an `sr-only` wrapper: `sr-only` on the box itself would clash with the box's own position, padding and border. A lint rule, `react/jsx-no-literals`, refuses a word typed into a screen, and `no-restricted-syntax` refuses one typed into an attribute a person sees or hears (`aria-label`, `aria-description`, `placeholder`, `title`, `alt`); `language.test.tsx` also opens every page in English and looks for any Swahili left. Both books carry the same keys and parts to fill in (`messages.test.ts`). The language choice is kept under `mpc.language`. A file that asks use-intl for words starts with `"use client"`: the area layouts are built as server components, and a part they import without the line stops `next build` at the first page in an area ("createContext is not a function"), which no Vitest test sees; `use-client.test.ts` reads every such file. The *Toleo jipya* bar sits on `ON_GREEN` too, with a `bar` button, and its status box stays on the page, empty, until a new version is ready.
**Tests:** `vectors.rounding.test.ts` · `vectors.units.test.ts` · `format.money.test.ts`. Built **(v1.43)**, those, and: `vectors.test.ts` (every answer-book file has its runner; the reader refuses bare numbers and twin names) · `decimal.test.ts` · `rounding.test.ts` · `units.test.ts` · `format.stock.test.ts` · `messages.test.ts` · `language.test.tsx` · `text-rule.test.ts` · `top-bar.test.tsx` · `language-button.test.tsx` · `app-frame.test.tsx` · `offline-banner.test.tsx` · `use-client.test.ts` · `update-prompt.test.tsx`; `button.test.tsx` checks the bar kind on the green.
**Commits:** `feat(frontend): swahili and english messages` → `feat(frontend): decimals and rounding from shared vectors` → `feat(frontend): app layout`. Built as six, one idea each **(v1.43)**: those three, with `feat(frontend): money and stock as people read them`, `feat(frontend): a button kind for the green bar` and `feat(frontend): office screens say when they need the internet` beside them.

### UI-2 · Typed API client
`M` · Claude-led · after CORE-6, UI-1 · branch `feat/ui-2-api-client`
**Build:** `pnpm gen:api` (schema → `openapi-typescript` → `src/api/schema.d.ts`); an `openapi-fetch` client that sends `X-CSRFToken`; TanStack Query; API error codes shown as translated messages; a CI step that regenerates both and fails on any difference (03 AD11).
**Watch out** **(v1.20)**: CORE-6's schema says what each route takes and answers, not how it refuses. Add the error shape (`code`, `message`, `fields`, `params`) as every operation's `default` answer, so `openapi-fetch` types the error. Every code the backend sends needs a message in `sw.json` and `en.json`: ours (`side.code_taken`, `invalid`, `version_conflict`, `id.taken`, `id.not_uuid7`, `server_error`…) and DRF's (`required`, `not_found`, `not_authenticated`…). A test that lists them keeps that true. From AUTH-6 **(v1.32)**: the API's `password.must_change`, `password.own`, `password.superuser_only`, `password.more_keys`, `password.no_login_name`, `staff.temporary_password_open`, `staff.reason_required`, and the password checker's `password_too_short`, `password_too_common`, `password_entirely_numeric`, `password_too_similar`. From AUTH-4 **(v1.34)**: `pin.wrong`, `pin.locked` (with `wait`, in seconds), `session.wrong_device`; and on a session's routes, `device.blocked`, as a 403. From AUTH-4's review **(v1.35)**: `pin.own`, `pin.superuser_only`, `pin.more_keys`, for the staff screen that sets a PIN (PPL-4). Settled while planning (Jack, 6 Nov 2026) **(v1.44)**: a lint rule keeps the counter screens off the API client and TanStack Query, as a sale screen that called the server would work in testing and stop at the shop the day the network drops (rule 5). Settled while building **(v1.44)**: the description is committed as `shared/openapi.json`, beside the answer books, as the backend writes it and the frontend reads it. `pnpm gen:api` writes it with `manage.py spectacular --skip-checks`, as Django's checks ask PostgreSQL about itself and the description needs no database, then writes `src/api/schema.d.ts` with openapi-typescript 7.13.0, a dev tool, which asks for TypeScript 5 and works with our 6 (Jack's yes). The guard refuses hand edits to both. Every route's `default` answer is the error shape, `ApiError` (`RouteSchema._get_response_bodies`, `apps/core/api/schema.py`: the private method drf-spectacular's FAQ points to, and a test fails if a new version stops calling it); its `params` hold text, whole numbers, yes or no, or nothing, as `_json_safe` lets through. The client is openapi-fetch 0.17.0 (Jack's yes): `api` in `src/api/client.ts` reads the `csrftoken` cookie on every change, as a login gives a new token, sends no header before there is one, and keeps calls on the page's own address. Tests make their own client with a full address and a fake fetch (`createApiClient`), as Node can't call a relative one. TanStack Query 5.106.1 (Jack's yes) wraps every page in the root layout, as the device's registration and PIN login need the server too (UI-3, UI-4). openapi-fetch hands a refusal back as a value, and TanStack sees only what is thrown, so office screens call through `answerOf()` (`src/api/refusal.ts`), which throws an `ApiRefusal` with the status and the shape; a body that isn't the shape, like the proxy's 502 page while the server restarts (Caddy on the real server, Render's on the demo), reads as `server_error`. A read refused under 500 is never asked again, as TanStack's default of 3 more tries would keep the person waiting about 7 seconds for the same no; a dropped network or a 5xx is asked twice more; a change keeps TanStack's default and is never sent again by itself. The sentences live under `errors` in both books, nested by the code's dots (`errors.pin.locked`), and `errorFallback` covers no answer at all, a code with no sentence, which is named so a worker can read it out to the owner (a newer server can meet an older app), and anything else thrown. `pin.locked`'s `wait` reads in minutes, rounded up. `errors.test.ts` lists 67 codes by hand: every code a route can send today, and the PIN screen's three. No test can find them all in the backend, as some are built as the server runs (`f"{secret}.own"`); codes that only stop a bug, like `device.key_not_hashed`, are left to the general sentence. A card that adds a code adds it to that list with its two sentences, and the list and the books must match both ways. `ApiErrorAlert` shows the sentence on the danger alert. Jack checked the Swahili on 6 Nov 2026. The counter lock is two rules: a second zone of `import/no-restricted-paths` (nothing in `app/(counter)/` imports `src/api/`, by any path), and `no-restricted-imports` for `@tanstack/*`, which lists the font ban again, as ESLint keeps only a rule's last settings for a file. Honest limit: it reads a counter screen's own imports, so a shared part that calls the server, imported by a counter screen, still needs review to catch it. CI checks both halves: `test_committed_schema_matches_the_code` in the backend job compares the description built from the code with the committed file, as text, and `pnpm check:api` in the frontend job, which has no Python, compares the types with that file (openapi-typescript's `--check`). Shown by hand: a made-up serializer field failed the first, and a description regenerated alone failed the second. From the review (Jack, 6 Nov 2026) **(v1.44)**: no must-fix, and Jack chose to fix everything else. A sentence's value the server didn't send never shows a raw key: a wait sent as text ("300") counts, the shortest wait reads "dakika 1", a sentence missing only its wait says to wait with no number (`errorFallback.waitAWhile`), and one missing any other value names its code. `throttled` says its minutes too. The counter lock also refuses `openapi-fetch` and `fetch` (`window.`, `globalThis.`, `self.`); honest limit: a dynamic `import("@tanstack/react-query")` passes, as the rule that could catch it is set again for every file by the text rule. A request Django finds suspicious, like a host it doesn't serve, answers in the one shape under `/api/` (`bad_request`, `handler400`), as 404 and 500 already did. The CSRF cookie is read as it is, as decoding a broken one threw on every change. The client throws `NoConnection` when no answer came at all, so a bug's `TypeError` reads as "Kuna hitilafu" and isn't asked again, and a read cancelled as its screen closes stays a cancel. Only all four keys, each of its kind, count as the shape. A code's sentence moved to `src/i18n/codes.ts`, outside the counter lock, as sync's refusals will carry codes the counter must show. The list grew to 73 codes: `bad_request`, DRF's `max_string_length`, `null_characters_not_allowed`, `surrogate_characters_not_allowed`, and PPL-4's `pin.not_four_digits` and `pin.too_easy`; it holds every code a route, or a model rule behind it, can send today, and the PIN screen's. Jack checked the new Swahili. The error shape's note is a comment, as drf-spectacular put its docstring on the public docs page. `pnpm gen:api` runs `uv run --locked`, so it never rewrites `uv.lock`. `.editorconfig` leaves `shared/openapi.json` as the generator wrote it, and `.gitattributes` says why it stays unfolded. Notes went to UI-4 (clear the store at login; the PIN screen lives outside `(counter)/`), UI-5 (allauth's routes need the CSRF header by hand) and PPL-4. Nothing checks the backend's codes against the list yet: Jack chose a task of its own for that, CORE-8, first in stage 5, over growing this card. From the second review (Jack, 7 Nov 2026) **(v1.44)**: no must-fix, and Jack chose to fix everything but one commit's scope, as the squash merge keeps only the PR title. The network check reads the whole answer, on a copy, so a 3G signal lost halfway through a list reads as `NoConnection`, not a bug. `client.ts` shares `fetchOrNoConnection` and `csrfHeader()` for routes the typed client can't call, and UI-5's note names them. Which values a sentence fills in is read by one scan, `src/i18n/blanks.ts`, which counts braces, so a sentence that counts ("{minutes, plural, one {minute} other {minutes}}") is filled in, not shown as a code; `messages.test.ts` uses the same scan. The counter lock also refuses `XMLHttpRequest`, `WebSocket`, `EventSource` and `navigator.sendBeacon`, and lets a counter screen's test watch `fetch`. A test pins the wait-a-little fallback to the sentences about tries, `pin.locked` and `throttled`. A code finds only the book's own sentences (`Object.hasOwn`), so `constructor.name` reads as unknown, not "Object". Honest limits, in the code's comments: a time limit added to a read later (`AbortSignal.timeout()`) would read as a bug until it maps to `NoConnection`, and a single call given its own `fetch` skips the check.
**Done when:**
- [x] Changing a serializer without regenerating turns CI red.
- [x] An API error shows its Swahili message.
**Tests** **(v1.44)**: `test_every_route_refuses_in_the_error_shape` · `test_committed_schema_matches_the_code` (`apps/core/tests/test_api_docs.py`) · `test_api_description_refused` (`.claude/hooks/test_guard.py`) · `client.test.ts` · `refusal.test.ts` · `query-client.test.ts` · `provider.test.tsx` · `errors.test.ts` (every code, both books, both ways) · `api-error-alert.test.tsx` (the second Done line: a 409 through the real client shows its Swahili sentence) · `import-rule.test.ts` (the counter rule). From the review **(v1.44)**: `test_suspicious_api_request_answers_in_the_error_shape` · `test_suspicious_page_outside_the_api_keeps_djangos_page` (`test_error_handler.py`) · `src/i18n/codes.test.ts` (a code's sentence, the wait, the fallbacks) · in `client.test.ts`, a broken cookie, `NoConnection` and a cancelled read · in `query-client.test.ts`, a bug isn't asked again · in `refusal.test.ts`, all four keys · in `import-rule.test.ts`, `openapi-fetch`, `fetch` and `@/i18n/codes` on a counter screen. From the second review **(v1.44)**: in `client.test.ts`, a drop halfway through an answer, `csrfHeader` and `fetchOrNoConnection` · `src/i18n/blanks.test.ts` · in `codes.test.ts`, a sentence that counts, JavaScript's hidden names, and the wait-a-little sentence's list · in `import-rule.test.ts`, the four more ways to call a server, `self.fetch`, and a counter screen's test watching `fetch`
**Commits:** `build(frontend): generate api types` → `feat(frontend): typed api client` → `ci: api types up to date`. Built as eight, one idea each **(v1.44)**: `feat(core): every route names the error shape in the schema` → `build(frontend): generate api types` → `feat(frontend): typed api client` → `feat(frontend): office screens share one store of server answers` → `feat(frontend): api errors in the shop's words` → `chore(frontend): counter screens cannot import the api client` → `ci: api types up to date` → `docs(plan): ui-2 says what it built`. From the review **(v1.44)**: `fix(frontend): a wait the server didn't send never shows a raw key` → `fix(frontend): the counter lock also refuses openapi-fetch and fetch` → `fix(core): a request django finds suspicious answers in the one shape` → `fix(frontend): a broken csrf cookie can't stop every change` → `fix(frontend): a bug isn't shown as a lost connection` → `fix(frontend): only the whole error shape counts as a refusal of ours` → `refactor(frontend): a code's sentence lives beside the word books, for counter screens too` → `feat(frontend): a throttled request says how many minutes to wait` → `feat(frontend): sentences for five more codes the api can send` → `fix(core): the error shape's note stays off the public docs page` → `fix(frontend): gen:api never rewrites uv.lock on its own` → `chore: editors leave the api description as gen:api wrote it` → `docs(frontend): comments say what is true now` → `docs(plan): ui-2 says what the review settled` → `docs(plan): a task to check the server's codes against the list`. From the second review **(v1.44)**: `fix(frontend): a drop halfway through an answer reads as a lost connection` → `feat(frontend): login forms share the client's network check and csrf reader` → `fix(frontend): a sentence that counts is filled in, not shown as a code` → `fix(frontend): the counter lock also refuses xmlhttprequest, websocket, eventsource and sendbeacon` → `fix(frontend): a counter screen's test may check that nothing called the server` → `test(frontend): the wait-a-little sentence stands in only for tries` → `fix(frontend): a code finds only the book's own sentences` → `docs(frontend): comments count right and keep their width` → `docs(plan): ui-2 says what the second review settled`

### UI-3 · Device registration screens
`M` · Claude-led · after AUTH-5, UI-2 **(v1.27)** · branch `feat/ui-3-device-screens`
**Build:** *Sajili kifaa hiki* (shows the pairing code and waits); the manager's *Sajili kifaa* screen (code, name, home side); the device list (block, unblock, rename).
**Done when:**
- [x] A device registers end to end between two browsers.
- [x] A blocked device's app locks on its next request, with a clear message.
**Watch out** **(v1.45)**: Settled while planning (Jack, 7 Nov 2026): the top bar names the shop and the device's home side in words, with no band, as no side says which look it gets yet; SHOP-4 gives a side its band (Jack chose a task of its own over the parking lot). Settled while building: the device asks `GET /api/devices/v1/me/` as the app opens, when its window comes back to the front, when the network returns, and every 5 minutes (`DeviceCheck`, `src/features/devices/device-check.tsx`), beside every page in the root layout. That is the device asking about itself, not a counter screen asking for the shop's data (rule 5), and offline it keeps its last answer; without it, a stolen laptop left on a counter screen would never lock (Jack agreed to this reading of rule 5, 7 Nov 2026, at UI-3's review). The answer is kept on the device as `mpc.device` (`this-device.ts`, which never calls the server, so a counter screen may read the device's number for receipt numbers later); a "not registered" (401 `not_authenticated`) forgets it. A block, 401 or 403 `device.blocked`, from the check or from any read or change through the store (`noticeBlock`, which watches both of TanStack's caches), is kept there too, and `DeviceLock` puts the lock in place of every page, through a reload with no network as well; an unblock opens it at the next check, or at once with *Jaribu tena*. Honest limit: a device kept offline from before its block hears of it only at its next request. *Sajili kifaa hiki* lives at `/register-device/`, in a `(setup)` area of its own (the top bar and *Inahitaji mtandao*), outside the counter lock. It asks `me` every 3 seconds while its code works, and stops once the code expires. The countdown runs on the server's clock: a shop laptop's clock is often wrong, so the code's life is measured from the answer's `Date` header, less the second the header cuts off. A tap faster than the app's first `me` asks it for the CSRF cookie first. The code reads in two groups, `K7QM-4RXT`. The manager's screen is `/devices/` (*Vifaa*), an office screen. *Sajili kifaa* sends an id made on the phone (`src/lib/ids.ts`, a UUIDv7 of our own, no new package), one per form, so a tap sent again makes one device. It offers only the sides where the person holds `device.manage`, read from `me` (`src/features/people/who-is-here.ts`), and picks a lone one already. A refusal of the whole form that is about one box (`pairing.*`, `device.name_*`, `device.reason_required`) shows above that box. The list shows every device the person sees, by number, with when it was last seen ("Kilionekana dakika 3 zilizopita"), and rename, *Zuia* and *Fungua* only on the devices of the sides the person manages; a rename sends the version it read, and a conflict asks the list again. Device names are level-2 headings. Two pieces joined `components/design/`: `ButtonLink`, a real link with exactly the button's classes, as Base UI's docs say its button must not be a link; and `Choice`, a big row per option, over shadcn's `radio-group` (Jack's yes for the download; no new package). The test `render` gives each screen a store of its own, and `src/test/fake-server.ts` lets a screen's test call the real client against a fake server. Shown by hand between two browsers (Jack, 7 Nov 2026): a code, the manager's yes, the shop's name on the laptop, a block, the lock at the laptop's next request, an unblock. The local database was six migrations behind before it: check `showmigrations` before a hand test. Until UI-5, a manager logs in for such a test with one line in the browser's Console, through allauth's login route. From the review (Jack, 7 Nov 2026) **(v1.45)**: no must-fix, and Jack chose to fix everything. The lock shows *Inahitaji mtandao* while the browser is offline, as TanStack then asks nothing, and a tap on *Jaribu tena* would otherwise say nothing. Opening a form puts the keyboard in its first box; closing it puts it back on the button that opened it. Two sentences agree with *kifaa* whatever the device's name: *Kifaa {device} kimesajiliwa*, *Kinasubiri meneja…*. A new code keeps the waiting view while it comes, and a code the manager confirms in its last second still shows the device registered (`pairing.already_paired` asks the check again). The list keeps its rows when a refresh fails, with the reason above them, says it is a list to Safari (`role="list"`), holds a time ahead of the phone's clock at *sasa hivi*, and is asked again after *Ghairi*, as a lost answer may have made the device. `/design` shows `ButtonLink`. Sync's pull and push run outside the store, so SYNC-7 to SYNC-9 call `saveBlocked()` themselves; the device's identity lives in `mpc.device` only (03 §6.1, SYNC-6); UI-5's office menu links to *Vifaa*. Jack checked the Swahili on 7 Nov 2026. Left for later: a suspended shop's device gets 401 `shop.suspended` from `me`, and the app is neither locked nor told; the card that checks a shop's status for devices decides what it shows.
**Tests** **(v1.45)**: `ids.test.ts` · `this-device.test.ts` · `device-check.test.tsx` · `device-lock.test.tsx` (the second Done line) · `pairing.test.ts` · `register-this-device.test.tsx` · `confirm-device.test.tsx` · `device-list.test.tsx` · `choice.test.tsx` · in `button.test.tsx`, `ButtonLink`'s classes · in `app/page.test.tsx`, the home page's way to register · in `app-frame.test.tsx`, the setup area · in `design/page.test.tsx`, the choice samples. From the review **(v1.45)**: in `device-lock.test.tsx`, the offline banner · in `confirm-device.test.tsx`, focus, and *Ghairi* asking the list again · in `device-list.test.tsx`, focus, a failed refresh, a clock ahead of the phone, and the next try's version after a clash · in `register-this-device.test.tsx`, the new code's view, a yes in the last second, and the token the check brings · in `design/page.test.tsx`, `ButtonLink`

Built as eight, one idea each **(v1.45)**: `feat(frontend): ids made on the device are uuidv7` → `feat(frontend): the app knows which device it is` → `feat(frontend): a blocked device locks the app` → `feat(frontend): register this device` → `feat(frontend): a choice piece in the shop's look` → `feat(frontend): managers register a waiting device` → `feat(frontend): managers rename, block and unblock devices` → `docs(plan): ui-3 says what it built`. ### UI-4 · PIN login screen
`M` · Jack-led · after AUTH-4, UI-3 · branch `feat/ui-4-pin-login`
**Learn:** React state for a PIN pad; component tests with Testing Library.
**Build:** a staff picker (each person's photo; with no photo, their full name in large letters with the nickname under it **(v1.14)**) and a big PIN pad; quick switch; idle lock; logout.
**Watch out** **(v1.34)**: from AUTH-4: `POST /api/auth/pin-login/` sends `staff` and `pin`, with `X-CSRFToken`, and answers 204 with nothing, so ask `GET /api/auth/me/` next. A login gives the browser a new CSRF token, so read the `csrftoken` cookie again before the next change. `pin.locked` carries `wait`, in seconds. `me` says `must_change_password` for someone on a temporary password: they may sell, and the office pages are closed until they choose their own. Selling at the counter uses the device's key, not the session, so it doesn't push the server's idle time forward: the screen keeps an idle timer of its own, and a `me` that answers `not_authenticated` means ask for a PIN again. `POST /api/auth/logout/` works on a blocked device and for a suspended person. A person with no PIN gets the same answers as a wrong PIN, so the picker can't tell them apart either. From UI-2 **(v1.44)**: the store of server answers (`src/api/provider.tsx`) lives as long as the page, and everyone who logs in on the device shares it: empty it after each PIN login, logout and quick switch, or Juma sees Asha's rows from her last screen, offline too, where the new read waits (UI-4 built `forgetTheLastPerson` for it, as `clear()` leaves a screen on show with the last person's answer) **(v1.46)**. The counter lock refuses `src/api/`, TanStack Query, `openapi-fetch` and `fetch` in `app/(counter)/`, so the PIN screen and quick switch, which call the server, live outside it, in a group of their own such as `app/(login)/`; loosening the lock needs a decision on this card. Show a refusal with `ApiErrorAlert`: `pin.locked` reads its `wait` in minutes, and `pin.wrong` has its sentence. From UI-3 **(v1.45)**: the home page `/` shows a registered device's name: the PIN screen goes there. The device's saved copy (`mpc.device`) lives outside the store, so clearing the store at a login keeps the device known. Send the PIN login through the store (`useMutation`): a `device.blocked` there then locks the app by itself (`noticeBlock`, `src/features/devices/device-check.tsx`), and a call sent around the store would not. Ask `me` through `useWhoIsHere()` (`src/features/people/who-is-here.ts`). The top bar shows the device's home side until someone logs in: show the side they work on then. Settled while planning (Jack, 7 Nov 2026) **(v1.46)**: no route listed a shop's people for a device, and sync, which will carry them (SYNC-2, SYNC-10), comes in stage 2, so this card builds one first (Jack chose that over moving UI-4 after sync). `GET /api/devices/v1/staff/`, with the device's key, answers the people who can log in today, as `pin_login` takes them (active, not left, not switched off), A to Z by full name: their id, full name and nickname, never a phone, an email, a PIN's hash, nor whether they have a PIN. One plain list, with no pages: a shop has a handful of people. The screens as Jack pictured them: everyone at work at the shop, on both sides, on the tiles; a wrong PIN says so, and the dots empty; a lock says its minutes; the 4th digit sends, with no OK key; *Badilisha mtu* and *Toka* both end the session at once and bring the tiles back; so does the idle lock; someone logged in sees only *Karibu, {name}* until the counter screens come. Settled while building **(v1.46)**: `me` answers `idle_minutes` for a PIN's session, read from the session itself (`pin_session_idle_minutes`, `apps/people/api/pin_session.py`), as the server ends that session by it whatever the setting says since; for a password, the shop's setting today, as a password names no device and the shared laptop's screen locks the same, whoever logged in there and however (from the review). A login, a logout and a quick switch call `forgetTheLastPerson` (`src/api/query-client.ts`): it empties the store's changes, the PIN login's PIN among them, and resets every read. Not `clear()`: it drops the answers without telling the screens that show them, so the home page kept the last person's `me` until it happened to draw again (TanStack's `QueryCache.remove`); a reset tells them, and asks again for those on show. The PIN login lives on the home page `/` of a registered device (`AtTheDevice`, `src/features/login/at-the-device.tsx`), in a `login` area of `AppFrame` with *Inahitaji mtandao*, outside the counter lock; a new device still gets *Karibu* and *Sajili kifaa hiki*, and the line naming the device went, as the bar names the shop and the side. It asks `me`: an answer shows *Karibu, {name}*, with the person's nickname and side in the bar (`sideAt`, `who-is-here.ts`: the device's home side if they hold a key there, or else their first side); a refusal under 500 shows the PIN login, with its reason unless it is plain `not_authenticated`; a 5xx or no answer shows the reason, and someone already shown stays through a dropped network. The pad (`pin-pad.tsx`) takes the laptop's number keys and Backspace too, but no key held down or pressed with Ctrl, Alt or Cmd; it empties itself as it sends, never shows the digits, and tells a screen reader how many are typed. A tile is the shop's second button, grown to two lines, with a space between the two names, so a screen reader doesn't say them as one word. The PIN goes through the store (`useMutation`), and the keys and *Rudi* stay off until `me` has answered for the new person. The logout is sent again while no answer comes, with no limit, so a press with no network still ends the session once the network is back: given up, the session would outlive the press, and `me` would bring the person back. The person is hidden from the press, and the screen says *Unatoka…*, not the tiles: a PIN login sent meanwhile could be ended by the logout landing after it. A refused logout says why, and the person stays. The idle lock (`idle-lock.ts`) writes down the last tap or key press, and looks at the clock every 10 seconds and as the window comes back, so a laptop that slept locks as it opens; it locks once, and only while someone is here. Honest limits: the idle lock runs on the home page only, as no counter screen exists yet (SYNC-11); reading without touching counts as idle; a reload while a logout waits for the network forgets the logout, and the server then ends the session at its own idle time; two windows of the app on one laptop share one session, as they share its cookie, so a login in one ends the other's, and a logout waiting in one could end a login made meanwhile in the other; a PIN login needs the network until SYNC-10. Jack checked the Swahili on 7 Nov 2026. A hand test's person gets a PIN with `set_pin` in `manage.py shell`, typed through `getpass`, so it shows nowhere. Shown by hand between two browsers (Jack, 7 Nov 2026): Edge as a brand-new laptop, paired from the manager's Chrome; Meneja's tile; a wrong PIN, then the right one; *Karibu, Meneja*, with *Badilisha mtu* and *Toka* in the bar; after 15 minutes untouched, the tiles again; a login again; *Toka*. From the review (Jack, 7 Nov 2026) **(v1.46)**: one must-fix, and Jack chose to fix everything. A session the server ended stays ended through a failed refresh: the store kept the person's last answer beside a 502 after it, which brought them back with *Toka* and the idle lock, so a refusal with an answer still held forgets them at once. A session refused for a reason, as a suspension, is told once and ended on the server, which went on refusing it with the same reason to every next person: the logout carries who pressed and why (`Leaving`, `at-the-device.tsx`), and the reason goes at the first tile tap, *Rudi* included; a refused logout's reason shows only to whoever pressed. No PIN stays once the server has answered: the try is let go of as no screen holds it (`gcTime: 0`), and its PIN is wiped from what was sent, as a wrong PIN may be someone's real one. Focus moves to each step's heading, as UI-3's forms do. `me` gives a password the shop's idle minutes, as a password names no device and the shared laptop's screen locks the same. The pad's listener is put on once (`useEffectEvent`) and skips a key still being composed. The status line is always on the page, so every screen reader says *Unatoka…*, and it says *Subiri kidogo…* while `me` comes. `forgetTheLastPerson` drops a change waiting offline too (UI-5's note), and the older notes that said `clear()` say so in place. Jack checked the new Swahili on 7 Nov 2026.

The card's `PinPad.test.tsx` and `StaffPicker.test.tsx` are named in the repo's own way. ### UI-5 · Password login screens
`M` · Claude-led · after AUTH-3, AUTH-6 **(v1.29)**, UI-2 · branch `feat/ui-5-password-login`
**Build:** login (email or phone + password), forgot password, set a new password from the email link, logout — the first forms, with React Hook Form + Zod. Forgot password tells a person with no email to ask the owner, who sets a temporary one face to face (ADR-0038); a temporary password leads straight to a "choose your own password" form (AUTH-6). allauth's routes (`/_allauth/`) answer in allauth's own error shape, not the API's one shape (CORE-6): the forms read its codes **(v1.29)**. From AUTH-3 **(v1.30)**: a login takes a phone only as `+255…`, and refuses `0712…` as `invalid_phone`, so the form writes the number out in full before it sends it. A reset link opens `/password-reset/#<key>`, with the page's own slash **(v1.48)**: the form reads the key from `location.hash`, and sends it back in `key`. The codes: `email_password_mismatch`, `phone_password_mismatch` (an unknown phone too), `invalid_phone`, `phone.invalid`, `too_many_login_attempts`, `invalid_password_reset`. From AUTH-3's review **(v1.31)**: `+255 712 345 678` with spaces is refused as `invalid_phone` too, so the form takes the spaces out. Logging in while already logged in answers 409. A limit can answer 429, and a switched-off person with the right password gets 401, both with no error code to read. Once the form has read the reset key, it clears it from the browser's history (`history.replaceState`). From AUTH-6 **(v1.32)**: any office route answering 403 `password.must_change` opens the "choose your own password" form, which sends `current_password` and `new_password` to `POST /_allauth/browser/v1/account/password/change`. A 200 keeps this session and opens the screens, with no second login. Its codes: `required`, `enter_current_password`, `password.same_as_temporary` (the reset link's form meets this one too), and the checker's. A limit answers 429 after 5 tries in a minute. From AUTH-4 **(v1.34)**: `GET /api/auth/me/` also answers for a password's session, with `device` and `home_side` empty, and says `must_change_password`, while every other office route answers 403 `password.must_change`. `POST /api/auth/logout/` ends a password's session too; allauth's own way still works. From AUTH-4's second review **(v1.36)**: on a browser holding a PIN's session, `auth/session` answers 401 but `auth/login` answers 409, so the form sees nobody, tries, and gets a bare conflict: log out first. From UI-2 **(v1.44)**: allauth's routes aren't in the API's description, so the typed client (`api`) can't call them: call them with `fetchOrNoConnection`, so a form offline says "check the internet" and not "something went wrong", and put `csrfHeader()` on each change, the client's own reader of the `csrftoken` cookie (both in `src/api/client.ts`, from UI-2's second review). Empty the store of server answers after a login and a logout, as one browser may serve two people: with `forgetTheLastPerson`, not `clear()` (UI-4) **(v1.46)**. Office routes' refusals show with `ApiErrorAlert`, and `password.must_change` has its sentence. From UI-3 **(v1.45)**: until this form, a manager logs in for a hand test with one line in the browser's Console, through allauth's login route. *Vifaa* (`/devices/`) reads the person's keys from `me` (`useWhoIsHere()`), so the store's clearing at a login makes it read the new person's. Nothing links to *Vifaa* yet, though the new laptop tells the manager to open it: after a login the manager lands in the office area, whose menu shows *Vifaa* to whoever holds `device.manage` (Jack, 7 Nov 2026, at UI-3's review). From UI-4 **(v1.46)**: empty the store with `forgetTheLastPerson(store)` (`src/api/query-client.ts`) after a login and a logout, not `clear()`, which leaves a screen on show with the last person's answer. A registered laptop's home page is the PIN login; a phone that is only a manager's own still opens on *Karibu*. `AppFrame` takes `person` and `actions` for the bar, and *Toka* there follows the PIN screen's (`at-the-device.tsx`): sent again until the server hears it, the person hidden from the press. `forgetTheLastPerson` drops a change waiting offline too, as TanStack sends again only what its list of changes holds: let any such change go through before a login or a logout calls it. `me` gives a password the shop's `idle_minutes`, so a password on the shared laptop locks too; a phone that is only the person's own never shows that screen.
**Done when:**
- [x] A manager logs in from a phone browser, resets a password through the console email and reaches the office area.
**Watch out** **(v1.47)**: Settled while planning (Jack, 7 Nov 2026): one card, sized `M` and built in a longer sitting, about 4–6 hours. Its four forms (log in, forgot password, a new password from the email link, choose your own), the logout and the office area's frame come to more than twice `S`; Jack chose one card over a split that moved the three forms choosing a new password to a card of their own. After a login the manager lands on an office home page, `/office/`: *Karibu, Asha*, and a big button for each office screen the person may open, which grows as each screen comes. Today that is *Vifaa*, for whoever holds `device.manage`. Jack chose it over landing straight on a screen with a menu in the bar, as big buttons are easy to tap on a phone.
**Watch out** **(v1.48)**: Settled while building. allauth's routes are outside the API's description, so `callAllauth` (`src/api/allauth.ts`) calls them as the typed client calls an office route, and reads allauth's answer into the same `ApiRefusal`: an error naming a box goes beside it under `invalid`, as DRF's do; one for the whole form is the code. allauth sends three answers with no code, which the app names: 409 `login.already_in`, 401 at a login `login.switched_off`, 429 `throttled`. With no CSRF cookie yet, it asks `auth/session` for one first. The login has one box, *Simu au barua pepe*: an @ makes it an email, sent as typed; anything else is a phone, its spaces, dashes and brackets taken out and `0…` or `255…` written `+255…` (`login-name.ts`); the server's cleaner judges the rest. After a login, the store forgets the last person and the person goes to `/office/`; someone already logged in goes straight on, and the page shows *Subiri kidogo…* until `me` first answers, as the form flashed for a moment otherwise. *Karibu* on a phone that is only its owner's gets *Ingia* as its main button, and *Sajili kifaa hiki* becomes the second kind. The office's frame (`OfficeFrame`, `src/features/office/office-frame.tsx`) names the person and their shop in the bar, as a manager's phone knows no device; sends anyone `me` calls `not_authenticated` to the way in, `/login/` on a phone and `/` at a registered laptop, forgetting a session that ended first, or the login would read the old answer and send them back; shows a person refused for a reason why, in place of the screen, forgets them at once, so a 5xx a moment later can't bring their name back, and offers *Toka*, as the session still stands and would refuse a new login (409); puts *Toka* in the bar, sent again until the server hears it, with the screen hidden from the press; locks a registered laptop after `me`'s idle minutes, a password's too; and shows *Chagua nenosiri lako* in place of the screen while `me` says `must_change_password`. `me` alone says it, as a temporary password set later ends the person's sessions (AUTH-6), so every session meets the mark at its login. The office's home page (`office-home.tsx`) has one button today, *Vifaa*, for whoever holds `device.manage`: a new office screen adds its line there. The forgotten password says first that a person with no email asks the owner, face to face; its email box takes anything with an @, and says only that a link went *if* the email has an account. The email link's page reads the key after the #, takes it out of the address bar and the history at once, checks it (`GET auth/password/reset`), and then sets the password, where 401 means done, as a reset logs nobody in; a dead key says so with *Omba kiungo kingine*. Chosen by Jack while building (8 Nov 2026): every password box has an eye at its end that shows and hides what is typed (`PasswordField`, `src/components/design/password-field.tsx`, over `Field`'s new `end` slot), with the browser's spelling check and the phone's corrections off, shown or not; and a new password is still typed twice, as many people never press the eye. From the hand test (8 Nov 2026, normal Chrome standing in for the manager's phone): a new link pasted into an open reset page changes only what follows the #, which reloads nothing, and the old link's answer stayed on show; each key now gets its own check. Two traps for the next hand test: open the app at `localhost:8080`, where Caddy sends `/_allauth/` and `/api/` to Django, never Next.js's `:3000`, where they answer Next's 404 page; and the console prints an email the way mail carries it, so a link over 76 characters is split in two by an `=`: join it, or make the key as allauth does (`user_pk_to_url_str` and the reset key generator) and open it in a new tab. Tests swap Next.js's router for a stand-in in every file (`src/test/router.ts`, `vitest.setup.ts`). From the review (8 Nov 2026): the password login reads whose session holds the browser: the person's own password session goes on to the office; a PIN's, at the shared laptop, shows *{name} ameingia kwenye kifaa hiki* and *Toka*; a refused one, the reason and *Toka*; a login answered 409 asks `me` again. Before, a reset at the laptop and then *Ingia* went into the cashier's session. Every change in the store waits in one queue (`CHANGES_IN_ORDER`, `src/api/query-client.ts`), sent one at a time in the order made, offline too, so a logout goes only after a rename made before it: the UI-4 note above, "let any such change go through before a login or a logout", is kept by the store itself. No password stays in the store: the password forms' changes drop as nothing shows them (`gcTime: 0`), and the reset's form closes once done. The reset key leaves the address each time the address holds it, and the history entry is replaced with `null`, so Next.js's router forgets the old address too. Honest limits: the browser's own list of pages visited, and its suggestions as an address is typed, keep a reset link as it was opened, key and all, which no page can reach; the key works once, for an hour, and on a shared laptop a private window avoids it. The Done line's "phone browser" was Chrome on the laptop, standing in for a manager's phone, which never registers (Jack chose to say so over a test on a real phone); UI-6 can run the same steps at a phone's size. Chosen by Jack at the review: under the shop laptop's tiles, *Ingia kwa nenosiri*, for an owner with a password and no PIN. The Swahili was checked by Jack on 8 Nov 2026.

### UI-9 · Each person's language and font
`M` · Claude-led · after UI-4, UI-8 **(v1.40)** · branch `feat/ui-9-language-and-font`
**Build:** `User.font` holds `default` or `second`, with a database rule like the one on language. `PATCH /api/auth/me/` changes only your own `language` and `font`, and `me` answers both. A screen *Mipangilio yangu* changes them at once; offline it shows *Inahitaji mtandao*. A login or a quick switch applies that person's choices (ADR-0039).
**Watch out:** the font is stored by its role, not its name, so swapping a font later changes no rows. Office routes ask every change for a key, but your own settings need none: build this route beside `LogoutView`. The staff rows sent to a device must carry both, so an offline PIN login still shows the person's choices (SYNC-2, SYNC-10). A reload may show the default font for a moment; that is accepted. From UI-8 **(v1.42)**: `globals.css` has a rule for each role, `[data-font="default"]` and `[data-font="second"]`, and `data-font` changes the font only where `font-sans` is on the same element; `/design` shows both side by side. From UI-4 **(v1.46)**: after every PIN login, logout and quick switch, `me` is asked again at the home page (`AtTheDevice`, `src/features/login/at-the-device.tsx`), as `forgetTheLastPerson` resets it: that is where the person's choices apply. From UI-5 **(v1.48)**: a password's login, on a phone or the shared laptop, lands on `/office/`, whose frame asks `me` again after the store forgets the last person (`OfficeFrame`, `src/features/office/office-frame.tsx`): apply the person's choices there too, and *Mipangilio yangu* gets its button on the office's home page (`office-home.tsx`).
**Watch out** **(v1.49)**: Settled while planning (Jack, 8 Nov 2026): the bar's language button shows only while nobody is logged in. It is the laptop's own language, kept on this browser, for the tiles and the login pages; once someone is in, they change theirs in *Mipangilio yangu*, the one place for it, as Google's sign-in page and account settings do. Three fixes to this card, in the plan Jack approved. The route is `PATCH /api/auth/me/settings/` (`OwnSettingsView`), not a `PATCH` on `me/`: Django gives one address one view, and `me/` is an office route, which asks every change for a key (PPL-5). It is on the sweep's `NOT_SHOP_ROUTES` (`backend/tests/sweep_lists.py`), the one route there whose login reads shop rows: the caller's own job and its shop, and for a PIN's session the device that holds the key, each inside the shop its login door names, through the office routes' own login, so a suspension, a temporary password, a copied badge and a blocked laptop are refused as on every office route; `test_settings_answer_holds_nothing_of_a_shop` proves its answer is the two choices alone. And *Mipangilio yangu* has a button on the PIN home's welcome too (`at-the-device.tsx`), as a seller logged in by PIN never sees the office's home page. Settled while building: `Font` (`default`, `second`) and `OWN_SETTINGS` live beside `User`; the Django admin shows the font but can't change it, and doesn't ask for it when a person is added. `change_own_settings` writes only the changed columns, with no lock, no version and no audit line: it changes no shop's rows, nobody's rights and no money. The answer has a read-only serializer of its own, so the app's types say both choices are always there. *Mipangilio yangu* (`/my-settings/`, `src/features/people/my-settings.tsx`) saves a tap at once and sends only that choice; its dots show every tap still on its way; the answer goes into the store's `me`, for the person who tapped only; offline, both choices are switched off; *Rudi* goes to `/` for a PIN's session and to `/office/` for a password's. `Choice` takes a row's own `lang` and `font`, so "English" is said as English and each font's row shows that font. The app follows the person through `useFollowThePerson` (`src/features/people/follow-the-person.ts`), which both frames call with the `here` they show: the person's language opens over the laptop's own, in memory only, never saved on the browser (`setPersonsLanguage`, `src/i18n/language.ts`), so the tiles after them stay in the laptop's; their font goes on `<html>` as `data-font`. Nobody here, or the frame leaving the page, as a blocked laptop's lock does, brings back the laptop's own. An honest limit, from the review: on a phone that is only a manager's own, the home page `/` (*Karibu*) knows nobody, so it shows the phone's own language and the bar's button even while the manager is logged in; managers work in `/office/`, which follows them. The Swahili was checked by Jack on 8 Nov 2026.
