### UI-6 · End-to-end tests in CI
`M` · Claude-led · after UI-4, OPS-1 · branch `test/ui-6-e2e`
**Build:** Playwright in CI against the backend image and PostgreSQL in Docker (03 §11); the first test: pair a device → PIN login → idle lock → log in again → log out.
**Watch out** **(v1.46)**: from UI-4: the new laptop is a browser context that never saw the site; by hand, a Chrome Incognito window shares one session with every other one open, which kept an old laptop in UI-4's hand test, so Edge was the new one. The idle lock waits `me`'s minutes, 15 by default, and looks at the clock every 10 seconds: move Playwright's clock (`page.clock`) rather than wait. The PIN screen needs a person with a PIN at the shop (DATA-1). From UI-5 **(v1.48)**: a second test can follow UI-5's hand test: `/login/` → `/office/` → *Toka* → *Umesahau nenosiri?* → the email link → a new password → log in with it. Read the link from the server's email, joining a line the email split with `=` (the console's, and any mail's, form), or make the key as allauth does; open it in a new page, as a reset page already open changes only what follows the #.
**Watch out** **(v1.50)**: Two fixes to this card, in the plan Jack approved (8 Nov 2026). The test needs a shop with a manager and a seller with a PIN, and DATA-1, which the note above names for it, comes after this card, with no management command yet: `backend/tests/e2e_shop.py` makes that shop through the services (`set_up_shop`, `add_staff`, `assign_role`, `set_pin`) until DATA-1's seed replaces it. And "a broken login turns CI red" blocks a merge only as a required check: `e2e` joined `main.json` once it had run green on its pull request (#164), as the runbook asks. Settled while building. The tests run against the backend image and an in-memory PostgreSQL (`compose.e2e.yml`, project `mpc-e2e`), the image started as the demo starts (`start-demo.sh`), at `http://localhost:8090`: `localhost`, never `127.0.0.1`, as the production settings mark the cookies Secure, which Chrome takes over plain http from localhost only; 8090 runs beside `make dev`. `scripts/run_e2e.sh` wipes anything an earlier run left, starts the pair, makes the shop (the file's text passed to `manage.py shell --command`, as `shell` reads stdin only if it is ready the moment it looks), runs `pnpm e2e`, prints both containers' logs on a failure, and always wipes them. `make check-e2e` is part of `make check`; `make build-image` is shared with `check-image`, so `make check` builds the image once. CI's `e2e` is a job of its own, which builds the image again on its own machine (about 1.5 minutes in all) and installs the browser's system libraries with `playwright install-deps`, which needs sudo; a laptop does that once by hand (Jack's had them all). Only Chromium's headless shell (`--only-shell`), as the shop's devices run Chrome or Edge; no retries, as a test that passes on its second go hides a fault; a trace and a screenshot on a failure, which CI keeps for 7 days. The words come from `messages/sw.json`, never typed in a test. The manager's browser is a phone (`devices["Pixel 7"]`), as UI-5's note asked. The laptop's clock is installed before its first page (`context.clock.install()`) and keeps normal time until `fastForward` jumps it 15 minutes, as a lid closed and opened. Left out while building, as told to Jack before the commit: a "14 minutes, still in" check, as a real browser can prove nothing happened only by waiting, and a lock firing early could still pass it before its logout landed; `idle-lock.test.tsx` proves the minute. Found while building: the cleanup first ran from `frontend/` and found no compose file, which left the containers running unseen: every path now starts from the repo root, and a failed cleanup says so. The PIN screen's heading is the person's name; *Andika PIN yako* is the line under it. Proven red: with `pin_matches` broken on purpose, the run failed at *Asha logs in with her PIN*. Not done here: UI-5's second test (the password reset) waits for a card that needs it; and UI-7's count of the fonts in the service worker's list stays in `smoke_image.sh`, as this test doesn't look at fonts. From the review (8 Nov 2026), all fixed by Jack's choice but one. `e2e_shop.py` makes its shop, whose manager's password is public, only where the container is marked (`E2E_SHOP=yes`, in `compose.e2e.yml` alone) and no shop exists yet, so pasted into the demo's or the real server's shell it refuses. The test reads `me`'s answer after the PIN and checks `idle_minutes` is 15 (`session.idle_minutes`), as a lock set to 1 minute by mistake still fires at a 15-minute jump: with the default broken to 1, the old test passed and the new one failed. The two browsers are fixtures (`laptop`, with its clock, and `managersPhone`), which Playwright closes after a test, passed or failed, once it has kept the failure's screenshots and trace; a `try/finally` inside the test would close them first and lose the pictures. `forbidOnly` is on on a laptop too, as a stray `test.only` would run alone and pass `make check`; one test runs by naming its file. A test may take 90 seconds, not Playwright's 30. A failed run names no culprit and prints both logs; a leftover Docker refuses to remove says why. The laptop's one-time libraries come with `cd frontend && pnpm exec playwright install-deps chromium`, without `sudo`, which can't find a pnpm in the user's folders (Playwright calls sudo for apt itself), and the README says so. `make help` lists `check-e2e`: its pattern skipped names with a digit. Dependabot reads `compose.e2e.yml` too and skips `mpc-backend`, which no registry holds: after the merge, check *Insights → Dependency graph → Dependabot*. The `e2e` job's limit is 15 minutes, like the others: it took about 1.5. Left out by Jack's choice: caching Playwright's browser in CI, as Playwright's own guide advises against it (restoring takes about as long as downloading, and apt's libraries can't be cached).
**Done when:**
- [x] The test runs on every PR, and a broken login turns CI red.
**Tests:** `e2e/pair-and-pin-login.spec.ts`. Built too **(v1.50)**: in `scripts/test_same_postgres.py`, `test_the_browser_tests_name_the_same_postgres`. From the review **(v1.50)**: `test_e2e_shop.py` (`test_e2e_shop_refused_outside_the_test_container`, `test_e2e_shop_refused_where_a_shop_exists`, `test_e2e_shop_made_on_an_empty_test_database`) · in the story, the server's idle minutes.
**Commits:** `test(e2e): playwright in ci` → `test(e2e): pair and pin login` → `ci: the browser tests must pass before a merge` → `docs(plan): ui-6 says what it built` **(v1.50)**. From the review **(v1.50)**: `fix(e2e): the test shop is made only on the tests' empty database` → `test(e2e): the server's idle minutes are checked, not only the lock` → `docs: a fresh clone learns the browser's one-time setup` → `fix(build): make help lists check-e2e` → `ci(deps): dependabot leaves the tests' local image alone` → `test(e2e): a stray test.only fails on a laptop too` → `test(e2e): the story gets 90 seconds, not the default 30` → `test(e2e): the story's browsers are fixtures, closed pass or fail` → `fix(e2e): a failed run names no wrong culprit and shows both logs` → `docs: ui-6's comments say what is true` → `docs(plan): the ui-6 card and its notes follow the review`

### DATA-1 · Demo seed, first version
`S` · Claude-led · after PPL-3, OPS-2 · branch `feat/data-1-demo-seed`
**Build:** `manage.py seed_demo`: a demo shop with sides D and F, staff with made-up Swahili names, roles and PINs, and a manager with a password; each demo deploy — and a nightly scheduled redeploy — resets and reseeds it (03 §12); development and E2E tests use it too.
**Watch out:** the manager is the demo's first account with a password, and `/admin/login/` has no rate limit yet (PPL-1 review), so anyone could try passwords there without end. Before the seed runs on the demo, limit the login attempts, or keep that manager out of the admin (`is_staff` off) **(v1.8)**. Make the shop with `create_shop` (SHOP-2), which today makes only live shops: add a `kind` argument, so the shop the seed makes is a demo one. A live one would be refused by the seed itself at its next run, since it refuses a shop that isn't a demo **(v1.15)**. From PPL-3 **(v1.24)**: make the shop with `set_up_shop` (`apps/people/services.py`), which calls `create_shop` and copies the six roles in, so the `kind` argument goes on both; give the staff their roles with `assign_role`. From UI-4 **(v1.46)**: the PIN screen's tiles are the shop's people who can log in today (`GET /api/devices/v1/staff/`), so the demo's staff need PINs, set with `set_pin`, for a visitor to try it. From UI-6 **(v1.50)**: until this seed exists, the browser tests make their own shop with `backend/tests/e2e_shop.py`, which `scripts/run_e2e.sh` runs inside the test container. Replace it: run the seed there instead, delete the file, and move the test's logins in `frontend/e2e/pair-and-pin-login.spec.ts` (the manager's email and password, the seller's names and PIN, the side) to the demo's people. The test container's database is new on every run. When the file goes, its guard goes too (`E2E_SHOP` in `compose.e2e.yml`, and `backend/tests/test_e2e_shop.py`): the seed has a refusal of its own, of any shop that isn't a demo.
**Watch out** **(v1.51)**: Three fixes to this card, in the plan Jack approved (8 Nov 2026). *Once a day, not on every deploy:* the free demo sleeps after 15 minutes with no visitor and every wake-up runs its start again, so a start keeps a demo shop made today, in the shop's own time, and makes it again only when it was made on an earlier day (`--fresh` always does): a visitor back from a break meets the PIN lock (UI-4) with their work still there. A deploy during the day keeps the day's shop, so a changed seed shows the next morning. *The sign stays:* the card dropped `E2E_SHOP` with `e2e_shop.py`, but a brand-new real server has no shop yet, so the kind check alone would let a shop with public logins land there; the seed also needs `DEMO_SEED=yes` (`config/settings/base.py`), set only by `render.yaml`, `compose.e2e.yml` and `make seed` (Jack: A). *Fixed random seed:* this first version picks nothing at random, so the repeatable test compares two builds; the first random choice (DATA-2's clock-ins) takes a fixed seed. Settled with Jack: the people, as drafted (Jack: keep the list): *Duka la Mfano*, code DEMO, sides D *Dawa* and F *Vyakula*, the app's own word (Jack); *Meneja Mfano* (Meneja), manager on every side, `meneja@example.com` with the password `jaribu-demo`, no PIN; *Asha Mfano* (Asha), seller on Dawa, PIN 2580; *Baraka Mfano* (Baraka), seller on Vyakula, PIN 1470. All public on purpose (README, `docs/runbooks/demo.md`). Built. The seed is `apps/demo/services.py`, run by `manage.py seed_demo`: in a new app on a layer of its own above every other, as it will need staffpay, catalog and sales, and the demo's image holds `backend/apps` only (ADR-0041). It refuses without the sign or with any shop that isn't a demo, before anything is written, then, in one transaction, wipes the database with `flush` (TRUNCATE, which the ledgers' no-delete triggers don't watch; safe only because of the two refusals) and makes the shop through the services (`set_up_shop(kind=DEMO)`, `create_user`, `add_staff`, `assign_role`, `set_pin`), so a failed seed leaves yesterday's demo as it was. `create_shop` and `set_up_shop` take a `kind`, live unless told; a practice shop is still refused, as it must name the shop it copies. Nobody the seed makes is `is_staff`, so `/admin/login/` turns even the right password away (the v1.8 note above). The command prints one line at the start and one at the end, with counts and no names, emails, PINs or password. `make seed` runs it on a laptop with the sign and `--fresh`; on Jack's laptop it refuses, as the TRY shop there is live. The demo's start (`backend/start-demo.sh`) runs it after the migrations; `render.yaml` carries the sign. `deploy-demo.yml` runs every night at 21:30 UTC, 00:30 in Arusha: the same run as a merge's, whose start finds yesterday's shop. The browser tests' container starts the same way, so it seeds itself and `scripts/run_e2e.sh` has no seed step; `e2e_shop.py`, `test_e2e_shop.py` and `E2E_SHOP` are gone, and `pair-and-pin-login.spec.ts` logs in as the demo's people. Found while building: PostgreSQL won't empty a table while link checks on it still wait in the same transaction ("pending trigger events"), which a second seed in one test met; the seed runs the waiting checks first (`connection.check_constraints()`). Proven red: with the live-shop refusal broken on purpose, `test_seed_demo_refuses_live_shop` failed. Not done here: no side is switched on (no service does that yet, ADR-0033); OPS-3 shows the logins on the screen. From the review (8 Nov 2026), all fixed by Jack's choice but two. The nightly run's smoke test passed at once, as the demo already showed the commit, so a night whose seed failed stayed green: a night's image now carries a version of its own (`<commit>-nightly-<run>`), which only the restarted demo answers with, and is pushed under that name, so a night never moves a merge's tag. Tests at the edges: midnight in Arusha both ways (`timezone.now` moved), a live shop beside the demo shop, a practice shop, every table unchanged by a refusal, the public logins written in the test, and nobody logged in after the admin's form; each turned red when the code was broken on purpose (the day read in UTC, a check for live shops only, Baraka's PIN changed). On the demo nobody changes a password or a PIN (Jack: A): a visitor who changed Meneja's password would lock out everyone after them until the next day. One rule, `check_logins_may_change` (`apps/people/services.py`, code `demo.logins_fixed`, in both word books), read from the sign rather than a shop's kind, as the own-password form has no shop open; it stops a person's PIN change, a temporary password and one's own password (`ChangeOwnPasswordInput.clean`), and the system (the seed) still sets PINs. The browser tests' container carries the sign too, so a test there can't change a password or a PIN; the email reset is not stopped, as only the demo's log holds its link. A deadlock with the old demo during a deploy (PostgreSQL's 40P01) gets one more try; a second, or any other database error, stops the start. Comments made true: a failed seed on a wake-up leaves the demo down with only Render's Events and Logs to say why (runbook); the daily reset needs TRUNCATE, which a later database user must keep on the demo (03 §5.4, `apps/core/operations.py`); the layers' ADR is ADR-0041; flush leaves the cache tables and the list of migrations; ADR-0041 says what keeps the seed shut: its sign and its refusals, not the layer. One commit's `Why:` reworded (seven layers, now eight). Left by Jack's choice: the split second between the seed's look and its wipe (B: nothing on the demo can make a shop, and the real server has no sign; a comment says so), and the reset's hour (00:30 in Arusha is afternoon in the US, but any hour is someone's day, and a wake-up wipes before the visitor starts).
**Done when:**
- [x] The seed builds the same shop every time (fixed random seed). This version picks nothing at random, so the test compares two builds **(v1.51)**.
- [x] It refuses to run against a shop that isn't a demo.
**Tests:** `test_seed_demo_refuses_live_shop` · `test_seed_demo_is_repeatable`. From the review: `test_seed_demo_refuses_a_live_shop_beside_the_demo_shop`, `test_seed_demo_refuses_a_practice_shop`, `test_seed_demo_refused_without_the_sign_writes_nothing`, `test_seed_demo_makes_the_shop_again_after_midnight_in_arusha`, `test_seed_demo_keeps_a_shop_made_after_midnight_in_arusha`, `test_seed_demo_tries_again_once_after_a_deadlock`, `test_seed_demo_stops_after_a_second_deadlock`, `test_seed_demo_stops_at_once_on_another_database_error`; `test_own_password_stays_on_the_demo`, `test_no_temporary_password_on_the_demo`, `test_nobody_sets_a_pin_on_the_demo`, `test_the_system_still_sets_pins_on_the_demo`.

From the review: `fix(ci): the nightly run waits for the restarted demo` → `test(data): the demo seed's day and refusals are proven at their edges` → `fix(people): nobody changes a password or a PIN on the demo` → `fix(data): the demo seed tries once more after a deadlock` → `docs: comments and docs say what the demo seed really does`.

## 7. Stage 2 — Offline engine

**Goal:** a document made offline on a shop device reaches the server exactly once and in order; reference data flows back; the app updates without losing anything. The clock-in is the first document (BP6).
**Done when:** the Playwright test *offline clock-in → reload → online → exactly one event on the server, and pushing again changes nothing* passes (SYNC-11); a visitor can try it on the demo.
**You'll learn:** IndexedDB and Dexie; the outbox pattern; idempotency; row locks and commit order; WebCrypto; Web Locks; testing offline with Playwright.
**Order:** top to bottom. The server side (SYNC-1 to ATT-1) and the device side (SYNC-6 to SYNC-10) can be interleaved.

### SYNC-1 · Change log in commit order
`M` · Claude-led · after AUTH-1 · branch `feat/sync-1-change-log`
**Learn:** why change numbers must follow commit order, not start order (03 §6.4); row locks.
**Build:** `core.Change` (shop, seq, entity, record id, op: upsert or archive), append-only; `record_change()` collects changes during a transaction and, as the last step inside it, takes their numbers from the shop's locked `ShopSequence` row — through a context manager that every service uses.
**Watch out** **(v1.52)**: Two fixes to this card, in the plan Jack approved (9 Nov 2026). The test commit moves last: a commit of tests alone would be red, so each step brings its own tests, written first and shown red, and `test(core): commit-order numbering` holds the proofs on two connections. And the context manager "every service uses" is built and proven here, but no service uses it yet: SYNC-2 picks which records travel, and wires the services that change them. Built. A saving service opens `with recording_changes(shop):` (`apps/core/changes.py`) in place of `transaction.atomic()`, and calls `record_change(row)` after each change it makes (`ChangeOp.ARCHIVE` when the record leaves the lists). The block collects the records in a context variable and, as its last step inside the transaction, takes all their numbers at once from the shop's `change` counter (`next_numbers`, `apps/core/sequences.py`: one lock and one write however many), then writes the lines in one INSERT. The counter stays locked until the commit, so the shop's next save waits only for that last moment. A line names its record by its table's link type code (`apps/core/links.py`) and its id, and holds none of its data. A record changed twice in one save is one line, with the last `op`. A block of the same shop inside another joins it, so services that call services number once, at the outer end; if the inner part fails and the outer carries on, what it recorded goes with its writes. A block that recorded nothing takes no number and no lock. `record_change` outside a block or for another shop's row, and a block for a shop that isn't open, are refused loudly (T1). `core.Change` is a ledger (`AppendOnly`, its trigger in `core`'s 0002): one line per number in a shop (`core_change_one_per_seq`, also the pulls' index), numbers from 1 (a cursor of 0 means nothing read yet), and `op` and the shape of `entity` checked by the database. A code's shape lives in `apps/core/type_codes.py`, which `models.py` and `links.py` both import. Proven red, each by breaking the code on purpose: numbers taken at the block's start, no lock on the counter, one lock on every shop's counter. The last one passed at first: a shop's first counter row, not yet committed, is invisible to the other shop, so the test now starts from yesterday's change in both shops. Not done here: a save that records changes in several shops at once (SYNC-2's note), and handing a document its last number as `posted_seq` (SYNC-4's note). From the review (9 Nov 2026), all fixed by Jack's choice. A change is named only at its block's own level: inside an `atomic()` or `editing()` part within it, which could roll back alone while the block carries on, `record_change` is refused, as it would leave a line for a record never saved; a block opened inside such a part doesn't join the outer one, and numbers at its own end (`levels`, Django's `connection.atomic_blocks`). Code that calls more than one saving service opens the block itself, never a plain `atomic()`: there the first block to end would hold the shop's counter until the commit, and Django has no step before a commit to hook into, so a rule says it (`backend.md`). The log has one index on its shop, not two (`db_index=False`). Tests for blocks of two shops one inside the other, a normal day's rollback, no shop open, a table with no code, a failed block; one refusal's message no longer passes for another's. The comments say what is true, and cite ADR-0041.
**Done when:**
- [x] Two overlapping transactions in one shop get numbers in the order they commit (tested with two database connections).
- [x] A rolled-back transaction leaves no change rows and no gap.
- [x] Shops never wait for each other.
**Tests:** `test_change_numbers_follow_commit_order` · `test_rollback_leaves_no_changes` · `test_shops_do_not_block_each_other`. Built too **(v1.52)**: in `apps/core/tests/test_change_order.py`, `test_late_numbers_wait_for_the_shops_last_commit`; in `test_changes.py`, `test_changes_numbered_in_order_at_the_end`, `test_same_record_twice_is_one_change_with_the_last_op`, `test_record_change_needs_a_block`, `test_record_change_refuses_another_shops_row`, `test_nested_block_numbers_once_at_the_outer_end`, `test_nested_block_rolled_back_drops_its_changes`, `test_block_with_no_change_takes_no_number`; in `test_change_model.py`, `test_change_log_refuses_update_and_delete_in_database`, `test_change_op_must_be_known`, `test_change_entity_must_be_a_type_code`, `test_one_change_per_number_in_a_shop`, `test_change_numbers_start_at_one`, `test_change_log_has_a_counter_of_its_own`; in `test_sequences.py`, `test_counter_gives_several_numbers_at_once`, `test_counter_refuses_fewer_than_one_number`. From the review: in `test_changes.py`, `test_record_change_refused_inside_a_part_that_can_fail_alone`, `test_block_inside_a_part_that_can_fail_alone_numbers_on_its_own`, `test_blocks_of_two_shops_one_inside_the_other`, `test_a_shops_block_inside_another_shops_block_numbers_on_its_own`, `test_rollback_of_a_later_save_gives_its_numbers_back`, `test_block_needs_an_open_shop`, `test_record_change_refuses_a_table_with_no_type_code`, `test_record_change_refused_after_a_failed_block`; in `test_change_model.py`, `test_change_log_has_one_index_on_its_shop`.
**Commits:** `test(core): commit-order numbering` → `feat(core): Change model` → `feat(core): record_change context manager`. As built **(v1.52)**: `feat(core): a shop's counter can give several numbers at once` → `feat(core): Change model` → `feat(core): record_change context manager` → `test(core): commit-order numbering` → `docs(plan): sync-1 says what it built`. From the review: `fix(core): a change is named only where its block can roll it back` → `test(core): blocks of two shops, one inside the other` → `perf(core): the change log keeps one index on its shop, not two` → `refactor(core): the type code shape lives in a module of its own` → `test(core): the change log's refusals and rollback at their edges` → `docs: comments and rules say how to take a shop's numbers safely` → `docs(plan): the sync-1 card and its notes follow the review`.

### SYNC-2 · Bootstrap
`M` · Claude-led · after SYNC-1, AUTH-4 · branch `feat/sync-2-bootstrap`
**Build:** a registry of syncable entities (a queryset plus the fields devices may see); `GET /api/sync/v1/bootstrap` (device key) → a snapshot of sides, payment accounts, staff (name, photo, status, `pin_changed_at`, `language` and `font` **(v1.40)**), permissions per side, and settings in force and future-dated — plus the cursor it is valid at, compressed.
**Watch out** **(v1.49)**: from UI-9: a staff row's `language` and `font` live on the person (`User`), who belongs to no shop, and change through `change_own_settings` (`apps/people/services.py`), which records no change today. Once staff rows travel to devices, a choice made on Asha's phone must reach the counter laptop's copy, or her offline PIN login shows the old one until a new bootstrap: record a staff change in each shop where she works, in the same transaction. The service then opens each of those shops in turn, where today it needs none open.
**Watch out** **(v1.52)**: from SYNC-1: no service records a change yet. Every service that changes a record the registry sends must name it in the log: open its transaction with `recording_changes(shop)` (`apps/core/changes.py`), not `transaction.atomic()`, and call `record_change(row)` after each change, `ChangeOp.ARCHIVE` when the record leaves the lists, at the block's own level: after `with editing(...)`, never inside it (refused, as that part could roll back alone). Code that calls several saving services opens the block itself, so they join it; an office route's create wraps one service in an `atomic()` of its own (`CreateOnceMixin`), which is fine for one. Prove it per table, with a change that lands in the log. A line names its table by its link type code (`apps/core/links.py`), so key the registry by those codes, and give a code to a table it sends that has none yet. The bootstrap's cursor is the highest number in the shop's log (0 for a shop with none), read before the rows, or with them in one `REPEATABLE READ` transaction: under Django's default, each query reads the database as it is at that query, so a cursor read after the rows could pass a change that committed between the two, which the rows don't hold, and the device would never get it; read first, a change the rows already hold is pulled again, which does no harm. The UI-9 note above records one change in several shops in one transaction: one block per shop, opened one after another, never one inside another (nested blocks take their numbers in the reverse of the order they opened, as the inner one ends first), and the shops taken in a fixed order (by id), or two such saves could each hold one shop's counter and wait for the other's, and PostgreSQL would end one with a deadlock error. A stronger guard to weigh here: `transaction.atomic(durable=True)` for a block that opens a transaction, which refuses one opened inside another (Django lets its test transactions through), at the cost of the tests that wrap a block in an `atomic()` to hold its numbers (from SYNC-1's review).
**Watch out** **(v1.53)**: settled while planning (Jack, 9 Nov 2026): the card held two jobs, about twice its size, so writing each change in the log moves to a new card, SYNC-15, right after it, with the two notes above on it (UI-9's, and SYNC-1's but for the cursor); SYNC-3 waits for SYNC-15. SYNC-2 builds the registry and the bootstrap, and its cursor is the last number in the shop's log, which SYNC-15's services fill: the bootstrap needs only that number, but a pull needs every change written. No photo: a staff member has none until PPL-4, which adds it to the registry.
**Watch out** **(v1.53)**: Built. The registry (`apps/sync/registry.py`) is the one list of the tables a device keeps a copy of, by link type code (`apps/core/links.py`, where a rule's row gets `setting`), in the bootstrap's order: each entry names its rows (`apps/sync/selectors.py`) and its fields (`apps/sync/api/serializers.py`, named one by one, never `__all__`). Sides: all of them, by letter. Payment accounts: those in use, each with its sides' ids; an archived one isn't sent. Staff: active and suspended, A to Z, with full name, nickname, status, `pin_changed_at`, language, font and keys; someone who left, or was switched off (`User.is_active`), isn't sent, as the PIN screen shows neither. Keys are worked out on the server (`held_permissions`), so the device does no role arithmetic: `every_side`, plus `sides`, each side's own codes; a suspended person holds none (X2). Rules: for each rule and side, the row in force on the shop's today and every later row, one per day, the row written last, as `get_setting` picks; never a side's row of a rule kept for the whole shop, nor a rule no longer in the list in core. Each rows function reads inside the shop it is given, which must be the one open (T1). `GET /api/sync/v1/bootstrap/` (`BootstrapView`, on `DeviceView`) answers `snapshot(shop)` (`apps/sync/bootstrap.py`): `cursor`, the shop's last change number (`last_change_number`, 0 for none), read first; then one list per registry code; then `setting_keys`, each rule's key, kind, scope and default as its kind writes it, as a new shop has no rule rows at all. The cursor is read before the rows rather than in one `REPEATABLE READ` transaction: that must be a transaction's first statement, which no test transaction allows, and a hand-written `SET` would join the sweep's `RAW_SQL`. Compressed (`gzip_page`) on this route only: an answer that mixes a secret with words a stranger chooses can leak through its compressed size (BREACH), and this one holds neither. `BootstrapAnswer` describes the answer for the schema, and a test holds it to the registry. The sweep calls the route as a device of each shop by itself; its two shops got dated rules with amounts only shop A has (`sweep_settings`, `backend/tests/conftest.py`). Nothing secret leaves: the answer is searched for a real person's PIN hash, password hash, phone and email and the device's key and its hash, and every field the registry sends is checked by its name and by what it reads, so a buying price a later card adds turns the test red. Proven red, each by breaking the code on purpose: the first row of a day kept instead of the last, the cursor read after the rows, every shop's rules read, and the PIN's hash added to the staff fields. `pin_changed_at` is empty for someone with no PIN, which tells only that nobody can log in as them on that device: UI-4's PIN screen hides even that, but SYNC-10 needs the time.
**Watch out** **(v1.53)**: from the review (Jack, 9 Nov 2026), all fixed by Jack's choice but two. The secrets check looks inside every kind of field: what a link shows of its row (`slug_field`), a many-link's link, a list's and a dict's items, a nested record; `profit` and `margin` join its words; a field that looks secret travels only by a decision written on its card, on `TRAVELS_BY_DECISION` (`test_bootstrap_secrets.py`), as customers' phones may with the contacts card. The answer's choices are named after their own types (`StaffStatusEnum`, `PaymentAccountKindEnum`, `SettingScopeEnum`, `SettingKindEnum`, and `LanguageEnum`, which a person and a shop share), so a later table's own `status` or `kind` renames none (`ENUM_NAME_OVERRIDES`, `config/settings/base.py`). A person's own login, on their phone, is refused: only a paired device's key opens the bootstrap. Each list names its own shop, proven with every shop open, as a system job has them. A device learns its shop's time zone, currency and language from `GET /api/devices/v1/me/`, not from the bootstrap: a shop's own row has no line in the change log, so a copy sent once would go stale, and the device asks that route every 5 minutes. The reads are pinned: more sides, accounts or rules cost no more, and each person costs two, for their keys. Each rule in `setting_keys` names its kind (`money`, `percent`, `whole`, `time`, `yes_no`: `KINDS`, `apps/core/settings_registry.py`), so a device reads "07:35" as a time. The answer is never cached (`never_cache`). Comments say what is true. Jack's choice on the third *Done when*: the lists are read one after another, the cursor first, not in one snapshot, which would need a hand-written `SET TRANSACTION` on the sweep's `RAW_SQL`; so one answer can be slightly out of step with itself (a drawer naming a side added between the two reads, a person caught mid-suspension, shown suspended or holding no keys, neither able to sell), and the next pull, right after, mends it. Not changed: the two commit subjects that are the card's own names, and the schema's allowing no login on device routes, from AUTH-2's `DeviceView`, left for later.
**Done when:**
- [x] A new device gets everything its counter needs in one response.
- [x] A test proves that no buying price, PIN hash or password ever leaves the server.
- [x] The cursor never counts a change the rows miss: it is read first, then the rows (Jack, from the review) **(v1.53)**.
**Tests:** `test_bootstrap_has_reference_data` · `test_bootstrap_never_sends_costs_or_pins` · `test_bootstrap_cursor_consistent`. Built too **(v1.53)**: in `apps/sync/tests/test_registry.py`, `test_registry_names_each_table_by_its_link_type_code`, `test_rows_are_read_only_inside_their_own_shop`, `test_every_side_is_sent`, `test_accounts_in_use_are_sent_with_the_sides_they_serve`, `test_people_at_work_are_sent_and_people_gone_are_not`, `test_a_persons_row_holds_what_the_counter_needs`, `test_each_persons_keys_are_sent_side_by_side`, `test_a_suspended_person_is_sent_holding_no_keys`, `test_each_rules_row_in_force_and_every_later_one_are_sent`; in `test_bootstrap.py`, `test_bootstrap_cursor_is_the_shops_last_change`, `test_bootstrap_is_compressed`, `test_bootstrap_needs_a_paired_device`, `test_answer_shape_names_every_registry_table`, `test_setting_keys_hold_every_rule_in_core`; in `test_bootstrap_secrets.py`, `test_no_registry_field_reads_a_secret`, `test_secret_check_finds_what_it_must`. From the review: in `test_registry.py`, `test_each_list_keeps_to_its_own_shop_with_every_shop_open`; in `test_bootstrap.py`, `test_bootstrap_reads_each_list_once_and_two_per_person`, `test_bootstrap_is_never_cached`; in `apps/core/tests/test_settings_registry.py`, `test_each_kind_has_a_name_of_its_own`.
**Commits:** `feat(sync): syncable registry` → `feat(sync): bootstrap endpoint` → `test(sync): nothing secret leaves`. As built **(v1.53)**: `docs(plan): sync-2 splits, and sync-15 writes each change in the log` → `feat(sync): syncable registry` → `feat(sync): bootstrap endpoint` → `test(sync): nothing secret leaves` → `docs(plan): sync-2 says what it built`. From the review: `docs(sync): the sync app's packages say what they hold` → `test(sync): the secrets check looks inside every kind of field` → `fix(api): the bootstrap's choices are named after their own type` → `test(sync): a person's own login can't open the bootstrap` → `test(sync): each list keeps to its own shop with every shop open` → `feat(people): a device learns its shop's time zone, currency and language` → `test(sync): the bootstrap reads each list once, and two per person` → `feat(sync): each rule's default says its kind` → `fix(sync): the bootstrap is never cached` → `docs(sync): comments say what is true` → `docs(plan): the sync-2 card and its notes follow the review`.

### SYNC-15 · Every change is written in the log
`M` · Claude-led · after SYNC-2 · branch `feat/sync-15-change-lines` **(v1.53)**
**Learn:** a block that joins the one around it; why several shops are locked in a fixed order (deadlocks).
**Build:** every service that changes a record SYNC-2's registry sends names it in the change log: it saves inside `with recording_changes(shop):` (`apps/core/changes.py`) in place of `transaction.atomic()`, and calls `record_change(row)` after each change, `ChangeOp.ARCHIVE` when the record leaves the lists. Today: `add_side` (the side, its cash drawer, which carries the sides it serves) and `rename_side`; `add_staff`, `suspend`, `unsuspend`, `mark_left` (archive: a person who left is no longer sent), `set_pin` (`pin_changed_at` travels); `assign_role` and `grant_permission` (a person's keys travel on their staff row); and `change_own_settings`, in each shop where the person works. Code that calls several saving services opens the block itself: `set_up_shop`, `create_shop` and the demo seed (`apps/demo/services.py`).
**Watch out** **(v1.53)**: moved here from SYNC-2, whose two notes on it, UI-9's and SYNC-1's, say it in full. Name a change at the block's own level: after `with editing(...)`, never inside it (refused). A person's language and font live on `User`, who belongs to no shop: record a staff change in each shop where they work, in the same transaction, one block per shop, opened one after another, never one inside another, the shops taken by id, or two such saves could deadlock. Finding those shops runs before any is open: decide whether `shops_of_person` (`apps/people/login_doors.py`), which reads two at most for a login, grows, or a door of its own joins it (ADR-0037). A person switched off or on (`is_active`), today only in Django's admin, leaves or joins the bootstrap's staff, so it needs a service that records each of their jobs too. Weigh `transaction.atomic(durable=True)` for a block that opens a transaction (SYNC-1's review). Prove it per table, with a change that lands in the log.
**Watch out** **(v1.54)**: settled while planning (10 Nov 2026). Jack's choice (A): the admin changes a person's name and language too, not only `is_active`, and all three travel on the staff row, so the admin's edit writes its lines as well, in a fourth feature commit. The technical choices, in the plan Jack approved: the person's door grows rather than a second door joining it, as it answers the same question; a door then serves more than a login, so ADR-0042 supersedes ADR-0037; and `durable=True` is not used, as a block is rightly opened inside a transaction already open in five places (an office create's `CreateOnceMixin`, the person's save over several shops, the admin's own transaction, the demo seed's wipe, and a part that may fail alone), each of which it would refuse. Built. `add_side` names the side and its drawer, which carries the sides it serves; `rename_side` the side, after `editing()` ends; `add_staff`, `suspend`, `unsuspend`, `set_pin`, `assign_role` and `grant_permission` the person's job, last, after the audit line or the grant's save; `mark_left` the job as `archive`. Each keeps its own refusals before the block, so their codes stay. `create_shop` and `set_up_shop` take an optional `shop_id`: the new shop's id is given as its row is made, so `set_up_shop`, and the demo seed around it and its people, open the shop and its block before the shop exists, and every line is numbered once, at the very end, after the roles. A person's change (`change_own_settings`; the admin's edit, through `change_person`, called by `UserAdmin.save_model`) runs `_record_each_job`: inside the transaction that saved the person, the shops from `shops_of_person`, which reads every current job's shop, ordered by id, not two at most; one block per shop, one after another; `archive` when the person is switched off. A line is written only when a field a laptop shows changed (`FIELDS_ON_STAFF_ROWS`: name, language, font, `is_active`), which a sync test pins to `StaffRecord`, as `people` can't read the registry. Old tests that read the log count from a mark (`backend/tests/change_log.py`: `last_line`, `lines_after`, `lines_written`), as the fixtures' services now write lines too. `test_two_managers_suspending_each_other_both_go_through` lets the second wait for the first one's number, which is correct, and still turns red with full locks (proven by breaking `_locked` on purpose: PostgreSQL's deadlock). Honest limit: a job added at another shop between the door's read and the person's commit misses that one change; it can happen only once AUTH-7 lets a person work at two shops, and the next change mends it. From the review (Jack: A, 10 Nov 2026): `OwnSettingsView` stays on the sweep's `NOT_SHOP_ROUTES`, though its save now reads the person's own job in each shop where they work, inside the shop the person's door names, and writes a staff line in each: its answer still holds nothing of a shop's (`test_settings_answer_holds_nothing_of_a_shop`), and it touches only the person's own jobs, so there is nothing of another shop's for the sweep to find; the list's reason says so (`backend/tests/sweep_lists.py`).
**Done when:**
- [x] Each service that changes a record the registry sends lands one line in the log, with the right `op`.
- [x] A person's own language or font lands a staff line in every shop where they work, in one transaction.
- [x] Setting up a shop numbers its lines once, at the end.
**Tests:** `test_each_service_records_its_change` · `test_own_settings_recorded_in_every_shop` · `test_setting_up_a_shop_numbers_once`. Built too **(v1.54)**: `test_each_service_records_its_change` in both `apps/shops/tests/test_change_lines.py` and `apps/people/tests/test_change_lines.py`; in `apps/shops/tests/test_create_shop.py`, `test_create_shop_numbers_its_lines_once`; in `apps/demo/tests/test_seed_demo.py`, `test_seed_demo_numbers_every_record_once`; in `apps/people/tests/test_own_settings.py`, `test_own_settings_all_or_nothing_across_shops`, `test_the_route_records_the_change`; in `apps/people/tests/test_change_lines.py`, `test_a_person_switched_off_leaves_every_shops_laptops`, `test_a_persons_name_or_language_reaches_every_shop`, `test_a_change_no_laptop_shows_writes_no_line`; in `test_admin.py`, `test_switching_a_person_off_in_the_admin_reaches_their_shops`; in `apps/sync/tests/test_registry.py`, `test_a_shop_with_no_change_is_at_zero`, `test_the_people_app_knows_which_of_a_persons_fields_travel`.
**Commits:** `feat(shops): side services record their changes` → `feat(people): staff and permission services record their changes` → `feat(people): a person's own settings reach every shop's devices`. As built **(v1.54)**: `feat(shops): side services record their changes` → `feat(people): staff and permission services record their changes` → `docs: a person's door finds every shop a change to them must reach (ADR-0042)` → `feat(people): a person's own settings reach every shop's devices` → `feat(people): the admin's changes to a person reach every shop's devices` → `docs(plan): sync-15 says what it built`

### SYNC-3 · Pull
`M` · Claude-led · after SYNC-15 **(v1.53)** · branch `feat/sync-3-pull`
**Build:** `GET /api/sync/v1/pull?cursor=N` → up to 1,000 changes after N with the current row data, the next cursor, the server time and the minimum app version; archived records arrive as `archive`.
**Watch out** **(v1.52)**: from SYNC-1: read `core.Change` (`apps/core/models.py`) by `seq > N`, in order; its unique rule (`core_change_one_per_seq`, shop then `seq`) is the index for it. Numbers start at 1, so 0 means nothing read yet. A line holds no data: read each record as it is now from its own table, found through SYNC-2's registry by the line's `entity`, a link type code (`apps/core/links.py`). A rollback takes its numbers back, so the log has no gaps today, but the device must not rely on that: compaction (OPS-7) would leave some.
**Watch out** **(v1.53)**: from SYNC-2: a line's `entity` is a key of `REGISTRY` (`apps/sync/registry.py`): read its records with `entry.rows(shop).filter(pk__in=...)` and send them through `entry.serializer`, the bootstrap's own fields, so a pull and a bootstrap can't disagree. A record a line names that is no longer among its entry's rows has left the device's lists (a person who left or was switched off, an archived account), so it can go as `archive` whatever the line's `op`. A rule's row replaced by a newer one may stay on a device, which reads the newest row on or before each day; a fix for a day already sent comes later in the log, so the device keeps the last row it was given for each rule, side and day. The bootstrap's cursor is `last_change_number` (`apps/sync/selectors.py`).
**Watch out** **(v1.54)**: from SYNC-15: every service that changes a record the registry sends now writes its line (03 §6.4), so a pull can be tested through the services themselves, not lines written by hand. `archive` lines come from `mark_left` and from a person switched off in the admin; a person's own change lands a line in each shop where they work. The tests read the log from a mark (`backend/tests/change_log.py`), as the fixtures write lines too.
**Watch out** **(v1.55)**: settled while planning (10 Nov 2026). One fix to the card, in the plan Jack approved: `test(sync): pull rules` held tests alone, a red commit, so each step brings its own tests, written first and shown red, as on SYNC-1. The technical choices, in the same plan: `min_app_version` is sent empty (`MIN_APP_VERSION`, `config/settings/base.py`) until SYNC-13 gives it a form a device can compare, as the app's version is a git commit; and what a restore does to a device's cursor is left to OPS-11. Built. `page(shop, cursor)` (`apps/sync/pull.py`) reads the shop's lines after the cursor, oldest first, one more than the 1,000 a page holds (`changes_after`, `apps/sync/selectors.py`, on the log's own index), then each record they name as it is now, one read per table for all its records, through the registry's rows and serializer, the bootstrap's own. Each change is `{seq, entity, id, op, data}`: `upsert`, with the record's row, when it is among its table's rows; `archive`, with none, when it isn't, whatever its line's `op` said, so a person switched off and on again comes back at both lines. Every line once, in order; a line of a table the registry doesn't send is passed over but counted. The page says `cursor`, its last line (the cursor asked when nothing came), `has_more`, `server_time` (UTC) and `min_app_version`. Lines first, then records: the ids come from the lines, and every change up to the last line had committed before the records are read. `GET /api/sync/v1/pull/` (`PullView`, on `DeviceView`) checks the cursor (`PullQuery`: whole, from 0 up to the log's biggest number; 0 when left out, so the tenancy sweep calls the route as it is, from the start of the log; anything else, a blank one too, 400 `invalid`, under `cursor`), and answers compressed and never cached, as the bootstrap does. `PullAnswer` describes the page for the schema: each change told apart by `entity`, one type per registry table (`CHANGE_RECORDS`) carrying that table's record, so the app's types know a `staff` change holds a person; a test holds it to the registry. The choices are named `ChangeOpEnum`. One commit more than planned, found while writing the docs: a page that passed lines over could hold fewer than 1,000 changes while more wait, so it says itself (`has_more`), from one line read past the page. Proven red, each by breaking the code on purpose: the line's `op` trusted instead of the record; every shop's log read, in the page's test and, through the route, by the sweep; one read per line; more changes counted from the page's changes rather than its lines.
**Watch out** **(v1.55)**: from the review (Jack, 10 Nov 2026), all fixed by Jack's choice but one. A pull is searched for secrets as the bootstrap is: Asha's PIN hash, password, phone and email and the laptop's key, as sent, and every field a page adds around a record, by name (`test_bootstrap_secrets.py`). The read order is proven with two connections: a rename that commits between the two reads shows in the record under its earlier line, and its own line comes on a later page. A blank cursor (`?cursor=`) is refused, as DRF would read it as left out, so as 0, the whole log again at every sync (`ChangeNumber`). The Done-when test asks again while a page says more wait, over pages of two lines, one of them holding only lines passed over. A rule's rows travel as `get_setting` reads them: the row in force and later ones as upserts, a row replaced or for a past day as archive. Each change carries its record as the bootstrap sends it, in all four tables; a page of exactly 1,000 says none wait; a person switched off in the admin goes as archive; the reads stay the same with a rule's lines on the page. Comments say what is true: why `data` is added in `get_fields()` (mypy, not DRF), the cursor's description (the last line read, sent or passed over), `null`, not empty, and "a later page". Proven red, each by breaking the code on purpose: a secret beside each change, and one as a change's field; the cursor worked out after the records; the naive rule for more; each record read by id from its table; 1,000 lines read with no line past; a rule's rows read one by one; the staff list forgetting who is switched off. Not changed, by Jack's choice: the three commit subjects that are the card's own names. From a second, focused review: a page sends exactly the fields its schema names, checked as sent, so the secrets check of those fields reads everything a page holds (shown red by a field added to the page alone); a row a same-day fix replaced goes as archive only to a laptop that hadn't got it, so a laptop reads the last row it was given for each rule, side and day (SYNC-7's and SHOP-3's notes); and a pull from 0 misses the rules' defaults and any record saved without a line, so it never stands in for a bootstrap.
**Done when:**
- [x] Pulling repeatedly gives every change once, in order.
- [x] Archived records arrive as `archive`; every page carries the server time.
**Tests:** `test_pull_returns_changes_after_cursor` · `test_pull_pages_of_1000` · `test_pull_sends_archive_ops`. Built too **(v1.55)**: in `apps/sync/tests/test_pull.py`, `test_pulling_again_gives_every_change_once_in_order`, `test_a_page_says_more_wait_though_it_passed_lines_over`, `test_a_person_switched_on_again_comes_back`, `test_each_change_carries_its_record_as_the_bootstrap_sends_it`, `test_a_line_of_a_table_devices_dont_keep_is_passed_over`, `test_pull_keeps_to_its_own_shop`, `test_pull_refuses_a_shop_that_is_not_the_one_open`, `test_a_page_reads_the_log_once_and_each_table_once`, `test_every_page_carries_the_server_time_and_the_oldest_app_allowed`; in `test_pull_route.py`, `test_pull_sends_the_changes_after_the_cursor`, `test_every_page_carries_the_server_time`, `test_a_pull_with_no_cursor_starts_from_the_beginning`, `test_a_cursor_that_is_no_change_number_is_refused`, `test_pull_needs_a_paired_device`, `test_pull_is_compressed`, `test_pull_is_never_cached`, `test_answer_shape_names_every_registry_table`. From the review: in `test_pull.py`, `test_a_change_between_the_two_reads_comes_again_with_its_own_line`, `test_a_rules_rows_come_as_the_device_reads_them`, `test_a_page_of_exactly_1000_says_none_wait`, `test_a_person_switched_off_goes_as_archive`; in `test_bootstrap_secrets.py`, `test_pull_never_sends_costs_or_pins`, `test_no_pull_field_reads_a_secret`.
**Commits:** `test(sync): pull rules` → `feat(sync): pull endpoint`. As built **(v1.55)**: `feat(sync): pull rules` → `feat(sync): pull endpoint` → `fix(sync): a page says whether more changes wait` → `docs(sync): how the pull is built`; from the review: `test(sync): a pull is searched for secrets as the bootstrap is` → `test(sync): a save that lands while a laptop pulls is never passed over` → `fix(sync): a blank cursor is refused, not read as 0` → `test(sync): every change once means asking again while a page says more wait` → `test(sync): a rule's rows reach a laptop as get_setting reads them` → `fix(sync): the pull's comments and its cursor's description say what is true` → `test(sync): the pull's smaller cases are each proven` → `docs(sync): the sync-3 card and its notes follow the review`; from the second review: `test(sync): a page sends exactly the fields its secrets check reads` → `docs(sync): a replaced rule row and a pull from 0 are described truly`

### SYNC-4 · Push: idempotent documents in device order
`M` · Jack-led · after SYNC-1, CTRL-1, AUTH-2 · branch `feat/sync-4-push`
**Learn:** idempotency; one document = one transaction (03 §5.5, §6.3).
**Build:** the `CounterRecord` base (device, `device_seq`, `client_time`, `received_at`); `POST /api/sync/v1/push` (up to 100 documents); a document-type registry (type + schema version → handler and the permission it needs); posting in `device_seq` order, each document in its own transaction, recording the person and the device (X3); answers `accepted` (with `posted_seq` and flags), `duplicate` (the stored answer), `invalid` (plus a flag) and `retry` (stop here); a UUID that exists in another shop → `invalid`, revealing nothing.
**Watch out:** inside the push's shop context, a lookup by id sees the current shop only (CORE-2), so a UUID from another shop looks new until the insert fails on the primary key. Request code may not read across shops, so that failure, caught for the one document, is what becomes `invalid`; follow what CORE-6 decided **(v1.13)**. CORE-6 decided **(v1.20)**: after any refusal, a rule's check or the primary key, look the id up again in this shop only. A twin that finished while this copy was checking makes its checks refuse too, so the look comes after every refusal, not only the primary key's. Found: two copies of one document raced, and the stored answer is the answer: for a document, its stored result (03 §5.5), not the row as it is now, which is what an office route's repeat returns. Not found, with a primary-key clash (psycopg's `constraint_name` is `<table>_pkey`): another shop has the id, and the document is refused without a word about that row (`CreateOnceMixin`, `backend/apps/core/api/base.py`).
**Watch out** **(v1.52)**: from SYNC-1: open each document's transaction with `recording_changes(shop)` (`apps/core/changes.py`), not `transaction.atomic()`: the services its handler calls then join it, and the change numbers come once, at the document's very end. A document's `posted_seq` is the last number its transaction took, as a device drops its pending effects once its cursor passes it (03 §6.4); `recording_changes` hands no number back today, so this card adds that.
**Watch out** **(v1.56)**: settled while planning (10 Nov 2026). Two fixes to the card, in the plan Jack approved: `test(sync): push matrix` held tests alone, a red commit, so each step brings its own tests, written first and shown red, as on SYNC-1; and two commits more than listed: the change block's last number, which this card's own *Watch out* asks for, and the answer log, where "the stored answer" lives. The technical choices, in the same plan: the answer log is a table of its own, as an invalid document has no row of its own but its answer must still be kept; the permission a type names is only listed here, and SYNC-5 checks it (`permission_break`); after a `retry`, the documents behind it get no answer; a broken envelope refuses the whole push (400), as the laptop's sync code writes it, not a person, while a broken payload is that document's own `invalid`; the body names no device, as the key does, and `app_version` and `unsent_count` come with SYNC-5, which records them; and a laptop's number is not unique in the answer log, as a laptop wiped and started again from 1 would have every new sale refused (SYNC-5's note). A decision for the tenancy sweep's list, written here as it asks: the push has no GET for the sweep to read, so it names its own leak test, `test_push_answers_hold_nothing_of_another_shop` (`DEVICE_ROUTES_WITHOUT_A_LOOK`, `backend/tests/sweep_lists.py`). Built. `push(device, documents, received_at=...)` (`apps/sync/push.py`) takes the batch in `device_seq` order. A document with a line in the answer log gets `duplicate` and its first answer, with `first` saying what that was. Otherwise its type and shape version are looked up on the list (`DOCUMENT_TYPES`, `apps/sync/document_types.py`: name and version → shape, saving code and permission; `document.type_unknown`, `document.version_unknown`), its maker among this shop's staff (`document.maker_unknown`, which says nothing of other shops), and its payload checked against its type's shape. Then one transaction: `acting_as(maker, device=device)`, so both books name them (X3); a `recording_changes` block, which the saving code's services join; and, after the block, the document's line, which keeps the block's last number (`ChangeNumbers.last`, `apps/core/changes.py`) as its `posted_seq`. After any refusal the answer log is read again, as CORE-6 decided: a twin's line is the answer; otherwise a primary-key clash (`<table>_pkey`) is `id.taken`, revealing nothing of the row, a refusal of the saving code is `invalid` with its code, and any other error is `retry`, logged with the document's id, type and laptop. An invalid document's line and an `invalid_document` flag about it are written in one small transaction of their own, so a repeat raises no second flag. The answer log (`sync.PushedDocument`, on `AppendOnly` and `CounterRecord`, its trigger in sync's 0001) holds one line per document in a shop (`sync_pusheddocument_one_per_document`, also the catch for two copies at once), never a `duplicate` or a `retry`, and only answers that fit their status. `CounterRecord` (`apps/core/models.py`) adds the device, `device_seq`, `client_time` and `received_at`, none of them empty. The route (`PushView`, `apps/sync/api/views.py`) checks each envelope (`PushedEnvelope`: a UUIDv7 id, a type of at most 40 letters, numbers from 1, the laptop's time, the maker's id, a payload that is an object) and at most 100, and answers `{"answers": [...]}`; `refusal_parts` (`apps/core/api/errors.py`) names an invalid one's reason as every refusal is named. A pretend ticket document (`tests/probe/documents.py`) stands for a sale in the tests. Proven red, each by breaking the code on purpose: no sorting, no stop after a retry, and each of the two second looks for a twin on its own: after a clash on a rule of the saving code's tables other than the id (a twin of the test-only Ticket, one per laptop number), and after an invalid document's line. The new codes have their sentences, Jack's Swahili, with *kumbukumbu* for a record (02 §21).
**Watch out** **(v1.56)**: from the review (10 Nov 2026), all fixed by Jack's choice but three, left as notes. The push's book of answers is named the answer log (Jack): "receipt book" is the paper book for sales in a blackout (02 §18). Proven, each by breaking the code on purpose: a document and its line, and an invalid line and its flag, are saved together, or neither; a pushed document's edits name its maker and its laptop (X3); the primary-key check alone makes `id.taken`, a clash on any other rule being a `retry`; a push of exactly 100 is taken; and the answers name only ids the laptop sent. Fixed: an id taken in this shop gets the same answer as one taken in another, a saving code's own `unique` on a device-made id left out, as CORE-6's create does; a block of its own inside a part hands its last number to the block around it, so a push's `posted_seq` is never empty when its save took a number; a lost database while the answer log is read is that document's `retry`, not a refused push; an accepted line holds no reason fields, an invalid one no flags (`sync_pusheddocument_invalid_has_only_a_reason`); the schema names the push's enums after their own lists (`PushAnswerStatusEnum`, `PushedStatusEnum`, `FlagKindEnum`), says at most 100 (`maxItems`), and numbers `posted_seq` from 1; the comments cite the ADRs in force. Left as notes: a reason's values (`params`) for SALE-2, `id.taken`'s sentence on a laptop for SYNC-8, and the commit subjects that are the card's own names, as on SYNC-3.
**Done when:**
- [x] The same batch sent twice posts once and gets the same answers.
- [x] Documents post in device order, even when sent out of order.
- [x] One invalid document doesn't stop the others; a server error stops the batch at that document.
**Tests:** `test_same_batch_twice_posts_once` · `test_posts_in_device_seq_order` · `test_invalid_document_flagged_and_skipped` · `test_uuid_from_other_shop_rejected_silently` · `test_server_error_answers_retry`. Built too **(v1.56)**: in `apps/sync/tests/test_push.py`, `test_unknown_type_is_invalid`, `test_accepted_answer_carries_its_change_number_and_flags`, `test_maker_of_another_shop_is_invalid`, `test_document_records_who_made_it_its_laptop_and_both_clocks`, `test_a_refusal_of_the_saving_code_is_invalid`, `test_another_database_rule_answers_retry`, `test_twin_sent_at_the_same_moment_answers_duplicate` (two connections: a clash on the id, on an invalid document's line, and on another rule); from the review, `test_a_document_and_its_answer_line_are_saved_in_one_transaction`, `test_an_invalid_line_and_its_flag_are_saved_together`, `test_a_pushed_documents_edits_name_its_maker_and_its_laptop`, `test_a_database_error_looking_up_a_document_answers_retry`, `test_an_id_taken_in_this_shop_gets_the_same_answer_as_in_another`, and in `apps/core/tests/test_changes.py`, `test_a_block_inside_a_part_passes_its_last_number_to_the_block_around_it` and `test_numbers_of_a_part_that_failed_are_not_handed_back`; in `test_push_route.py`, `test_push_saves_and_answers_each_document`, `test_push_needs_a_paired_device`, `test_push_needs_csrf`, `test_push_takes_at_most_100_documents`, `test_a_broken_envelope_refuses_the_whole_push`, `test_push_saves_the_keys_laptop_and_the_servers_clock`, `test_push_answers_hold_nothing_of_another_shop`; in `test_pushed_document.py`, `test_a_stored_answer_is_never_changed`, `test_one_answer_per_document_in_a_shop`, `test_another_shop_keeps_its_own_answer_for_the_same_id`, `test_an_answer_must_make_sense`, `test_an_invalid_document_raises_its_own_flag`, `test_pushed_documents_keep_the_shop_guard`; in `test_document_types.py`, `test_a_listed_type_is_found_by_name_and_version`, `test_the_previous_version_is_still_found`, `test_an_unknown_type_is_refused`, `test_an_unknown_version_of_a_known_type_is_refused`, `test_every_type_is_listed_under_its_own_name_and_version`; in `apps/core/tests/test_counter_record.py`, `test_counter_record_keeps_the_device_its_number_and_both_clocks`, `test_counter_record_needs_all_four_facts`, `test_a_device_with_counter_records_is_never_deleted`, `test_counter_record_keeps_the_shop_guard`;
