**Commits:** `test(sync): push matrix` → `feat(core): CounterRecord base` → `feat(sync): document registry` → `feat(sync): push endpoint`. As built **(v1.56)**: `feat(core): a change block hands back its last number` → `feat(core): CounterRecord base` → `feat(sync): document registry` → `feat(sync): every pushed document keeps its answer` → `feat(sync): post a batch of documents in device order` → `feat(sync): push endpoint` → `docs(sync): how the push is built`; from the review: `test(sync): a document and its answer line are saved in one transaction` → `test(sync): a pushed document's edits name its maker and its laptop` → `test(sync): a twin that clashes on another rule still gets the first answer` → `test(sync): only a primary-key clash counts as a taken id` → `fix(sync): an id taken in this shop gets the same answer as in another` → `fix(core): a block inside a part passes its last number to the block around it` → `fix(sync): a database error while looking up a document answers retry` → `fix(sync): a stored answer holds only what fits its status` → `fix(sync): the push's schema names its enums and its limits` → `test(sync): a push's answers name only the ids it sent` → `refactor(sync): the push's answer log has a name of its own` → `docs: the push's comments and docs say what is true`.

### SYNC-5 · Push checks: device health, gaps and rule breaks
`M` · Claude-led · after SYNC-4 · branch `feat/sync-5-push-checks`
**Build:** each push records `unsent_count`, `app_version`, the last `device_seq` and the sync times; a jump in `device_seq` raises a `device_gap` flag that resolves itself if the missing record arrives; reported offline periods become `DeviceOutage` rows; the table and the Device's sync columns (`last_synced_at`, `last_device_seq`, `unsent_count`, `app_version`) are built here, moved from AUTH-1: an outage is reported in the device's own time, so its shape is chosen with the push it arrives in, and columns changed at every sync stay out of the edit history (skip list) **(v1.26)**; the maker's permission is checked again (`permission_break` flag); a document made after its maker was suspended is kept and flagged (`suspended_staff_activity`, X2).
**Watch out** **(v1.37)**: from AUTH-8: the Device's sync columns move at each push, so its edit history must skip them, or each push writes a history row. `ChangeRecordEdits` changes a skip list, but writes the old rule, which names no device, and it stays that way as old migrations list it. So this card adds a form of it that writes `control_record_edit_v2()`, as `RecordEditsV2` does for `RecordEdits`. Using the old one turns the history check red (`backend/tests/test_edit_history.py`). `test_the_move_to_v2_kept_each_skip_list` in that file pins the device's list, so it changes with the migration **(v1.38)**.
**Watch out** **(v1.56)**: from SYNC-4: the push's body (`PushBatch`, `apps/sync/api/serializers.py`) holds only `documents`: add `app_version` and `unsent_count` there, as 03 §6.3 names them. A laptop's number is not unique in the answer log (`sync.PushedDocument`): a laptop wiped and started again from 1 would have every new sale refused, so this card decides what a repeated number means, beside the gaps. The permission each document type needs is on its list entry (`DocumentType.permission`, `apps/sync/document_types.py`), not yet checked. The push finds a suspended maker among the shop's staff (`_post`, `apps/sync/push.py`), so their document is saved, ready for its flag.
**Watch out** **(v1.57)**: settled while planning (10 Nov 2026). "Resolves itself" goes against CTRL-1's rule that only a person closes a flag (`_step`, `apps/control/services.py`, refuses a step with no person). Jack chose that the system closes it: once every number a `device_gap` was missing has arrived, the system resolves the flag with a note of its own, and names no person; it is the one kind the system may close (02 v1.22). The plan splits the three commits below into one idea each: the laptop's report, a gap's flag, the late record closing it, the outages, a permission break, a suspended maker.
**Watch out** **(v1.57)**: built (11 Nov 2026). A second choice by Jack, asked while building: a record made after its maker left is flagged as one made while suspended (`suspended_staff_activity`), as a person's keys stay given when they go, so the key check alone would let it pass. The technical choices, each told to Jack in its step. **The report:** the push's body holds `app_version` (at most 64 letters), `unsent_count` (the records the laptop holds besides this batch) and `outages` (up to 100), all three needed, as the envelope's parts are; an empty push is how a laptop with nothing to send still reports. `record_sync` (`apps/people/services.py`) keeps `last_synced_at` (the server's clock), `unsent_count` and `app_version` on the device in one UPDATE, never an older report over a newer one, with the batch's documents left without a final answer (a `retry` and those after it) added to the count, so the office never reads 0 while a sale waits on the laptop (M8). **The numbers:** `Device.last_device_seq` is the number its next record follows. Each record's transaction locks the device's row first (`lock_device_numbers`, `select_for_update(no_key=True)`), so two pushes of one laptop take turns, and moves the number after its line (`move_device_number`), so a record rolled back takes its number with it. A jump raises one `device_gap` flag about the line of the record that came, with `expected` the number awaited and `actual` the one that came (`difference`: how many are missing), on the device's home side, naming nobody; an invalid record's number counts as come. A number below the count that a gap waits for is late: once every number of the gap has a line, the system closes its flag (`resolve_flag_by_system`, `apps/control/services.py`, only for `device_gap`, its note the code `flag.device_gap_filled`; a flag a manager closed stays as they left it). Any other number below the count means the numbers went back (a laptop reset): kept, one flag (`expected` above `actual`, which never closes by itself), and the count carries on from there. A number is at most 999,999,999,998 (`HIGHEST_DEVICE_SEQ`, `apps/sync/push.py`), so a flag's columns (`numeric(15,3)`) can always hold it and the one awaited after it. **The outages:** `people.DeviceOutage` (`AppendOnly`, its trigger in people's 0015), one row per period, its id the laptop's own, kept as reported, even one that ends before it starts, as refusing it would refuse the whole push at every sync; written by `record_outages` with one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so a resend keeps it once and an id another shop holds is left out, with no error. **The maker:** the key a type names (`DocumentType.permission`) is checked among the keys the person was given (`given_permissions`), whatever their status, on the device's home side, so a suspended maker gets one flag, not two (`permission_break`). Whether the maker was working when the record was made, by the laptop's clock, is read from the audit trail's suspensions and returns (`was_working_at`, `apps/people/selectors.py`); someone who left stopped at the earlier of when it was recorded and the start of the day after their last day, in the shop's time zone (`suspended_staff_activity`). The push's own flags are about the record's line in the answer log and stay out of the laptop's answer, so a resend gets exactly its first answer. The device's sync columns join its edit history's skip list through a new migration step, `ChangeRecordEditsV2` (`apps/control/operations.py`), which keeps the rule that names the device. Proven red, each by breaking the code on purpose: the old-report guard, the unsent count, the device lock, the late-record check, the full-gap check, the outage's keep-once, the laptop's side for keys, the status by the laptop's clock, and the day after a last day. From the review, all fixed (Jack: "yes all"): the highest number was one too high for the flag after it, which would have made every later push of that laptop a `retry`; an error of the push's own checks after the line (a flag that can't be written) is now a `retry` (`CheckFailed`), never read as the record's own refusal and set aside as invalid; a gap counts only the lines saved after its flag, so a record of the count before a reset can't fill the new count's place and hide a deletion; the maker's keys and status are read before the record's block, so the shop's change counter, locked from the block's numbers to the commit, waits on writes alone (a test reads the queries' order); `was_working_at` reads the person's status as stored, not from the copy the push read before the record's transaction; a laptop whose number the server never kept (one that pushed before SYNC-5) counts on from its highest line in the answer log; `resolve_flag_by_system` refuses a `device_gap` whose numbers went back itself; `unsent_count` is at most 2,147,483,547 (`HIGHEST_UNSENT_COUNT`), as the push adds back up to 100 documents; and tests for another shop's gap, a late record racing a manager's close, a suspension's very moment, a suspended worker who leaves, and the lock outside a transaction. Two notes left for later: a single stray lower number (neither late nor a reset) moves the count back, so the next normal number raises a second, false gap; and a key is checked as held now, not when the record was made (PPL-4's note).
**Done when:**
- [x] A gap raises one flag, and the late record resolves it.
- [x] Reported outages appear on the device.
- [x] A document beyond its maker's permissions, or made after a suspension, is accepted and flagged.
**Tests:** `test_device_seq_gap_raises_flag` · `test_late_record_resolves_gap_flag` · `test_outages_recorded` · `test_offline_permission_break_flagged` · `test_document_after_suspension_flagged`
**Commits:** `feat(sync): device health and gaps` → `feat(sync): outage reports` → `feat(sync): permission and suspension checks` As built **(v1.57)**: `docs(plan): a missing-number flag closes itself when the number arrives` → `feat(control): a migration step changes what a history skips, keeping the rule that names the device` → `feat(sync): a push records the laptop's unsent count, app version and last sync` → `feat(sync): a jump in a laptop's numbers raises a device_gap flag` → `feat(sync): the late record closes its missing-number flag` → `feat(sync): a laptop's offline times are saved` → `feat(sync): a record beyond its maker's keys is kept and flagged` → `feat(sync): a record made by a suspended worker is kept and flagged` → `docs(plan): sync-5 says what it built`. From the review: `fix(sync): a laptop's highest number leaves room for the one after it` → `fix(sync): a check of the push's own that fails is a retry, never an invalid record` → `fix(sync): a gap counts only the records that came after its flag` → `perf(sync): a pushed record's maker is read before the shop's change counter is taken` → `test(sync): a reset's flag stays open when a lower number reaches the gaps` → `fix(people): whether a person was working reads their status as stored now` → `fix(sync): a laptop that pushed before its number was kept counts on from its last record` → `fix(control): the system never closes a flag of a device's numbers gone back` → `fix(sync): a laptop's unsent count can't overflow its column` → `test(sync): the push's checks hold at their edges` → `docs(people): comments say what is true` → `docs(rules): a skip list changes through ChangeRecordEditsV2` → `docs(plan): the sync-5 card follows its review`.

### ATT-1 · The first document: clock in and out
`M` · Jack-led · after SYNC-5, PPL-2 · branch `feat/att-1-clock-events`
**Learn:** device clocks can't be trusted, so we keep evidence instead (02 §12).
**Build:** `staffpay.AttendanceEvent` (append-only, with the one-time void stamp — Appendix A): kind (in, out), device, photo (added in ATT-3), `client_time`, `trusted_time`, `time_check` (server, device_ok, unverified), `entered_by` + reason; the handler for the clock document; the time check from the evidence (`device_seq` order, the last server time the device saw, its clock error at sync); an `unverified_time` flag.
**Watch out:** the void stamp as CORE-3's *Watch out* says: a complete stamp, in the event's own shop, filled by one guarded `update()` **(v1.16)**.
**Watch out** **(v1.56)**: from SYNC-4: the clock document is the first real type on the list (`DOCUMENT_TYPES`, `apps/sync/document_types.py`), its saving code written as the pretend ticket's is (`tests/probe/documents.py`): inside a `recording_changes` block, which joins the push's; an INSERT with the document's id (`force_insert`), so an id another shop holds clashes and is never overwritten; the flags it raised handed back; and a `ValidationError` only for what no flag can mend. `AttendanceEvent(AppendOnly, CounterRecord)`, its Meta from both. mypy's Django plugin calls the two bases' `get_next_by_created_at` a clash, so the class line ignores that one error, as `PushedDocument`'s does (`apps/sync/models.py`).
**Watch out** **(v1.57)**: from SYNC-5: the push already flags a record made while its maker wasn't working, suspended or gone, by its `client_time` (`was_working_at`, `apps/people/selectors.py`), for every type: the suspended worker's clock-in needs no check of its own, only its test, through the clock document. The key a type names is checked on the device's home side (`DocumentType.permission`): give the clock document the key every worker holds.
**Watch out** **(v1.58)**: built (11 Nov 2026). One choice by Jack, asked at the start: clocking in needs no key, as anyone working at the shop may clock in on any of its laptops (Baraka, of Vyakula, on the Dawa laptop when Vyakula's is off). A type's `DocumentType.permission` may be `None`, and the push then checks no key; the suspension check still runs (X2). The technical choices, each told to Jack in its step. **The evidence:** the push's body holds `sent_at`, the laptop's clock as it sends, needed as the report's parts are; each document's saving code gets it (`Document.sent_at`), and the laptop's own time on its record numbered last before this one, of those the answer log holds, whatever its type or answer, the newer one after a reset (`Document.time_before`), read under the laptop's lock through a new index on the answer log, `(shop, device, device_seq)`, which SYNC-5's reads use too (sync's 0002). Only the record before, not the one after: after a reset, the old count's higher numbers would look like later records and raise false signs. The clock document's payload holds `kind` and `last_server_time`, the last server time the laptop had seen at the tap. **The book:** `staffpay.AttendanceEvent(AppendOnly, CounterRecord)`, its void-stamp trigger in staffpay's 0001: `staff` (whose line: today always its maker, a column of its own as a ledger can't be filled in later), `kind`, `trusted_time`, `time_check`, `last_server_time`, `clock_error` (the laptop's clock minus the server's, as it sent), and the stamp `voided_at`, `voided_by`, `void_reason`; `void_approval` comes with the Approval table (R1). Its rules: a kind of the two; the trusted time fits its check (`server`: the tap's time by the server, written `client_time - trusted_time = clock_error`, two times compared, so no session's time zone moves it; `device_ok`: `client_time`; `unverified`: empty), each kept time said to be there, as a comparison with an empty value passes a check; and a void stamp all filled or all empty. Its link type code is `attendance_event`. **The check:** `check_time` (`apps/staffpay/calculations.py`, pure, server only, so no TypeScript twin): sent within 2 minutes of the tap, both by the laptop's clock (`ONLINE_WITHIN`), is online, and takes the server's time of the tap, the push's arrival less the wait, however wrong the laptop's clock; otherwise the laptop's time, unless one of four signs (`Sign`): its record before has a later time, its time is before the last server time it had seen, its clock was more than 2 minutes off the server's as it sent, or its time is more than 2 minutes after the laptop's own clock as it sent (`CLOCK_SLACK`, each a gap between two times). One sign: `unverified`, no trusted time, and one `unverified_time` flag about the line, naming the worker, and the maker of the record before when that record is the sign, on no side (attendance is the shop's), in the laptop's answer. **The saving code:** `post_clock` (`apps/staffpay/documents.py`), written as the pretend ticket's: inside a `recording_changes` block, with a change line (no device keeps the book yet, so a pull passes it over), and an INSERT with the laptop's id. `staffpay` is below `sync`, so it can't import the push's `Document`: it names the parts it reads in a shape of its own (`Pushed`, a Protocol), and the type checker proves the Document fits. The card's four tests are in `apps/sync/tests/test_clock_in.py`, as only the layer above may call the push. Left for ATT-6, the first card that needs them: `entered_by` and a reason (a manager entering a missed clock-in), and the void service. From the review, all fixed, two by Jack's choice: a 4th sign, a tap timed more than 2 minutes after the laptop's own clock as it sent (the clock moved forward for it and set right before the push), which no other sign saw; the signs compare the gap between two times, never a time moved by 2 minutes, which a year-1 time crashed into a `retry` at every push; every time a laptop sends must name its zone (`ZonedDateTimeField`, `apps/core/api/times.py`, DRF's own `invalid` code), as one without was read as UTC, 3 hours off in Arusha; a tap sent at once is the server's time of the tap, the push's arrival less the wait the laptop reports (the book's rule, written as two times compared after the second review), so a slow line costs no late fine; the check names the signs it found (`Sign`), and the push hands the saving code who made the record before (`Document.made_before_by`), so when a later record has an earlier time the flag names both makers, as the server can't tell whose clock moved (Jack); a void reason holds at least one character that isn't a space; and rule 6's four parts for the clock-in span this card and SYNC-11, which builds the laptop's side (Jack). Kept as is: the record before is read for every type of document, one indexed row.
**Done when:**
- [x] An online clock-in gets the server's time.
- [x] An offline one keeps the device time and is `device_ok` when the evidence agrees.
- [x] A device clock moved backwards makes it `unverified`, with a flag.
- [x] A suspended worker's offline clock-in is stored and flagged.
**Tests:** `test_online_clock_in_uses_server_time` · `test_offline_clock_in_kept_when_evidence_agrees` · `test_clock_moved_back_is_unverified` · `test_suspended_worker_clock_in_flagged` Built too **(v1.58)**: the card's four in `apps/sync/tests/test_clock_in.py`, with `test_a_toka_timed_after_sending_is_unverified`, `test_a_clock_moved_forward_names_both_workers`, `test_a_record_before_by_nobody_known_names_only_the_worker`, `test_clock_in_needs_no_key_on_any_laptop`, `test_clock_in_sent_twice_is_saved_once`, `test_clock_in_of_a_wrong_shape_is_invalid`, `test_a_clock_in_from_year_one_never_stops_the_push` and `test_a_clock_in_id_another_shop_holds_is_refused`; in `test_maker_checks.py`, `test_a_type_that_needs_no_key_raises_no_key_flag`; in `test_push_route.py`, `test_push_needs_the_laptops_time`, `test_saving_code_gets_the_laptops_time_at_sending`, and cases of `test_push_needs_the_laptops_report` and `test_a_broken_envelope_refuses_the_whole_push` for a time with no zone; in `test_push.py`, `test_saving_code_gets_the_time_of_the_record_before_it` and `test_after_a_reset_the_record_before_is_the_new_counts`; in `apps/staffpay/tests/test_attendance_event.py`, `test_a_clock_in_is_never_changed_or_deleted`, `test_a_clock_in_is_voided_once`, `test_a_void_says_who_and_why`, `test_a_clock_in_must_make_sense`, `test_the_three_checks_each_fit_their_trusted_time`, `test_the_trusted_time_rule_holds_in_any_sessions_time_zone`, `test_clock_ins_keep_the_shop_guard`; in `test_time_check.py`, `test_sent_at_once_takes_the_servers_time_of_the_tap`, `test_the_wait_on_the_laptop_is_not_charged_to_the_worker`, `test_a_clock_moved_back_gains_nothing_online`, `test_two_minutes_is_still_online_and_a_second_more_is_not`, `test_the_waits_lower_edge`, `test_sent_later_keeps_the_laptops_time_when_nothing_disagrees`, `test_the_first_record_has_no_record_before_to_disagree`, `test_a_record_before_with_a_later_time_is_a_sign`, `test_a_time_before_the_last_server_time_seen_is_a_sign`, `test_a_clock_off_at_sync_is_a_sign`, `test_a_time_after_the_laptops_clock_at_sending_is_a_sign`, `test_the_check_names_each_sign_it_found`, `test_two_minutes_off_is_not_yet_a_sign`, `test_times_at_the_ends_of_the_calendar_still_get_an_answer`, `test_the_clock_error_is_the_laptops_clock_minus_the_servers`; in `apps/core/tests/test_zoned_times.py`, `test_a_time_with_its_zone_is_read_as_that_moment`, `test_a_time_with_no_zone_is_refused`, `test_what_is_no_time_at_all_gets_drfs_own_answer`.
**Commits:** `test(staffpay): clock event rules` → `feat(staffpay): AttendanceEvent` → `feat(staffpay): clock document handler` As built **(v1.58)**: `feat(sync): a document type may need no key` → `feat(sync): the push carries the laptop's clock as it sends` → `feat(sync): the saving code sees the time of the record before it` → `feat(staffpay): AttendanceEvent` → `feat(staffpay): the time check reads the evidence` → `feat(staffpay): clock document handler` → `docs(plan): att-1 says what it built`. From the review: `fix(staffpay): a clock-in timed after the laptop's clock at sending is unverified` → `fix(staffpay): the time check compares gaps, so no date can crash it` → `fix(sync): a time a laptop sends must name its zone` → `fix(staffpay): two clock times that disagree flag both workers` → `fix(staffpay): an online clock-in's time is the tap's, not the push's arrival` → `test(staffpay): the wait's lower edge and a server line with no time are tested` → `fix(staffpay): a void reason of only spaces is no reason` → `docs(staffpay): comments say what is true after the review` → `test(sync): the clock-in tests find their rows by id` → `docs(plan): the att-1 card follows its review`. From the second review: `fix(staffpay): the trusted-time rule holds in any session's time zone` → `docs(staffpay): comments name the tap's time, not the push's arrival` → `docs(plan): the att-1 card says its rules once`.

### SYNC-6 · The device database
`M` · Jack-led · after UI-1 · branch `feat/sync-6-dexie`
**Learn:** IndexedDB, Dexie and database versions inside the browser (03 §6.1).
**Build:** `offline/db.ts`: one database per shop with the stores `meta`, the reference stores, `pin_checks`, documents, `outbox`, `pending_effects` and `sent_archive`; typed tables; version 1; Vitest with fake-indexeddb.
**Watch out:** `dexie` and `dexie-react-hooks` (03 §3) are new dependencies again. SPIKE-1 added Dexie for its test page and removed it with the page, so both need Jack's yes before `pnpm add` **(v1.10)**. From UI-3 **(v1.45)**: which device this is (its id, name, code, shop, home side, and a block) already lives in `mpc.device` (`src/features/devices/this-device.ts`), outside every shop's database, as the shop must be known before its database opens (03 §6.1). `meta` keeps no copy of it, so receipt numbers read the device's code from one place.
**Watch out** **(v1.59)**: built (11 Nov 2026). `src/offline/db.ts`: `openShopDatabase(shopId)` opens the shop's database, `mpc-shop-<shop id>` (`databaseName`), typed (`ShopDatabase`); Dexie opens it at its first read or write. `VERSIONS` lists every version in order, version 1 locked by a test (`db.versions.test.ts`): a change is a new version at the end, with an upgrade if old rows must change. Version 1's drawers: `meta`; the bootstrap's five lists by their codes, `side`, `payment_account`, `staff`, `setting` (by `key` too) and `setting_keys`, so a pull's `entity` names its drawer, each later list coming with its card as a new version; `pin_checks` (by `staff_id`); `documents`, every type in one drawer, each row the push's envelope but its `device_seq` (by `type`, and `[type+client_time]` for one kind on one day); `outbox`, unsent documents only (`&device_seq`, unique, and `[status+device_seq]`); `pending_effects` (by `document_id` and `posted_seq`); `sent_archive` (by `sent_at` and `device_seq`). `dexie` 4.4.7, and `fake-indexeddb` 6.2.6 for tests, turned on for every test in `vitest.setup.ts`; `dexie-react-hooks` moves to SYNC-7.
**Done when:**
- [x] Two shops in one browser get two separate databases.
- [x] A version bump runs its upgrade.
**Tests:** `db.stores.test.ts` · `db.versions.test.ts`
**Commits:** `test(frontend): device database` → `feat(frontend): dexie schema`

### SYNC-7 · Device pull and bootstrap
`M` · Claude-led · after SYNC-3, SYNC-6 · branch `feat/sync-7-device-pull`
**Build:** bootstrap on a new device; each pull page applied in one Dexie transaction before the cursor moves; the server-time offset kept in `meta`; live-query hooks for reference data.
**Watch out:** From UI-3 **(v1.45)**: a bootstrap or pull refused with `device.blocked` must lock the app (03 §5.9, step 4). Sync's calls run outside TanStack Query, so the store's watcher (`noticeBlock`, `src/features/devices/device-check.tsx`) never sees them: call `saveBlocked()` (`src/features/devices/this-device.ts`) on that code. On `not_authenticated`, the key opens no device: `saveDevice(null)`.
**Watch out** **(v1.53)**: from SYNC-2: the bootstrap's answer (`BootstrapAnswer` in `src/api/schema.d.ts`) holds `cursor`, then one list per registry table by its code (`side`, `payment_account`, `staff`, `setting`), then `setting_keys`; a pull's `entity` is the same code, so one store per code serves both. A person's keys: on a side, `every_side` plus that side's own codes in `sides`; on the whole shop, `every_side` alone (02 §5). A rule on a day: the side's newest row on or before it, else the shop's, else its default in `setting_keys`, as `get_setting` works it out (`apps/shops/selectors.py`). The answer comes gzipped, and the browser unpacks it by itself. From SYNC-2's review: each rule in `setting_keys` names its kind (`money`, `percent`, `whole`, `time`, `yes_no`), which says how to read its stored text; the lists are read one after another, so an answer may name a side its `side` list doesn't hold yet: accept it until the next pull, which comes right after; and `GET /api/devices/v1/me/` now answers the shop's `timezone`, `currency` and `default_language`: keep them with the device's saved copy (`mpc.device`, `src/features/devices/this-device.ts`), and work out today, and so the rule in force, in the shop's own time zone.
**Watch out** **(v1.55)**: from SYNC-3: a page (`PullAnswer` in `src/api/schema.d.ts`, from `GET /api/sync/v1/pull/?cursor=N`) holds `changes`, in order, each `{seq, entity, id, op, data}` told apart by `entity`, the bootstrap's list codes, so one store per code serves both. `upsert`: save `data` as the record now is, new or changed. `archive`: `data` is null; take the record off the lists, and do nothing if the device never had it. A record may come on several lines of one page, each time as it is now: apply them in order. Then set the cursor to the page's `cursor`, in the same Dexie transaction, and ask again at once while `has_more` is true; never count the changes to decide, as a page passes over the lines of tables no device keeps. Every page carries `server_time` (UTC), an empty one too, for the clock offset. `min_app_version` is null until SYNC-13. The first pull asks from the bootstrap's `cursor`; a pull with no cursor reads from 0, the whole log, but a blank one (`?cursor=`) is refused, so send none rather than an empty one. From SYNC-3's review: `data` and `min_app_version` are `null`, never `""`. A change whose `entity` the app has no store for must not be skipped past: stop the pull there, as an app that meets one is older than the server (SYNC-13's note). A rule's rows: for one rule, side and day, read the last row the device was given, never only the newest day on or before. A row a same-day fix replaced stays on a device that got it before the fix, as no archive reaches a device already past its line, and a bootstrap or a pull hands rows over in the order they were written, so the last one given is the fix.
**Watch out** **(v1.59)**: from SYNC-6: open the shop's database with `openShopDatabase` (`src/offline/db.ts`), the shop's id from `mpc.device`. Each list's drawer is named by its code (`side`, `payment_account`, `staff`, `setting`, `setting_keys`), so a change's `entity` is its drawer's name. A list the server adds later is a new version in `VERSIONS`, never a drawer added into version 1, which a test locks. A rule's rows are found by `key`; never add a `[key+side]` search key, as IndexedDB leaves out the rows whose `side` is null, every shop-wide rule. The cursor, the clock offset and the last server time seen go in `meta`. The live-query hooks need `dexie-react-hooks`, a new dependency: Jack's yes before `pnpm add`. Each pull drops the pending effects its cursor has passed: `pending_effects.where("posted_seq").belowOrEqual(cursor)`.
**Watch out** **(v1.60)**: built (11 Nov 2026). `src/offline/sync/`: `bootstrap.ts` runs while `meta` holds no cursor (`needsBootstrap`); the answer is asked for before the transaction opens, as a Dexie transaction ends by itself on any wait outside the database; then every list is emptied and filled, and the cursor set, in one transaction. `pull.ts` asks from the cursor until `has_more` is false; each page is checked first (a list with no drawer throws `AppTooOld`, an upsert with no record, a cursor that goes back or, with more waiting, stays), then applied in one transaction with the cursor, the clock offset (`clock_offset_ms`, the server's time less the middle of the call on the laptop's clock, kept only when the time reads) and `last_server_time` (`meta.ts`), and drops the pending effects up to the cursor. A rule's row replaces the laptop's other rows of its rule, side and day (`applySetting`): the server's fix is the row it saved last, and its id, made in the manager's browser, can't say so. `answer.ts` (`syncAnswer`) keeps `device.blocked` (`saveBlocked()`) and `not_authenticated` (`saveDevice(null)`). `sync.ts` (`syncShop`) bootstraps if needed, then pulls, one at a time per shop in a tab; `DeviceCheck` starts it after each answer naming a working device, and logs to the console any failure that isn't the server's answer or a dropped network (`reportSyncFailure`). `src/offline/this-shop.ts` opens each shop's database once for the app (`shopDatabase`, `useShopDatabase`); `reference.ts` gives `useSides`, `usePaymentAccounts`, `useStaff` and `useRule(key, side)`, the rule in force on the shop's today (`rules.ts`: the side's row only for a rule of scope `side`, else the shop's, else the default), the day in the shop's zone on the server's clock as the offset says. `mpc.device` keeps the shop's `timezone`, `currency` and `language`; a copy saved before, or naming a zone the browser doesn't know, reads as no device, with its block kept. `dexie-react-hooks` 4.4.0 (Jack's yes). `vitest.setup.ts` closes and deletes every shop database after each test. Shown by hand (Jack, 11 Nov 2026) on `make dev`: a laptop in Edge bootstrapped and pulled; the Meneja's language changed in another browser reached its staff row at the next check. The local database was eight migrations behind before it. From the review (Jack, 11 Nov 2026), all fixed: the same-day fix above, in place of the greatest id; a shop-wide rule never reads a side's row; tests for a pending effect posted at the cursor itself and a pull's `not_authenticated`; unexpected failures logged; the cursor must move on; only a real offset kept; an unknown zone refused; a sync never joins another shop's. Honest limits: the bootstrap's answer names no shop, so a laptop paired to another shop between the check and the bootstrap would fill the wrong database (rare, as pairing a working laptop is refused; SYNC-13's note); and a rule's value is worked out again when its rows change, not at midnight (SYNC-10's and SYNC-11's notes).
**Done when:**
- [x] A fresh device fills itself; a change made in the office shows on the device after the next pull.
- [x] Closing the tab in the middle of a page leaves the cursor where it was.
**Tests:** `bootstrap.test.ts` · `pull.apply.test.ts`
**Commits:** `feat(frontend): bootstrap a device` → `feat(frontend): apply pull pages`

### SYNC-8 · Device outbox and push loop
`M` · Jack-led · after SYNC-4, SYNC-7 · branch `feat/sync-8-outbox`
**Learn:** the outbox pattern; one local transaction per document (03 §6.2).
**Build:** `saveDocument()` writes the document, the next `device_seq` and the outbox row in one Dexie transaction; the push loop sends in order, handles the four answers, retries with backoff and stops on `retry`; a *needs attention* list for invalid documents.
**Watch out:** From UI-3 **(v1.45)**: a push refused with `device.blocked` calls `saveBlocked()`, as a pull does (SYNC-7), and leaves the outbox as it is: an unblock brings back the same key, so its records can still arrive (03 §5.9, step 4).
**Watch out** **(v1.56)**: from SYNC-4: the push answers `{"answers": [...]}` in `device_seq` order, one per document up to the first `retry` and none after it: send those again, in order. A `duplicate` says in `first` whether its first answer was `accepted` or `invalid`, with that answer's `posted_seq`, `flags`, `code` and `fields`, so treat it as that answer. An `invalid` one's `code` and `fields` are the error shape's, each code with its sentences (`errors.document.*`, `errors.id.*`). `id.taken`'s sentence ends "Try again.", right for an office create but not here, as a laptop's resend gets the same answer: give the needs-attention list words of its own (SYNC-4's review). A broken envelope refuses the whole push (400 `invalid`, under `documents.N.<field>`): a bug in the sync code, never one document to set aside. The body holds only `documents`, as the device is the key's.
**Watch out** **(v1.57)**: from SYNC-5: the push's body needs three more parts, beside `documents`: `app_version` (at most 64 letters), `unsent_count` (the records still in the outbox *besides* this batch: the server adds back any of the batch it couldn't save) and `outages` (up to 100, each `{id, started_at, ended_at}`, its id a UUIDv7 made on the device, sent again until a push answers 200). Send an empty push when there is nothing else to send, so the device still reports. A laptop must never number again from 1: a reset that starts its count again keeps its records on the server, but raises a flag, and a sale's number taken twice (one receipt number per laptop, D2-0457) would be refused at every retry (SALE-2's note). So keep the counter where a reset of the shop's database can't reach it, or carry on from the device's `last_device_seq`. A number is at most 999,999,999,998, and `unsent_count` at most 2,147,483,547.
**Watch out** **(v1.58)**: from ATT-1: the push's body needs `sent_at` too, the laptop's clock as it sends, read afresh at each try: a retry sent with the first try's time would show the clock as off by the wait, and every offline clock-in in it would be `unverified`. A clock document's `last_server_time` is the one `meta` holds when the tap is saved, written into the document then, never at the push. Every time a laptop sends names its zone (`Z`, as `toISOString()` writes it), or the whole push is refused (`ZonedDateTimeField`).
**Watch out** **(v1.59)**: from SYNC-6: a document's row (`DocumentRow`, `src/offline/db.ts`) holds the push's envelope but its `device_seq`, which its outbox row keeps: `id`, `type`, `schema_version`, `client_time`, `created_by`, `payload`. The outbox holds unsent documents only, `waiting` or `needs_attention`, read in order with `[status+device_seq]`: an accepted one leaves it, in the same transaction, for `sent_archive` with its `device_seq` and `posted_seq`, and its pending effects get the `posted_seq` too; the document stays in `documents`. Where the next `device_seq` is kept is still this card's choice (v1.57's note): `meta` is wiped with the shop's database.
**Watch out** **(v1.60)**: from SYNC-7: send the push through `syncAnswer` (`src/offline/sync/answer.ts`), which keeps `device.blocked` and `not_authenticated` as the pull does. The push goes before the pull in `syncShop` (`sync.ts`), the one place a sync runs.
**Watch out** **(v1.61)**: built (12 Nov 2026). The bootstrap answers `last_device_seq` beside its cursor (`last_device_number`, `apps/sync/selectors.py`: the device row's number, else its highest line in the answer log, else 0), and the laptop keeps the larger of it and its own in `meta` (`last_device_seq`, `src/offline/sync/meta.ts`). `meta` is wiped with the database, but the server's number survives a wipe, as the key's cookie does, where the browser's other storage would go with the database. A database with no cursor or no number bootstraps (`needsBootstrap`), so a laptop filled before this card fills itself once more. `saveDocument(db, draft, effects)` (`src/offline/outbox.ts`) checks the draft as the server checks an envelope (`BrokenDraft`), then writes the document, the next number, its outbox row (`waiting`) and its pending effects in one transaction, the number read inside it, so two tabs take turns; before any bootstrap it refuses (`NeverFilled`). `push.ts` sends up to 100 `waiting` rows in number order (`[status+device_seq]`), with `sent_at` read at each try, `APP_VERSION`, `unsent_count` and `outages: []`, through `syncAnswer`. `unsent_count` counts the `waiting` rows outside the batch only: a set-aside one is on the server already, and counted it would hold up a drawer's approval for good (M8), a reading of v1.57's "records still in the outbox". The answers are applied in one transaction: `accepted`, or a `duplicate` whose `first` was, moves the row to `sent_archive` with its `posted_seq` and stamps its pending effects (an accepted one with no `posted_seq`, whose save changed nothing a laptop keeps, is archived with none and its effects dropped); `invalid`, or a duplicate of one, sets it aside (`needs_attention`, its code in `last_error`) and drops its pending effects; `retry` stops, that row's `attempts` going up. It goes on while a batch was full; with nothing waiting, an empty push still reports. A 400 is thrown as `BrokenPush`, which `reportSyncFailure` logs; a block or a lost network is thrown with the outbox as it was. `syncShop` pushes before it pulls; a failed push still lets the pull run, unless the device is blocked or unknown (`isAboutTheDevice`, `answer.ts`), and is thrown after it. After a `retry` or a failed push the next push waits (`backoff.ts`, in memory, per shop): 30 s, doubling, at most 5 minutes, a clock put back never making it longer, and back to 30 s after a push that settles; the pull runs meanwhile. `useNeedsAttention()` lists the set-aside records live, each with its document, number and code; the screen and its words are SYNC-11's (Jack, 12 Nov 2026). No new package. From the review (Jack, 12 Nov 2026), all fixed: a laptop SYNC-7 filled bootstraps once more, as it had no number and could never save; a failed push still lets the pull run, so a suspension still reaches the laptop (X2); an accepted record with no `posted_seq` leaves the outbox instead of stopping it; a draft the push couldn't send is refused at the save, as it would stop every push after it; a broken push is logged; a refused record's pending effects go; tests that read by number, give two tabs a connection each, check a retry's fresh time, an all-or-nothing batch of answers, the push going after each wait and the list going once the laptop is unregistered; and the two server reads check their shop is the one open (T1). Honest limits: no screen saves a document yet, so the save and the push are proven by tests alone until SYNC-11; the wait lives in memory, so a reload pushes at once; `attempts` counts `retry` answers only.
**Done when:**
- [x] Documents saved offline go out in order when the push runs.
- [x] An invalid document goes to *needs attention* and the rest continue.
- [x] A `retry` answer stops the batch, and the next attempt waits longer.
**Tests:** `outbox.save.test.ts` · `push.loop.test.ts` · `push.backoff.test.ts`
**Commits:** `test(frontend): outbox rules` → `feat(frontend): saveDocument` → `feat(frontend): push loop`

### SYNC-9 · When sync runs
`M` · Claude-led · after SYNC-8, SPIKE-1 · branch `feat/sync-9-sync-triggers`
**Build:** sync runs on app start, when the network comes back, 2 s after a new document, every 30 s while online, and from *Tuma sasa*, which sends even during a wait (03 §6.5); one sync at a time across tabs (Web Locks), the wait after a failed push shared by every tab. Background Sync is SYNC-16's **(v1.62)**.
**Watch out** **(v1.60)**: from SYNC-7: until this card, a sync runs after each device check that names a working device (`DeviceCheck`, `device-check.tsx`, calling `syncShop`); move that trigger here, and keep the failure report (`reportSyncFailure`). `syncShop` joins a sync already running for the same shop in the same tab only; the Web Lock covers the tabs.
**Watch out** **(v1.61)**: from SYNC-8: `syncShop` is bootstrap → push → pull. The wait after a failed push (`mayPush`, `pushFailed`, `pushSettled`, `src/offline/sync/backoff.ts`) lives in memory, per shop and per tab: with the Web Lock, keep it where every tab sees it (a `meta` note), or one tab's wait won't hold another's push. While a wait runs, "2 s after a new document" and *Tuma sasa* skip the push too: decide whether *Tuma sasa*, pressed by someone waiting for the answer, goes anyway. A sync with a full last batch pushes again in the same sync, so 250 waiting records go as three pushes. A failed push is thrown after the pull, and `reportSyncFailure` logs a `BrokenPush`; Background Sync needs the same `push()`.
**Watch out** **(v1.62)**: settled while planning (Jack, 12 Nov 2026). Background Sync moves to a new card, SYNC-16, after SYNC-11: no screen saves a record before SYNC-11, so its test on the shop phone can't run here; UI-3's note on it goes with it. Jack's choice: *Tuma sasa* sends even during a wait, as someone is there waiting to see it go. The technical choices, in the plan Jack approved: the wait moves to `meta`, so every tab sees it, and a reload no longer forgets it (03 §6.5); a sync asked for while one runs in the tab runs once more after it; a second tab waits for the lock, never skips, so a record it just saved still goes; the first save starts the 2 s, and later saves don't restart it; a window back in front no longer syncs, as the 30 s covers it; this card makes the way to press *Tuma sasa*, and the button itself goes on the first counter screen (SYNC-11).
**Done when:**
- [x] Sync runs on app start, when the network comes back, 2 s after a new document, every 30 s while online, and from *Tuma sasa*, even during a wait **(v1.62)**.
- [x] Two tabs never push at once, and share the wait after a failed push **(v1.62)**.
**Tests:** `sync.triggers.test.ts` · `sync.single-tab.test.ts`
**Commits:** `docs: move background sync to its own card` → `feat(frontend): send now, and a sync asked mid-sync runs once more` → `feat(frontend): sync triggers` → `feat(frontend): one sync at a time` → `feat(frontend): every tab keeps the same wait after a failed push` **(v1.62)**

### SYNC-10 · Offline PIN check
`M` · Jack-led · after AUTH-4, SYNC-6 · branch `feat/sync-10-offline-pin`
**Learn:** key derivation (PBKDF2) with WebCrypto; salts; lockouts (03 §5.9).
**Build:** at a person's first online PIN login on this device, store a PBKDF2 check value with a per-device salt; offline, check the PIN against it and start a local session; five wrong PINs lock that person here for a minute, doubling, up to an hour **(v1.33)**; a PIN changed on the server (`pin_changed_at`) drops the check value at the next pull; a person suspended on the server can't log in offline once the device has pulled (X2); a local session shows the person's own language and font, from their staff row (UI-9) **(v1.40)**.
**Watch out** **(v1.34)**: the same three numbers as the server's, in `apps/people/pins.py`: 5 tries, a first lock of 1 minute, a longest of 60. The server counts a try before it checks it (AUTH-4), which the device should do too, so a crash in the check can't give a free try. From UI-4 **(v1.46)**: the picker reads `GET /api/devices/v1/staff/` online (`staff-picker.tsx`); offline it must read the staff rows on the device (SYNC-2), and this card decides whether the route stays as the online way or goes. The PIN login (`pin-login.tsx`) sends the PIN to the server; offline it checks here. The idle lock's minutes come from `me`; offline, from the settings on the device. From UI-9 **(v1.49)**: the app shows a person's language and font through `useFollowThePerson` (`src/features/people/follow-the-person.ts`), which reads them from `me`; a local session has no `me`, so hand it the person's `language` and `font` from their staff row on the device.
**Watch out** **(v1.53)**: from SYNC-2: each staff row carries `pin_changed_at`, the time to compare a check value with; empty means the person has no PIN. A suspended person comes with `status` `suspended` and no keys; someone who left, or was switched off, doesn't come at all, so the picker shows the active ones, as the online route does. The idle lock's minutes offline: the rule `session.idle_minutes` from the rows and `setting_keys`.
**Watch out** **(v1.59)**: from SYNC-6: the check values go in `pin_checks`, one row per person by `staff_id` (`PinCheckRow`, `src/offline/db.ts`: `salt`, `check`, and the `pin_changed_at` it was made at); its fields may change here, as only a drawer's name or search keys need a new version.
**Watch out** **(v1.60)**: from SYNC-7: the staff rows are `useStaff()`, and the idle lock's minutes `useRule("session.idle_minutes", null)` (`src/offline/reference.ts`), stored text to read by the rule's kind. A rule is worked out again when its rows change, not at midnight: a lock left open across the shop's midnight keeps yesterday's value until the screen draws again.
**Watch out** **(v1.63)**: settled while planning and building (Jack, 12 Nov 2026). Jack's choice: a local session lives in the tab's memory only, so a reload, a crash or a closed tab asks for the PIN again. In the plan Jack approved: the tiles read the device's copy of the staff, online too, so `GET /api/devices/v1/staff/` goes, as two lists of who works here could disagree, and a new worker shows within the 30 s sync; the server comes first, and the device checks a PIN itself only with no network, no answer to `me`, a logout not yet heard, or no answer or a 5xx to the PIN itself; after *Toka* with no network the tiles come back at once, as UI-4's "hidden from the press" would keep the counter shut until the network returns; a fresh salt for every check, which is per device and more (two people with one PIN differ), with its 600,000 turns stored in it. Built: `src/offline/pin.ts` (`rememberPin` after each server yes, `checkPinOffline`, and `dropOutdatedChecks` in each pull page's and bootstrap's transaction), `PinCheckRow`'s new fields (no new version), the local session (`src/features/login/offline-session.ts`), and the device's one code of its own, `pin.offline_first`. A suspended person keeps their check and is told so. The size: past the card's M, under twice. Honest limits in 03 §5.9. The route's removal: an app from before this card, still cached on a device, gets 404 for its tiles until it updates; only the demo could meet it, as no real shop uses the app yet. From its review (Jack, 12 Nov 2026), every finding fixed, each in its own commit: `forgetTheLastPerson` keeps a change still on its way in its place in the queue, as a logout it dropped went on trying and, once the internet was back, ended the next person's online login; the arrival's logout goes first, and only if none waits; the re-checks after the blender, tested with a pull landing mid-check, read the PIN time too; a lock lasts at most an hour from the try that finds it, whatever the clock did; a block, or the device forgotten or paired again, ends a local session (`watchThisDevice`, `this-device.ts`); the side and the idle minutes moved beside their kin (`sideOfStaffRow`, `who-is-here.ts`; `wholeNumber`, `rules.ts`); the no-check sentence no longer says "no network", as it shows when the server is away too; and one way to fill the test laptop. Jack checked the Swahili on 12 Nov 2026.
**Done when:**
- [x] Someone who logged in once online can log in offline; someone who never did can't.
- [x] The lockout doubles.
- [x] After a PIN change or a suspension, the old PIN stops working offline once the device has pulled.
**Tests:** `pin.check.test.ts` · `pin.lockout.test.ts` · `pin.changed.test.ts` · `pin.suspended.test.ts` · `offline-session.test.tsx` **(v1.63)**
**Commits:** `test(frontend): offline pin rules` → `feat(frontend): pin check values` → `feat(frontend): the pin tiles read the laptop's copy` → `refactor(people): the device's staff route goes` → `feat(frontend): offline session` → `docs: what the offline pin check settled` **(v1.63)**

`M` · Jack-led · after ATT-1, SYNC-9, SYNC-10, UI-6 · branch `feat/sync-11-clock-screen`
**Learn:** how the device and server halves meet; testing offline with Playwright.
**Build:** the counter screen *Mahudhurio*: pick yourself, enter your PIN, tap *Ingia* or *Toka*; today's list of who is in; it reads only from Dexie; a CI budget for the counter code (under 300 KB compressed — 03 §14).
**Watch out** **(v1.46)**: from UI-4: the first counter screen, so the idle lock (`useIdleLock`, `src/features/login/idle-lock.ts`) and the person in the bar must reach the counter area, which never calls the server: the person logged in needs a copy on the device, as the device's own is (`mpc.device`), and the logout stays outside the counter lock, as the PIN screen does. The picker and the pad (`staff-picker.tsx`, `pin-pad.tsx`) are ready to reuse; the picker reads the server today (SYNC-10). The tests share one database and run one at a time, so a new test pairs its own laptop. The test shop has one seller on *Dawa* (`backend/tests/e2e_shop.py`, or DATA-1's seed by then). From DATA-1 **(v1.51)**: it is the demo's shop now, made by the demo seed as the test container starts (`apps/demo/services.py`): Asha sells on *Dawa* (PIN 2580), Baraka on *Vyakula* (PIN 1470), and Meneja, on every side, confirms a laptop with `meneja@example.com` and `jaribu-demo`. A clock-in test that needs another person adds them to the seed's `PEOPLE`, so the demo's visitors meet them too. The story's browsers are fixtures, `laptop` (with its movable clock) and `managersPhone`, in `pair-and-pin-login.spec.ts`: move them to a shared file in `e2e/` when this second test needs them.

An answer whose time can't be trusted names an `unverified_time` flag in `flags`: the clock-in still counts as sent. `last_server_time` names its zone, as every time a laptop sends must.
**Watch out** **(v1.59)**: from SYNC-6: today's list reads the `documents` drawer by `[type+client_time]`, the day's range in the shop's own time zone, made UTC, as `client_time` is written with `Z`.
**Watch out** **(v1.60)**: from SYNC-7: the last server time is `meta`'s `last_server_time`, the clock offset its `clock_offset_ms` (`src/offline/sync/meta.ts`), and the shop's zone `mpc.device`'s `shop.timezone`; `shopToday` (`src/offline/rules.ts`) gives the shop's day. Live hooks redraw on a change to what they read, not at midnight: today's list must turn to the new day by itself.
**Watch out** **(v1.61)**: from SYNC-8: save a tap with `saveDocument(db, {type: "clock", schema_version: 1, created_by, payload: {kind, last_server_time}})` (`src/offline/outbox.ts`), `db` from `useShopDatabase()` and `last_server_time` read from `meta` as the tap is saved. It refuses with `NeverFilled` on a laptop not filled yet (a new laptop before its first sync): words of its own, "wait for the first sync", not an error; and with `BrokenDraft`, a bug in the screen. The needs-attention screen is this card's (Jack, 12 Nov 2026): `useNeedsAttention()` gives each set-aside record with its document, its number and the server's code (`document.type_unknown`, `document.version_unknown`, `document.maker_unknown`, `id.taken`). Give each code words of its own in `sw.json` and `en.json`, as the office's sentences say "Try again.", which a resend can't mend. A refused clock-in stays in `documents`: decide whether today's list shows it, and how. If the screen pushes the card past its size, split it.
**Watch out** **(v1.62)**: from SYNC-9's plan, which Jack approved (12 Nov 2026): the *Tuma sasa* button goes on this first counter screen. **Watch out** **(v1.63)**: from SYNC-10: someone logged in with no network is `useOfflinePerson()` (`src/features/login/offline-session.ts`): their staff row, live, while the device's copy still lets them in. In the plan Jack approved, each told to him in its step: the counter's note of who is here lives in the tab's memory only (`src/features/login/at-the-counter.ts`), written by the home page, as a local session does (SYNC-10), so a reload on a counter screen sends the person to the home page: the tiles after an offline login, her welcome again after a server one, as `me` still names her; *Toka* on a counter screen hides the person and asks the home page, opened next, to log them out, as the logout calls the server; the frame (`src/app/(counter)/counter-frame.tsx`) names the person and their side, follows their language and font, and keeps the idle lock; once tapped, the button goes for the rest of the visit, so a double tap can't clock her out; "today" by the laptop's own clock, the one its taps are written with, in the shop's zone (`src/offline/clock-today.ts`), turning at midnight by itself; a refused tap counts for neither her button nor today's list, and the red warning above the button names it, with words of its own for each reason (`src/app/(counter)/needs-attention.tsx`), in place of the plan's mark in the list; *Tuma sasa* is switched off with no network, and says why (`send-now.tsx`); the end-to-end test reads the server's answers, as it can't read its database. Built: `/attendance/` (`src/app/(counter)/attendance/`), the *Mahudhurio* button on the welcome, `formatTime` (`src/lib/format.ts`), `readLastServerTime` (`meta.ts`), the waiting rows' query moved beside the save (`waitingInOrder`, `outbox.ts`) so a counter screen can count them, the shared parts of the browser tests (`e2e/the-shop.ts`), and the budget check (`frontend/scripts/counter-budget.mjs`, run by `make check-frontend` after the build). At SYNC-11, `/attendance/` weighs 234.6 KB of its 300, nearly all of it the code every page shares: the home page alone weighs 233.2. The size: about twice the card's M, as Jack chose both screens.
