### SYNC-16 · Background Sync
`M` · Claude-led · after SYNC-9, SYNC-11 · branch `feat/sync-16-background-sync` **(v1.62)**
**Build:** Background Sync on Chrome for Android, so records saved offline leave when the network returns, even after the app is closed (03 §6.5); browsers without it carry on as before. Chrome and Edge on the laptops have it too **(v1.65)**.
**Watch out** **(v1.62)**: moved here from SYNC-9 (Jack, 12 Nov 2026): no screen saves a record before SYNC-11, so the phone test can't run sooner. From SYNC-11 **(v1.64)**: *Mahudhurio*'s clock-in is that record: register the phone, log in once online, then clock in offline and close the app. From UI-3 **(v1.45)**: Background Sync runs in the service worker, which has no `localStorage`, so it can't call `saveBlocked()`: a block it meets must reach the page another way, a message to the open pages, or a mark in Dexie that `this-device.ts` reads too. Decide here. From SYNC-8: it needs the same `push()` (`src/offline/sync/push.ts`), which reaches `this-device.ts` through `answer.ts`, and whose client reads the CSRF token from `document.cookie` (`src/api/client.ts`); a service worker has neither, and `tsconfig.sw.json` checks it with the worker's types, so the build shows it. It has no `mpc.device` either, so it learns which shop's database to send some other way. From SYNC-9: the lock and the shared wait are in its card; the service worker takes the same lock and keeps the same wait. **Settled (v1.65)** (Jack, 13 Nov 2026): the hand test runs on the laptop's Edge before the PR, and on the shop phone with the live demo after the merge, ticked in the tick PR: a phone runs a service worker only on https, so it can't use a laptop's local app over Wi-Fi. The technical choices, in the plan Jack approved: a block or a "not registered" the worker hears is a note in the shop's `meta` (`device_refused`), which the page takes as it opens, offline too, and before each sync; the worker reads the CSRF token with the Cookie Store, learns the shop from the sync's name (`mpc-sync-<shop id>`), sends nothing when nothing waits, and only pushes; the push and its wait are shared by the page and the worker (`pushKeepingTheWait`); the worker's type check refuses page-only code (`make-client.ts` and `waiting.ts` keep `document` and React out of it).
**Done when:**
- [x] On the shop Android phone, a record saved offline, with the app then closed, reaches the server when the network returns.
- [x] Browsers without Background Sync carry on as before.
**Tests:** `sw.sync.test.ts` (the service worker's push, its lock and its wait) · `sync.no-background-sync.test.ts` (a browser without it)
**Commits:** `feat(frontend): background sync`

### SYNC-12 · Keeping offline data safe
`M` · Claude-led · after SYNC-8 · branch `feat/sync-12-data-safety`
**Build:** ask for persistent storage and show its state; install guidance; a note at *Toka* while records are unsent, which says how many wait and that they are safe, and still logs out **(v1.66)**; export unsent records to a file and import them on another device of the same shop (03 §6.7), on a new counter page, *Kompyuta hii*: anyone logged in exports, only `device.manage` imports, and set-aside records stay out of the file **(v1.66)**.
**Watch out:** on `localhost`, Chrome refused persistent storage even to the installed app, and granted it on the demo address (SPIKE-1's report). So the *not persistent* state shows in development and in the end-to-end tests (UI-6); only the demo or a real device proves the granted state **(v1.10)**.
**Watch out** **(v1.61)**: from SYNC-8: the unsent records are the outbox's `waiting` rows (`src/offline/db.ts`); the set-aside ones (`needs_attention`) are on the server already, as refused. An imported record must keep its id, so the server answers `duplicate` for one it already has, and take the importing laptop's next number: `saveDocument` makes a new id, so the import writes its rows its own way, in one transaction as the save does.
**Done when:**
- [x] *Toka* with unsent records says how many wait and that they are safe; a test proves logging out keeps them **(v1.66)**.
- [x] An exported file, imported on another device, posts each record once.
**From its review** **(v1.67)**: every finding fixed, by Jack's choice, each in its own commit: a stricter import; a carried record's number counted for the laptop that sent it; `carried_from` on the push and the answer log, so a rescued record is checked against the laptop that made it and names who loaded it; and the smaller fixes. From a second review, the same: a rescued clock-in always waits for a manager (Jack's choice), and six more fixes.
**Commits:** `feat(frontend): persistent storage and install` → `feat(frontend): unsent note at logout` **(v1.66)** → `feat(frontend): export and import unsent`

### SYNC-13 · Safe app updates
`M` · Claude-led · after SYNC-8, SPIKE-1 · branch `feat/sync-13-updates`
**Build:** the *Toleo jipya* bar SPIKE-1 built (`frontend/src/components/update-prompt.tsx`) switches only when no counter screen is busy; the app checks for a new version regularly, because the browser checks only when a page loads *(default: every hour, and when the network comes back)* **(v1.10)**; the order: send the outbox → update → upgrade the device database; documents carry `schema_version`, and the server accepts the current and the previous one; `min_app_version` from pull makes an old app send its outbox, then update (03 §6.8).
**Watch out:** from SPIKE-1's report. A waiting version also takes over, with no bar, when every window of the app is closed. After a switch, an old tab is served by the new service worker, which has deleted the old version's code, so a tab that needs code it never loaded fails until it reloads **(v1.10)**.
**Watch out** **(v1.53)**: from SYNC-2: the rules' defaults reach a device only with a bootstrap (`setting_keys`, `apps/sync/bootstrap.py`), so a rule a later release adds stays unknown to a device until it bootstraps again: after an update whose app reads a new rule, bootstrap again, or let the app carry that rule's default.
**Watch out** **(v1.55)**: from SYNC-3: every pull page carries `min_app_version`, null today (`MIN_APP_VERSION`, `config/settings/base.py`), so any app may sync. Give it a form a device can compare with its own version: today the app's version (`APP_VERSION`, `src/lib/version.ts`, and the server's) is a git commit, which can't say whether it is older or newer than another, so pick an ordered number, such as the release version or a build number, and keep both sides on it. From SYNC-3's review: a release that adds a table to the registry must reach devices whole. A device already past that table's lines never pulls them, so after the update it bootstraps again; and an older app, which has no store for the new `entity`, must not move its cursor past such a change: raise `min_app_version` with that release, so the old app sends its outbox and updates first.
**Watch out** **(v1.60)**: from SYNC-7: a pull that meets a list with no drawer throws `AppTooOld` (`src/offline/sync/pull.ts`) before that page, and the app only logs it, at every check, until this card updates the app. The bootstrap's answer names no shop: give it the shop's id and check it, so a laptop paired to another shop between its check and its bootstrap can't fill the old shop's database.
**Watch out** **(v1.61)**: from SYNC-8: a bootstrap keeps the larger of the server's `last_device_seq` and the laptop's own, so a laptop filled again with records still waiting never hands a number out twice; a database with no cursor or no number bootstraps (`needsBootstrap`). "Sends its outbox, then updates": `push(db)` (`src/offline/sync/push.ts`) answers `sent` once nothing more waits, `retry` if the server said to try later.
**Watch out** **(v1.68)**: settled while planning (13 Nov 2026). Jack's choices: the bar stays hidden while a sale is open and shows once it is finished, rather than waiting after a tap; and a too-old app updates by itself, with no tap. In the plan he approved: eight commits, one idea each; the switch goes ahead, after trying to send, with no network, a failed send or no answer in 10 s; part 3 is a guard test, as documents already carry `schema_version` and the server checks it (SYNC-4, SYNC-8); the app's number is a whole number kept by hand, not a release number, as every merge deploys before its release exists; and SYNC-7's note on the bootstrap's shop is done here.
**Watch out** **(v1.68)**: built. A counter screen with work open calls `useCounterBusy(true)` (`src/offline/busy.ts`): it holds a shared Web Lock, `mpc-counter-busy`, so every tab sees it, and a tab closed mid-sale lets it go by itself. No screen does yet, as a clock-in is one tap: SALE-3's sale screen will, while it holds items. The bar (`src/components/update-prompt.tsx`) watches it only while a version waits, at once for its own tab and every 2 s for the others (`QUIET_CHECK_EVERY`), and looks once more as the tap lands. A tap sends what waits first (`sendBeforeUpdate`, `src/offline/sync/before-update.ts`: a sync with `sendNow`, at most `SEND_LIMIT`, 10 s), saying *Inatuma kumbukumbu kwanza…*, then looks at the counter again, then switches; with no network, a failed send or no answer it switches anyway: the records stay in the outbox for the new version, the server reads the shape before its newest, and the browser switches by itself anyway once every window is closed. The page asks the browser to look for a new version every hour (`UPDATE_CHECK_EVERY`), when the network comes back, and as soon as the app is found too old (`src/offline/service-worker.ts`). The app's number is `APP_NUMBER` (`src/lib/version.ts`), 1, and every pull page carries the server's `MIN_APP_VERSION` (`config/settings/base.py`), now a whole number; `test_app_number.py` reads both and keeps the minimum at or below the app's. A page whose minimum is above the app's number, or that names a list the app has no drawer for, stops the pull before it (`AppTooOld`, `pull.ts`), after the push has gone; the sync tells it (`too-old.ts`), and the bar switches by itself once a version is ready and the counter quiet, the records first, once per version, leaving the bar for a tap if the reload doesn't come. `test_document_types.py` keeps each type's shape before its newest on the server's real list. The bootstrap names its shop, and a laptop writes nothing from another shop's copy (`WrongShop`, `bootstrap.ts`). The database upgrade needs nothing new: the new version opens the database after the switch, and upgrades it then. A real deploy reaching a laptop left open is shown only on the demo, after the merge (SPIKE-1: `localhost` proves less).
**Watch out** **(v1.68)**: notes for later cards. SALE-3: the sale screen calls `useCounterBusy(lines.length > 0)`, and every counter screen with work open does the same. CAT-6, and any card that adds a list to the registry: raise `APP_NUMBER` and `MIN_APP_VERSION` together, so an older app updates before its cursor passes the new list's lines; and give the new database version an upgrade that removes the cursor, so the laptop bootstraps again and gets the list's rows from before, as a laptop past their lines never pulls them; the same for a release whose app reads a rule a bootstrap hasn't brought (SYNC-2's note). The first card that gives a document a version 2: keep version 1 on the server's list; the guard test fails otherwise. A shape leaves the list only in a release after the one that raised both numbers past the apps that write it, as a too-old app sends its outbox first, in its own shapes **(v1.69)**.
**From its review** **(v1.69)**: every finding fixed, by Jack's choice, each in its own commit. A tab left on the old version after another tab switched reloads by itself once the counter is quiet; the bootstrap names the lowest app it serves, so an old tab fills nothing; a too-old app tries again at every sync; the pull checks the minimum before the cursor, so a restored server still tells an old app; one send at a time from the bar, which never sticks on a failure, a failed look counting as busy; a laptop moved to another shop asks about its device at once; `test_app_number.py` keeps each app number's lists in step with the registry and the app's drawers; a record shape leaves the server only a release after the minimum passed the apps that write it; and the comments say what updates do now. Left as a follow-up: the pull and the page's push name no shop, so a laptop paired to another shop between two device checks could pull that shop's changes into the old shop's database, or push into the new one; the service worker's push asks `/me` first, the page's don't.
**From a second review** **(v1.70)**: every finding fixed again, by Jack's choice, each in its own commit. `test_app_number.py` holds the server's minimum to the lists too, and reads the app's `LISTS` however Prettier lays them out; a bootstrap that finds the app too old still sends the outbox first; the tab that tapped *Sasisha* reloads, like every other tab, only once the counter is quiet, as taking over can take seconds; an automatic try a sale refused comes again at the next look; a tap's switch counts for the automatic try, so nothing sends or switches twice; a failure inside a tap is logged and the bar is ready again; a run of failed looks is logged once; a page hears a switch from before it registers; and `appIsTooOld`, no longer read, is gone. A last look at those fixes: an automatic try refused, or whose send failed, comes again only at the next look, never at once, so a send that kept failing can't repeat many times a second; and the comments say the tab that tapped is taken over too. That tab sends once more as the new version takes over, usually nothing, but a sale made in those seconds leaves before the reload (Jack's choice). Left as a follow-up, beside the one above: the at-the-device test that sends Neema's logout twice under load (SYNC-10's code).
**Done when:**
- [x] An update never interrupts a busy counter screen.
- [x] An app below `min_app_version` sends its outbox, then updates.
- [x] A counter left open all day still gets the *Toleo jipya* bar after a deploy, with no reload **(v1.10)**.
**Commits:** `feat(frontend): updates wait for a quiet counter` → `feat(frontend): send unsent records before an update` → `feat(frontend): regular update checks` → `test(sync): the server keeps the previous record shape` → `feat(sync): a minimum app number the laptop can compare` → `feat(frontend): a too-old app updates itself` → `feat(sync): the bootstrap names its shop` **(v1.68)**; from its reviews, twenty-five commits: fifteen `fix`, three `test`, one `refactor` and six `docs` **(v1.70)**

### SYNC-14 · Outage log and sync health page
`M` · Claude-led · after SYNC-5, SYNC-8 · branch `feat/sync-14-sync-health`
**Build:** the device records when it goes offline and comes back, and reports it at sync; the office page *Hali ya vifaa* shows each device's last sync, unsent records, clock error, app version and outages this week (03 §5.17, §6.11).
**Watch out** **(v1.57)**: from SYNC-5: the server side is built. The device's row holds `last_synced_at`, `unsent_count` and `app_version`, empty until its first push, and `people.DeviceOutage` its periods, read with the index `(shop, device, started_at)`. A period's times are the device's clock, kept as reported, so one may end before it starts: show it as the device said, and say so. The clock error the page shows is not kept by SYNC-5 (03 §6.6: the device stores its own offset): decide here how it reaches the server.
**Watch out** **(v1.58)**: from ATT-1: it reaches the server with each push, as `sent_at`, the laptop's clock as it sends: its clock error is `sent_at` minus `received_at`. ATT-1 keeps it on each clock-in (`AttendanceEvent.clock_error`), not on the device's row: keep it there for the page, in the edit history's skip list through `ChangeRecordEditsV2`, as SYNC-5's sync columns are.
**Watch out** **(v1.61)**: from SYNC-8: the push sends `outages: []` (`src/offline/sync/push.ts`): fill it here, up to 100 a push, sending each period again until a push answers 200. Its `unsent_count` leaves out the set-aside records, which the page may show apart, from the device's outbox.
**Watch out** **(v1.62)**: from SYNC-9: each open tab of a laptop syncs every 30 s on its own (`SYNC_EVERY`, `src/offline/sync/triggers.tsx`); the lock puts them in turn but doesn't merge them, so a laptop with two tabs open reports twice as often. Its `last_synced_at` stays right; don't read the number of reports as the number of laptops.
**Watch out** **(v1.71)**: settled while planning and building (Jack, 14 Nov 2026). The page as Jack said yes to it: one card per laptop, by its number, naming its side, with its last sync, the records waiting on it, its clock, its app, and "offline this week: N times, M in all" over each period, newest first; a laptop not synced on the shop's today says so at the top of its card, in the warning colour, and its card stands out; a blocked one says it is blocked instead. In the plan Jack approved, seven commits, one idea each, as the server needed two of its own: a cut is no network, or a sync that got no answer, so a Wi-Fi with no internet behind it counts; back is the first answer the pull gets, so a push that lost its line while the pull after it worked is no cut; one open period at a time in the shop's database, so two tabs note one; each moment sees it again, and a period the app lost sight of for more than 3 minutes (`LOST_SIGHT`), closed or asleep, ends where it was last seen, so a laptop shut at 19:00 with no internet isn't counted offline all night; the periods wait in a new drawer, `outages` (database version 2, with no upgrade: a new drawer, not a list, so no app number moves), and go with the push, up to 100 a batch, forgotten once the server answers, a retry included, as the server keeps them whatever the documents' answers; the clock error is the device row's `clock_error` (`people` migration 0016), `sent_at` minus `received_at` at each push, outside the edit history, newer over older; the route is `GET /api/v1/people/devices/health/`, one answer, no pages, shown to whoever sees the side, as the device list is, its button for `device.manage` holders, so the guard on a show action behind a key (PPL-5) still waits for debts and reports; "this week" is the 7 days before now, by when a period ended; a clock within 2 minutes is right, the clock-ins' slack. Built: `src/offline/sync/outages.ts`, its notes from `sync.ts` and `triggers.tsx`, the push's `periodsToSend`, `Device.clock_error`, `outages_this_week` (`apps/people/selectors.py`), `DeviceHealthSerializer`, the page (`src/app/(office)/device-health/`, `src/features/devices/device-health.tsx`, its rules in `health.ts`) and its button on the office home. The end-to-end test runs the laptop's clock on with Playwright's `runFor`, as `fastForward` fires each timer once only; its moved clock, ahead of the server's, shows the clock error too. Parking lot 23 stays open: an office page can't reach a laptop's own database (rule 5). Jack checked the Swahili on 14 Nov 2026.
**Done when:**
- [x] Ten minutes offline shows as one outage of about ten minutes after the next sync.
- [x] A device that hasn't synced today is highlighted.
**From its review** **(v1.72)**: every finding fixed, by Jack's choice, each in its own commit. Jack's choices: the page's week counts each cut by when the server heard of it (`created_at`, `outages_this_week`), not by when it ended on the laptop's clock (v1.71), so a laptop whose clock battery died, its clock in 2020, still shows today's cut, and one whose clock runs ahead doesn't show its cuts for good; and a cut the laptop's app lost sight of, as the laptop went off in a power cut or the app was closed, shows its end as unknown, "18:59 jioni – ? (kifaa kilizimika baada ya 19:04 usiku)", with "angalau" in the week's total, never as the short cut it would read as: `end_unknown` on the laptop's row, an optional field of the push's period, so an app from before still pushes, and a column of `people.DeviceOutage` (migration 0017). And: the page asks the server again every minute (`ASK_AGAIN_EVERY`), so a page left open past midnight reads the new day's syncs; a cut that began after the pull was asked stays open, as the line went again as the answer came; a clock past the slack never reads as "2 minutes", and the server rounds the clock error away from zero, so 120.4 s, which already makes clock-ins wait, is never called right; tests for the too-old and other-shop answers; and the sync rules name `outages.ts` and `lib/ids.ts` among the files the service worker loads.
**Tests:** `outages.test.ts` · `sync.outages.test.ts` · `push.outages.test.ts` · `health.test.ts` · `device-health.test.tsx` · `test_device_health_route.py` · `e2e/device-health.spec.ts` **(v1.71)**
**Commits:** `feat(frontend): record offline periods` → `feat(frontend): send offline periods with each push` → `feat(people): keep each laptop's clock error` → `feat(people): the device health route` → `feat(frontend): device health page` → `test(e2e): ten minutes offline shows as one outage` → `docs: what the sync health page settled` **(v1.71)**; from its review, nine commits: six `fix`, one `test` and two `docs` **(v1.72)**

### OPS-3 · One-tap demo
`S` · Claude-led · after SYNC-11, DATA-1 · branch `feat/ops-3-demo-device`
**Build:** where the only shop is a demo shop, *Jaribu demo* registers the visitor's browser as a demo device, and the PIN screen shows the demo staff with their PINs (BP15); throttled; switched off whenever a live shop exists.
**Watch out** **(v1.51)**: from DATA-1: the demo staff and their PINs are `PEOPLE` in `apps/demo/services.py` (Asha 2580 on Dawa, Baraka 1470 on Vyakula); show them from there, not from a second list. The demo's database also carries the sign `DEMO_SEED=yes` (`settings.DEMO_SEED`): asking for it as well as "no live shop" keeps *Jaribu demo* shut on a brand-new real server, which has no shop yet. The seed makes the demo shop again on the first start of each day, so a demo device registered yesterday is gone: its saved key meets an unknown device, and the visitor taps *Jaribu demo* again. The laptop drops yesterday's saved data with it, its copy of the shop and its outbox (SYNC-6 to SYNC-9), or it would show yesterday's shop and send documents of people who no longer exist. Passwords and PINs can't change on the demo (`demo.logins_fixed`), so the PINs the screen shows stay true all day.
**Watch out** **(v1.73)**: settled while planning and building (Jack, 14 Nov 2026). Two fixes to this card, in the plan Jack approved. *In `demo`, not `people`* (Jack: A): `people` sits below `demo` and may not import it (ADR-0041), so a route there couldn't show the PINs from `PEOPLE`; the routes live in `apps/demo/api/` beside the seed, ADR-0043 supersedes ADR-0041 to say so, and the first commit's scope is `demo`. *Yesterday's data:* each shop's copy on a laptop is a database of its own, named by the shop's id (`databaseName`, SYNC-7), and the seed makes the shop with a new id each day, so yesterday's copy is never opened again, nor its outbox sent (the background push checks its shop first, SYNC-16): deleting it is tidying, not a safety fix. The tap still deletes every other shop's copy on the laptop (`forgetOtherShops`), which is safe because the demo opens only where no other shop exists. Five commits, one idea each, as the button and the hints are two; about `M` in the building, inside the stop line. Built. The server: `demo_shop_if_open` and `register_demo_device` (`apps/demo/services.py`). Open only with the sign, no shop but a demo one, and the demo shop made. The device comes through the two steps of every pairing (`start_pairing`, `confirm_pairing`), confirmed by Meneja, who holds `device.manage` on every side; it is named by its pairing code ("Demo K7P2-MXQ9"), which no two requests share, so two visitors never clash, and it is on Dawa (`DEMO_DEVICE_SIDE`). A browser whose key already works is refused (`pairing.already_paired`); one holding yesterday's key gets a new device. Two routes, decided on this card for the sweep's lists: `GET /api/devices/v1/demo/open/` (`DemoOpenView`), no login, one true or false, reading the sign and the shops' kinds alone, so on `NOT_SHOP_ROUTES` with `test_demo_open_answer_holds_nothing_of_a_shop`; and `POST /api/devices/v1/demo/` (`DemoDeviceView`), a `DeviceView` with `device_optional`, CSRF-checked, counted with the pairing start's limit (10 an hour per network), on `DEVICE_ROUTES_WITHOUT_A_LOOK` with `test_one_tap_demo_refused_with_live_shop`. Its answer holds the PINs by staff id, the key in the cookie only. A new code, `demo.closed`. The app: *Jaribu demo* on the Karibu welcome, under its two ways on (above them, it would push Ingia down as it comes, a moment after the page), only where the open check says true, so never with no network (`src/features/devices/try-the-demo.tsx`, `demo.ts`); the PINs kept in localStorage by staff id (`demo-pins.ts`, `mpc.demo-pins`) and shown on each seller's tile as "PIN 2580" (`staff-picker.tsx`), offline too. A staff id is a UUIDv7 no two shops share, so a PIN kept from yesterday matches no tile, and a real shop's laptop shows none. The browser test reads the PIN off Asha's tile, logs her in, takes the network away and clocks her in: 3.6 s on Jack's laptop, on an awake server; the free demo asleep first spends about a minute waking up (README), before the visitor's minute starts. Proven red: the live-shop check removed (`test_one_tap_demo_refused_with_live_shop`), the delete left out, and the PIN line emptied. Jack checked the Swahili on 15 Nov 2026.
**Done when:**
- [x] A visitor goes from the demo link to an offline clock-in in under a minute.
- [x] A test proves it is refused when a live shop exists.
**Tests:** `test_one_tap_demo_refused_with_live_shop` · `test_demo_device.py` · `try-the-demo.test.tsx` · `staff-picker.test.tsx` · `e2e/one-tap-demo.spec.ts` **(v1.73)**
**Commits:** `feat(demo): one-tap demo device` → `feat(frontend): one-tap demo button` → `feat(frontend): demo pin hints` → `test(e2e): one tap to an offline clock-in` → `docs: what the one-tap demo settled` **(v1.73)**

## 8. Stage 3 — Ready for real data

**Goal:** the production server, deploys, backups and alerts all work — and a restore has been tested — before any real data exists (BP14).
**Done when:** a release deploys to production after approval; the weekly restore test passes; an alert reaches Jack's phone.
**You'll learn:** a Linux server and its security; Docker in production; HTTPS with Caddy; backups you can trust; monitoring.
**Needs:** OWN-3 (name and web address), OWN-4 (budget sign-off).
**Order:** top to bottom. The stage starts after stage 4's build cards, once the owner has seen attendance on the demo, and ends before ATT-7 and LIVE-1 (BP14) **(v1.74)**.

| ID | Task | Size | After | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPS-4 | Production server — Lineserve 2 GB, Dar es Salaam *(Jack-led)* | M | OPS-1 | SSH keys only, firewall (web and SSH), automatic security updates, Docker; `docs/runbooks/server-setup.md` |
| OPS-5 | `compose.prod.yml`, the Caddyfile and the `web` image | M | OPS-4 | HTTPS on the real address; long cache for hashed files, none for pages and the service worker; `/api` and `/_allauth/` to Django **(v1.2)**; security headers; CSP report-only; no container runs as root; outgoing email for reset links, through a mail service's settings in the environment, or none at all, since resets are face to face (ADR-0038): never the console, which would write reset links into the server's logs **(v1.29)**; the setting is `MAIL_BACKEND` (`config/settings/prod.py`), today Django's dummy backend, which sends nothing, and the console only on the demo (`render.yaml`) **(v1.30)**; send it outside the request, or a slow mail service would answer late for a known email and at once for an unknown one, telling a stranger who has an account; and set `DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL`, today `webmaster@localhost` **(v1.31)**; from PPL-13 **(v1.132)**: `docs/runbooks/open-a-shop.md` runs each command as `docker compose -f compose.prod.yml exec backend python manage.py …`, so a different file or service name changes the runbook |
| OPS-6 | `deploy-prod.yml` | M | OPS-5, REPO-7 | A release waits for approval, rehearses migrations on a scratch copy on the server, deploys and smoke-tests; it runs as a job of the release workflow, so the release and the deploy follow each other in one run, in a fixed order **(v1.4)**; after the migrations, `manage.py createcachetable`, for the throttles' cache (AUTH-5) **(v1.28)**; secrets in a GitHub environment; `docs/runbooks/rotate-secrets.md` |
| OPS-7 | Worker and scheduler | M | OPS-5 | The django-tasks-db worker and supercronic run; jobs ping a heartbeat; a job run twice does no harm; the first jobs prune old task rows and compact the change log monthly (03 §6.4) — from SYNC-1 the change log is a ledger whose trigger refuses every DELETE (`core`'s 0002), so compacting it means lifting the trigger on purpose in a reviewed step (03 §5.6), or not compacting at all: decide on the card **(v1.52)**; and prune expired rows from the cache tables, which keep a hash of each email asked about until then, and old sessions (`clearsessions`) **(v1.31)** |
| OPS-8 | Backups *(Jack-led)* | M | OPS-5 | Lineserve's server backups on; nightly encrypted `pg_dump` + photos with restic; 7 daily, 4 weekly and 12 monthly kept; the weekly USB copy script runs on the shop laptop; the password is held by the owner and Jack |
| OPS-9 | Restore test | M | OPS-8 | Every week the latest backup is restored into a scratch database and checked (the ledger checks too, once they exist); a failure alerts; `docs/runbooks/restore.md` |
| OPS-10 | Monitoring | M | OPS-5 | JSON logs with no personal data; Sentry with personal data stripped; an uptime check every minute; disk and memory alerts; `/api/health/` checks the database and the worker heartbeat; from AUTH-6 **(v1.32)**: passwords are hidden from Django's own error reports by `sensitive_variables`, which only Django's reporter obeys, and Sentry reads a crash's variables itself: set `include_local_variables=False`, or prove with a crash in the hash that no password reaches it. Today a crash on a server leaves no report at all (no `ADMINS` or `LOGGING`), though `apps/core/api/errors.py` says it is logged: this card makes that true; from AUTH-4's review **(v1.35)**: Django's error reports print the `sessionid` cookie, as its name holds no word its filter hides, unlike `device_key`, and a session alone is a login: hide it before any report is sent, by renaming the cookie or with a filter of our own |
| OPS-11 | Replay after a restore | M | OPS-9, SYNC-8 | Devices keep sent documents 30 days and resend them after a restore, with nothing doubled; runbooks for a stolen device, a replaced device and stuck unsent records — from SYNC-3 **(v1.55)**: after a restore the change log's numbers go back, so a device's pull cursor may be ahead of the restored log, and a pull from it would skip every new change up to it. Decide how each device starts again from the restored log (a fresh bootstrap, once its outbox is sent): a check of the cursor against the shop's last number misses it once new lines pass the old cursor — from SYNC-8 **(v1.61)**: an accepted document goes to `sent_archive` with its `device_seq`, `sent_at` and `posted_seq` (null when its save changed nothing a laptop keeps), in one transaction with its outbox row's leaving; nothing clears the archive after 30 days yet; from CTRL-6 **(v1.98)**: a photo goes up only once its record is in `sent_archive` (`src/offline/sync/attachments.ts`), so a clearing must keep a record while a photo of it still waits on the laptop, or that photo never goes; after a restore, a photo whose row the restore lost answers `attachment.not_found` and waits, longer each time, until the replay brings its record back |
| OPS-12 | Dress rehearsal | S | OPS-11, SYNC-11 | On the real server with a scratch shop: pair a shop device, clock in offline, restore a backup, replay; on throttled 3G, 200 documents push in under 10 s and a new device sets up in under a minute (03 §14); then the scratch shop is removed |

## 9. Stage 4 — Attendance live (R1b)

**Goal:** every worker on both sides clocks in and out on a shop device; the month's pay is calculated; workers see their own payslips.
**Done when:** the first full month runs in production, and the system's payslips match the hand-calculated ones — or every difference is explained.
**You'll learn:** business rules written test-first; time and clocks; dated rules; photos taken offline; office screens and forms.
**Needs:** OWN-5 (PDPC), OWN-6 (emails), OWN-7 (attendance rules; answered 16 Nov 2026, #55 **(v1.86)**), OWN-8 (workers and daily rates, and who the cook is **(v1.78)**).
**Order:** top to bottom, before stage 3. ATT-7 and LIVE-1 wait for stage 3: ATT-7 needs OPS-7's scheduler, and no real data exists before the server, its backups and a tested restore **(v1.74)**.

### SHOP-3 · Settings: the server
`M` · Claude-led · after CORE-4, PPL-5, SYNC-15 · branch `feat/shop-3-settings-server` **(v1.75)**
**Build:** the one way a rule's value is written, a side's on/off switch, and their routes.
- `set_setting(shop, *, setting_id, key, value, effective_from, side=None, note="", by)` (`apps/shops/services.py`) writes one new `Setting` row, never an edit (ADR-0013). It runs `full_clean`, as `Setting.clean()` holds the checks and `objects.create` skips them; checks the side is of the shop (T1); refuses a day before the shop's today (`setting.date_past`); and refuses a side's rule with no side (`setting.side_required`). It saves inside `recording_changes(shop)` and calls `record_change(row)`, so the laptops get the row at their next pull (SYNC-2).
- `switch_side(side, *, version, is_live, by)` turns a side on or off (ADR-0033), with `record_change(side)` in the same block; an old `version` is refused (`version_conflict`), as `rename_side` does.
- Routes under `/api/v1/shops/`. `GET settings/`: one item per rule in the registry, and one per side for a side's rule: its key, kind, scope, side, default, the value in force today, and its rows newest first (value, from, note, who set it, when). Not in pages: the registry is a short list. `POST settings/`: a new value, with the client's id (ADR-0007, `CreateOnceMixin`), needing `settings.manage` on the row's side, or on the whole shop for a shop's rule. `PATCH sides/{id}/` takes `is_live` beside `name`, under the same `settings.manage`.
**Choices by Jack (15 Nov 2026):** a new value starts on the shop's today or later, never before: an earlier day would change what past days answer, and a draft pay run with them. A side's rule (scope `SIDE`: `cash.tolerance`, `count.tolerance_pct`, `expiry.alert_days`, `stock.block_negative`) is set one side at a time, with no row for the whole shop: CORE-4's side row beats even a newer shop row, so a shop value written next year would be hidden by a side's old row, and nobody would see why. `get_setting` doesn't change; the service just never writes such a shop row.
**Watch out:** `shop_today(shop)` lives in `apps/people/selectors.py`, which `shops` sits below and may not import: move it down into `shops`, and `people` and `sync` import it from there. A same-day fix is still a new row for the same day, which wins (T2), today included. Reading the rules is `SEES_THE_SIDE` *(default)*: every rule already travels to every laptop of the shop (SYNC-2), so the route hides nothing a counter doesn't hold, and PPL-5 refuses a show action behind a key of its own until a card builds one. On the demo, visitors share Meneja's login, and a visitor who switched Dawa off would stop every later one-tap demo, whose devices are on Dawa (`DEMO_DEVICE_SIDE`), until the morning's seed; so on the demo a side's switch is fixed, `demo.sides_fixed`, as `check_logins_may_change` fixes logins *(default)*. The rules stay open there: trying them is the point, and the seed puts them back. The pull needs nothing new: it sends a rule's rows as `get_setting` reads them (`setting_rows`, SYNC-3). SYNC-3's `test_a_rules_rows_come_as_the_device_reads_them` writes its change lines by hand until this service exists: it writes through `set_setting` now, with the clock set back for its rows of earlier days, as the service refuses a past day. The registry's comment on `Scope.SIDE` ("a shop value that each side may override") changes with the choice, and 02 §4's Setting gets the two choices in the card's last commit.
**Built** **(v1.76)**: as planned, in five commits after the card's. `shop_today` moved into `apps/shops/selectors.py`, and every caller asks it there. `set_setting` returns every mistake at once, each beside its field: its own two codes and `Setting.clean()`'s. `switch_side` follows `rename_side`, and the side route takes one change at a time: a new name or the switch, as each is its own service with its own version check; both or neither is the plain `invalid`. `GET settings/` answers `{today, rules}`, one object, not pages (`ShopRulesAnswer`, `@extend_schema_serializer(many=False)`): the page's date box starts at the shop's today. Each rule's value comes through `get_setting` itself, so the page and the pay run can't disagree; each row says who set it by nickname (`set_by`, through `created_by`, as `shops` may not import `people`), or None for the system. A side's rule shows any whole-shop row too, which only code that skips `set_setting` could write, as it still counts behind the side's own. Who sees no side sees no rule. The keys are a choice in the schema (`SettingKeyEnum`); the key sent stays text, so an unknown one gets `setting.key_unknown`. Seven `setting.*` codes and `demo.sides_fixed` have their two sentences; Jack checked the Swahili on 15 Nov 2026. SYNC-3's test writes through the service, told that each row's day is today (`unittest.mock.patch`, no new dependency). Proven red: the past-day and side checks removed.
**From the review** **(v1.77)**: Jack chose to fix every finding, each in its own commit. *Rows of the whole shop:* the base's read kept to a person's sides with `side IN (…)`, which never matches an empty side, so a person whose keys cover only some sides saw a rule's value and none of its rows; a table whose side link may be empty now shows its rows of no side to whoever sees a side, as a table of the whole shop does (`get_queryset`, `apps/core/api/base.py`). The settings route is the only one with such a link today: a device's home side is required. Proven red by `test_settings_list_keeps_to_the_persons_sides`, which now writes a row of the whole shop and one on each side. *The demo's sides:* the seed left both off, so a visitor could never switch Dawa off, and the lock stopped the system too; now the seed switches both on through `switch_side`, as the system, and the lock stops a person only, as `set_pin`'s does. *Swahili:* the demo's sentence says *pande*, the app's word for sides, not *sehemu*, a form's box; and *Jina hili la kanuni* reads one way; Jack checked both. *Tests:* a whole-shop row of a side's rule shows on each side, proven red; the demo's refusal through the route; the settings routes in the suspended person's test. *Comments:* the test rows say how they differ from `set_setting`'s; the error list says where each setting code comes from (`setting.key_invalid` and `setting.value_blank` never come through `set_setting` today, and keep their words); three comments no longer credit SHOP-3 with a rename screen or an account screen; the services header reads as one paragraph.
**Done when:**
- [x] A new value from today or a later day is saved as a new row and lands one change line; nothing is ever edited.
- [x] A day before the shop's today, a wrong value, a side of another shop, and a side's rule with no side are each refused with their code, and write nothing.
- [x] Only someone with `settings.manage` sets a value or switches a side; a side switched on or off lands its change line; on the demo the switch is refused.
- [x] `GET settings/` shows every rule in the registry, once per side for a side's rule, with its value today and its rows.
**Tests:** `test_set_setting_saves_a_new_row` · `test_past_day_refused` · `test_today_allowed_and_same_day_fix_wins` · `test_side_rule_needs_a_side` · `test_shop_rule_refuses_a_side` · `test_side_of_another_shop_refused` · `test_wrong_value_refused` · `test_new_value_lands_a_change_line` · `test_switch_side_lands_a_change_line` · `test_side_switch_fixed_on_demo` · `test_setting_needs_settings_manage` · `test_settings_list_shows_every_rule`; and SYNC-3's `test_a_rules_rows_come_as_the_device_reads_them`, through the service. Built too **(v1.76)**: in `test_set_setting.py`, `test_past_day_is_the_shops_day` · `test_side_rule_set_for_one_side`; in `test_switch_side.py`, `test_switch_side_turns_a_side_on_and_off` · `test_switch_side_refuses_an_old_copy` · `test_switch_side_refuses_someone_of_another_shop`; in `test_api_sides.py`, `test_edit_switches_a_side_on` · `test_edit_sends_one_change_at_a_time`; in `test_api_settings.py`, `test_settings_list_shows_a_sides_rule_per_side` · `test_settings_list_is_one_answer_not_pages` · `test_create_setting_saves_and_answers_the_row` · `test_create_setting_refused_answers_its_codes` · `test_create_setting_with_another_shops_side_is_not_found`; in `backend/tests/test_route_permissions.py`, `test_side_switch_needs_settings_manage` · `test_settings_list_keeps_to_the_persons_sides` · `test_who_sees_no_side_sees_no_rule` · `test_settings_routes_name_their_permissions`. From the review **(v1.77)**: `test_the_system_switches_sides_on_the_demo` · `test_seed_demo_switches_its_sides_on` · `test_a_shop_row_of_a_sides_rule_shows_on_each_side` · `test_side_switch_refused_on_the_demo`; and the settings routes in `test_suspended_member_gets_nothing_from_routes`
**Commits:** `docs: the shop-3 and shop-5 cards` → `refactor(shops): the shop's today lives with the shop` → `feat(shops): set a rule's value from a day on` → `feat(shops): switch a side on or off` → `feat(shops): settings routes` → `docs(plan): shop-3 says what it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the shop-3 card follows the review` **(v1.77)**

### SHOP-5 · Settings: the page
`M` · Claude-led · after SHOP-3, UI-8 · branch `feat/shop-5-settings-page` **(v1.75)**
**Build:** *Mipangilio ya duka*, an office screen at `/settings/` (API client only, ADR-0020), linked from the office home. Every rule, grouped by its area (Mahudhurio, Pesa, Kompyuta, …), with its value today; a side's rule has one column per side. *Badilisha* opens a small form: a box that fits the rule's kind (money, percent, whole number, time, yes or no), the day it starts, from the shop's today on, an optional note, and *Hifadhi*. Under it the rule's history, newest first, the row in use now and any not started yet marked. At the bottom, each side with its on/off switch; switching one off asks first.
**Watch out:** each rule's name and a line of help live in `sw.json` and `en.json`, by key (ADR-0040): the registry's description is for developers. Make the key a choice in the API schema, so a test walks every key in both books, as `errors.test.ts` walks the codes; a key a later card adds (ATT-2's) then shows by itself once it has its words. Money and percents are strings and big.js, never numbers (ADR-0024): "1,000" typed is sent as "1000". The date box starts at the shop's today, and the server refuses an earlier day anyway. Every code the page meets has its two sentences: `setting.date_past`, `setting.side_required`, `setting.value_invalid`, `setting.side_not_allowed`, `setting.key_unknown`, `demo.sides_fixed`, `version_conflict`; a box's code shows beside the box (`useApiErrorWords().fieldMessages`). *Badilisha* and the switches show only to someone holding `settings.manage`; everyone else sees the values only. Built from our look's pieces in `components/design/` (ADR-0039).
**Watch out** **(v1.76)**: from SHOP-3. `GET /api/v1/shops/settings/` answers `{today, rules}`: each rule's `key`, `kind`, `scope`, `side` (null for the whole shop's), `default` and `value` today, as text in the kind's one form, and its `rows`, newest first (`value`, `effective_from`, `note`, `set_by`, a nickname or null for the system, `created_at`). A side's rule may show a row with no side, the shop's: show it as such. The keys are already a choice in the schema (`SettingKeyEnum`): a `Record<SettingKeyEnum, …>` of the words makes `pnpm typecheck` fail for a key that has none, beside the test that walks both books. A new value is `POST` with the client's id; the switch is `PATCH /api/v1/shops/sides/{id}/` with `{is_live, version}` alone, as a name and the switch go one at a time. Every code the page meets already has its two sentences, checked by Jack. Who may: `me` carries each person's keys per side. The word for a side is *Upande* (02 §21), as the app's sentences say, not the *Sehemu* of the first sketch. From SHOP-3's review **(v1.77)**: the demo's sides start switched on, and a visitor's switch there answers `demo.sides_fixed`, so the page shows that sentence beside the switch.
**Choices by Jack (15 Nov 2026)** **(v1.79)**: a side's rule has one line per side under the rule, each with its own button, not a column per side: on the owner's phone two columns of values and buttons don't fit. The demo's Meneja holds `settings.manage` on the whole shop beside the Manager role, given by the seed through `grant_permission` (a `keys` list on `DemoPerson`, `apps/demo/services.py`): a manager alone may not change the rules, the demo has no owner, and without it no visitor, nor the owner, could try *Badilisha*. One key, not the Owner role, whose `staff.manage` would let a visitor take Meneja's role away from everyone after them (PPL-4's note). A visitor's value lasts until the nightly reset, and since the second review starts tomorrow or later, with no note, so it never shows to the next visitor **(v1.81)**; the sides stay as the seed made them (`demo.sides_fixed`): no switch, and since the review no rename or new side either **(v1.80)**.
**Built** **(v1.79)**: in the plan Jack approved, in five commits and this one. The page is `features/settings/` and `/settings/`, with a button on the office home for anyone who works on a side, as reading needs no key. Someone without the key on a line gets *Historia* in place of *Badilisha*, which opens the history alone; the switch asks first both ways, *Hapana* holding the keyboard (choices in the plan). The words are `shopSettings.rules.<key>`: a `name`, a `help` and `shown`, the value in its sentence ("Dakika {value}"); `RULE_AREA` (`setting-words.ts`) is a `Record<SettingKeyEnum, Area>`, so a new key fails `pnpm typecheck` until it has an area, and the typed `t()` until both books hold its words; a key the app doesn't know, a newer server's, is left off the page. The value today is the server's; the rows to come and the row in use are read from the same rows by `get_setting`'s own rule (`rules.ts`): the side's own row that has started wins, even an older one, then the shop's, then the default. The form's box fits the kind: money and percents take up to two places, as the server keeps them (since 16 Nov 2026, money whole shillings only, with the number keypad, §20 item 25) **(v1.82)**, with a comma or a space only between thousands, taken out before sending **(v1.80)**; a whole number digits only; a time the phone's clock box; a yes or no *Ndiyo*/*Hapana*. An empty box, a value that isn't one of its kind, and a day before today are said at once in the server's own sentences, and nothing is sent; the server's refusals show beside their box, and one about the rule itself (`side`, `key`), which has no box, above the buttons. The id is kept through every try of the same value, day and note (ADR-0007), and a changed one gets a new id **(v1.80)**; *Hifadhi* is off while offline. The sides come from `GET sides/`'s first page of 50, sorted by letter; a `version_conflict` reads them again. `format.ts` gained `formatMoneyExact` (a rule's amount, cents shown when it has some, never rounded), `formatDay` (a day with no zone, "01/12/2026") and `clockPart` (the part of the day of "07:35", for *asubuhi*). Jack checked the Swahili, step by step.
**From the review** **(v1.80)**: Jack chose to fix every finding, each in its own commit. *The demo's sides:* `settings.manage`, which the demo's Meneja now holds, is the key a side's every change asks for, so a visitor could rename Dawa or add sides for everyone after them; `add_side` and `rename_side` now refuse a person on the demo as `switch_side` does (`_check_sides_fixed_on_demo`, `apps/shops/services.py`), and `demo.sides_fixed` says it for all three: "Kwenye demo, pande hazibadilishwi, ili kila mgeni aweze kujaribu." (03 v1.63). *The switch:* its question keeps what it asked as it opened, the new state and the version read then, as the page reads the sides again when the phone comes back to it and a question that followed could turn "Zima?" into "Washa?" with the new version; its answers carry the question for a screen reader (`aria-describedby`), and *Hapana* is off while a switch is on its way. *The form:* a changed value, day or note is sent under a new id, as the server answers a repeated id with the row it stored; a comma counts only between thousands, so "2,5" is refused, not sent as 25; a yes or no starts the keyboard on its picked choice; each tap clears the last send's refusal, and a refusal of a field the form doesn't know shows in the alert. English writes "Minutes: 15" and "Days: 90", never "1 minutes". Tests now hold *Hifadhi* and the switch off while offline, and the browser test saves the day after tomorrow, which a run crossing midnight in Dar can't turn into today.
**From the second review** **(v1.81)**: a short one, of the fix commits only; nothing had to be fixed, and Jack chose to fix the four it found. *Choice by Jack (15 Nov 2026):* on the demo a visitor could still change a rule from today, as idle minutes of 1 that lock every demo laptop, or leave a note every later visitor reads; so there a person's new value starts tomorrow or later, and a note is refused, each beside its box (`demo.rules_from_tomorrow`, `demo.no_note`, `set_setting`): the night's seed makes the shop again before the value comes, and a visitor can still try everything (03 v1.64). The form says a refusal of a field it has no box for in that field's own sentence, not "check the marked boxes"; a grouped number starts with 1 to 9, so "0,500" is refused, not sent as 500; and the comments say what the code does.
**Done when:**
- [x] The owner sets the late fine to 2,000 from tomorrow: the page shows 1,000 today and 2,000 coming, and the history shows both.
- [x] Every rule in the registry is on the page, a side's rule once per side, with its words in Swahili and English; Jack has checked the Swahili.
- [x] A refused value shows its Swahili sentence beside its box.
- [x] The owner switches a side on or off, after a confirm.
**Tests:** `settings-screen.test.tsx` · `setting-form.test.tsx` · `side-switch.test.tsx` · `setting-words.test.ts` (every key, both books) · `e2e/shop-settings.spec.ts`. Built too **(v1.79)**: `rules.test.ts` (the row in force on a day and the rows to come, by `get_setting`'s rule), the three new helpers in `format.money.test.ts` and `format.time.test.ts`, and `test_seed_demo_lets_its_manager_change_the_shops_rules`, with the seed's repeatable test reading each person's extra keys
**Commits:** `feat(frontend): the shop's rules on one page` → `feat(frontend): set a rule's new value from a day on` → `feat(frontend): switch a side on or off` → `feat(demo): the demo's manager may change the shop's rules` **(v1.79)** → `test(e2e): the owner changes the late fine` → `docs(plan): shop-5 says what it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the shop-5 card follows the review` **(v1.80)**; then the second review's, and `docs: the shop-5 card follows the second review` **(v1.81)**

### SHOP-4 · A side's band
`M` · Claude-led · after SHOP-5, UI-3 · branch `feat/shop-4-side-band` **(v1.83)**
**Build:** each side says which band it shows under the top bar, blue or striped, and the owner picks it on *Mipangilio ya duka*, under the side's switch; the top bar draws it wherever it names a side.
- `Side.band` (02 §4): `blue` | `striped`, default `blue`, with a check constraint (`side.band_unknown`). A field of the side, not a setting (§20, item 19): a setting is dated and kept forever (ADR-0013), and its kinds have no choice of looks, but a colour has no "from 1 December" and changes nobody's pay. The side already travels to every laptop (SYNC-2) and in `me`, so the band rides with it, as `sale_flow` and `is_live` do. `NewSide` and `add_side` take it, as they take `sale_flow`; the demo's seed makes Dawa blue and Vyakula striped, like the Foods bags (UI-8).
- `set_side_band(side, *, version, band, by)` (`apps/shops/services.py`), as `rename_side` and `switch_side`: one change, its version checked (`version_conflict`), `record_change(side)`, so each laptop learns it at its next pull. `PATCH sides/{id}/` takes `band` as a third change, one at a time, under `settings.manage` on that side.
- The band travels on `SideSerializer` (the office), `SideRecord` (the laptop's copy), `MeSideSerializer` (the device's `me`, and `home_side` in `GET /api/auth/me/`) and `MeSideKeysSerializer` (each side in `me`'s permissions).
- The top bar draws it wherever it names a side: before a login, the device's home side from its saved copy (`mpc.device`: `app-frame.tsx`, `device-lock.tsx`); at the counter and the PIN login, the person's side from the laptop's copy (`sideOfStaffRow`); in the office, `sideAt(me)`.
- On *Mipangilio ya duka*, under each side's switch, *Rangi*: the side's band beside its word (*Bluu*, *Mistari*), and *Badilisha*, which opens two `Choice` rows, each showing the band itself beside its word, with *Hifadhi* and *Ghairi*. *Hifadhi* sends the band with the side's version read as the form opened. Only for someone holding `settings.manage` on that side; everyone else sees the band.
**Choices by Jack (16 Nov 2026):** on the demo a person's band change is refused (`demo.sides_fixed`, `_check_sides_fixed_on_demo`), as a rename is: one visitor's colour would greet every visitor after them until the morning's seed. The page changes a band with *Badilisha* and *Hifadhi*, as it changes a rule, not with one tap: the page works one way everywhere, and a thumb that brushes a row while scrolling changes nothing.
**Watch out:** a saved copy (`mpc.device`) from before this card has no band: `isThisDevice` must still take it, or the laptop forgets it is registered until its next check, so the band is optional there and the next check fills it. A side row pulled before this card has no band until the side changes again; no real shop's laptop holds one (none is live before LIVE-1), and the demo's laptops start a new shop each morning, so no new Dexie version forces a fresh copy: such a row shows no band, never a crash. Colour only, no words (UI-8): the band stays `aria-hidden`, and the settings choice names each look in words. `Side` carries the edit history (`RecordEditsV2`), so the new column is recorded with the rest. `side.band_unknown` gets its two sentences and its line in `errors.test.ts`. The "no band yet" comments in `app-frame.tsx` and `top-bar.tsx` change with the code, and §20 item 19 is settled in the card's last commit.
**Built** **(v1.84)**: as planned, in four commits after the card's. `Side.band` and its rule (`shops_side_band_known`) came in migration 0009, which makes every side already stored blue; the schema names its choices `SideBandEnum` once, for the office's side, the laptop's copy and `me`. `set_side_band` follows `switch_side`, and a person's band is refused on the demo through `_check_sides_fixed_on_demo`, whose words now name the band too. The side route's edit takes a name, the switch or a band, exactly one (`SIDE_CHANGES`, `SideEditSerializer`). On the device, a saved copy with no band is still this device, and since the review so is one with a band the bar can't draw, which drops the band (`withABandItCanDraw`) **(v1.85)**; every bar that names a side hands in its band: the device's home side before a login and on the lock, the person's side from `me` in the office and at the PIN login, and from the laptop's copy at the counter and offline. On the page, *Rangi* is a part of its own (`side-band-choice.tsx`) in each side's box, under the switch; `sides.ts` keeps the store's place for the sides and puts a change's answer there, for the switch and the band alike. *Badilisha* opens two `Choice` rows with the keyboard on the side's own band; `Choice` gained a row's `picture`, drawn before its words and hidden from a screen reader, which shows each band as a swatch. *Hifadhi* sends the band with the version read as the form opened, and sends nothing for the band the side already has; the form closes either way, and a refusal shows in the side's box, as the switch's does. Jack checked the Swahili: *Rangi*, *Bluu*, *Mistari*, *Rangi ya {side} imehifadhiwa.* and `side.band_unknown`'s sentence.
**From the review** **(v1.85)**: nothing broke a rule, and Jack chose to fix every finding, each in its own commit. *The device's copy:* a band the bar can't draw, as a later app's new look saved by today's app, made a registered laptop read as a new one; now the band goes and the device stays. *The bar:* after a save, the page asks `me` and the device's check again, as the office's bar reads the band from `me` and the bar before a login from the saved copy; it stayed blue under "saved" until the next page. *One change at a time:* in a side's box the switch hides while the band's form is open, and *Badilisha* while the switch asks, as each sends the version read as it opened, and a switch confirmed between would have left the owner a conflict with himself. *The HTML:* `SideBand` draws a `<span>` shown as a block, as the page puts a band in a line of text and in a `Choice`'s `<label>`, where a `<div>` broke the HTML and React warned; `/design` shows the choice samples with their bands. *Tests:* the form's version, proven red by sending the newest one; the bar from an old side copy at the counter, and from `me` at the PIN login. *Comments:* `/design`, the top bar's tests and two settings comments no longer call the band undecided; the frontend rules name a `Choice` row's `picture`; the migration's header says why it depends on `people`. Left as it is: `side.band_unknown` never reaches a screen, as `invalid_choice` answers first, the same as `side.sale_flow_unknown`.
**Done when:**

- [x] The owner picks a side's band on *Mipangilio ya duka*, with *Badilisha* and *Hifadhi*; a laptop shows it after its next pull and check.
- [x] The top bar shows the band before a login, at the counter, at the PIN login and in the office; a laptop with an old saved copy still knows it is registered.
- [x] Only someone with `settings.manage` on that side changes it; it lands one change line; on the demo it is refused.
