### ATT-2 · Attendance rules and the switch
`M` · Claude-led · after SHOP-3 · branch `feat/att-2-attendance-rules` **(v1.86)**
**Build:** the rules that say what a worker's day was, as dated settings in the registry (`apps/core/settings_registry.py`), so they show on *Mipangilio ya duka* by themselves (SHOP-5) and travel to every laptop (SYNC-2); and the `attendance.enabled` switch (ADR-0033).
- Six new times on the shop's clock, beside `attendance.on_time_until` (07:35): `attendance.second_fine_from` (08:00), `attendance.review_from` (08:30), `attendance.afternoon_from` (12:00), `attendance.midday` (13:00), `attendance.morning_leave_until` (13:30) and `attendance.closing` (18:40).
- `attendance.second_fine`, money, beside `attendance.late_fine`, which is the first fine. Both start at 0.
- `attendance.enabled`, yes or no, off until a shop switches it on.
- All of the whole shop (`Scope.SHOP`), as staff work for the shop (02 §5).
- A time is saved only if it keeps the day's order, each after the one before it, on its start day and on each later day another of the seven changes (`setting.out_of_order`).
- On the counter, *Mahudhurio* shows only while the switch is on.
**Choices by Jack (16 Nov 2026):** the owner's rules (OWN-7, #55), as 02 §12 writes them. Arriving: by 07:35, on time; after it and before 08:00, late with the first fine; from 08:00 and before 08:30, late with the second fine; from 08:30 and before 12:00, *dharura*, a day a manager or the owner decides; from 12:00 to 13:00, the afternoon's half day, staying until 18:40; after 13:00, *dharura*. Leaving, after a morning arrival: before 13:00, *dharura*; from 13:00 to 13:30, the morning's half day; after 13:30 and before 18:40, *dharura*; from 18:40, the full day; no clock-out, *dharura*. Sunday is always off and not paid: Sunday work is a deal between the owner and the worker, outside the system. A public holiday is closed and not paid, like Sunday. Holidays are a card of their own, ATT-8, as they are a list of dates with a table, a cancel and a part of the page. In the public repo the fines have no real amount (rule 10): both start at 0, so a shop has no fine until its owner types one; the demo seed types made-up ones (500 and 1,500), and the owner types the real ones on the real server. So E10's example takes the demo's 500 (spec §8.1, 02 E10).
**Watch out:** each time is a minute on the shop's clock, as the counter shows it (`07:35`, SYNC-11): a clock-in at 07:35:40 shows 07:35 and is on time. "By" and "from" take their own minute, "before" and "after" don't. Each key's description says its window, as ATT-4 reads it. The order is strict, 07:35 < 08:00 < 08:30 < 12:00 < 13:00 < 13:30 < 18:40, so no minute lands in two windows. `set_setting` checks it after the new row is saved, inside its block, so a refusal rolls the row back: it reads the seven through `get_setting` on the row's start day and on each later start day of a row of the seven, and a refusal names the rule it clashes with and the day. Two changes at once could each pass alone: decide in the step whether to lock the shop's row (`no_key=True`, as the change numbers take only a share lock). `LATE_FINE`'s default goes from an amount to 0: the registry's header forbids stricter limits once a key has rows, and a default is not a limit; no real shop has rows yet. The tests that read the old amount as the default change with it (`test_get_setting.py`, `test_bootstrap.py`, `frontend/src/test/settings.ts`, `e2e/shop-settings.spec.ts`). The server takes clock-ins whatever the switch says: an event is a fact, and a laptop that hadn't pulled the switch yet would see its taps refused (SYNC-11). The counter reads the switch with `ruleInForce` (`src/offline/rules.ts`) on the laptop's own day (`src/offline/clock-today.ts`): the welcome hides *Mahudhurio* (`at-the-device.tsx`), and `/attendance/`, opened by its address, says attendance hasn't started and shows no button. ATT-4 counts days only from the day it is on. The demo seed switches it on from its own day, through `set_setting` as the system, so the demo and `e2e/clock-in-offline.spec.ts` keep their button; a visitor can switch it off from tomorrow, which the night's seed undoes. Each new key gets its area in `RULE_AREA` (`setting-words.ts`), and its `name`, `help` and `shown` in both books; `setting.out_of_order` gets its two sentences and its line in `errors.test.ts`. `pnpm gen:api`, as the keys are `SettingKeyEnum`. The screen word for a day that needs review is ATT-5's choice (*dharura* is the owner's word).
**Built** **(v1.87)**: in the plan Jack approved, in five commits after the card's. One change of order from the card: the demo's rules came before the fines' default, so the demo never showed a fine of 0 and its browser test changed once. The registry lists the attendance day first, its switch (`ATTENDANCE_ON`), then the seven times and two fines in the day's order, each description saying its window; the page shows them in that order by itself. `LATE_FINE`'s default is 0. The demo seed sets `DEMO_RULES` (the switch on, the fines 500 and 1,500) through `set_setting` as the system, from the shop's today, so its change log starts with ten lines. The order is `DAY_IN_ORDER`, in the registry. `set_setting` checks it last, inside its block, before the row is saved, after taking the shop's row with a light lock (`select_for_update(no_key=True)`), so two changes at once wait for each other: it reads the seven through `get_setting` on the row's day and on each later start day of a row of the seven, up to the rule's own next row. Its code goes beside the value's box with no values, as a field's code carries none to the screen (`apiFieldMessages`): one plain sentence, as the page lists every time and those to come. On the laptop, `useAttendanceOn` (`src/offline/attendance-on.ts`) reads the switch through `useRule`, so on the rules' day, the server's clock as the laptop knows it (`reference.ts`), not the taps' day (`clock-today.ts`): every rule turns the same way. `useRule` takes the rule as the app carries it (`ATTENDANCE_SWITCH`, off), for a laptop filled before this release, which pulls the switch's rows but never got the rule (SYNC-13's note). The welcome shows *Mahudhurio* only once it reads on; the screen shows no button and no status line until it has read the switch, and while it is off says so, with *Rudi*. Jack checked the Swahili: the ten rules' names and help, *Faini ya kwanza ya kuchelewa* for the first fine, `setting.out_of_order`'s sentence, and *Mahudhurio bado hayajaanza kwenye duka hili*. Proven red: the order check, and the welcome's check of the switch, each taken out.
**From the review** **(v1.88)**: nothing broke a rule, and Jack chose to fix every finding, each in its own commit. *The fine:* no new comment, nor the card's own text, names an amount as the fine's old default (rule 10). *The order:* a test proves the lock, two managers changing times at once, the second refused once the first commits; another proves the check reads only its own shop's rows when a system job opens every shop; and the check compares the changed time with its two neighbours only, so a pair broken elsewhere, which only code that skips `set_setting` could write, refuses no unrelated change and blames no wrong one. *The laptop:* `useRule` reads again each minute, so a rule that starts today comes on at midnight on a laptop left open offline, where nothing else would wake it; the app's copy of the switch takes only a key the schema lists, and a backend test (`test_carried_rules.py`) holds its kind, scope and default to the server's, reading the app's file. *The demo:* a deploy kept the demo the old seed made that morning, with attendance off and no fine until the next day; the seed now keeps today's demo only if it holds every rule `DEMO_RULES` sets. *The screen:* switched off, *Mahudhurio* still shows the refused taps and *Tuma sasa*, and stays on a tap just saved if a pull switches it off a moment after; its sentence says attendance isn't switched on, *Mahudhurio hayajawashwa kwenye duka hili. Mwenye duka huyawasha kwenye Mipangilio ya duka.*, not that it hasn't started; `setting.out_of_order`'s sentence says *kuanzia siku uliyochagua*, not *leo*, as a value from next week is checked from next week. Jack checked both. *Docs:* LIVE-1 now switches attendance on from the 1st and has the owner type both fines; 03 §7.1 says how *Mahudhurio* follows the switch (03 v1.66). *Comments:* the lock is held until the commit, and lets the other saves' key-share locks through; a test header no longer names the old fine.
**Done when:**
- [x] The eight new rules show on *Mipangilio ya duka* under *Mahudhurio*, with the owner's times, in Swahili and English; Jack has checked the Swahili.
- [x] A shop with no rows has no fines; the demo shows its made-up fines.
- [x] A time out of the day's order is refused, with its sentence beside the box, whether it clashes on its start day or on a later day another time changes.
- [x] With attendance off, the counter shows no *Mahudhurio*; from the day it is switched on, it does; on the demo it is on.
**Tests:** `test_attendance_rules_have_the_owners_times` · `test_fines_start_at_nothing` · `test_a_time_out_of_order_refused` · `test_a_time_out_of_order_on_a_later_day_refused` · `test_a_time_in_order_saved` · `test_other_rules_skip_the_order_check` · `test_seed_demo_sets_its_fines_and_switches_attendance_on`; `at-the-device.test.tsx` (no *Mahudhurio* while off, and from the day it is on), `attendance.test.tsx` (the page says attendance hasn't started), `setting-words.test.ts` (every key, both books), `errors.test.ts`. Built too **(v1.87)**: in `test_day_in_order.py`, `test_a_later_row_of_the_same_rule_ends_the_check`; in `test_api_settings.py`, `test_create_setting_out_of_order_answers_beside_the_value`; `test_seed_demo_numbers_every_record_once` counts ten lines; `attendance-on.test.tsx` (off with no row, on from a row's day on a laptop that never learned the rule, the laptop's own copy of the rule first, a damaged row off); in `attendance.test.tsx`, *Ingia kazini* from the day it is on; `e2e/shop-settings.spec.ts` reads the demo's 500, set by the system. From the review **(v1.88)**: in `test_day_in_order.py`, `test_two_time_changes_at_once_keep_the_days_order` · `test_another_shops_rows_never_cut_the_check_short` · `test_a_broken_pair_elsewhere_refuses_no_other_time`; `test_seed_demo_makes_todays_shop_again_without_its_rules`; `test_the_apps_attendance_switch_is_the_servers`; in `attendance-on.test.tsx`, the switch coming on at midnight with nothing pulled; in `attendance.test.tsx`, the refused taps and *Tuma sasa* while it is off, and a saved tap kept as a pull switches it off. Each proven red
**Commits:** `docs: the att-2 card` → `feat(core): the attendance day's times, the second fine and the switch` → `feat(demo): the demo shop sets its fines and switches attendance on` → `feat(core): the fines start at nothing until a shop sets them` → `feat(shops): the day's times keep their order` → `feat(frontend): Mahudhurio waits for attendance to be switched on` → `docs(plan): att-2 says what it built` **(v1.87)**, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the att-2 card follows the review` **(v1.88)**

### ATT-8 · Closed days: the server
`M` · Claude-led · after ATT-2 · branch `feat/att-8-closed-days` **(v1.89)**
**Build:** the days the whole shop is closed, a public holiday or a family funeral, and the rules for adding, cancelling and moving them, in `apps/shops`. Nothing shows on a screen yet: ATT-9 is the page.
- `Closure` (02 §12): `first_day`, `last_day`, `name`, and `status`, active or cancelled. One day is a closure whose first and last day are the same. Each day of an active closure is off, and not paid, like Sunday (OWN-7). Who added it, and when, is the row's `created_by` and `created_at`.
- The database refuses a last day before the first (`closure.ends_before_it_starts`), a blank name (`closure.name_blank`), and two active closures of one shop that share a day (`closure.overlaps`).
- `add_closure`: from the shop's today on (`closure.date_past`), so a funeral can close the shop the same morning.
- `cancel_closure`, the page's *Ondoa*: with a reason (`closure.reason_required`), up to and on its first day (`closure.started`). The row stays, cancelled, and an audit line keeps who, when and why (rule 7).
- `change_last_day`: earlier or later, with a reason, never changing a day before today (`closure.date_past`) nor sharing a day with another closure (`closure.overlaps`). An audit line keeps the old and the new last day.
- `closed_days(shop, first, last)`, a selector: each day of an active closure between the two, for ATT-4.
- Routes under `/api/v1/shops/closures/`: the list, each active closure whose last day is today or later, soonest first, with the shop's `today`, for anyone who sees a side; adding one, `{id}/cancel/` and `{id}/last-day/`, each under `settings.manage` on the whole shop.
- The demo seed adds the coming fixed-date public holidays, so ATT-9's page has rows.
**Choices by Jack (16 Nov 2026):** one closure covers a span of days, from a first day to a last, as the shop closes for a family funeral as well as for a public holiday (the row had one date per holiday); so 02's `Holiday` becomes `Closure`, and the page's section is *Siku duka limefungwa*. The last day can move, earlier or later: the shop closes 20–24 November for a funeral, the family finishes on the 22nd, and the owner opens again on the 23rd. Every closed day is off and not paid, whatever the reason, like Sundays and public holidays. The card is split, as the server and the page together are twice a card: ATT-8 is the server, ATT-9 the page, right after it. Jack's picture of the page is ATT-9's row.
**Watch out:** a day is the shop's day (`shop_today`), not the server's, which runs in UTC. No change touches a day before today, so a day ATT-4 has worked out never changes under it: a closure is added from today on; cancelled only while its first day is today or later; and its last day moved only where every day that changes is today or later: moved earlier, the new last day is yesterday or later (yesterday reopens the shop from today); moved later, the old last day is yesterday or later, so a closure that ended before yesterday never grows back over past days. Moving the last day asks a reason too (Claude's default), as it changes who is paid; a reason is trimmed, and spaces alone are none. The overlap check runs in the service first, for its code, after taking the shop's row with a light lock (`select_for_update(no_key=True)`, as `_check_day_in_order`), so two adds at once take turns; the database's exclusion rule (`ExclusionConstraint` over the shop and the closure's `daterange`, active rows only) is the last guard. It needs PostgreSQL's `btree_gist` extension (`BtreeGistExtension` in the migration) to compare the shop's id in the same rule: the official image carries it and it is trusted since PostgreSQL 13, so the demo's database should take it; a red *Deploy demo* would say otherwise. Cancelling and moving lock the closure's row (`no_key=True`), so two managers take turns and the second reads the first one's change (`closure.not_active` on a cancelled one). The audit trail gets two actions, `closure.cancel` and `closure.last_day`, in a `control` migration, as its rule lists every action; `before` and `after` hold the status, or the last day. The edit history (ADR-0036): `RecordEditsV2` in the migration and the type code `closure` in `apps/core/links.py`; a change saves under `acting_as(by)`, as `people`'s `_save`, so the history names who. Not on the laptops: no `record_change`, and a plain `transaction.atomic()`, as the day calculation is the server's (the row's default). On the demo, a person's add, cancel or move touches only days from tomorrow on (`demo.closures_from_tomorrow`), as a rule's value does (`demo.rules_from_tomorrow`), so today's demo stays as the seed made it for every visitor; the system, as the seed acts, still may. The seed's holidays come from a short list of Tanzania's fixed-date public holidays in `apps/demo` (Eid and Easter move, so none of those), Christmas and Boxing Day as one closure of two days; the seed keeps today's demo only if it holds them, as it does for `DEMO_RULES`. A Sunday inside a closure needs nothing: it is off either way. A clock-in on a closed day is ATT-4's to decide. The tenancy sweep gets a closure of each shop (`backend/tests/conftest.py`). Each new code gets its two sentences and its line in `errors.test.ts`, and Jack checks the Swahili; `pnpm gen:api` after the routes.
**Built** **(v1.90)**: in the plan Jack approved, in five commits after the card's, with three changes found on the way. *The check of a closure:* Django asks the overlap rule even of a closure whose last day is before its first, and PostgreSQL can't build that range, which Django then reports as a clash too (`closure.overlaps`): so `Closure.clean()` files `closure.ends_before_it_starts` under `last_day` and `closure.name_blank` under `name`, and `full_clean` skips the rules that read a field already refused; `name` is `blank=True`, so Django's own `blank` never answers before our code. The overlap's code comes from that same `full_clean`, asked after the shop's row is locked, so the service needs no read of its own. *Moving a last day* takes the shop's row first, then the closure's, as an add takes the shop's: a closure added at the same moment waits, and two changes never wait for each other in a circle; the same last day writes nothing. A cancel of one already begun is `closure.started`, and on the demo a person's cancel of one starting today is refused too. *The list* reads through `coming_closures` (`apps/shops/selectors.py`): each active closure whose last day is today or later, a funeral already begun among them. *The demo* closes on the next three of `PUBLIC_HOLIDAYS` (`apps/demo/services.py`), from the seed's own today, and keeps today's demo only if it holds them, known by their first day and name, so a visitor's moved last day or cancel never makes it again; the test of a demo kept after midnight in Arusha now makes its shop at that moment, so it holds that day's holidays. Who may is pinned in `backend/tests/test_route_permissions.py`, beside every route's. Jack checked the Swahili of the nine codes. Proven red: the overlap rule, the shop's lock on an add, the closure's lock on a cancel, the sweep's closures, and the demo's check of its closures, each taken out.
**From the review** **(v1.91)**: nothing broke a rule, and Jack chose to fix every finding, each in its own commit. *A delete:* nothing links to a closure, so PROTECT never kept one, and the edit history watches updates only: a stray delete would have opened its days again, unseen. The table now carries a trigger that refuses a DELETE (`NoDeleteTrigger`, `apps/core/operations.py`, its SQL pinned as the ledgers' is), added to migration 0010, still on the branch; TRUNCATE, the demo's daily reset, passes. *A sentence:* `closure.date_past` also answers a last day moved back over a past day, so it now says *Siku iliyoshapita haiwezi kubadilishwa*, which reads right beside either box; *zimeshaanza* and *zimeshaondolewa* match it. Jack checked the Swahili. *Proofs:* a moved last day and an add at the same moment take turns on the shop's row, proven red; a suspended or gone person gets nothing from the closures routes; a key on one side changes no closure. *A comment:* the shop's lock lets through only saves of rows that link to the shop. Left as it is: the model's `blank=True` landed in the routes commit, and `main` keeps one squash commit.
**Done when:**
- [x] A closure from today on is saved; one before today, one ending before it starts, and one sharing a day with another are refused, each with its code.
- [x] A closure not yet started is cancelled with a reason and an audit line; a started one is refused.
- [x] A last day moves earlier or later with a reason and an audit line, and never changes a day before today.
- [x] `closed_days` gives each day of the active closures in a span, and none of a cancelled one.
- [x] Each route answers as above; each change is refused to someone without `settings.manage`; the sweep sees no other shop's closure.
- [x] The demo has the coming public holidays; Jack has checked the Swahili of the new codes.

### ATT-9 · Closed days: the page
`M` · Claude-led · after ATT-8 · branch `feat/att-9-closed-days-page` **(v1.92)**
**Build:** *Siku duka limefungwa*, a section of *Mipangilio ya duka* (`/settings/`), under the rules and above *Pande*: an office screen, through the API client only (ADR-0020), on ATT-8's routes. Nothing changes on the server.
- The list, from `GET /api/v1/shops/closures/`: each closure from today on, soonest first, a funeral already begun among them, with its days (`20/11/2026 – 24/11/2026`, or one date, `09/12/2026`) and its name. With none coming, one line says so.
- *Ongeza* opens *Kuanzia*, *Hadi* and *Jina*, with *Hifadhi* and *Ghairi*.
- Beside each closure, *Ondoa* with *Sababu*, while its first day is today or later; and *Badilisha siku ya mwisho*, with the new last day and *Sababu*.
- The buttons are only for someone with `settings.manage` on the whole shop; everyone else sees the list without them.
- Each refusal shows its code's sentence beside its box; one that no box owns shows above the buttons.
- A cancel sent again, whose first answer was lost, is refused as `closure.not_active`, as the first one already cancelled it: the page reads that as done, says so, and reads the list again (ATT-8's review).
**Choices by Jack (16 Nov 2026):** the page as he pictured it at ATT-8, in this card's row: the section's place, its list, the three buttons with their boxes, and the workers seeing the list without the buttons.
**Watch out:** the list answers `{today, closures}`, one answer, not pages; each closure has `id`, `first_day`, `last_day`, `name`, `status`, `added_by` and `created_at`. Days are written with `formatDay` (`src/lib/format.ts`). The closures get their own place in the store of server answers, apart from the rules, so a failed read shows in their section and a change asks only them again. The key is read on the whole shop (`me.permissions.whole_shop`), not on a side: a closure closes both sides, and its routes ask `settings.manage` there. An add is a POST with the client's id (ADR-0007): the same id for a try of the same days and name, a new one once any of them changed, as `setting-form.tsx` does. *Hadi* left empty is one day: the page sends *Kuanzia*'s day as the last *(default)*. *Kuanzia* starts empty, so a quick *Hifadhi* never closes today by accident; left empty, it is `required` *(default)*. Each date box offers nothing before the shop's `today`; the new last day's box starts at the closure's last day, and offers nothing before the later of its first day and yesterday, as yesterday reopens the shop from today (ATT-8). A last day moved before today ends the closure, so it leaves the list. The cancel and the last day carry no id: a cancel sent twice is `closure.not_active` (above); a last day sent twice finds the same day, which writes nothing, and answers the row. Where each code shows: an add's `closure.date_past` and `demo.closures_from_tomorrow` beside *Kuanzia*, `closure.ends_before_it_starts` beside *Hadi*, `closure.name_blank` beside *Jina*; `closure.reason_required` beside *Sababu*; a last day's `closure.date_past`, `closure.ends_before_it_starts` and `demo.closures_from_tomorrow` beside its box; above the buttons, `closure.overlaps` (the database's rule, of no one field), `closure.started`, a cancel's `demo.closures_from_tomorrow`, and a last day's `closure.not_active`, as someone else cancelled it, so the list is read again. Every code already has its two sentences, checked by Jack at ATT-8. *Hifadhi* and *Ondoa* are off while sending and while offline. On the demo, a person's change touches only days from tomorrow on, so the browser test uses days no seed holiday has, far enough ahead that a run crossing midnight in Dar es Salaam can't make them today (SHOP-5's review). Every new word goes in both books; Jack checks the Swahili.
**Built** **(v1.93)**: in the plan Jack approved, in five commits after the card's, with what it found on the way. *The order:* the list came first with no button, and each button came with its own form, so no commit showed a button that did nothing. *The page:* `features/settings/closures-section.tsx` holds the section, `closure-form.tsx` *Ongeza*, `closure-cancel.tsx` *Ondoa* and `closure-last-day.tsx` *Badilisha siku ya mwisho*; `closures.ts` keeps the list's place in the store of server answers (`CLOSURES`), a closure's days as the page writes them (`useDaysOf`) and the day before a day (`dayBefore`). One form at a time on a line: its buttons step aside while one is open, and *Ghairi* gives the keyboard back to the button that opened it and reads the list again, as a send whose answer was lost may have changed it. *What is said:* the section says each change once, in one status line at its top, not on the line, as a closure can leave the list and its line with it: one removed, one whose last day moved to yesterday (the shop opens today, and the closure is over), and one someone else cancelled while a move was on its way, said in `closure.not_active`'s sentence as the list is read again. When a line leaves, the keyboard goes to the section's heading. *Ondoa* asks which closure first, *Ondoa Krismasi, 25/12/2026 – 26/12/2026? Duka litakuwa wazi siku hizo.*, and the new last day's box says the last day now (*Sasa ni 24/11/2026*). Each form says at once what it can see, in the server's own sentences, and sends nothing: an empty *Kuanzia* (`required`), a past day, a last day before the first, an empty name or reason, and a moved last day before yesterday. Jack checked the Swahili, step by step. Proven red: the key on the whole shop, *Hadi* left empty as one day, *Ondoa* only until the first day, a repeated cancel read as done, nothing before yesterday, and a closure that ends before today leaving the list, each taken out.
**From the review** **(v1.94)**: nothing broke a rule, and Jack chose to fix every finding, each in its own commit, the last two in one, as they work as one. *An unchanged last day:* the server writes nothing for it and keeps no reason, yet the page said the last day changed; the form now sends nothing, and says beside the box *Hii tayari ndiyo siku ya mwisho. Chagua siku nyingine.* *A clash:* a closure saved whose answer was lost made a changed try, under a new id, clash with a closure the page didn't show; on `closure.overlaps` the add and the move read the list again, so the closure it clashes with shows. *At once:* each change puts the server's answer into the page's copy of the list (`keepClosure`, `dropClosure`, `closures.ts`) before reading it again, so a cancelled closure never comes back with its buttons, even when that read fails. *Both refusals:* the server names a problem with the whole request as the refusal's code, beside the fields' codes; the forms showed only the boxes' then, so a demo's add from today that also clashed said the clash on the next try; `aboutTheWholeRequest` shows both. The settings form keeps the same habit, as its whole-request codes never come through `set_setting` today. *Already removed:* a cancel answered `closure.not_active` is said in that code's own sentence, true whoever cancelled it, not as this owner's own, whose reason may not be the one kept. *A page left open:* each form reads the list again as it opens, so today is the shop's today; a closure that leaves the list while its form is open is said, *Siku ya Uhuru: siku hizi hazipo tena kwenye orodha.*, and the keyboard goes to the heading. The section keeps which form is open, and closes it after its own change with `flushSync`, before the list's change reaches the page after TanStack's `setTimeout`, so it never reads its own change as someone else's. *Comments:* the demo's yesterday on the last day's box, the seed's holidays within six months, a header rewrapped. *Browser tests:* the date helpers live in `e2e/the-shop.ts`. Jack checked the two new sentences. Left as it is: the list commit's subject, as `main` keeps one squash commit. Proven red: each fix taken out.
**Done when:**
- [x] The owner opens *Mipangilio ya duka* and sees the coming closures, soonest first, a span as two dates and one day as one; a worker sees the same list with no buttons.
- [x] The owner adds *Msiba wa bibi* from 20/11 to 24/11, and it shows in its place; a refused add shows its sentence beside its box, and an overlap above the buttons.
- [x] The owner removes a closure not yet begun, with a reason; a begun one has no *Ondoa*; a cancel sent twice reads as done.
- [x] The owner moves a last day earlier or later, with a reason; a refusal shows its sentence beside its box.
- [x] Jack has checked the Swahili; the closures show on the live demo.
**Tests:** `closures-section.test.tsx` · `closure-form.test.tsx` · `closure-cancel.test.tsx` · `closure-last-day.test.tsx` · `e2e/closed-days.spec.ts`. Built too **(v1.93)**: `closures.test.ts` (the day before a day, across a month, a year and a leap day), and in `settings-screen.test.tsx`, the section's place between the rules and *Pande*. From the review **(v1.94)**: in `closure-last-day.test.tsx`, the last day it already has said and nothing sent, and a clash with a box's refusal both at once; in `closure-form.test.tsx`, the same both at once; in `closures.test.ts`, `keepClosure` and `dropClosure`; in `closures-section.test.tsx`, a clash reads the list again (an add and a move), a removed or moved closure changes on the page when the read after fails, an already-removed closure said in the server's words, a form that opens reads today again, and a closure gone while its form is open
**Commits:** `docs: the att-9 card` → `feat(frontend): the shop's closed days on the settings page` → `feat(frontend): close the shop from a first day to a last` → `feat(frontend): remove a closure with a reason` → `feat(frontend): move a closure's last day` → `test(e2e): the owner closes the shop for a funeral` → `docs(plan): att-9 says what it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the att-9 card follows the review` **(v1.94)**

### CTRL-3 · Attachments: the server
`M` · Claude-led · after SYNC-8 · branch `feat/ctrl-3-attachments-server` **(v1.95)**
**Build:** the one place the shop's photos are kept on the server, and its two doors: a laptop puts a photo's file in, and an office screen takes it out for someone allowed to see it (03 §5.13). No screen and no record carries a photo yet: ATT-3 (the clock-in photo), PPL-4 (the worker's photo) and STK-4 (the supplier's paper) do.
- Files are kept through Django's storage API: on the server, in a folder outside the code and the static files (`FILES_ROOT`), which OPS-5 mounts and OPS-8 backs up with the database; on the demo, in a table of its own *(default)*, as Render's free plan loses its disk at every restart and every deploy, and the nightly wipe takes them with the rest. That table keeps each file's shop, so the tenancy guard holds there too. No address serves the files: no `MEDIA_URL`, nothing in Caddy or WhiteNoise.
- `control.Attachment` (02 §13): its id, made where the photo is taken (ADR-0007); its `kind`; its subject, a type code and a UUID (`apps/core/links.py`), as a flag's; the file's `content_type`, `size` and `sha256`, as the record that brought it declared them; `device` and `created_by`; the file, empty until it arrives, and `received_at`; and who removed the file, when and why. The file only moves forward, waiting → kept → removed, and the row is never deleted (rule 7).
- The kinds, one list in `control`, each naming its subject's table and the key that opens it: `clock_photo`, on an attendance event, opened with `attendance.manage` on the whole shop; `staff_photo`, on a staff member, opened by anyone working at the shop, as it shows on the PIN tiles anyway *(defaults: ATT-3 and PPL-4 may change them before they first use them)*. A kind whose table doesn't exist yet, the supplier's paper or a receipt, comes with that table's card.
- `expect_attachment(shop, *, id, kind, subject, content_type, size, sha256, by, device=None)` writes the row, waiting for its file. A record's saving code calls it in its own transaction (ATT-3's clock-in), so the row arrives with the record that names it.
- `PUT /api/sync/v1/attachments/{id}/` (device key, `DeviceView`): the file itself, as the request's body. Safe to repeat: the same file again, or a file already removed, answers 204, so the laptop drops its copy. Refused: an id the shop doesn't hold, 404 `attachment.not_found`, saying nothing of other shops, which a laptop also gets when it sends a file before its record; a file over 1 MB *(default)*, `attachment.too_big`; a file that isn't a JPEG or a WebP by its first bytes, whatever it is called, `attachment.not_an_image`; and a size or a SHA-256 other than the record's, `attachment.file_differs`.
- `GET /api/v1/control/attachments/{id}/file/` (staff session): the file, for someone holding its kind's key, with its type, `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. A file not yet arrived answers `attachment.file_waiting`; a removed one, `attachment.file_removed`.
- The hook for retention rules: `remove_attachment_file(attachment, *, reason, by=None)` removes the file and keeps the row, with who, when and why. ATT-7's nightly job calls it for clock-in photos 90 days after their pay run is approved (03 §5.13).
**Choices by Jack (17 Nov 2026):** the row held two cards' work, so it splits as ATT-8 and ATT-9 did: this card is the server's half, and CTRL-6 the laptop's, a photo waiting on the laptop and sent later.
**Watch out:** a file's name in the storage is `<shop id>/<attachment id>`, never a person's name or the laptop's file name: a path is no place for personal data (03 §10). Save the file first and the row after it: a rollback then leaves at most a file no row names, which a retry writes over, never a row naming a file that isn't there. A removal goes the other way: the row first, the file after the commit (`transaction.on_commit`). Two PUTs of one id at once (two tabs, or a tab and the service worker) take turns on the row's lock, and the second answers 204. Read the body's length (`Content-Length`) before the body, so a 50 MB upload is refused unread. The upload is the device's: the person who took the photo is on the row already. Any device of the shop may send the file, as a rescue file (SYNC-12) can carry a record to another laptop; the SHA-256 makes sure it is the same file. The PUT has no GET, so it goes on `DEVICE_ROUTES_WITHOUT_A_LOOK` with its own leak test; the GET's table gets a row for the sweep's two shops. The demo's file table is written once and never edited: it goes on `NO_EDIT_HISTORY`, by this card's decision. PPL-4's office upload shrinks the photo in the browser first, as 03 §5.13 says, since a phone's photo is far over 1 MB. A laptop's way to read a photo (the faces on the PIN tiles) is PPL-4's. Each new code gets its two sentences and its line in `errors.test.ts`.
**Built** **(v1.96)**: in the plan Jack approved, with one commit more: the tenancy sweep couldn't call a device route whose address names a row, so step 4 split in two (Jack, 17 Nov 2026), and the sweep learned it first. *Where files live* (`apps/core/storage.py`): `FolderStorage` on the real server (`FILES_ROOT`, required there, with no default) and on Jack's laptop (`backend/files/`, gitignored), its files readable by the server's own user alone (0600, folders 0700); `DatabaseStorage` on the demo (`FILES_IN_DATABASE=yes`, `render.yaml` and `compose.e2e.yml`), a table of its own, `core.StoredFile`, with each file's shop, on `NO_EDIT_HISTORY`. Both check a name's shop against the one open, write a name sent again over the file, and refuse to make an address (`NoAddress`); the tests write to a folder of each run's own. If the demo starts before Render's Blueprint sync adds the new variable, it stops at `FILES_ROOT is not set` and the old version keeps running (`docs/runbooks/demo.md`). *The row* (`control.Attachment`, migration control 0009): eight database rules, the edit history (`RecordEditsV2`, type code `attachment`) and a delete refused (`NoDeleteTrigger`); the kinds and their keys in `ATTACHMENT_KINDS`, `LARGEST_FILE` 1 MiB, `AttachmentType` JPEG or WebP. `expect_attachment` takes no `shop`, unlike the card's line: the shop is the record's, as for `raise_flag`; it asks the type itself, so a wrong one is `attachment.not_an_image`, not Django's `invalid_choice`. A photo that never came may be removed too, so a late upload is dropped. *The laptop's door* (`AttachmentFileView`, `sync-attachment-file`): `DeviceView.model` names the table the address's id is in, and the sweep asks about each of shop A's rows as shop B's laptop and with no key, expecting the answer an id no shop has gets (`device_row_problems`); `receive_attachment_file` locks the row, checks the file (`too_big`, `not_an_image` by its first bytes, `file_differs` for its size, its SHA-256 or its type), saves the file, then the row; the arrival lands in the edit history with the laptop. *The office's door* (`AttachmentViewSet`, `/api/v1/control/attachments/{id}/file/`): the route names `SEES_THE_SIDE`, as a show action may name no key of its own yet (PPL-5), and the kind's key is asked inside the action on the whole shop, before the file's state, so someone without it learns only that they may not open it, not whether the file came nor, since the review, its kind **(v1.97)**; `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. *Removing a file*: `remove_attachment_file` marks the row, then deletes the file once the commit is kept (`transaction.on_commit`, the codebase's first, in the file's own shop), and tells PostgreSQL who acts, so the history names the remover outside a visit too (found while testing). Six codes have their two sentences; Jack checked the Swahili. Proven red: the shop check on names, a leaky upload door, a leaky office door, and a file deleted before the commit. Honest limits: no screen or record sends a photo yet (ATT-3, PPL-4, STK-4), so the live demo shows none; a file whose delete fails after the commit stays with no row naming it, for a later clean-up job; the label's facts are kept by the services and the edit history, with no trigger of their own as a flag's.
**From the review** **(v1.97)**: no rule broken and no leak found; Jack chose to fix every finding, each in its own commit, two of them in one, as they are one change. *The removal:* `on_commit` ran its delete as a plain step, so one failed delete skipped every later delete of the same transaction, after the rows were committed "removed", and a second run skipped them again: photos outlived the retention rule. Now the delete is `robust=True` (a failure is logged, the rest still run), and every removal deletes the photo's own fixed name, `<shop id>/<attachment id>`, a row already removed and a photo that never came included, so a second run clears what a failed delete, or an upload undone after its file was written, left. *The demo's file rows:* `DatabaseStorage.delete` deletes a `core.StoredFile` row when its photo is removed, the one place rows are deleted outside a wipe: the retention rule wants the photo gone, not hidden, as a file in the folder is, and the `Attachment` row keeps who removed it, when and why (rule 7), now written here and in 03 §5.13. *No length:* a request with no `Content-Length` (sent in pieces, as a proxy may) was read as an empty body and refused as no image, which CTRL-6 would set aside for good; now it answers 411 `attachment.length_required`, unread, and the laptop sends it again; Jack checked the Swahili. *The office's door:* a removal committed between the route's read and its open crashed with a 500; it now answers `attachment.file_removed`; and the refusal's message no longer names the photo's kind. *Tests:* the demo's storage proven from upload to removal (a `StoredFile` row checked, not only the bytes); a photo of exactly 1 MB kept and one byte more refused; the storages' shop test named for both (`test_a_file_keeps_to_its_shop`). *Else:* `apps/control/api/__init__.py` has its header; each test run deletes its files folder as it ends; two comments no longer say a request with no length reaches the service.
**Done when:**
- [x] A laptop's PUT keeps the file once; the same file sent again answers 204, and one copy is kept.
- [x] A file of another size or SHA-256, too big, or not an image is refused with its code, and nothing is kept.
- [x] An id of another shop answers as one that doesn't exist, through both routes.
- [x] The office route gives the file only to someone holding its kind's key; no address serves the files.
- [x] `remove_attachment_file` removes the file and keeps the row, with who, when and why; the database refuses a delete.
- [x] On the demo, a file survives a restart.
