**Commits:** `docs: the ctrl-3 and ctrl-6 cards` → `feat(core): files kept through Django's storage, in the database on the demo` → `feat(control): an attachment waits for its file` → `test(sweep): device routes that take a row's id` **(v1.96)** → `feat(sync): a laptop sends a photo's file` → `feat(control): the office opens a file with its key` → `feat(control): remove a file and keep its row` → `docs(plan): ctrl-3 says what it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, two in one, and `docs: the ctrl-3 card follows the review` **(v1.97)**

### CTRL-6 · Attachments: the laptop
`M` · Claude-led · after CTRL-3 · branch `feat/ctrl-6-attachments-laptop` **(v1.95)**
**Build:** a photo taken on a laptop waits there while there is no internet, and goes up later (03 §5.13). Nothing takes a photo yet: ATT-3's camera is the first, then STK-4's.
- A new store in the laptop's database, version 3: `attachment_files`, one row per photo, with its id, the record it travels with, its `kind`, `content_type`, `size` and `sha256`, the file itself (a `Blob`), its state (`waiting`, `set_aside`) and its tries.
- `saveDocument` takes a record's photos with it: the record, its photos and their rows are written in one transaction, so a record never names a photo the laptop lost, and no photo waits for a record that was never saved.
- After the push, `sendAttachments` sends each waiting photo whose record the server has accepted (in `sent_archive`) to CTRL-3's `PUT`, oldest first, one at a time. A 204 takes the photo off the laptop. A 404 waits for the next sync. A refusal (`attachment.file_differs`, `attachment.too_big`, `attachment.not_an_image`) sets that photo aside with its code, as the outbox does with a record, and the rest go on. A lost network or a server error stops the round, and the next sync tries again.
- A photo whose record was set aside (`needs_attention`) waits with it: the server holds no row for it.
- Wi-Fi first, where the browser can tell: when `navigator.connection` says the laptop is on mobile data, or asks to save data, the photos wait *(default)*. Where it can't tell, as Chrome on a Windows laptop can't, they go. This replaces 03's "otherwise a device setting" *(default)*: about twenty clock-in photos a day come to some 3 MB, too little for a setting; a shop that asks for one gets it as a card of its own. Jack chose this (17 Nov 2026): where the laptop can't tell, the photos go, with no device setting **(v1.98)**.
**Watch out:** work out the SHA-256 before the Dexie transaction opens: `crypto.subtle` is not Dexie's, and a Dexie transaction ends at the first promise of another kind. A photo goes only after its record was accepted, as the server writes its row with the record (CTRL-3). A new store is a new database version with an upgrade; versions 1 and 2 stay as they are. The tab's sync sends the photos, not the service worker's (SYNC-16) *(default)*: a few minutes' wait for a photo costs nothing. The rescue file (SYNC-12) carries records, not their photos *(default)*: a rescued clock-in arrives with no photo, and its row waits on the server. The tests' photos are a few bytes of a made-up JPEG, never a real photo. 03 §5.13 changes with the card's last commit.
**Watch out** **(v1.96)**: from CTRL-3. The PUT goes to `/api/sync/v1/attachments/{id}/`, the photo's id the one its record named, its body the file's bytes alone (no form), with `Content-Type: image/jpeg` or `image/webp`, the key's cookie and the CSRF token, as the push's. It answers 204 for a file kept, the same file again, or a photo already removed: each takes the photo off the laptop. 404 `attachment.not_found` is a record not yet arrived, so the photo waits. A refusal names no field: `attachment.too_big` (over 1 MB, 1,048,576 bytes, read from the request's length first), `attachment.not_an_image` (by its first bytes) and `attachment.file_differs` (its size, its SHA-256 or its type isn't the label's) each set the photo aside; their sentences are in both books. The label's SHA-256 is 64 lower-case hex digits; the record's saving code writes the row with `expect_attachment` (ATT-3). From CTRL-3's review **(v1.97)**: a PUT with no length is answered 411 `attachment.length_required`, unread: the photo waits and goes again, as after a lost network; never sent in pieces with no length, as a `Blob` body gets its length from the browser.
**Built** **(v1.98)**: in the plan Jack approved, three commits as the card says, with these differences. *The file:* kept as its bytes (an `ArrayBuffer`), not a `Blob`: the bytes are read anyway for the SHA-256, and the tests' pretend database (fake-indexeddb) hands a `Blob` back empty, so a test of one would prove nothing; a browser keeps both alike, and fetch gives the bytes their length as it gives a `Blob`. *Saving* (`src/offline/attachments.ts`, `outbox.ts`): a screen labels a photo first with `labelPhoto(file, kind)`: its type by its first bytes, never by `file.type`, as the server sniffs them; its size; its SHA-256; a new id. It refuses a file over 1 MB, or one that is no JPEG or WebP (`BrokenPhoto`), as the server would. The screen puts `photo.label` in its payload, and `saveDocument(db, draft, effects, photos)` writes the photos with the record; it refuses a photo the payload doesn't name (`not_named`), as the server could write no row for it. Each row keeps its record's `device_seq`, so photos go in their records' order, and a `wait_until`; database version 3 is `attachment_files: "id, document_id, [state+device_seq]"`, with no upgrade, and, since the review, `attachment_bytes: "id"`, the bytes in a drawer of their own **(v1.99)**. *Sending* (`src/offline/sync/attachments.ts`): at the end of the page's sync, after the pull (since the review, after the laptop is noted back too **(v1.99)**), so a week of photos never holds back a suspension coming down (X2), and only after a push the server answered: it had its CSRF token, its wait was over, and the records it just accepted are in `sent_archive`. A 404 of another code than `attachment.not_found` (a route the server lacks) stops the round, as a server error does. Two additions to the card, to spare the shop's bundles: each failed send makes that photo wait, 1 minute, then twice as long each time, at most an hour (a clock put back so far that more than an hour is left ends the wait **(v1.99)**), since a sync every 30 s would send a 150 KB photo the server keeps refusing 2,880 times a day; and at most 10 photos went in one sync, so a record saved meanwhile never waits behind a week of them; since the review, 1.5 MB (`MOST_BYTES`) **(v1.99)**. *Wi-Fi first* (`onCostlyLine`): `navigator.connection` saying `cellular`, or a data saver on (`saveData`), keeps the photos; it is asked before each photo, and a wait for Wi-Fi is no failed send. Proven red: a photo sent before its record was in, a photo sent after a failed push, and the Wi-Fi rule switched off. Honest limits: nothing takes a photo yet (ATT-3, STK-4), so the live demo shows none; a photo set aside stays on the laptop and shows on no screen, until a card adds both.
**From the review** **(v1.99)**: no rule broken and nothing the card asked for missing; Jack chose to fix every finding, each in its own commit. *The offline report:* the photos went before the laptop was noted back, so a cut ending at 10:00, with four minutes of photos after it, reached the office as ending at 10:04 (SYNC-14); now `syncOnce` notes the laptop back, then sends them, and a trouble of their own (a full disk as a photo is marked) is logged, never failing a sync whose records went and whose changes came down; a block is still thrown on. A photo's call that times out on a slow line is not noted as a cut: the line may be only slow. *Never found:* a photo whose record is in, and whose row the server still lacks, is sent every hour for good, some 3.6 MB a day; now its 54th try, after some two days of waits (`MOST_TRIES_NOT_FOUND`), sets it aside as `attachment.not_found`, leaving a replay after a restore the time to bring its record back (OPS-11). *The bytes:* each sync read every waiting photo whole to skip it, a photo beside a record set aside for good; the bytes moved to `attachment_bytes`, written and deleted with their row, read only for the photo that goes (version 3 isn't on main yet, so it changed in place). *Only a 204* takes a photo off the laptop: any other answer that says all went well proves nothing, and stops the round. *By size:* 1.5 MB a sync, the first photo always going, in their order, as a supplier's paper may be far bigger than a face. *Too big* is refused from the file's size, before it is read. *Tests:* no photo goes with no CSRF token, nor while the push waits after a failure. *Else:* two comments put right (`make-client.ts` now names the one route that takes a PUT; `test/photos.ts`'s WebP keeps no length), and the import put in order.
**Done when:**
- [x] A record saved with a photo while offline keeps both; once the record is accepted, the photo goes up and leaves the laptop.
- [x] A photo whose record isn't accepted yet waits; a refused one is set aside with its code, and the rest go on.
- [x] Where the browser says the laptop is on mobile data, the photos wait for Wi-Fi.
**Tests:** `attachments.save.test.ts` · `attachments.send.test.ts` · `attachments.network.test.ts` · version 3 in `db.versions.test.ts`
**Commits:** `feat(frontend): a photo waits on the laptop with its record` → `feat(frontend): photos go up after their records` → `feat(frontend): photos wait for Wi-Fi where the laptop can tell` → `docs(plan): ctrl-6 says what it built`

### PPL-4 · Workers: the server
`M` · Jack-led · after PPL-3, CTRL-2, AUTH-6 · branch `feat/ppl-4-workers-server` **(v1.100)**
**Learn:** two managers acting on each other at the same moment: both rows locked in one order, and who may asked after the lock (AUTH-8).
**Build:** the routes the workers' page (PPL-6) calls, on `StaffViewSet` (`/api/v1/people/staff/`), each behind `staff.manage` on the whole shop. No screen yet.
- *The list and one worker:* `GET …/staff/` and `GET …/staff/{id}/`: each worker's full name, nickname, job title, first day, status and leaving day, and each role and extra key they were given, with its side (none: all sides). Everyone the shop has had, those who left included. Behind `staff.manage` on the whole shop *(default)*, as the page shows roles, and later pay (WAGE-2). So `ShopViewSet` learns to show rows behind a key of their own, for a route whose rows belong to no side (`side_field = None`): the key is asked on the whole shop. A side's rows behind a key (debts, reports) still wait for the first card that shows them (PPL-5).
- *Add a worker:* `POST …/staff/`, with the client's id (ADR-0007): full name, nickname, job title (may be empty) and first day (today if empty). One step, one transaction: the person (`create_user`, with no password, as a worker logs in with a PIN) and their job (`add_staff`). No phone (PPL-10), role (PPL-7) or PIN (PPL-8) yet, so a new worker can't log in until those cards.
- *Suspend, bring back, mark as left:* `POST …/staff/{id}/suspend/`, `…/unsuspend/` and `…/leave/`, each with a reason, and the leaving day for the last. The three services get their check of who may, the PIN's rule (02 §5) *(default)*: `staff.manage` on the whole shop; never oneself (`staff.own`); never someone holding a key the one acting lacks (`staff.more_keys`), so a manager can't suspend the owner; an owner, or a person who can open the admin, only by the superuser (`staff.superuser_only`). It is asked after `_locked_pair`, so when two managers suspend each other at once, the first wins and the second is refused, as a suspended person can't act (AUTH-8) **(v1.39)**. A route-level test: a suspension from the route writes both books, naming the person (CTRL-2's review) **(v1.23)**.
- *The demo* (Jack, 17 Nov 2026: A): every visitor logs in as Meneja, so the seed gives Meneja `staff.manage`, and visitors can try the page. The people the seed made (Meneja, Asha and Baraka) stay as made: suspending, bringing back or marking them left is refused there (`demo.people_fixed`), as the sides are (`_check_sides_fixed_on_demo`), or a visitor would stop every one-tap demo until the next morning's seed (OPS-3) **(v1.73)**. A worker a visitor added can be changed. The seed's people are those the system made (`created_by` empty) *(default)*.
**Split** (Jack, 17 Nov 2026): the old row's page, roles, PIN, password, photo and details went to PPL-6, PPL-7, PPL-8, PPL-9 and PPL-10, below, each with its notes and their marks; a note elsewhere that names PPL-4 for one of them means that card. Editing a role's keys is parking lot item 26.
**Watch out:** `_check_may_act_as` (`apps/people/services.py`) already holds the PIN's and the password's rule: generalise it, don't copy it. `test_two_managers_suspending_each_other_both_go_through` becomes `…_the_first_wins`: the second now waits on the lock, not on the shop's change number (SYNC-15) **(v1.54)**. `confirm_pairing` doesn't check that its `by` can still act (OPS-3): on the demo, the guard keeps Meneja working. Each new code gets its two sentences and a line in `errors.test.ts` (UI-2); Jack checks the Swahili. The list must pass the tenancy sweep. `pnpm gen:api` after each route.
**Built** **(v1.101)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the six commits it named after the card's, one more for the comments, and what it found on the way. *The base:* `ShopViewSet` shows rows behind a key of their own on a route of the whole shop's rows (`side_field = None`), asked on the whole shop in `initial()`, before any row is read; a side's rows behind a key still raise `ImproperlyConfigured`, as the route walk (`permission_problems`) and `.claude/rules/backend.md` now say. *Who may:* `_check_may_act_as` takes `what` ("pin", "password" or "staff") in place of `secret`, its English neutral; `_locked_for_a_change` takes both jobs with `_locked_pair`, then asks it, for `suspend`, `unsuspend` and `mark_left`, so for anyone at the shop a person who left is `staff.left` before anything else; `by` None still locks the one row. *The list:* `StaffSerializer`, with `full_name` from the person and `roles` (`id`, `role`, `template`, `name`, `side`) and `keys` (`id`, `permission`, `side`); `given_to` (`apps/people/selectors.py`) reads them for a whole page in two queries, through `StaffListSerializer`. *Adding:* `add_worker` makes the person (`create_user`, no password) and the job in one block of `recording_changes`; `add_staff` takes the app's id (`staff_id`). *The three routes* answer with the worker as they are now; the leaving day is sent, never guessed *(default)*: the page fills in today. *The demo:* `_check_people_fixed_on_demo`, in `_locked_for_a_change`, refuses a person acting on someone the system made; the seed gives Meneja `staff.manage`. *The extra commit:* some 25 comments that named PPL-4 for a photo, a role, the PIN screen or a rename now name PPL-9, PPL-7, PPL-8 or PPL-10; migration 0005 keeps its note (rule 9). Jack checked the Swahili of the nine new sentences: `staff.own`, `staff.superuser_only`, `staff.more_keys`, `staff.display_name_taken`, `staff.not_active`, `staff.not_suspended`, `staff.left_in_future`, `staff.left_before_start` and `demo.people_fixed`. Proven red: the list behind a key, each rule of who may for each action, the first of two managers winning, the one Juma sent twice, the two queries, the demo's fixed people. *For PPL-6:* the list comes newest first (`CursorPage`), so the page sorts as it shows; `staff.display_name_taken` is the whole request's code, as the database rule is of `lower(display_name)`, no field: show it beside *Jina la kuitwa* by its code; a blank name is `blank` beside its box; the refusals the page meets have their sentences. *For PPL-7:* an ended row must stop counting in `given_to` too, and `_check_people_fixed_on_demo` is where the seed's roles stay as made.
**From the review** **(v1.102)**: one hole and ten smaller findings; Jack chose to fix them, each in its own commit. *The hole:* a create sent again answered with the stored row as a read, and asked no key: Asha, who may not read the workers, sent Baba's id, which every laptop knows, as a new worker's, and got his row, Owner role and all. `_replay` (`apps/core/api/base.py`) now asks the key the route names for reading one row (`retrieve`, else `list`), on the row's side; the create's own key is still not asked again. *The demo's tiles* (Jack: A, over a cap of five added workers a day): a laptop shows a tile only for someone with a PIN (`staff-picker.tsx`, 03 §5.9), so a visitor's made-up workers, who never get one there, stay off every laptop, and a real shop's new worker gets a tile with his PIN. *Switched off:* the list says who the superuser switched off (`switched_off`), apart from the job's `status`. *Proofs:* the workers' routes join `test_suspended_member_gets_nothing_from_routes`; Meneja can't suspend or let himself go on the demo, refused as `demo.people_fixed`, which a comment now says, not `staff.own`; an id another shop holds is `id.taken` and makes no person; one worker sent twice at once is one worker and one person. Left as it is: `add_worker` reads the adder's key with no lock on his row, as every add does. Written on the rows: the first day takes any date (PPL-6), a worker comes back as a new person (PPL-10), a tile waits for a PIN (PPL-8).
**Done when:**
- [x] The owner's list shows his shop's workers with their roles and sides, and none of another shop's; someone without `staff.manage` on the whole shop is refused.
- [x] Adding Juma makes the person and his job in one step; sent twice, it makes one Juma; a nickname another current worker has is refused with its code.
- [x] Suspend, bring back and mark left each need a reason, write both books, and follow who may; of two managers suspending each other at once, the first wins.
- [x] On the demo, Meneja, Asha and Baraka can't be changed; a worker a visitor added can.
- [x] Every new code has its two sentences, and Jack has checked the Swahili; the schema and the types are regenerated.
**Tests:** `test_staff_routes.py` · `test_who_changes_a_worker.py` · `test_two_managers_suspending_each_other_the_first_wins` · `test_a_suspension_from_the_route_writes_both_books` · `test_demo_people_fixed` · a show route behind a key in `test_route_permissions.py`. Built too **(v1.101)**: `test_the_list_reads_the_roles_in_the_same_few_queries_however_many_workers`, `test_a_worker_sent_twice_is_one_worker`, `test_a_nickname_of_someone_who_left_may_be_used_again`, `test_the_service_asks_the_key_again`, `test_both_jobs_are_locked_lightly_and_in_id_order`, `test_the_system_still_changes_the_seeds_people_on_the_demo`, and in `test_seed_demo.py`, `test_seed_demo_lets_a_visitor_manage_the_workers_they_add`. From the review **(v1.102)**: `test_a_create_sent_again_asks_the_key_to_read_its_row`, `test_a_create_sent_again_shows_no_worker_to_whoever_lacks_the_key`, `test_a_person_the_superuser_switched_off_is_shown_so`, `test_an_id_another_shop_holds_is_refused_and_makes_no_person`, `test_one_worker_sent_twice_at_once_is_one_worker`, the workers' routes in `test_suspended_member_gets_nothing_from_routes`, Meneja in `test_seed_demo_lets_a_visitor_manage_the_workers_they_add`, and `gives someone with no PIN no tile` in `staff-picker.test.tsx`
**Commits:** `docs: the ppl-4 card, and rows for ppl-6 to ppl-10` → `feat(core): show a whole shop's rows behind a key` → `feat(people): who may suspend, bring back or mark a worker left` → `feat(people): the shop's workers, listed and one by one` → `feat(people): add a worker in one step` → `feat(people): suspend, bring back and mark left from the office` → `feat(demo): visitors manage the workers they add` → `docs: comments name the cards ppl-4's split made` **(v1.101)** → `docs(plan): ppl-4 says what it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the ppl-4 card follows the review` **(v1.102)**

### PPL-6 · Workers: the page
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-4 · branch `feat/ppl-6-workers-page` **(v1.103)**
**Build:** *Wafanyakazi*, an office screen at `/workers/`, through the API client only (ADR-0020), on PPL-4's routes, with its button on the office home for whoever holds `staff.manage` on the whole shop. Nothing changes on the server.
- *The list*, from `GET /api/v1/people/staff/`: each worker's nickname, full name, roles with their sides, and status (*Anafanya kazi*, *Amesimamishwa*, *Ameondoka*); one the superuser switched off (`switched_off`) says so, apart from the status (PPL-4's review). Those who left sit under their own heading at the bottom *(default)*, so the list a manager uses each day stays short. Every page is read, as `device-list.tsx` does.
- *Ongeza* opens *Jina kamili*, *Jina la kuitwa*, *Kazi* (may be empty) and *Siku ya kwanza*, with *Hifadhi* and *Ghairi*. The date box starts at the shop's today; the server takes any date, so a day more than 30 days before or after today asks once more *(default)*. After the add, one line says the new worker gets a laptop tile once PPL-8 gives them a PIN: no field says who has one, so the worker's page can't say it *(default)*.
- *The worker's page*, `/workers/view/?id=…` (no `[id]` pages, ADR-0016), opened by tapping a name: full name, nickname, job title, first day, status, the leaving day if any, each role with its side, and the buttons.
- *Simamisha* and *Rudisha*, each with *Sababu*; *Ameondoka*, with *Sababu* and the leaving day, which starts at today and offers nothing after it. The status decides which show: working, *Simamisha* and *Ameondoka*; suspended, *Rudisha* and *Ameondoka*; left, none. On one's own page there is no button, and one line says why (`staff.own`).
**Choices by Jack (17 Nov 2026):** the page as he pictured it at PPL-4: tap a worker's name and see everything of theirs, with the buttons.
**Watch out:** the list comes newest first (`CursorPage`): keep its order. A role shows by its template's words (`owner`, `manager`, `seller`, `order_writer`, `cashier`, `worker`, ADR-0032), or by its own `name` when it has no template; the six words come with this card, not PPL-8, as this page shows roles first. A side shows by its name from `me`'s sides; a role with none is *Pande zote*. Extra keys (`keys`) show as a count only, *Ruhusa 2 za ziada* *(default)*: their names come with PPL-8, which gives them. An add is a POST with the client's id (ADR-0007): the same id for a try of the same boxes, a new one once any of them changed, as `closure-form.tsx` does. An empty name or *Sababu* is said at once, in the server's own sentence, and nothing is sent. From UI-2 **(v1.44)**: a refusal shows with `ApiErrorAlert`, and each box's code with `useApiErrorWords().fieldMessages`; a code this card adds gets its two sentences and a line in `errors.test.ts`. Where each code shows: `blank` beside its box; `staff.display_name_taken`, the whole request's code, beside *Jina la kuitwa* (PPL-4); `staff.reason_required` beside *Sababu*; `staff.left_in_future` and `staff.left_before_start` beside the leaving day; above the buttons, `staff.own`, `staff.more_keys`, `staff.superuser_only`, `staff.not_active`, `staff.not_suspended`, `staff.left`, `demo.people_fixed` and `id.taken`. After `staff.not_active`, `staff.not_suspended` or `staff.left`, the page reads the worker again, as someone else changed them. Every code already has its two sentences (PPL-4); the new words go in both books, and Jack checks the Swahili. *Hifadhi* is off while sending and while offline. On the demo, the seed's people show their buttons: the page can't tell the system made them, so the server's `demo.people_fixed` says it. WAGE-2 adds the pay so far to the worker's page.
**Built** **(v1.104)**: in the plan Jack approved, in five commits after the card's and the browser test, with what it found on the way. *The page:* `features/workers/`: `workers.ts` holds the list's place in the store of server answers (`WORKERS`, every page read, as `device-list.tsx` does), one worker under it (`workerKey`, `useWorker`, shown at once from the list's copy, then asked again; `placeholderData` since the review, below), `keepWorker`, which puts the server's answer in both before they are read again, `mayManageStaff` (the key on the whole shop) and `useRoleWords`; `workers-screen.tsx` the list and *Ongeza*; `worker-form.tsx` the form; `worker-page.tsx` the worker's own page, at `/workers/view/?id=…`; `worker-actions.tsx` the three buttons and their form. The page reads the id with `useSearchParams()` inside `Suspense`, which the build needs, and hands it to the screen; outside a running app the hook gives `null`, though its type says otherwise. *A new piece:* `TextLink` (`components/design/text-link.tsx`), a link in words, the text's own colour underlined, its ring at full strength; a worker's name on the list is one, stretched over its row, so a finger anywhere on the row opens the page. `.claude/rules/frontend.md` lists it, the two pages, and how a page reads its id. *A loop:* a part that mounts on a failed read asks the store again (TanStack's `retryOnMount`), which brings the wait back, which takes the part away, which mounts again on the next failure, asking the server many times a second; the list and the worker are asked in the screen alone and handed down, and a test counts one request. *Mipangilio ya duka* has the same loop on `main`: issue #272, its own task (Jack's yes). *What is said:* a first day more than 30 days from today asks once, in a line under its box read by a screen reader, not in the hint; after a change, one line says what it did (*Asha amesimamishwa.*), and the keyboard goes to it, as the button may be gone; *Ghairi* gives it back to the button that opened the form. A service's refusal comes as the whole request's code, with no field (`apps/core/api/errors.py`), so `staff.reason_required`, `staff.left_in_future` and `staff.left_before_start` show beside their boxes by their code, as `staff.display_name_taken` does. `staff.not_active`, `staff.not_suspended` and `staff.left` close the form, say it in their own sentence, and read the worker again, so the buttons fit them as they are; a suspension sent twice, its first answer lost, ends there too. Each form reads the shop's today as it opens (`shopToday`, the shop's zone), so a page left open overnight starts on the new day. *Words:* `workers.*` in both books, the six roles under `workers.roles`; *cheo* for a role, as *Kazi* is the job title's box. Jack checked the Swahili, step by step. *A test's lesson:* a test that fires the window's `offline` leaves TanStack's own network watcher offline for every test after it, whose reads then wait for good; switch the network off before the form draws instead, as `closure-form.test.tsx` does. Proven red: one request on a refused read, the worker kept on the list when the read after the add fails, the nickname taken beside its box, the list's copy at once, a change meanwhile read again, and no button on one's own page, each taken out.
**From the review** **(v1.105)**: one hole and eight smaller findings; Jack chose to fix them all, each in its own commit. *The hole:* the worker's page showed the list's copy at once through `initialData`, which TanStack keeps as the state a query goes back to; `forgetTheLastPerson`'s reset put it back, so after Neema's *Toka* on the Dawa laptop, Juma, a seller, who opened Asha's page from the browser's history saw Neema's copy of her, roles and buttons, beside his own refusal (UI-4). It is `placeholderData` now, which is never kept. *Proofs:* the server's `staff.reason_required` and `staff.left_before_start` beside their boxes, and `staff.not_suspended` and `staff.left` closing the form and reading the worker again, as two managers marking Baraka left at once: any of their lines could go with every test green. *Ongeza:* shown as soon as the page knows who is here, not after the list's wait, which comes back at each new read of a list that never answered, and took the half-typed form with it. *Ghairi* on the three changes reads the worker again, as a send whose answer was lost may have changed them, as *Ongeza*'s *Ghairi* reads the list. *The wait:* both pages wait on `isPending` alone, through the store's own tries, which left them empty for some 3 seconds; the office home and *Mipangilio ya duka* do the same on `main`, written on #272 (Jack's yes). *The rules:* `.claude/rules/frontend.md` says where an office screen asks its reads, how its wait waits, and `placeholderData` for a copy from another answer. *Tidier:* the list works out the roles' words once, not once a row. Written on PPL-10's row: a worker whose first day is still to come can't be marked left before it. Left as it is, ADR-0007's trade-off, as `closure-form.tsx` has it: a send whose answer was lost, then a box changed, goes under a new id; if the first was saved, the nickname is refused as taken, which is safe, but a nickname changed too makes a second worker, whom the owner marks left. Proven red: each fix taken out.
**Done when:**
- [x] The owner opens *Wafanyakazi* from the office home and sees every worker with their roles, sides and status; someone without `staff.manage` on the whole shop has no button, and opening the address shows the refusal.
- [x] The owner adds Juma, and he shows in the list; a nickname another worker has shows its sentence beside *Jina la kuitwa*.
- [x] Tapping Juma opens his page with everything of his; the owner suspends him with a reason, brings him back, and marks him left; each refusal shows its sentence.
- [x] Jack has checked the Swahili; the page works on the live demo.
**Tests:** `workers-screen.test.tsx` · `worker-form.test.tsx` · `worker-page.test.tsx` · `worker-actions.test.tsx` · `e2e/workers.spec.ts` (the demo's Meneja adds a worker and suspends him). Built too **(v1.104)**: `text-link.test.tsx`; in `office-home.test.tsx`, *Wafanyakazi* for `staff.manage` on the whole shop and none for the key on one side; in `workers-screen.test.tsx`, *Ongeza*'s worker at the top with its line, kept when the read after fails, the first day the shop's today in its own zone, the list read again on *Ghairi*, and every page of the list read. From the review **(v1.105)**: in `worker-page.test.tsx`, the next person after a logout sees nothing of the last one's copy, and the wait through the store's tries; in `worker-actions.test.tsx`, the server's reason and last day beside their boxes, someone else bringing back or marking left first, and *Ghairi* reading the worker again; in `workers-screen.test.tsx`, *Ongeza*'s form kept while the list is read again, and the wait through the store's tries
**Commits:** `docs: the ppl-6 card` → `feat(frontend): the shop's workers on one page` → `feat(frontend): add a worker` → `feat(frontend): a worker's own page` → `feat(frontend): suspend, bring back or mark a worker left` → `test(e2e): the demo's manager adds a worker and suspends him` → `docs(plan): ppl-6 says what it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the ppl-6 card follows the review` **(v1.105)**

### OPS-13 · The demo's manager on the PIN screen, and a back arrow on every page
`M` · Claude-led · after OPS-3, PPL-4 · branch `feat/ops-13-demo-manager-tile` **(v1.106)**
**Build:** one tap on *Jaribu demo* also hands over Meneja's public login, and *Wewe ni nani?* shows his tile, which opens the password login with both boxes already filled. Since PPL-4's review a laptop shows a tile only for someone with a PIN, and Meneja has none, so his tile went, and a visitor found the manager's way in only in the README. And every page reached from another gets the back arrow the office pages wear (`PageHeader`).
- *The server:* the one-tap answer (`POST /api/devices/v1/demo/`) gains `logins` beside `pins`: for each person of `PEOPLE` with a password and no PIN (today Meneja), their staff id, the login name and the password. `_demo_logins()` sits beside `_demo_pins()` (`apps/demo/services.py`) and finds each person the same way, the seed's own row, the first made. The password comes from `PEOPLE`, the seed's public list, never from the database, which keeps only its hash.
- *The laptop keeps it:* `saveLogins()` and `demoLogin(staffId)` beside the PINs (`demo-pins.ts`), under a key of their own, saved by the same tap (`demo.ts`).
- *The tile:* a laptop shows a tile for each working person with a PIN, **or** with a demo login kept on this laptop. Meneja's tile is a link to `/login/?as=<staff id>`, never the PIN pad, with *Ingia kwa nenosiri* under his names, where a seller's tile shows *PIN 2580*.
- *The login page:* `/login/` reads `?as=` (`useSearchParams()` inside `Suspense`, as `/workers/view/` does). If this laptop kept a demo login for that id, both boxes start filled, and one line says this is the demo's public login. Any other id, or none, shows the empty form as today.
- *The password button* (*Ingia kwa nenosiri*, `at-the-device.tsx`), today under both the tiles and the PIN pad: it leaves the PIN pad on every shop, as someone who tapped their tile logs in with their PIN. Under the tiles it goes on the demo, where Meneja's tile does its job, and stays on a real shop, where an owner with no PIN has no tile and no other way in at the laptop.
- *The back arrow* (`PageHeader`, ADR-0039), on seven places that have none: the PIN pad, `/login/`, `/forgot-password/`, `/password-reset/`, `/attendance/`, `/this-laptop/` and `/register-device/`. It takes the person back to where they were, as on the office pages: back in the browser after a move between the app's own pages, else to a fallback. The PIN pad is a step inside `/`, so its arrow goes back to the tiles, as its *Rudi* does today. Fallbacks: `/` for `/login/`, `/attendance/`, `/this-laptop/` and `/register-device/`; `/login/` for the two password pages. Each page's *Rudi* button goes, as the arrow is the one way back (frontend rules); the ways forward stay (*Endelea*, *Omba kiungo kingine*, *Ingia*). No arrow during the clock-out's goodbye, where *Rudi* is hidden today. The home pages, `/` and `/office/`, have none, as the arrows lead there. The frontend rules say every page reached from another wears it.
**Choices by Jack (18 Nov 2026):** A, the demo only: a real shop keeps PPL-4's rule, a tile only for someone with a PIN. The tile opens the password login directly, never the PIN pad. The password button only where it is someone's way in. A back arrow on every page from now on, taking the person back to where they were: all seven on this card (Jack: B, over a card of their own).
**Watch out:** "passwords never leave the server" (`.claude/rules/backend.md`) stays true for every real password. The demo's is public, as the README prints it, and travels only in the one-tap answer, as the sellers' PINs do (OPS-3); 03 §5.9 and the backend rules get this demo exception written in. A login kept from yesterday's demo matches no tile, as the daily reset makes everyone again with new ids; a real shop's laptop keeps none. A laptop still locks a password's session after the shop's idle minutes (UI-4): Meneja taps his tile again, which asks for no PIN. The README's demo lines name the tile.
**Built** **(v1.107)**: in the plan Jack approved, in nine commits, one idea each. *The server:* `DemoLogin(staff_id, login, password)` and `_demo_logins()` (`apps/demo/services.py`), each person of `PEOPLE` with a password, an email and no PIN; both lists find the seed's own row by one helper (`_seeds_own`), the first made, so a guard missed never stops a tap. The answer holds `pins` and `logins` and nothing else, proven whole; a second test logs Meneja in with what it handed over. *The laptop:* `saveLogins()` and `demoLogin()` (`demo-pins.ts`), under `mpc.demo-logins`, apart from the PINs, so an older version of the app reads its PINs as before; a kept login without both its strings reads as none. The PIN-reading test moved beside them, into `demo-pins.test.ts`. *The tile* (`staff-picker.tsx`): a person with no PIN and a login kept for their id opens the password login (`opensThePassword`); someone with a PIN keeps the pad, a login kept or not; the words under it are the password button's own (`login.withPassword`). *The password button* moved from the home page (`at-the-device.tsx`) into the tiles' part, after them, which knows whether a tile opens the password: so never under the pad, and not on the demo; it waits for the tiles' first read, so the demo's never flashes while they are read. *The login page:* `/login/` reads `?as=` inside `Suspense` and hands it to `PasswordLogin` as `forStaff`; the kept login is read once, as the boxes start, and the line is `login.password.demo`, whose Swahili Jack checked. What is sent, and every refusal, is the same as for a login typed by hand. *The arrow* (`PageHeader`): `onBack` for a step inside one page, the PIN pad's way back to the tiles; `backDisabled`, the arrow held while a PIN is on its way, as *Rudi* was; `arrow={false}` for the clock-out's goodbye, its title kept. The four *Rudi* words nobody says now left both books (`login.pin.back`, `password.forgot.back`, `attendance.back`, `thisLaptop.back`). *Register:* the arrow stays once registered, beside *Endelea*. The counter pages stay in their budget (`/attendance/` 248.1 KB, `/this-laptop/` 245.0 KB). The browser test also takes the arrow from the login back to the tiles, through the browser's real history. Honest note: on the PIN pad and the password pages (the sixth commit) the tests were written with the code, not first, so they weren't seen red; each needs the arrow those pages never had.
**From the review** **(v1.108)**: no rule broken; the password path holds (only the seed's constant, only behind `demo_shop_if_open`, never logged, kept in the store or cached), and a real shop's laptop never gets the copy. Jack chose to fix every finding, each in its own commit. *Built-in names:* a kept login was looked up in a plain object, which answers `constructor` and `__proto__` with its own, so a link to a real shop's `/login/?as=constructor` showed the demo's line above empty boxes; `demoLogin` and `demoPin` read only the id's own entry now (`Object.hasOwn`). *The login's arrow* is held while a login is on its way, after it worked, and while a *Toka* is: the store's own `onSuccess` opens the office even once the page is left, so at a shared laptop a tap on the arrow just after *Ingia* showed the tiles, then the office in front of the next person. *Before the first sync* the demo showed the password button, which led to an empty form, as the tiles' copy wasn't there to say a tile opens the password; a laptop that keeps a demo login is the demo's, and shows none then (`keepsDemoLogins`). *The PIN check* on the tile had no test: deleting it passed every one; now Asha, with a login kept for her id, still opens the pad, proven red by breaking the code on purpose. *A redirect:* the tab's note counted any change of address as a move inside the app, so a bookmark to `/office/` opened from Chrome's new tab while logged out landed on `/login/`, whose arrow went back to Chrome's page; a move counts now only when it added a page to the tab's history (`in-app-moves.tsx`), which a redirect never does. *Comments* that named the old *Rudi* name the arrow; the demo login's comment names the email alone; the backend rules' demo row names the manager's login. For the hand test: the PIN pad's name now sits at the left, a little smaller, *Andika PIN yako* in the pale grey, and the counter pages' titles a little smaller, as on the office pages.
**On the live demo** **(v1.109)**: checked by Jack on 18 Nov 2026, on a phone in a private window (#279's comment): the three tiles, Meneja's filled login and his office, the PIN pad's arrow and the login's arrow back to the tiles. Honest limit, seen on his installed app: a laptop that tapped *Jaribu demo* before this card's deploy kept the PINs and no login, so it shows the plain password button with empty boxes until the next daily reset, after which it taps again and keeps the login; only laptops tapped in on the deploy's day are touched. No code change (Jack: A, over a route that hands the login again to a laptop already in, which would be a card of its own).
**Done when:**
- [x] After one tap on *Jaribu demo*, *Wewe ni nani?* shows Meneja's tile with *Ingia kwa nenosiri*; a tap on it and on *Ingia* opens his office, with nothing typed.
- [x] A real shop's laptop still shows no tile for someone without a PIN.
- [x] Asha's and Baraka's PIN pads show no password button; a real shop's tiles still do.
- [x] Each of the seven places has the back arrow, which goes back to where the person was, and no *Rudi* button.
- [x] Jack has checked the Swahili; it works on the live demo.
**Tests:** `test_demo_device.py` (`test_one_tap_hands_over_the_managers_login`) · `demo-pins.test.ts` (new) · `staff-picker.test.tsx` · `at-the-device.test.tsx` (the password button) · `password-login.test.tsx` · `page-header.test.tsx` · each page's own test (the arrow and its fallback) · `e2e/one-tap-demo.spec.ts` (Meneja's tile → *Ingia* → *Wafanyakazi*). Built too **(v1.107)**: `test_one_tap_demo_managers_login_logs_in`; in `try-the-demo.test.tsx`, the tap keeps the login; in `staff-picker.test.tsx`, no tile from yesterday's login, none for a suspended manager, the button under a real shop's tiles, before the first sync and on an empty shop, and none on the demo; in `pin-login.test.tsx`, `forgot-password.test.tsx`, `password-reset.test.tsx`, `attendance.test.tsx`, `this-laptop.test.tsx` and `register-this-device.test.tsx`, the arrow, its fallback and no *Rudi*; in the browser test, the arrow back to the tiles. From the review **(v1.108)**: in `demo-pins.test.ts`, built-in names give no login or PIN, and whether a laptop keeps a demo login; in `password-login.test.tsx`, `?as=constructor` starts empty with no line, and the arrow held while the login is on its way; in `staff-picker.test.tsx`, no button before the demo's first sync, and a seller with a PIN keeps the pad; in `in-app-moves.test.tsx`, a redirect notes no move
**Commits:** `docs: the ops-13 card` → `feat(demo): one tap hands over the manager's login` → `feat(frontend): the demo's manager gets a tile that opens the password login` → `feat(frontend): the password button only where it is someone's way in` → `feat(frontend): the login starts with the demo manager's login filled` → `feat(frontend): the pin pad and the password pages get the back arrow` → `feat(frontend): the counter and setup pages get the back arrow` → `test(e2e): the demo's manager logs in from his tile` → `docs(plan): ops-13 says what it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the ops-13 card follows the review` **(v1.108)**

### PPL-10 · A worker's details
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-6 · branch `feat/ppl-10-worker-details` **(v1.110)**
**Build:** fix a worker's full name, nickname, job title or first day, typed wrong, and add, change or take off their phone, which a temporary password needs (`password.no_login_name`, PPL-8). Each change with *Sababu*, on the worker's page.
- *The worker's row keeps a version:* `StaffMember` becomes `Editable` (`updated_at`, `updated_by`, `version`), so two people's edits of one worker never undo each other (409 `version_conflict`). The history trigger stays as it is.
- *The service:* `change_worker(staff, *, version, reason, by, …)`: only the fields sent change. Who may is the PIN's rule (02 §5) *(default)*, through `_locked_for_a_change`, both jobs locked first: `staff.manage` on the whole shop; never oneself (`staff.own`); never someone holding a key the one acting lacks (`staff.more_keys`); an owner's, or an admin person's, only by the superuser (`staff.superuser_only`); one who left is `staff.left`; a suspended one may be fixed. Then `editing()` refuses a stale version. *Sababu* is required (`staff.reason_required`) (Jack: A). One audit line, `staff.details_changed`, on the job, with each changed field before and after, and the reason: the full name and the phone are the person's, no shop's row, so the edit history can't keep them (ADR-0036). A new `AuditAction` is a migration of `control`.
- *The person's fields:* the full name and the phone belong to the person (User), across shops (D12). The phone goes through the phone cleaner (`apps/core/phones.py`, `phone.invalid`) and is unique among all people: taken, it is `unique` beside the box, which says only that the number is in use, never by whom or where. An empty box takes the phone off *(default)*.
- *The laptops:* the full name and the nickname travel on the staff row (`StaffRecord`); the job title, the first day and the phone never do. A changed full name writes a staff line in every shop where the person works (`_record_each_job`, SYNC-15); a changed nickname, the job's line in this shop **(v1.54)**.
- *The route:* `PATCH /api/v1/people/staff/{id}/` with `version` (`EditMixin`), behind `staff.manage` on the whole shop, answering with the worker as now. `StaffSerializer` gains `phone` and `version`; no laptop gets the phone (`test_bootstrap_secrets.py`).
- *The page:* *Simu* on the worker's page (*Hakuna* when empty), and *Badilisha* beside the buttons, as Jack pictured it (18 Nov 2026): the details turn into boxes in place, filled in, with *Sababu*, *Hifadhi* and *Ghairi*. A first day more than 30 days from today asks once more, as on *Ongeza*. After `version_conflict`, the page says someone else changed the worker first, and reads them again.
- *The demo:* the seed's people stay as made (`demo.people_fixed`). A worker a visitor added can be fixed, but gets no phone (Jack: A): the box is off, with one line saying why, as a phone serves no login there (`demo.logins_fixed`), and a stranger could type a real person's number for every later visitor to see. The page knows the demo from `askDemoOpen()` (`features/devices/demo.ts`); the server refuses a phone there too (`demo.phones_fixed`).
- *A first day still to come* (PPL-6's review) **(v1.105)**: such a worker can't be marked left before it (`staff.left_before_start`), as the last day lies between the first day and today; fixing the first day to today lets one who never started go.
**Choices by Jack (18 Nov 2026):** the screen as he pictured it; the row's "a worker who comes back" split off to PPL-11, a row of its own right after this card; *Sababu* on every change (A); no phone on the demo (A).
**Watch out:** a full name change writes in more than one shop: SYNC-15's rule, one `recording_changes` block per shop, in id order, one after another, never one inside another, in one transaction with the save, as `change_own_settings` does. The phone is a plain unique column of `User`, so its save takes that row's full lock (`.claude/rules/backend.md`): think through any lock taken after it. Reuse `_locked_for_a_change`, never copy its rule. A job at another shop isn't looked at until AUTH-7, as for the PIN. The phone in the audit line stays on the server; logs never hold it. Each new code gets its two sentences and a line in `errors.test.ts` (UI-2); Jack checks the Swahili. `pnpm gen:api` after the route. A first day moved after a clock-in already kept is ATT-4's to decide (a note on its row).
**Built** **(v1.111)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the four commits it named. *The version:* `StaffMember` is `Editable` (people 0018); the history's trigger already skips `version`, `updated_at` and `updated_by_id`, so it stays as it is; a suspension, a leaving or a PIN locks the row its own way and leaves the version as it is, so a fix typed meanwhile still saves. *The service:* `change_worker` (`apps/people/services.py`): the reason and the demo's phone first, then one transaction: both jobs locked and who may asked (`_locked_for_a_change`); a job that ended refused for the system too (`staff.left`); a stale version refused before its boxes are judged; the person locked lightly after the jobs; every sent box checked at once on the rows in hand (`full_clean`, of the person's sent fields alone), so a wrong phone and an empty nickname come back together; nothing written when nothing changed; then `editing()`, which moves the version even when only the person's details changed, as the page's copy holds them too. One audit line, `staff.details_changed` (control 0010), with each changed detail before and after. A new full name reaches every shop where the person works (`_record_each_job`), a new nickname this one. *The route:* `PATCH …/staff/{id}/` (`EditMixin`, `StaffEditSerializer`), behind `staff.manage`; `StaffSerializer` gains `phone` and `version`. *The page:* `worker-details-form.tsx`, opened by *Badilisha*, first of the buttons (`worker-actions.tsx`), the details card hidden while it is open; only the changed boxes are sent, with the version and each box's start kept from the moment the form opened, as the page may read a newer copy behind it; a phone written `0712 345 678` (`formatPhone`, `src/lib/format.ts`); the demo's sign asked by the page once a form opens (`askDemoOpen`). One new code, `demo.phones_fixed`; Jack checked the Swahili of it and of the page's eight new sentences. Proven red: seven of the service's lines and six of the page's, each taken out.
**From the review** **(v1.113)**: no must-fix and no rule broken; Jack chose to fix every finding, each in its own commit. *The words:* the three who-may sentences (`staff.own`, `staff.superuser_only`, `staff.more_keys`) cover a worker's details as well as their status (Jack: A, over three codes of the fix's own), so the line on one's own page covers *Badilisha* too. *A way in kept:* a phone can't be taken off someone with a password and no email, as they could never log in again (`phone.needed_to_log_in`, beside *Simu*; Jack: A); a new number is fine, as they log in with it and the same password, and a worker with a PIN alone may lose theirs. *The door:* ADR-0044 supersedes ADR-0042, as the person's door is now read inside a shop's visit too; AUTH-7's and PPL-11's rows say what that leaves open for a person at two shops. *At the same moment:* a phone or a nickname two fixes save in the same second answers the second as taken, the checks asked again after the database's refusal, not a server error. *The admin* saves only what its form changed (`change_person`), so a form opened before a fix never writes the old phone back. *The page:* each worker gets buttons and forms of their own (`key`), so a form open on Asha closes on Baraka's page; a moved first day is asked about only when it is far from both today and the day it was *(default)*; a fix refused as old says the worker changed since the form opened, true too of Meneja's own fix whose answer was lost. *Proofs:* a suspended worker can be fixed; *Badilisha* shows only for a working or suspended worker; *Hifadhi* is off offline and while sending; a fix locks the person; the taken phone is of a person at another shop. *Comments and names* say what the fix changed; `AuditAction.STAFF_DETAILS_CHANGED`. Jack checked the Swahili of the four sentences reworded and the new one.
**On the live demo** **(v1.114)**: checked by Jack on 18 Nov 2026, in a private window, after the deploy of #282 (its comment): as Meneja, *Badilisha* on a worker he added saved the fix; on one of the seed's workers it was refused (`demo.people_fixed`); the *Simu* box was off, with its line.
**Done when:**
- [x] Meneja fixes Asha's full name, nickname, job title and first day on her page, with a reason; the laptops show her new names after their next sync, in every shop where she works.
- [x] He adds her phone, typed as 0712 345 678; a phone another person has is refused, saying only that it's taken.
- [x] Of two managers fixing Asha at once, the second is told someone changed her first, and nothing is lost.
- [x] Who may follows the PIN's rule: nobody fixes their own details, a manager can't fix the owner's, and a worker who left can't be fixed.
- [x] Every change has an audit line with who, when, why, before and after.
- [x] On the demo, the seed's people can't be changed and no worker gets a phone; a visitor's worker's other details can.
- [x] Every new code has its two sentences, and Jack has checked the Swahili; the schema and the types are regenerated.
**Tests:** `test_worker_details.py` (new: a stale version, a line in every shop, the nickname's line, the reason, one audit line, the phone's one form, a taken phone, an empty box, no phone on the demo) · `test_who_changes_a_worker.py` (each rule, for an edit) · `test_staff_routes.py` (the PATCH, its key pinned, a refusal without it) · `test_demo_people_fixed` · `test_bootstrap_secrets.py` (no phone on a laptop) · `worker-details.test.tsx` (new) · `errors.test.ts` Built too **(v1.111)**: in `test_worker_details.py`, `test_a_fix_from_an_old_copy_is_refused_before_its_boxes_are_judged`, `test_a_fix_of_the_persons_details_alone_moves_the_version_too`, `test_every_refusal_comes_back_together`, `test_a_box_typed_back_as_it_was_writes_nothing` and `test_a_first_day_still_to_come_fixed_to_today_lets_one_who_never_started_go`; the fix in every rule of `test_who_changes_a_worker.py`, where each action now writes its one audit line; the fix in `test_suspended_member_gets_nothing_from_routes`; `format.phone.test.ts`; and in `worker-details.test.tsx`, the version kept from the moment the form opened. From the review **(v1.113)**: `test_a_phone_that_is_someones_only_way_in_cant_be_taken_off`, `test_such_a_phone_may_change_and_may_go_once_there_is_an_email`, `test_a_suspended_worker_can_still_be_fixed`, `test_a_nickname_taken_at_the_same_moment_answers_as_taken`, `test_a_phone_taken_at_the_same_moment_answers_as_taken`, `test_the_admin_doesnt_quietly_undo_a_fix`; where *Badilisha* shows, in `worker-actions.test.tsx`; and in `worker-details.test.tsx`, *Hifadhi* off offline and while sending, a form closing on another worker's page, a long-time worker's first day, and the phone someone logs in by
**Commits:** `docs: the ppl-10 card, and a row for ppl-11` → `feat(people): a worker's row keeps a version` → `feat(people): fix a worker's details, with who may and why` → `feat(people): fix a worker's details from the office` → `feat(frontend): badilisha on the worker's page` → `docs(plan): ppl-10 says what it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the ppl-10 card follows the review` **(v1.113)**

### PPL-11 · A worker who comes back
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-10 · branch `feat/ppl-11-worker-comes-back` **(v1.115)**
**Build:** a worker who left and is hired again comes back as the same person, with a new job at the shop, as 02 §5 says, not as a second person through `add_worker`: their old job keeps what it said, its history with it, and their phone and password stay theirs. Two ways in, from a picture of the page Jack said yes to (18 Nov 2026): *Mwajiri tena* on the page of a job that ended, and a *Simu* box on *Ongeza* that finds them. Split from PPL-10 (Jack, 18 Nov 2026), from PPL-4's review **(v1.102)**.
- *The service:* `hire_again(old, *, display_name, job_title, started_on, staff_id, by)` (`apps/people/services.py`): a new job (`add_staff`) for the person of `old`, a job at this shop that ended. Who may is the add's, `staff.manage` on the whole shop *(default)*: the person comes back with no role, no extra key and no PIN, like a new worker, so whoever hires them gains nothing over them, and from then on the PIN's rule guards their details (02 §5). Refused: a job that hasn't ended (`staff.not_left`); a person who works here again already (`staff.already_member`, the rule `people_staffmember_one_current_job`); a person with a current job at another shop (`staff.works_elsewhere`), until AUTH-7, as they would then work at two shops, whose login AUTH-3 refuses, and this shop could fix their name and phone (ADR-0044, PPL-10's review **(v1.112)**); a first day before the old job's last day (`staff.back_before_left`); the same day may, so a leaving marked by mistake is mended at once *(default)*. The nickname is checked again, as another current worker may hold it by now (`staff.display_name_taken`). The full name stays the person's: *Badilisha* fixes it. No audit line, as for an add: the new job's `created_by` and `created_at` say who and when.
- *A phone on Ongeza:* `add_worker` takes an optional phone, through the phone cleaner (`apps/core/phones.py`, `phone.invalid`), so a new worker gets their phone as they are added, not only later on *Badilisha*. A phone nobody has goes on the new person. A phone of a person whose last job here ended, and who works nowhere now, makes nothing: the answer is `staff.left_here`, with that job's id, the person's full name and their last day (`params`), all of which the list already shows whoever may add. Every other phone a person has, of someone working here or at another shop, or of a person with no job here, is `unique`, which says only that the number is in use, never by whom or where, and joins nobody: a manager of shop B who could join shop A's owner by his phone could then set his PIN or a temporary password, and log in as him at shop A.
- *The route:* the add (`POST /api/v1/people/staff/`) takes `phone`, and `hire_again`: the id of a job that ended, found in this visit's shop (another shop's is "not found"). With it, no full name is sent. The new job has its own id, as every add (ADR-0007), so a send repeated gets the job the first one made. Behind `staff.manage`, as now. `StaffSerializer` gains `hired_again_as`: for a job that ended, the id of the person's current job here, once they are back, else null.
- *The page:* on the page of a job that ended, *Mwajiri tena*: the add's boxes for the job, filled in from the old one (*Jina la kuitwa*, *Kazi*), and *Siku ya kwanza* from the shop's today, with *Hifadhi* and *Ghairi*; a first day far from today asked once more, as on *Ongeza*. Saved, the page opens the new job. Once they are back, the old job's page says so, with a link to the new one, in place of the button. On *Ongeza*, *Simu* (optional, written 0712 345 678); after `staff.left_here`, under it: "Simu hii ni ya Baraka Mfano, aliyeondoka 12/03/2026." with *Mwajiri tena*, which hires them again with the boxes already typed. *Wafanyakazi*: the person with those working now, and the old job still under *Walioondoka*, as the history it is (Jack: A).
- *The demo:* no phone there (PPL-10): *Ongeza*'s *Simu* is off, with its line, as on *Badilisha*, and the server refuses one (`demo.phones_fixed`). A worker a visitor added and marked left can be hired again; the seed's people are never marked left there (`demo.people_fixed`).
- *The laptops:* the new job is a new staff row (`record_change`, in `add_staff`), with no PIN, so no tile until PPL-8; the old one left them when it ended (SYNC-15).
**Choices by Jack (18 Nov 2026):** the two ways in, from the picture of the page (yes); the old job stays under *Walioondoka* once the person is back (A).
**Watch out:** finding whether the person works at another shop reads the person's door (`shops_of_person`, `apps/people/login_doors.py`) inside the visit, for a refusal, not for a change that must reach each shop: a new use, so ADR-0045 supersedes ADR-0044, the door still reading shops' ids alone. The person's row (User) is global, so finding them by phone reads no shop's table; their old job is read through the guard, in this shop. Lock the person's row (`select_for_update(no_key=True)`) before the checks, so two managers hiring Baraka again at once take turns and the second gets `staff.already_member`, not a server error; `change_worker` locks the jobs before the person, and this locks no job, so the two never wait in a circle. The phone never goes in `params`, an audit line or a log. A day both jobs share (left and back the same day) is ATT-4's to read: a note on its row. Each new code gets its two sentences and a line in `errors.test.ts` (UI-2); Jack checks the Swahili. `pnpm gen:api` after the route.
**Built** **(v1.116)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the eight commits it named. *The decision:* ADR-0045 supersedes ADR-0044, as the person's door now also serves a hire again; 03 §5.4 says so (03 v1.77). *The service:* `hire_again` (`apps/people/services.py`): staff.manage asked first, then one block: the person's row locked lightly, and every check read after it, so of two managers hiring Baraka at once the second waits and hears `staff.already_member` (proven red: without the lock, the database's error); his jobs here read with the shop named too, as under `system_context()` the guard shows every shop (proven red); a current job at another shop found through the door, by ids alone (`staff.works_elsewhere`); the first day measured from the latest last day of every job here that ended (`staff.back_before_left`, beside *Siku ya kwanza*); then `add_staff`, which checks the nickname again and names the new job for the laptops. *A phone on Ongeza:* `add_worker` takes `phone` ("" or left out: none); a phone of a person whose every job here ended, and who works nowhere now, makes nothing and answers `staff.left_here` with the latest such job's id, the full name and the last day, a refusal of the whole add, as only that carries `params`; any other phone is `unique` from `create_user`, a leaver here who works elsewhere included (proven red); two adds saving one new phone in the same second answer the second as `unique`, the check asked again after the database's refusal, not a server error (proven red). *The route:* the add takes `phone` and `hire_again` (`StaffField`: a job of the visit's shop, else `does_not_exist`); a full name is `required` only without `hire_again`; `StaffSerializer` gains `hired_again_as`, read for a whole page in one query (`current_jobs_of`, `apps/people/selectors.py`; proven red by the query count). *The page:* *Mwajiri tena* on a job that ended opens *Ongeza*'s own form (`worker-form.tsx`, `again`), with no *Jina kamili*, its boxes filled from the old job, the first day checked against the last at once; saved, the page moves to the new job's (`router.push`); once back, the old page says "Baraka amerudi kazini." with a link to the new one. On *Ongeza*, *Simu* last, as the picture; `staff.left_here` is said under it in words, not in red, with *Mwajiri tena*, which hires him again with the boxes typed, sending neither his full name nor his phone; after it the list says he was added, as for any new worker. On the demo, *Simu* is off there too, the list asking the demo's sign once the form opens. Five new codes (`staff.not_left`, `staff.already_member`, `staff.works_elsewhere`, `staff.back_before_left`, `staff.left_here`) and six of the page's sentences; Jack checked the Swahili of all eleven.
**From the review** **(v1.117)**: no must-fix and no rule broken; Jack chose to fix every finding (A), each in its own commit. *The decision:* ADR-0045, 03 §5.4 and the door's file name *Ongeza*'s phone check as a caller of the door too, read only for a person whose every job here ended, and say truly what a manager learns: that a former worker works elsewhere now, from a hire again's refusal or, less directly, from his phone answering only in use; rule 1 in `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/rules/backend.md` and the sweep's reason name ADR-0045 (Jack: yes). *Proofs:* a phone is only in use for another shop's leaver, a leaver suspended elsewhere and one back already, the route's answer carrying no name, job or values; a hire again refused for another shop names no shop, its name, code or id; a visitor's own leaver is hired again on the demo; one hire again sent twice at once is one job (CreateOnceMixin's twin); a worker hired again shows with those working and his old job under *Walioondoka*, each linking to its own page. *At the same moment:* two adds, or an add and a hire again, naming one nickname in the same second answer the second as taken (`add_staff` asks its checks again after the database's refusal), not a server error. *Ongeza:* the form keeps whom the phone found until *Simu* changes, so a hire again whose answer was lost keeps its *Mwajiri tena*, which sends the same id again, never a second Baraka; that *Mwajiri tena* is the second kind of button, *Hifadhi* the form's one main. *Switched off:* a person the superuser switched off may be hired again, and stays off, as only the superuser switches a person on; their page already says so (Jack: A). *Comments:* the back arrow's after *Mwajiri tena*, the hire's lock, the route list, 02's change lines in order; one builder for a worker's page address (`workerPage`).
