### PPL-7 · Roles: the server
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-4 · branch `feat/ppl-7-roles-server` **(v1.119)**
**Build:** the server's half of giving and taking away roles and extra keys, for the worker's page (PPL-8). Today `assign_role` and `grant_permission` (`apps/people/services.py`) give one, but no route calls them, and nothing takes one away. Taking away is an edit, never a delete (rule 7): the row stays, marked ended, with who, when and why (02 §5). The row's notes are kept below, with their marks.
- *An end on the row:* `RoleAssignment` and `PermissionGrant` get `ended_at`, `ended_by` and `end_reason`, set together or not at all, as a void is on `staffpay`'s rows; `ended_by` empty is the superuser. The edit history records the end, before and after: both tables carry `RecordEdits`. Nothing links to these two tables, so PROTECT never stops a delete, and the history's trigger ignores DELETE: `NoDeleteTrigger` (`apps/core/operations.py`) refuses a DELETE on both, and lets updates through, as an end needs **(v1.91)**. The two `*_once` rules become partial, over rows not ended, so an ended role can be given again, as a new row **(v1.32)**.
- *An ended row stops counting:* in what a person holds and was given (`_read_permissions`, `apps/people/permissions.py`, under `effective_permissions` and `given_permissions`), in the read of the Owner role (`_check_may_act_as`), and in the workers' list (`given_to`, PPL-4) **(v1.32)**. A person's keys travel on their staff row, so each end names the person with `record_change` (SYNC-15), and their laptop drops the key at its next sync **(v1.54)**.
- *Taking away:* `end_role(given, *, reason, by)` and `end_permission(granted, *, reason, by)`. Who may is the rule of *Simamisha* (Jack: A), through `_locked_for_a_change`: `staff.manage` on the whole shop; never one's own (`staff.own`), as a manager who took away his own role by a slip of the finger would lock himself out; never from someone who holds a key the one acting lacks (`staff.more_keys`); an owner's, or an admin person's, only by the superuser (`staff.superuser_only`), so a manager handed `staff.manage` can't take the Owner's role away; a worker who left keeps theirs as history (`staff.left`); a suspended one may lose one. *Sababu* is required (`staff.reason_required`). A row already ended is refused (`role.already_ended`, `permission.already_ended`), so of two managers ending one row at once, the second hears that. No audit line: the row and its edit history keep who, when and why *(default)*.
- *The Owner role* (Jack: A): given and taken away only by the superuser (`by=None`), as an owner's PIN and password are. Whoever holds it can be suspended or changed only by the superuser, so a manager who held every key and gave himself the Owner role could no longer be stopped by the owner. A person giving it is refused (`role.superuser_only`). The superuser's way to do it comes with PPL-8, beside the owner's PIN.
- *Giving* keeps the giver rule (02 §5): `staff.manage` on that side, and only codes the giver holds there. It can answer `staff.temporary_password_open` ("they choose their own first"), as now **(v1.29)**. Each give takes the client's id (ADR-0007), as `add_side` takes `side_id`: the same id sent again answers as now, never `role.already_given` **(v1.24)**; an id another shop holds is `id.taken`.
- *A record made offline* (from SYNC-5) **(v1.57)**: the push checks a pushed record's key among the keys its maker was given (`given_permissions`, `apps/sync/push.py`). Once a key can be taken away, a sale made before that, but synced after, would get a false `permission_break`. So `given_permissions` takes a moment: the keys not ended by then, by the laptop's clock, as `was_working_at` reads a status, read from the rows' own `ended_at` rather than the audit trail. A key given after that moment still counts *(default)*: a laptop lets nobody sell with a key it hasn't heard of, so only a slow laptop clock could put a sale before its key, and that is no break.
- *The routes,* on `StaffViewSet`, behind `staff.manage`, each answering with the worker as now, as its other actions do: `POST …/staff/{id}/give-role/` (`id`, `role`, `side`), `…/give-key/` (`id`, `permission`, `side`), `…/end-role/` (`role_given`, `reason`), `…/end-key/` (`key`, `reason`). An empty side is every side. `permission` is a choice of the codes, so the schema hands the page the list. `GET /api/v1/people/roles/`: the shop's roles (`id`, `template`, `name`, `permissions`), behind `staff.manage` on the whole shop, for PPL-8's *Ongeza kazi*.
- *The demo* (from OPS-3) **(v1.73)**: the seed's people keep their roles and keys, for a give and an end (`demo.people_fixed`, PPL-4): a visitor who took Meneja's Manager role away would stop every tap until the next reset. A worker a visitor added may be given a role and lose it.
**Choices by Jack (19 Nov 2026):** taking away follows the rule of *Simamisha*, not the row's giver rule (A); the Owner role is the superuser's alone, to give and to take away (A).
**Watch out:** reuse `_locked_for_a_change`, never copy its rule; lock the row being ended after both jobs, in the order that rule keeps. The partial rules keep `nulls_distinct=False`, so "every side" still counts once. Read the migration line by line (rule 9). A role's codes are read as they are now, for an old record too: no card edits a role yet (parking lot item 26). Each new code gets its two sentences and a line in `errors.test.ts` (UI-2), and so do codes the routes now answer that have none yet (`role.already_given`, `permission.already_given`); Jack checks the Swahili. Pin each new action in `action_permissions`, with a refusal test by someone without the key. `pnpm gen:api` after the routes.
**Checked** **(v1.126)**: merged as #291 on 19 Nov 2026, its every check green. The server's half alone: no screen gives or takes away a role until PPL-8, so none of it can be tried on the live demo, and each box below is proven by its named tests (**Tests**), run in CI on #291. Jack checked the Swahili of its codes in its own session.
**Done when:**
- [x] Baba gives Asha the Seller role on Vyakula, and Juma one extra key on every side, through the office routes; the same give sent twice is one row.
- [x] He takes Asha's role away with a reason: she holds none of it at once, her laptop drops it at its next sync, the row stays with who, when and why, and a DELETE of it is refused.
- [x] Later he gives it to her again, as a new row.
- [x] Who may take away follows *Simamisha*: never one's own, never from someone holding a key one lacks, never a worker who left; the Owner role is given and taken away only by the superuser.
- [x] A sale Asha made offline before her role was taken away, synced after, raises no `permission_break`; one made after does.
- [x] On the demo, the seed's people keep their roles and keys; a visitor's own worker can be given a role and lose it.
- [x] The roles' list answers the shop's six roles; every code has its two sentences, and Jack has checked the Swahili; the schema and the types are regenerated.
**Tests:** `test_ending_roles.py` (new: an end with who, when and why; nothing held at once; given again as a new row; a DELETE refused; the staff line; the edit history's line; already ended; two ends at once) · `test_giving_roles.py` (the Owner role only by the superuser; the client's id; the demo) · `test_who_changes_a_worker.py` (each rule, for both ends) · `test_permissions.py` (an ended role and key count nowhere: held, given, the owner's read, `given_to`) · `test_maker_checks.py` (a sale made before its key ended is no break; one made after is) · `test_staff_routes.py` (the four actions and the roles' list, each key pinned, a refusal without it, a give sent twice, another shop's id) · `test_demo_people_fixed` · `errors.test.ts`.
**Commits:** `docs: the ppl-7 card` (on main, before the branch) → `feat(people): a role or a key ends, and stops counting` → `feat(people): take a role or a key away, with who may and why` → `feat(sync): a pushed record is checked against the keys of its own time` → `feat(people): give and take away roles and keys from the office` → `docs(plan): ppl-7 says what it built`.
**Built** **(v1.123)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the five commits it named. *The end:* `ended_at`, `ended_by` and `end_reason` on `RoleAssignment` and `PermissionGrant` (people 0019), whole or not there at all (`END_IS_WHOLE`: open, all three empty; ended, a time and a reason, who ended it empty for the superuser); the two `*_once` rules partial, over rows not ended, `nulls_distinct=False` kept; `NoDeleteTrigger` on both. An ended row counts nowhere: `_read_permissions` (held and given), the Owner read in `_check_may_act_as`, `given_to`, the demo's `_holds_its_keys`, and the tests' `give_role`. *Taking away:* `end_role` and `end_permission`, one helper (`_end`): the reason; `_locked_for_a_change`, reused, so who may is *Simamisha*'s and the demo's seed people stay as made; a worker who left keeps theirs, even from the superuser (`staff.left`); then the row locked, `role.already_ended` or `permission.already_ended`; saved with `acting_as`, so the edit history names who; `record_change` of the worker. No audit line *(default)*. *The push:* `given_permissions(staff, at=…)` counts a row that ended after `at`; `_maker_breaks` passes `made_at`. *Giving:* `assign_role` and `grant_permission` take the app's id (`given_id`, `granted_id`), looked for after the worker's lock (`_given_before`), so of two copies sent at once the second finds the first's row; another worker's row with that id is `id.taken`, and another shop's is found by the insert, which the route answers `id.taken` (`id_taken`, moved to `apps/core/api/ids.py` from a private copy in `base.py`). A person giving the Owner role hears `role.superuser_only`, asked before the giver rule, so three older tests now expect it. The demo's seed people keep their roles on a give too. `grant_permission` asks the giver rule inside its lock now, after the look for a repeat. *The routes:* `give-role`, `give-key`, `end-role`, `end-key` on `StaffViewSet`, each pinned to `staff.manage` and answering the worker as now; `role_given` and `key` are found only among the address's worker's rows (`_OfThisWorker`), or a manager who may act on Asha could end the owner's role from Asha's page; `GET /api/v1/people/roles/` (`RoleViewSet`). Five codes with their two sentences, whose Swahili Jack checked: `role.already_given`, `role.already_ended`, `role.superuser_only`, `permission.already_given`, `permission.already_ended`. Proven red: the four reads of an ended row, the false break of a sale made before its key ended, a missing `already_ended`, a missing staff line, a worker who left losing a role. *Honest note:* `_end` was written before its tests; each test was then seen to fail with the service broken on purpose. *For PPL-8:* a give sends `id` (UUIDv7), and `side` null for every side; the roles' list comes newest first, so the page sorts it; a refused end answers `staff.own`, `staff.superuser_only` or `staff.more_keys`, whose sentences still say "work status or details": decide whether they name roles and keys too; the superuser's way to give or take away the Owner role is this card's to add.
**Reviewed** **(v1.124)**: the reviewer's fresh look found one gap and some small things, all fixed (Jack: fix all), each in a commit of its own. *The gap:* Baba takes Asha's role at 11:00, she sells at 12:00 on a laptop that hasn't heard, he gives it back at 13:00, and the default counted the new row for the sale: no break. Now `_held_at` (`apps/people/permissions.py`) reads every row of the maker's, ended ones too, and a key given after the record counts unless that code had been taken away by then, whichever row gives it back, so a role returned as an extra key is caught too (02 v1.41); proven red, with the role and with a key; a sale at the very moment of the end is a break too. *Small:* a type that names no key, a clock-in, reads no keys (`_maker_breaks`; proven red by its queries); a give sent again with its id but another role, key or side is `id.taken`, not the old row back (`_given_before`; proven red); the demo's ended-key check has its test (`test_seed_demo_makes_todays_shop_again_when_a_seed_persons_key_ended`; proven red); a `(shop, staff)` index on both tables (people 0020), as the push's read of every row can't use the once rules' partial indexes; comments made true, and the delete test tries SQL typed by hand too. *Left as they are:* the branch is this session's (`ccr-…`), not the card's, as the cloud session can push only there; commit `8a304db` holds the giving changes beside the routes, as the card's commits named no place for them. *For PPL-8, from the review:* an end has no id of its own, so a retry after a lost answer hears `role.already_ended` or `permission.already_ended`: the page treats that as done.

### ATT-3 · Clock-in photo
`M` · Jack-led · after CTRL-3, CTRL-6, SYNC-11 · branch `feat/att-3-clock-photo` **(v1.120)**
**Learn:** how a web page uses the laptop's camera (`getUserMedia`, a live `<video>`, one frame drawn onto a canvas), and why a photo is shrunk on the laptop before it is kept.
**Build:** a photo of the worker at each tap of *Ingia kazini* and *Toka kazini*, taken by the laptop's camera, kept with the tap, and sent up after it (03 §5.13). Today a tap keeps only its time, so Juma, who knows Asha's PIN, could clock her in while she is still on the bus. As Jack pictured it (19 Nov 2026):
- *The screen:* the camera turns on as *Mahudhurio* opens, and shows the worker's face live above the button. One tap of the button takes the photo and saves the tap, with no second button. After it, she sees the photo kept, small, under *Umeingia kazini saa 07:55 asubuhi.* The camera turns off once the photo is taken, and whenever she leaves the screen, so its light never stays on. *Toka kazini* works the same way. The first time, the browser asks to use the camera: the manager allows it once, as the laptop is set up.
- *No photo* (Jack: A): with no camera, a camera the browser was told to block, or one that fails, the tap is saved all the same, with no photo and the reason, so nobody loses a day's pay because of a camera. The screen says so before the tap: *Hakuna kamera: utaingia bila picha.* A laptop with no camera can get a USB one, or the shop registers a phone of its own (*Sajili kifaa*); a worker's own phone still shows no *Mahudhurio* (SYNC-11).
- *Shrinking,* shared with PPL-9 and STK-4: the frame is drawn onto a canvas, its longest side at most 1,600 px, and saved as a JPEG, its quality stepped down until it is about 150 KB, never over 1 MB. A drawn copy carries none of the camera's hidden data, its location included.
- *Saving* (from CTRL-6 **(v1.98)**): the shrunk photo is labelled with `labelPhoto(file, "clock_photo")` (`src/offline/attachments.ts`), the label goes in the payload, and `saveDocument(db, draft, [], [photo])` saves both; the photos go by themselves after their records (`src/offline/sync/attachments.ts`). The clock document's shape becomes version 2: `kind`, `last_server_time`, and either `photo`, the label's five fields (`id`, `kind`, `content_type`, `size`, `sha256`), or `no_photo`, its reason, never both. Version 1 stays until every laptop has updated (`apps/sync/document_types.py`): a tap from a laptop still on the old app is saved, its reason `old_app`.
- *The server:* `post_clock` writes the event, then the photo's row with `expect_attachment` (kind `clock_photo`, the event as its subject, `by` the worker, the laptop), in the push's own transaction. The event keeps `no_photo`: empty when a photo came, else `no_camera`, `blocked`, `failed`, `old_app` or `demo`, one rule in the database. A label that breaks a rule makes the document `invalid` under its `attachment.*` code (from CTRL-3 **(v1.96)**), and the counter's red warning names it in words of its own (`needs-attention.tsx`). The photo opens with `attendance.manage` on the whole shop, as CTRL-3 left it *(default)*: the owner, and whoever he gives that key.
- *Who sees it:* no screen shows a clock-in photo yet. ATT-5's month view opens it from its day, and shows a tap with none and its reason (its row says so).
- *The demo* (Jack: A): every visitor shares Meneja's login, who holds `attendance.manage`, so a visitor's face kept there could be seen by the next. On the demo the camera works and the photo shows after the tap, but it never leaves the laptop: the tap goes up with `no_photo` `demo`, and the photo is dropped. The laptop must know it is the demo while offline, so the device's check (`device-check.tsx`) brings the demo's sign (`DEMO_SEED`) with the shop, kept in `mpc.device` (`this-device.ts`). A second lock on the server: on the demo, `post_clock` writes no photo's row even if a label comes, and keeps `demo`.
**Choices by Jack (19 Nov 2026):** a camera missing, blocked or broken never stops a tap: it is saved with no photo, and marked (A). On the demo, no visitor's photo leaves their laptop (A). The camera moment as pictured above: live above the button, one tap for both, the photo shown after, *Toka kazini* the same.
**Watch out:** `AttendanceEvent` is a ledger: its new column comes in a migration that changes no line (a column added with a value for the old lines, then the value dropped, as Django does, fires no UPDATE trigger); the old lines get `old_app`. Read it line by line (rule 9). `expect_attachment` needs a saved subject: the event first, then its photo's row; a broken label rolls both back with the push's savepoint for that document. `BrokenPhoto` from `labelPhoto` is a bug in the camera's code (from CTRL-6 **(v1.99)**): the tap is still saved, its reason `failed`, and the console names the problem, as no worker may lose a day to a bug either. A browser lets a page use the camera only on https or `localhost`: the demo and the shop's server are https, Jack's laptop is `localhost:8080`. The tests' browser (jsdom) has no camera and no canvas: Vitest gives the screen a pretend camera, and the drawing is tested in Chromium, which the browser tests give a pretend camera (`--use-fake-device-for-media-stream`, `--use-fake-ui-for-media-stream`) and the camera permission. A rescued clock-in (SYNC-12) brings its label but never its photo: its row waits on the server, as CTRL-6 says. The counter's budget (234.6 KB of 300 at SYNC-11): no new package. The tests' photos are made-up pixels, never a face (rule 10). After the merge, remake the README's GIF with the photo (FOLIO-2, `docs/media/README.md`).
**Built** **(v1.121)**: in the plan Jack approved, with one commit more, as the laptop must know the demo offline: `feat(people): a laptop knows it belongs to the demo`. At plan approval Jack said "you write to all and i approve", so every step was written whole, each commit with his yes. *The server* (`e11a7c6`): `NoPhoto` (`apps/staffpay/models.py`) and the line's `no_photo`, empty when a photo came, with its database rule; staffpay's 0002 adds it with `old_app` for the lines before, then drops that default, two changes to the table and no line rewritten. `ClockShapeV2` (`apps/staffpay/documents.py`): `photo`, the label's five fields as types only (`PhotoLabelShape`; their rules are the row's own, asked once, by `expect_attachment`), its kind only `clock_photo`, or `no_photo` of the four a laptop may give, never both nor neither (`no_photo`: `invalid`, `required`); version 1 is saved through `post_clock_v1`, as `old_app`. `post_clock` writes the line, then the photo's row, in the push's transaction for the tap; a label the row refuses goes through `_refused_label`: the first `attachment.*` code becomes the whole tap's, so the counter names it in words, and anything else is filed under `photo.*`, so a photo id already taken is answered `id.taken`, as any of the laptop's ids. On the demo (`DEMO_SEED`) no row is written whatever comes, and the line says `demo`. Its tests are a file of their own, `apps/sync/tests/test_clock_photo.py`. *The demo's sign* (`a277da4`): `MeShopSerializer.demo` (`apps/people/api/serializers.py`), from `DEMO_SEED`, in the answers of `/api/devices/v1/me/` and `/api/auth/me/`, both sharing the shop's part; kept in `mpc.device` (`ThisDevice.shop.demo`, optional) and read with `isTheDemo()` (`this-device.ts`). A copy saved before ATT-3 reads as a real shop's until the next check, at the app's start: on the demo the server's second lock keeps no photo meanwhile. *Shrinking* (`c1c5664`, `src/lib/shrink-photo.ts`): `fitWithin` (the longest side at most 1,600 px, a side never 0), `smallEnough` (JPEG at 0.85, then 0.75, 0.65, 0.55 and 0.45, the first at most 150 KiB, else the smallest) and `shrinkFrame` (a fresh canvas, `toBlob`); JPEG, as older Safari saves no WebP. *The camera* (`1a75ee8`, `src/features/camera/use-camera.ts`): `useCamera(on)` gives `state` (`off`, `starting`, `live`, `no_camera`, `blocked`, `failed`, the last three the server's own reasons), `videoRef` and `take()`; the front camera, ideally 1280 × 720, no sound; a refusal read by its name (`NotFoundError`, `NotAllowedError`…), never by `instanceof`, as an error made in another window is no instance of this one's; a page with no camera at all (not https nor localhost) reads as `no_camera`. A tap waits at most 5 seconds (`START_WAIT`) for a camera still starting and its first frame together, then goes on as `failed` *(default)*. The light goes off after the photo, when the screen no longer wants it, when it closes, and at once for a camera the browser handed over after the screen closed; a second `take()`, as after a save that failed, answers `failed`. Written to React's newer hook rules (no state set in an effect's own body, no ref read while drawing): the screen takes the hook's three parts by name, as the lint reads an object carrying a ref as a ref. *The screen* (`1fd43c5`): the live `<video>` above the button (`autoPlay`, `muted`, `playsInline`) while there is a button; the tap reads the last server time first, then takes the photo, labels it (`labelPhoto`; a `BrokenPhoto` is logged and the tap goes on as `failed`) and saves both with `saveDocument(db, draft, [], [photo])`; the photo kept shows small, through Next's `Image`, from an address freed as it changes or the screen closes. Each camera problem has its line before the tap, `{kind, select, …}` choosing *utaingia* or *utatoka*. On the demo the photo is taken and shown, the payload says `demo`, and nothing of it is saved; one line more there, told to Jack: *Hii ni demo: picha yako inabaki kwenye kompyuta hii, haitumwi kwenye seva.* `needs-attention.tsx` has words for `attachment.not_an_image`, `attachment.too_big`, `attachment.empty` and `attachment.sha256_invalid`. `CLOCK_VERSION` 2 and `ClockPayload` its photo or its reason (`clock-today.ts`). `APP_NUMBER` and `MIN_APP_VERSION` are 2, as the apps stop writing version 1 (the sync rule), with app number 2's lists in `test_app_number.py`; four sync tests that wrote the minimum as 1 read it from the settings now. Jack checked the Swahili. *The browser test* (`990f963`, `e2e/clock-in-photo.spec.ts`): Chromium's pretend camera and no "Allow camera?" question (`playwright.config.ts`), the laptop allowed the camera (`the-shop.ts`); on the demo, as the tests run there: the live picture, the photo shown, a JPEG under 1 MB at most 1,600 px with no Exif block, the tap accepted as `demo`, no photo waiting on the laptop and none sent. The upload road is proven by `test_clock_photo.py`, CTRL-3's and CTRL-6's tests, and Jack's hand test on a laptop that isn't the demo. At ATT-3, `/attendance/` weighs 256.7 KB of its 300. Proven red: the server's tests and the shrinking's and camera's before their code; the screen's demo rule switched off. Honest limits: no screen shows a clock-in photo yet (ATT-5); the tap's time is the laptop's clock once the photo is taken, a moment after the tap, at most 5 seconds when the camera is slow; the browser test never sees a photo go up, as the demo keeps none. From the review **(v1.122)**, every finding fixed, each in its own commit (Jack: A): a try after a save that failed uses the photo just taken, or says again why there is none, as the camera went off with the first; any trouble making the photo's label, not only a `BrokenPhoto`, saves the tap as `failed`; a camera that goes while live makes the next `take()` `failed` in the hook itself, never a photo of its frozen frame; one deadline of 5 seconds from the tap covers the camera's start and its first frame together, as each had its own, and a slow camera could hold a tap 10; on the demo the photo is named *Picha yako uliyopigwa sasa hivi*, never as kept; `attachment.empty` and `attachment.sha256_invalid` have their two sentences, as the push can send them; a test of a photo id another shop holds (`id.taken`, by the table's key); the demo's third lock, in `sendAttachments`: a demo laptop drops every clock-in photo it kept, before it knew, and sends none; tests of a tap sent twice with its photo and of the photo's address freed with the screen; comments made true. Kept as they are, with Jack: a `demo` reason sent to a real shop, which only a tampered laptop could send and which gains nothing over `no_camera`; and the camera on while the screen is open and untapped, as the counter's idle lock closes it. Jack checked the Swahili.

An old service worker from SYNC-16's hand test had kept the app before ATT-3 in Edge: unregistered at `edge://serviceworker-internals/`.

**PPL-8, PPL-12 and PPL-13 on the web** **(v1.127)**: PPL-8's row held some three sittings of work, so it became three cards (Jack: A, 19 Nov 2026), each built by Jack in a Claude Code web session of its own, one card a session, while other cards go on in VS Code. *The order:* PPL-8 first; PPL-12 in a new session once PPL-8 is merged, as both change the worker's page; PPL-13 at once, in a second session beside them, as it shares no file with them but this plan. Each session works on the branch the web gives it (`ccr-…`), as a web session can push only there (PPL-7). *Their files:* PPL-8 and PPL-12 own `frontend/src/features/workers/`, the `workers` and `errors` parts of `frontend/messages/sw.json` and `en.json`, `frontend/src/api/errors.test.ts` and `frontend/e2e/workers.spec.ts`; PPL-12 also `backend/apps/people/api/`, its tests, and the two files `pnpm gen:api` writes; PPL-13 owns `backend/apps/people/management/`, `backend/apps/shops/admin.py` and `docs/runbooks/open-a-shop.md`. A card built meanwhile in VS Code keeps out of them: not PPL-9 or WAGE-1, which change the worker's page, before PPL-12 is merged, and no change to the people routes. *Bringing main in* (`git merge origin/main`, never a rebase), before each push: a clash in this plan's change lines keeps main's and puts the card's above them under the next free number, its marks renumbered to match; a clash in `shared/openapi.json` or `frontend/src/api/schema.d.ts` is never mended by hand (rule 8): take main's, run `pnpm gen:api`, and commit what it writes; a clash in a word book keeps both sides' keys. *Done there:* every step of §3.3 up to the pull request: Jack's yes before each commit, `make check`, the reviewer, *What I learned* in his words. The tick, the board's Done and Hours, and the check on the live demo may come from VS Code, as PPL-7's did.

### PPL-8 · Roles and keys: the page
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-7, PPL-10 · branch `feat/ppl-8-roles-page` **(v1.127)**
**Build:** on the worker's page (PPL-6), *Ongeza kazi*, which gives a role or one extra key on a side, and *Ondoa* beside each, which takes it away with a reason, through PPL-7's routes. Nothing changes on the server. The old row's PIN and temporary password went to PPL-12, and the superuser's way for the owner to PPL-13 (Jack: A), each with its notes and their marks; a note elsewhere that names PPL-8 for a PIN or a password means PPL-12, and for the owner's PIN or the Owner role, PPL-13.
- *The page:* under the details and their buttons **(v1.135)**, *Cheo*: each role with its side, *Muuzaji (Dawa)*; then *Ruhusa za ziada*: each key by its name and side, *Kuhesabu mzigo (Vyakula)* **(v1.135)**, no longer a count (PPL-6's default). *Ondoa* beside each, and *Ongeza kazi* under them. The workers' list keeps its count, *Ruhusa 2 za ziada* *(default)*, so a row stays short.
- *Ongeza kazi* opens *Anapewa nini?*: *Cheo* or *Ruhusa moja ya ziada*; then the role or the key; then *Upande*: *Pande zote* and each side by its name; *Hifadhi* and *Ghairi*. The roles come from `GET /api/v1/people/roles/`, newest first, so the form sorts them: the templates' order (manager, seller, order writer, cashier, worker), then any role with a name of its own; each shows by its template's word (`useRoleWords`, PPL-6). The keys, 38 today, show by name, in eight groups **(v1.135)**, in the order `apps/core/permissions.py` lists them: selling; credit and debts; products, prices and contacts; stock; money and drawers; staff, attendance and pay; the shop itself; and Release 2's machine jobs and production. Each key's name and each group's are new words in both books, and Jack checks the Swahili.
- *Only what may be given* *(default)*: the form offers what the giver rule allows (02 §5), as the server refuses the rest: a role whose every code the person giving holds on that side, and a key they hold there, read from `me`'s `permissions` (the side's list, or `whole_shop` for *Pande zote*). *Mmiliki* never shows: it is the superuser's alone (PPL-7, PPL-13). A new side changes the lists; a choice no longer on them is cleared.
- *Ondoa* opens *Sababu*, with *Hifadhi* and *Ghairi*, its title naming what goes: *Ondoa Muuzaji (Dawa) kwa Juma*. Never beside *Mmiliki*, which only the superuser takes away (PPL-13).
- *Who sees the buttons* *(default)*: as PPL-6's: for a worker working or suspended (a suspended one may be given a role or lose one, which counts once they are back, PPL-7); none for one who left, whose roles stay as history; none on one's own page, where PPL-6's line says why (`staff.own`).
- *After a change*, one line says what it did (*Juma amepewa Muuzaji (Dawa). Kompyuta za duka zitajua baada ya kusawazisha.*), and the keyboard goes to it, as after PPL-6's buttons; the worker the route answers with is kept (`keepWorker`).
**Choices by Jack (19 Nov 2026):** split the row into PPL-8, PPL-12 and PPL-13, each built in a web session (A); the page as he pictured it: *Ongeza kazi* under the roles and keys, *Ondoa* beside each, the role or key first, then the side. Of the plan **(v1.135)**: the 38 keys as eight `Choice` groups under their headings, one key picked among them, so no shared design piece changes (A); no side picked at first, so a role on every side is never one careless tap away; the roles and keys in a card of their own under the status buttons, each set of buttons beside what it changes.
**Watch out:** from PPL-7 **(v1.119, v1.123, v1.124)**: *Ongeza kazi* and *Ondoa* call `give-role` (`id`, `role`, `side`), `give-key` (`id`, `permission`, `side`), `end-role` (`role_given`, `reason`) and `end-key` (`key`, `reason`), each answering with the worker as now. A give sends its own `id` (UUIDv7, ADR-0007): the same id for a try of the same choices, a new one once any changed, as `closure-form.tsx` does; `side` is null for *Pande zote*. `permission` is a choice in the schema: a test walks every code there, and fails when one has no name in both books or no group, so a key a later card adds can't show blank. An end has no id of its own: a retry after a lost answer hears `role.already_ended` or `permission.already_ended`, which the page takes as done: it reads the worker again and says the done line. The three who-may sentences (`staff.own`, `staff.superuser_only`, `staff.more_keys`) say "work status or details": they now name roles and keys too (PPL-7's note), and Jack checks the Swahili. Where each code shows: `staff.reason_required` beside *Sababu*, said at once with nothing sent, as the server answers a blank reason with it, never `blank` (`StaffReasonSerializer`) **(v1.135)**; above the buttons, `role.already_given`, `permission.already_given`, `role.superuser_only`, `staff.temporary_password_open`, `staff.own`, `staff.superuser_only`, `staff.more_keys`, `staff.left`, `demo.people_fixed`, `id.taken`, and the giver rule's bare 403, which the form's own lists make rare; after `staff.left`, the page reads the worker again, as PPL-6's does. Every code already has its two sentences (PPL-4, PPL-7). From UI-2 **(v1.44)**: a refusal shows with `ApiErrorAlert`, a box's code with `useApiErrorWords().fieldMessages`. *Hifadhi* is off while sending and offline. Reads are asked in the part always on the page, and handed down (`.claude/rules/frontend.md`). On the demo, the seed's people show the buttons, and the server's `demo.people_fixed` says why; a visitor's own worker can be given a role and lose it (PPL-7). From the old row **(v1.24, v1.103)**: a copied role's name is its template's code, translated in both books, whose six words PPL-6 brought.
**Checked** **(v1.137)**: merged as #303 on 19 Nov 2026, its every check green. On the live demo, Jack, as Meneja, added a fake worker, Zawadi; *Ongeza kazi* offered no *Mmiliki*; he gave her *Muuzaji* on Dawa, and the card showed it with the done line; he took it away with a *Sababu*, and the card said she holds none; his own page had no *Ongeza kazi* and no *Ondoa*. The rest (a key on every side, a give sent twice, an end after a lost answer, a worker who left, each refusal) is proven by the named tests (**Tests**), run in CI on #303. Jack checked the Swahili of each step at its commit.

**Tests:** `worker-roles.test.tsx` (new: a give, the same id for a try and a new one for a change, the choices allowed, no *Mmiliki*, a side changing the lists, an end with its reason, `already_ended` taken as done, each refusal, who sees the buttons, offline) · `worker-page.test.tsx` (keys by name) · `permission-words.test.ts` (new: every code in the schema has a name in both books and a group) · `errors.test.ts` · `e2e/workers.spec.ts` (the demo's Meneja gives a worker he added a role, and takes it away).
**Commits:** `docs: the ppl-8, ppl-12 and ppl-13 cards` (on main, before the branch) → `feat(frontend): a worker's extra keys show by name` → `feat(frontend): ongeza kazi gives a role or a key` → `feat(frontend): ondoa takes a role or a key away` → `test(e2e): the demo's manager gives a role and takes it away` → `docs(plan): ppl-8 says what it built`.
**Built** **(v1.135)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the four commits the card named after its own, with what it found on the way. *Keys by name:* `keys.ts` gives each of the 38 codes its group in a `Record` over the schema's `PermissionEnum`, so a code a later card adds fails `pnpm typecheck` until it has a group, and use-intl's typed keys fail it until it has a name (`workers.keys.<area>.<action>`, nested by the code's dots, as `errors.*` are); `permission-words.test.ts` checks the same against `shared/openapi.json` at run time, in both books and in the server's order. The roles and keys left the details for a card of their own under the status buttons (`worker-roles.tsx`), so *Badilisha* hides none of them; a worker with no key says *Hana ruhusa za ziada*; a code this app doesn't know yet shows as itself, last. *Ongeza kazi:* `giving.ts` holds the giver rule as plain functions (`heldOn`, `mayGive`, `rolesOffered`, `keysOffered`, `sidesOffered`), the server's `_check_giver` read from `me`, with tests of their own; the form (`give-form.tsx`) also leaves out what the worker already holds on that very side *(default)*, and each list narrows the other: a pick no longer on its list counts as none, worked out at each draw, never stored. The rule compares codes, never names: a manager trusted with the workers, who holds the Seller's keys on Dawa alone, may give the Cashier role there too, whose codes the Seller's include **(v1.136)**. Each side's row shows its band. The shop's roles are asked only once the form opens (`useRoles`, asked in `worker-page.tsx` and handed down). `id.taken` comes on the `id` field, which the form has no box for, so a field's code shows above the buttons. *Ondoa* (`end-form.tsx`): beside each role but *Mmiliki*, and each key; a screen reader hears what goes (*Ondoa Muuzaji (Dawa)*), the word seen kept inside the name; while a form is open no other button shows, on the whole page since the review **(v1.136)**; `role.already_ended` and `permission.already_ended` read the worker again and say the done line, *Umemwondolea Juma Muuzaji (Dawa).*, one sentence for a role (*cheo*) and a key (*ruhusa*), whose classes differ. The three who-may sentences name roles and keys. *A bug the old tests found:* the roles card first had the status buttons' React `key`, the worker's id, and React drew only one of the two; it has a key of its own now, and a test moves the page to another worker with a form open. Proven red: nine breaks of the give and nine of the end, each taken out on purpose and caught. Jack approved the Swahili of each step with its commit: the 38 keys, the 8 groups and the forms' 21 lines. *Honest note:* the browser test did not run in its session: building the image needs `pkg-containers.githubusercontent.com`, which the cloud environment's network refused, so CI's `e2e` job runs it first, on the pull request; every other check passed there. *For PPL-12:* *Weka PIN* and *Nenosiri la muda* can follow the roles card's way, one form open at a time on the page (`formIn`, `worker-page.tsx`) **(v1.136)**, a done line that takes the keyboard, *Ghairi* back to its button; `heldOn` and `mayGive` (`giving.ts`) read what a person holds on a side, for the line after a big key; the three who-may sentences say "roles and permissions" now, and the PIN's and the password's own codes keep theirs.
**Reviewed** **(v1.136)**: the reviewer's fresh look found no hole and nothing against a rule; Jack chose to fix what it found (A), in seven commits; each code fix, and the new tests of a pick off its list and of *Hifadhi* while sending, were seen to fail with the fix taken out. *Should fix:* the tests' Dawa-only giver held no `staff.manage` on the whole shop, so the server would never have shown her the page; she holds it now, and gives `staff.manage` itself on every side; no test walked a path that leaves a pick off its list, now one does, the role's own question asking again; *Simamisha*'s form and *Ongeza kazi*'s could be open together, two *Hifadhi* on one page, so the page keeps one form open (`formIn`), the other card's buttons hidden meanwhile; three comments said keys were counted until PPL-8. *Smaller:* each set of choices keeps its id while the form is open, not the last one's alone, so a give whose answer was lost, its side tried and left, comes back with its row, not `role.already_given`; *Hifadhi* turns off while there is nothing to pick, the line on the form saying why; the wait line is always on the form, so a screen reader reads it; a field's refusal the form has no box for shows above the buttons on *Ondoa* too (`aboutTheChange`); *Hifadhi* off while sending, and the English who-may sentences, have their tests. *A bug a new test found:* the page handed TanStack's whole result down, which draws a part again only when a value that part read changes, so a form opened after the roles' read had failed said *Subiri kidogo…* for good; the page reads `data`, `error` and `isPending` itself now (`RolesRead`), and `.claude/rules/frontend.md` says so. *Left as they are:* the band's swatch class is written in two places, as sharing it means a file outside PPL-8's (v1.127); `permission-words.test.ts` finds its file as `vectors.test.ts` does; the claim that `pnpm typecheck` misses an English key was wrong, as `MESSAGES` types English as Swahili's shape, which Claude proved by taking one out. *Checked in its session:* `make check`'s every line but the image's, whose host the cloud environment's network refused; the browser tests, all ten, on the image's own start steps run by hand on a fresh PostgreSQL 18.6; CI runs both on the real image.

### PPL-12 · PIN and temporary password: the page
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-8 · branch `feat/ppl-12-pin-password-page` **(v1.127)**
**Build:** on the worker's page, *Weka PIN*, so a new worker gets a PIN and with it a tile on the shop's laptops, and *Nenosiri la muda*, for one who works from their own phone. The PIN needs one route on the server; the password's is AUTH-6's. From PPL-8's row, with its notes and their marks.
- *The route:* `POST /api/v1/people/staff/{id}/pin/` (`pin`), on `StaffViewSet`, pinned to `staff.manage`, calling `set_pin(worker, pin, by=…)` with the person acting as `by` **(v1.35)**, and answering with the worker as now. Every variable is hidden from error reports (`@sensitive_variables`), as on the password's route: `pin` is the PIN. `StaffSerializer` gains `has_pin`, yes or no, never the hash *(default)*: the worker's page shows *PIN*: *Imewekwa* or *Haijawekwa*, so the owner sees who has no tile yet.
- *Weka PIN* **(v1.35)**: the owner hands his phone over, and the worker types their own PIN, face to face, as whoever types it knows it and nothing makes them change it. Two boxes, *PIN mpya* and *Andika tena*, four digits each, hidden, with the phone's number pad; two that differ are said at once, and nothing is sent. No *Sababu* *(default)*: `set_pin` takes none, as the worker types it himself, and the edit history keeps who set it and when (`pin_changed_at`; it skips the hash, ADR-0036). After it, one line **(v1.34)**: the tile shows on the shop's laptops after their next sync; a lock from wrong tries is lifted on every device; and logins made with the old PIN have ended (AUTH-4).
- *Nenosiri la muda* **(v1.29)**: the password, in a `PasswordField`, and *Sababu*, through `POST …/staff/{id}/temporary-password/`, answering 204. After it, one line: tell the worker this password; they log in with their phone number, shown, or with their email, and then choose their own (ADR-0038). The boxes empty, and the change keeps nothing in the store (`gcTime: 0`, UI-5). For a worker with no phone, the form says to add one with *Badilisha* first, as the server would refuse (`password.no_login_name`).
- *Before a big key* **(v1.29, v1.35)**: whoever set Juma's PIN or password knows it, so a big key given to Juma is given to them too. The password's case is the server's (`staff.temporary_password_open`). For the PIN, *Ongeza kazi* (PPL-8) gains one line under its choices: after giving a big role or key to someone whose PIN another person set, set them a fresh one yourself *(default)*.
- *Who sees the buttons:* as PPL-8's: a worker working or suspended; not one who left; not on one's own page.
- *The demo* (Jack: A): PINs and passwords stay as they are there (`demo.logins_fixed`), so both buttons are off, with one line saying why: a visitor's worker given a PIN would show his tile on every visitor's laptop until the night's reset (PPL-4's review **(v1.102)**). The page knows the demo from `askDemoOpen()`, as PPL-10's phone box does; the server refuses all the same.
**Choices by Jack (19 Nov 2026):** the two forms as he pictured them; on the demo, no PIN or password for anyone (A). Of the plan **(v1.146)**: Kuingia, a card of its own between the status buttons and the roles, as a way in is neither the job's status nor a role (A); the line under Ongeza kazi for any worker with a PIN, as the page can't tell who set it nor which keys are big (A); the demo asked once the Kuingia buttons show, so they are off before anyone taps (A); for a worker with no phone, a hint, not a block, as the page never sees an email and one with an email still logs in (A).
**Watch out:** reuse `set_pin` as it is: it asks who may (`pin.own`, `pin.superuser_only`, `pin.more_keys`), the demo, the PIN's strength, and lifts the locks, under its locks; the route only passes `by`. Where each code shows **(v1.44)**: `pin.not_four_digits` and `pin.too_easy` beside *PIN mpya*; the password's checks beside *Nenosiri*; `blank` and `staff.reason_required` beside *Sababu*; `password.no_login_name` above the boxes; above the buttons, `pin.own`, `pin.superuser_only` and `password.superuser_only` ("ask the superuser"), `pin.more_keys`, `password.own`, `password.more_keys`, `staff.left` and `demo.logins_fixed`. Every code already has its two sentences (AUTH-4, AUTH-6, UI-2); each new line of the page goes in both books. An owner's PIN and password are the superuser's alone: his PIN comes with PPL-13, his password from the admin, as today. *Hifadhi* is off while sending and offline. `pnpm gen:api` after the route. The live demo can show neither (Jack: A): check both by hand on the laptop's own stack (`make dev`), as ATT-3's camera was. Comments that name PPL-8 for the PIN or the password now name PPL-12, in the files this card touches (`apps/people/services.py`, `apps/people/api/views.py`, `worker-form.tsx`, `workers-screen.tsx`, `worker-page.tsx`, `errors.test.ts`, `test_admin.py`, `test_staff_routes.py`).
**Checked** **(v1.148)**: merged as #312 on 19 Nov 2026, its every check green, the browser test's step on the demo among them: Zawadi's page says *Haijawekwa*, with *Weka PIN* and *Nenosiri la muda* both off under the demo's line. Ticked by their tests: #2 (`test_a_new_pin_lifts_the_lock_and_ends_the_old_one`), #4 (`test_who_may_set_a_pin_reaches_the_route` and the forms' who-may tests), #5 (`worker-pin.test.tsx`, `worker-password.test.tsx`, `e2e/workers.spec.ts`), #6 (`test_committed_schema_matches_the_code`, `pnpm check:api`, Jack's yes to each step's Swahili). Open until Jack tries them by hand on `make dev` (Jack: A): #1 and #3, which need a laptop's sync and a phone's login. **Checked by hand** **(v1.152)**: on 20 Nov 2026, on `make dev`, once WAGE-1 had brought PPL-12 in: Meneja of the practice shop, given `staff.manage` for the test, added Juma, and Juma typed his own PIN twice; *Kuingia* said the PIN was set, and the registered laptop (Edge) showed Juma's tile beside Meneja's. Meneja added Asha with a made-up phone and set her a temporary password with a reason; in a separate window, as her phone, she logged in with the phone and that password, was asked to choose her own, chose it, and landed on her welcome. #1 and #3 ticked.

**Tests:** `test_staff_routes.py` (the PIN route: its key pinned, a refusal without it, the person acting as `by`, `has_pin`, no hash in the answer, the demo refused) · `worker-pin.test.tsx` (new: two boxes that differ, the strength codes beside the box, the line after, who sees the button, the demo, offline) · `worker-password.test.tsx` (new: the 204 and the line after it, with the phone; no phone; the boxes emptied; nothing kept in the store) · `errors.test.ts` · `e2e/workers.spec.ts` (on the demo, both buttons off).
**Commits:** `feat(people): set a worker's pin from the office` → `feat(frontend): weka pin on the worker's page` → `feat(frontend): nenosiri la muda on the worker's page` → `docs(plan): ppl-12 says what it built`.
**Built** **(v1.146)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the three commits the card named before this one. *The route:* `pin` on `StaffViewSet`, pinned to `staff.manage`, its every variable hidden from error reports, `by` the person acting; `SetPinSerializer` takes 16 characters at most, as the PIN login's does, so a page of text never reaches the slow hash, and leaves four digits and too easy to `set_pin`, so the shop hears one rule from one place; `has_pin` is `pin_hash != ""`, never the hash. Eleven new tests in `test_staff_routes.py`, twelve cases, and three old ones that now know `has_pin` and the route's key **(v1.147)**, Done when #2 among them: five wrong tries lock Asha on a laptop, a PIN through the route lifts it, the new PIN logs in and the old one is `pin.wrong`. *Kuingia* (`worker-logins.tsx`): *PIN: Imewekwa* or *Haijawekwa* for every worker; *Weka PIN* and *Nenosiri la muda* shown as PPL-8's buttons are; the page's one form at a time now spans three cards (`opened`, `elsewhere`, `worker-page.tsx`). *Weka PIN* (`pin-form.tsx`): two hidden boxes with the number pad, no eye, as the PIN pad has none; an empty first box and two that differ said at once; the strength codes beside *PIN mpya*. *Nenosiri la muda* (`password-form.tsx`): a `PasswordField` and *Sababu*, in the status forms' own words; `password.no_login_name` above the boxes; the done line with the phone as the shop writes it, or the email for one with none. Both boxes say `autoComplete="new-password"`, never `off`, which phones ignore on a hidden box: a box for a new secret is never filled with a saved one, so the owner's own password is never offered to a worker; a browser may still offer to save what is typed, and the owner says no **(v1.147)**. Both changes keep nothing in the store (`gcTime: 0`). *The demo:* both buttons off under one line, `demo.logins_fixed`'s own sentence. No new error code: every one already had its two sentences. Proven red: three breaks of the route, five of Weka PIN and five of Nenosiri la muda, each caught. Jack approved the Swahili of each step with its commit. *Honest notes:* the web session's network refused PostgreSQL 18's packages, so the backend tests ran on its PostgreSQL 16, with a stand-in `uuidv7()` in that local database only; and the browser test did not run, as building the image needs Docker: CI runs both for real, on the pull request.
