### PPL-13 · A shop and its owner, by the superuser
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-7 · branch `feat/ppl-13-shop-owner-commands` **(v1.127)**
**Build:** the superuser's way to open a real shop and set up its owner, typed on the server. Nobody in a shop may make someone Owner or set the Owner's PIN (ADR-0038, PPL-7), and the superuser is nobody's worker, so no office page can serve him; the admin can't either, as it opens no shop's rows (parking lot item 15). So two management commands in `people`, as `set_up_shop` is a `people` service. From PPL-8's row: the owner's PIN **(v1.35)** and the Owner role **(v1.119)**, the superuser's alone; and parking lot item 16 (Jack: A).
- *`manage.py open_shop`:* asks the shop's name and code, then each side: its letter, its name, its band (blue or striped) and how it sells (one screen, or writer, cashier and loader); then calls `set_up_shop`, which makes the live shop, its sides, each side's stock place and cash drawer, and the six roles, all or nothing. A code another shop holds is refused. It prints the shop's code and id, and the command to run next.
- *`manage.py owner add --shop MPC`:* asks the nickname first. Someone already working there by that name is given the Owner role on every side, after a yes. Anyone new: their full name, phone (may be empty), and PIN twice; then the person and job (`add_worker`), the Owner role on every side (`assign_role`) and the PIN (`set_pin`), all or nothing. A phone another person holds is refused, as on the office's form (PPL-10).
- *`manage.py owner set-pin --shop MPC --nickname Baba`:* asks the PIN twice and calls `set_pin`, which lifts any lock on every device and ends the logins made with the old PIN (AUTH-4). For any current worker of the shop *(default)*: the superuser may help anyone when no manager is there.
- *`manage.py owner take --shop MPC --nickname Baba`:* asks the reason, and ends the Owner role (`end_role`). Taking the last Owner's asks once more, as the shop is then left with nobody holding the owner's four keys *(default)*.
- *Who acts:* the superuser, as `by=None`, so each service's rules for the superuser hold (`staff.left`, the PIN's strength, `role.already_given`, `role.already_ended`), and the history names nobody, as for the seed. A refusal prints its code and English words, and stops, writing nothing.
- *A PIN is never an argument* *(default)*: read with `getpass`, never shown, so it stays out of the shell's history and the server's list of running programs; two that differ are asked again.
- *Refused on the demo* *(default)*: its seed makes its own shop and people, and keeps them as made (`settings.DEMO_SEED`, asked before anything else).
- *The admin's Add shop* goes (`has_add_permission` False on `ShopAdmin`): it made a shop with no sides, which couldn't work (SHOP-2); its header names `open_shop`. Editing a shop stays.
- *The runbook* `docs/runbooks/open-a-shop.md`: on the server, `docker compose exec backend python manage.py …` for each command, in order; then the owner's temporary password from the admin (ADR-0038), for his phone. LIVE-1 follows it (§16).
**Choices by Jack (19 Nov 2026):** what the superuser does for the owner, typed on the server: make Baba the Owner with his PIN, set him a new PIN, and take the Owner role away; and the shop too, so go-live needs no other card (A). While building **(v1.132)**, each A: `open_shop` and `owner add` show everything once more, and write only after `y` or `yes`, as a side's letter never changes (1); a PIN its rule refuses is asked again at once, not after the person is made (2); an owner with no phone is allowed, with a warning before the yes (3); `owner take` ends every open Owner row of the person, and only a working Owner counts as another, as a suspended one holds nothing (4); two parking lot items, 27 and 28 (5).
**Watch out:** each command finds the shop by its code, opens it with `shop_context(shop)` (never `system_context()`, rule 1), and opens the `recording_changes` block itself, so the services it calls join it (`.claude/rules/backend.md`). Reuse `set_up_shop`, `add_worker`, `assign_role`, `set_pin` and `end_role` as they are: no rule is copied, and no service changes. The nickname finds a current worker (unique among those who haven't left, `people_staffmember_nickname_unique`). A person at two shops waits for AUTH-7, so a phone that names someone working at another shop is refused like any taken phone. No migration and no route. Tests call each command with `call_command`, the answers handed in and `getpass` replaced.
**Done when:**
- [x] `open_shop` makes MPC with Dawa and Vyakula, each with its stock place and cash drawer, and the six roles; a taken code is refused and writes nothing.
- [x] `owner add` makes Baba MPC's Owner on every side, with his PIN, and his tile shows on the shop's laptops after their next sync; for a worker already there, it gives the role alone.
- [x] `owner set-pin` gives Baba a new PIN and lifts his lock; no PIN is ever shown, or taken as an argument.
- [x] `owner take` ends the Owner role with a reason, and asks once more for the last Owner.
- [x] Both commands are refused on the demo; the admin adds no shop.
- [x] The runbook gives the go-live steps in order.
**Built** **(v1.132)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the six commits it named. *The commands* are in `apps/people/management/`: `open_shop.py`; `owner.py`, one command with three subcommands (`add`, `set-pin`, `take`); and `asking.py`, what both share. It asks one question at a time, and asks again for an answer's shape (a blank, or a word off the list), and for one rule only: the PIN's strength, by its own function (choice 2). The PIN is read by `getpass`, twice. `refuse_on_demo` comes first, then `find_shop`, by a code typed in any case. `refused` prints each code, its field if any, and its words, and exits with 1. A band is typed as its value, a way of selling as `one` or `three`; a test holds every `SaleFlow` to a word. A nickname is found in any letter case among those who haven't left, as its own rule reads it, so `set-pin` and `take` find nobody who left. `set-pin` asks no yes: the PIN typed twice is one. `owner add` given a worker already there with no PIN names `owner set-pin`. The superuser acts as `by=None` everywhere. `owner` works inside `shop_context(shop)`, and opens `recording_changes` itself around more than one service, so a person's staff line is written once for each run. *The admin:* `has_add_permission` False on `ShopAdmin`; with no add page, its fields and read-only fields are two plain lists, every reason kept. *The tests* type their answers through a `keyboard` fixture (`apps/people/tests/conftest.py`; its `Keyboard`, `backend/tests/keyboard.py`, since the review) in place of `input()` and `getpass`: a question with no answer, or an answer never asked for, fails the test, so each test proves the questions too. *The runbook* runs each command as `docker compose -f compose.prod.yml exec backend python manage.py …`, names OPS-5 makes; never with `-T`, as `getpass` needs a terminal. *Found while building:* a rule with a code of our own (`shop.code_taken`, `side.code_taken`) is reported under no field, however many columns it has, as Django files a one-column rule under its field only when its code is plain `unique` (corrected by the review **(v1.133)**); a taken phone is refused by `create_user`'s own check, under `phone`, with Django's words; and a temporary password set again in the admin doesn't clear `staff.temporary_password_open`, as it writes no audit line and the first setter's still counts (it fails safe, and the runbook says so).
**Reviewed** **(v1.133)**: the reviewer's fresh look found no rule broken: the PIN is never shown or taken as an argument, every write is all or nothing, no service changed, and the merge of ATT-4 kept both cards' marks. Jack chose to fix all fourteen other findings (A), in eight commits. *Tenancy pinned:* only the guard kept `owner` inside one shop, and no test said so; now Baba at PILI is a new person at MFANO, and `set-pin` and `take` there find nobody (proven red with a lookup through every shop). *A nickname with a space* (Mama Juma), common in Swahili, is quoted in the `set-pin` line `owner add` prints (`shlex.quote`); the test runs the printed line. *The last-Owner words* claim only what holds: "No other Owner is working", and the owner's four keys stay only with someone given them one by one. *Comments* name ADR-0043, the server's command with its compose file, and what the admin does with a temporary password for someone with no phone: it takes it, and he can never log in with it (only the office's route refuses, `password.no_login_name`). *Tests:* the admin's edit page stores a typed code in capitals, the proof the add page's test carried; `Keyboard` moved to a helper module. *The runbook:* only `open_shop` and `owner add` wait for a final `y`; a rehearsal only on a database then thrown away, as a live shop on the laptop stops `make seed` for good, with the reset; no screen renames or adds a side (item 29); a nickname with a space in quotes; a refusal `owner add` can't meet taken off. Left by Jack's choice: the lookups as selectors (`selectors.py` is shared with other sessions, and only rare Unicode names differ), a friendly error for a shop with no roles (none exists), Ctrl-C's traceback (nothing is written), and commit `244fc27`'s subject (the squash title is what reaches `main`).
**Tests:** `apps/people/tests/test_open_shop.py` (new: the shop, its sides and roles; a taken code; the demo) · `apps/people/tests/test_owner_commands.py` (new: a new owner with his PIN; a worker already there; a taken phone; set-pin lifting the lock; take with its reason; the last Owner asked again; two PINs that differ; nothing written on a refusal; the demo) · `apps/shops/tests/test_admin.py` (no add page). As built **(v1.132)**, also: in `test_open_shop.py`, everything shown before the yes, no yes but `y` or `yes`, a code typed in small letters, an answer off the list asked again, two sides with one letter, and a word for every way of selling; in `test_owner_commands.py`, the questions asked and the PIN shown nowhere, `--pin` refused, a PIN its rule refuses asked again, the warning for no phone, an Owner already, an unknown shop, `set-pin` for a suspended worker, nobody found who left, every Owner row ended, a suspended Owner not counted, a blank reason asked again, and no Owner role to take; the `keyboard` fixture (`conftest.py`, new).
**Commits:** `feat(people): open a shop from the server` → `feat(people): the superuser makes a shop's owner, with his pin` → `feat(people): the superuser sets a pin or takes the owner role away` → `feat(shops): the admin adds no shop without its sides` → `docs(runbooks): open a shop and make its owner` → `docs(plan): ppl-13 says what it built`. From the review **(v1.133)**: `test(people): the owner commands never find another shop's worker` → `fix(people): a printed command quotes a nickname with a space` → `fix(people): the last-owner warning says only what is true` → `docs(people): comments name the current ADR, the server's command and what the admin's password does` → `test(shops): the admin's edit page stores a code in capitals` → `refactor(people): the keyboard helper lives in a test module` → `docs(runbooks): the runbook follows the review` → `docs(plan): ppl-13 follows its review`.

### ATT-4 · Attendance day: the rule
`M` · Jack-led · after ATT-1, ATT-2, ATT-8 · branch `feat/att-4-attendance-day-rule` **(v1.128)**
**Learn:** business rules written test-first: the owner's table becomes named tests, red before any code, green once the code says what he said; windows of time and their edges, where "by" and "from" take their own minute and "before" doesn't; a pure function, which reads no database and no clock, so each case is one short test.
**Build:** the rule that says what one worker's day was, from that day's taps and that day's rules, in `apps/staffpay/calculations.py`. Pure, as `check_time` is (03 §5.2): no database, no clock of its own. Nothing is saved and nothing shows on a screen: ATT-10 keeps each day, ATT-5 shows them.
- `shop_minute(moment, zone)`: a tap's time as a minute on the shop's clock, as the counter shows it: 04:35:40 in UTC is 07:35 in Arusha.
- `DayRules`: the seven times and the two fines of one day (02 §12), as `get_setting` answers them on that date; ATT-10 reads them (A1).
- `Tap`: in or out, and its minute, or none when no time is trusted (`unverified`, ATT-1).
- `work_out_day(taps, rules)` → `DayResult`: the status (`on_time`, `late`, `half`, `absent` or `needs_review`), the fine, the first clock-in and the first clock-out after it, which ends the day **(v1.130)**. The statuses are `DayStatus` in `apps/staffpay/models.py`, with `off` (a Sunday, a closed day), which is ATT-10's to give, so its table takes them as they are.
- *Arriving:* by `on_time_until`, on time; then late with the first fine, or with the second; from `afternoon_from` to `midday`, the afternoon's half day; any other minute, *dharura*. *Leaving,* after a morning arrival: from `closing`, the full day, or the late one with its fine; from `midday` to `morning_leave_until`, the morning's half day; any other minute, *dharura*. After an afternoon arrival: from `closing`, its half day; earlier, *dharura*. No clock-out: *dharura*.
**Choices by the owner (19 Nov 2026, through Jack):** a late arrival who leaves for the morning's half day is a half day less the fine of their arrival: in at 07:50, out at 13:10, a half day less the first fine (A). The question was ATT-2's **(v1.86)**; spec §8.1 and 02 §12 now say it (01 v1.6, 02 v1.42).
**Choices by Jack (19 Nov 2026):** the row asked for the rule and the book that keeps each day, twice a card together, so it is split: ATT-4 is the rule, Jack-led; ATT-10, a new row in ATT-4's place in the table, is the book, and takes every note the row had for it (A). ATT-5, WAGE-7, WAGE-2 and ATT-7 come after ATT-10.
**Claude's defaults:** of several taps, the first clock-in counts: *Ingia* tapped at 07:20 and again at 07:22 is one arrival at 07:20; the day ends at the first Toka after it (Jack, at the review) **(v1.130)**. A tap with no trusted time makes the day need review, whatever the other taps say (ATT-1). A clock-out with no clock-in needs review. No taps: `absent`; the absence's kind (office duty, permission, no-show) is ATT-6's. Only a late day, or a late morning half day, carries a fine; a fine of 0, as every shop starts with (ATT-2), still makes the day late, with nothing taken off. The answer gives no reason for *dharura*: the manager sees the taps beside the day (ATT-5). Server only: the day is worked out on the server (03 §5.8), so no TypeScript twin and no shared test vectors, as for `check_time`.
**Watch out:** "by" and "from" take their own minute, "before" and "after" don't (02 §12): 07:35 is on time, 08:00 takes the second fine, 13:00 is still the afternoon's arrival, 13:30 still the morning's half day, 18:40 the full day. The seven times keep their order on every day (ATT-2), so each window can start where the one before it ends; the rule trusts the order and checks nothing. Compare minutes, never times with seconds: `shop_minute` drops them, which a test at 07:35:59 proves. A fine is `Decimal` (rule 3), copied from the rules, never worked out. A voided tap never reaches the rule: ATT-10 leaves it out. The fines in the tests are the demo's made-up 500 and 1,500 (rule 10). The note in `apps/staffpay/models.py` that ATT-4 adds the index a month of events is read by now says ATT-10.
**Built** **(v1.129)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the five commits after the card's; at step 2 Jack chose that Claude writes the code as well as the tests, and he approves each step. One change of order: the shapes came with the arrival, where the tests first use them, so the minute's commit holds one idea. `shop_minute` refuses a time with no zone (`ValueError`): Python would read it on the server's UTC clock and turn Asha's 07:35 into 10:35. `work_out_day` reads the arrival's windows in the owner's order, then the leaving: from `closing` the full day, from `midday` to `morning_leave_until` the morning's half day with its arrival's fine, any other minute *dharura*; the taps may come in any order; `NO_FINE` is `Decimal("0.00")`. `DayStatus` sits in `apps/staffpay/models.py`, with no migration. 03 §6.6 says how a day reads its taps (03 v1.80). Proven red: "by 07:35" read as "before", and the late half day's fine dropped, each put in on purpose. `apps/staffpay/calculations.py` is covered in full by its tests, every line and branch.
**From the review** **(v1.130)**: nothing broke a rule, and Jack chose to fix every finding, each in its own commit. *Toka ends the day* (Jack): at MPC nobody taps out for lunch, so a day runs from the first Ingia to the first Toka after it, and a tap after that Toka counts for nothing; the rule read to the last Toka, which paid a half day to someone back from lunch who never clocked out. A Toka tapped by mistake, Mparee's at 11:06, sends the day to a manager, who mends it with a reason (ATT-6); a Toka before any Ingia ends no day. The answer's `last_out` is `first_out`, as 02 §12's column is now; and the counter, which offers Ingia again after a Toka, gets a note on ATT-6's row (Jack: A). *Seconds:* a tap refuses a minute with seconds (`ValueError`), should ATT-10 forget `shop_minute`: 07:35:40 is after 07:35, and a worker on time would be fined. *Proofs:* an afternoon arrival leaving at 13:10 needs review, proven red by letting it fall through to the morning's leaving; a late day sent to review takes no fine; a fine is a `Decimal` on every kind of day. *Comments:* why `NO_FINE` has two places; the answer's taps are the trusted ones; `models.py` owns `DayStatus`; a late morning's half keeps its fine; the book's jobs name ATT-10 (`shops`, the laptop's switch, 03 §5.8). *Docs:* ATT-10's row says a day reads each event's `trusted_time`, its taps grouped by the shop's date; ATT-6's row reads a dharura day's fine again when it is decided late; WAGE-2's row asks whether a day's pay stops at 0; 01 §8.1 and 02 §12 say Toka ends the day; 03 §6.6 follows.
**Done when:**
- [x] Every row of spec §8.1's table is a named test and passes, the owner's new row among them.
- [x] Each window's edge minute has its test; 07:35:40 on the shop's clock is on time.
- [x] A tap with no trusted time, or a clock-out with no clock-in, makes the day need review; no taps make it absent.
- [x] The rule reads nothing itself: the same taps and rules always give the same answer; calculations keep at least 90% coverage.
- [x] ATT-10's row holds every note ATT-4's row had for the book.
**Tests:** in `apps/staffpay/tests/test_attendance_day.py` (new), the table's rows: `test_arrives_0720_leaves_at_closing_full_day` · `test_arrives_0750_full_day_minus_fine` · `test_arrives_0810_full_day_minus_the_second_fine` · `test_arrives_0900_needs_review` · `test_arrives_1230_half_day` · `test_arrives_1330_needs_review` · `test_arrives_0720_leaves_1310_half_day` · `test_arrives_0720_leaves_1200_needs_review` · `test_arrives_0720_leaves_1600_needs_review` · `test_arrives_0720_never_clocks_out_needs_review` · `test_doesnt_come_absent` · `test_arrives_0750_leaves_1310_half_day_minus_fine`; and `test_shop_minute_reads_the_shops_clock` · `test_a_tap_at_073540_is_on_time` · `test_each_arrival_window_takes_its_own_minute` · `test_each_leaving_window_takes_its_own_minute` · `test_arrives_1230_leaves_1600_needs_review` · `test_a_shop_with_no_fine_takes_nothing` · `test_a_day_reads_the_rules_it_is_given` · `test_a_tap_with_no_trusted_time_needs_review` · `test_clock_out_without_clock_in_needs_review` · `test_a_tap_made_twice_counts_once` (`test_first_in_and_last_out_count` until the review) **(v1.130)**. Built too **(v1.129)**: `test_shop_minute_drops_the_seconds` · `test_shop_minute_refuses_a_time_with_no_zone`. From the review **(v1.130)**: `test_the_first_toka_ends_the_day` · `test_a_toka_before_the_ingia_ends_nothing` · `test_a_tap_with_seconds_refused` · `test_arrives_1230_leaves_1310_needs_review` · `test_a_late_day_sent_to_review_takes_no_fine` · `test_a_fine_is_a_decimal`.
**Commits:** `docs: the att-4 card` → `feat(staffpay): a tap's minute on the shop's clock` → `feat(staffpay): the arrival decides on time, a fine or dharura` → `feat(staffpay): the leaving decides a full day, a half day or dharura` → `feat(staffpay): a day with no taps, or a doubtful one` → `docs(plan): att-4 says what it built` **(v1.129)**, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the att-4 card follows the review` **(v1.130)**

### ATT-10 · Attendance days: the book
`M` · Claude-led · after ATT-4 · branch `feat/att-10-attendance-days` **(v1.138)**
**Learn:** a table of answers worked out from a ledger: the taps stay the truth, and each day can be worked out again from them as often as needed, until its pay run is approved; one row per worker per day, however often it runs (an upsert: add the row, or change the one already there); a day's edges on the shop's clock, as 21:30 in UTC on Monday is 00:30 on Tuesday in Arusha.
**Build:** the book of days, in `apps/staffpay`: every worker's day, worked out by ATT-4's rule and kept. No route and no screen: ATT-5 shows the days, WAGE-2 pays them, ATT-7 works them out again each night.
- `AttendanceDay` (02 §12): the job (`staff`), its date on the shop's clock, `status` (`DayStatus`), `fine`, `first_in` and `first_out` (minutes on the shop's clock, or none), and `rules_used`, the day's rules copied (T4). One row per job and date. Not a ledger: a day is worked out again freely until its pay run is approved (WAGE-3), so it carries the edit history's trigger, as every shop table that is not a ledger does (ADR-0036), with its link code `attendance_day`. `override` and `absence_type` come with ATT-6, `meal_share` with WAGE-7, the lock with WAGE-3, each with its migration.
- `day_rules(shop, day)` in `apps/staffpay/selectors.py`: the `DayRules` in force on that date, each from `get_setting(…, on=day)` (A1).
- `work_out_days(shop, first, last)` in `apps/staffpay/services.py`: works out each day from `first` to `last`, both counted, for every job of the shop, keeps them, and answers them. ATT-5, WAGE-2 and ATT-7 call it, when they ask for a month and each night *(the row's default)*.
- *Which days:* a date counts only while `attendance.enabled` is on (ADR-0033), only from the job's first day (`started_on`) to its last (`left_on`), and only before the shop's today. Sunday is `off`. A closed day (`closed_days`, `apps/shops/selectors.py`) is `off`, unless the job has a tap that day: then it needs review (Jack). Every other date is `work_out_day(taps, day_rules(shop, day))`.
- *Which taps:* the job's events, a voided one left out (T2), each read by its `trusted_time` as a minute on the shop's clock (`shop_minute`), never by its `client_time`, the laptop's own clock. An `unverified` one has no trusted time, so it goes in as a tap with no minute (`Tap(kind, None)`), and its day needs review. Each tap falls on its date on the shop's clock (`ZoneInfo(shop.timezone)`): its trusted time's, or, for one with none, its laptop's time's, the only one it has.
- The index that reads a month of events, leading with `shop`, by their trusted time (the note in `apps/staffpay/models.py`).

**Choices by Jack (19 Nov 2026):** the row asked for the book and for the taps the server flagged, twice a card together, so it is split: ATT-10 is the book; ATT-11, a new row in its place in the table, takes the flagged taps: a suspended or gone worker's clock-in, a tap named in another's `unverified_time` flag, a tap before a first day moved later (A). WAGE-2 comes after ATT-11 too. *A tap on a closed day* sends its day to review: the owner closes the shop at 10:00 for a funeral, and Asha clocked in at 07:20, so the owner or a manager decides whether her morning is paid (B). 01 §8.1 and 02 §12 say so (01 v1.7, 02 v1.43).
**Claude's defaults:** *Today* is not kept until it is over: at 11:00 Asha, in since 07:20, has no Toka yet, and the rule would call her day dharura. A span reaching today stops at yesterday; ATT-5 shows today's taps as they come. *Sunday* stays `off`, even with a tap: Sunday work is a deal outside the system (01 §8.1). *Two jobs on one day* (PPL-11): a person who left and is hired again the same day has two jobs, and each reads only its own taps (`staff`, the job whose PIN tapped), so the old job's last day and the new one's first are two days; a day split between them, Ingia on the old and Toka on the new, needs review on both, so it is never paid twice. *A day that comes out the same is not written again,* so the edit history keeps only real changes: a tap that came late, a tap voided (ATT-6), a job's first or last day moved (PPL-10); never a rule, as none starts on a day gone by (`setting.date_past`) **(v1.140)**. *A kept day that no longer counts,* as its job's first day was moved later (PPL-10): its row stays (rule 7), and `work_out_days` no longer answers it; ATT-11 decides what such a day shows. *Two runs at once* (two managers open the month, or the night's job runs): one row per job and date, and one run per shop at a time, the second waiting for the first and reading the taps afresh, where the card first had an upsert (the review) **(v1.140)**. *The rules are read once for the span,* for every worker, where the card first had once per date (the review) **(v1.140)**. Server only, as the rule is (03 §6.6).
**Watch out:** a day's edges are the shop's midnights, not UTC's: a tap at 21:30 UTC on Monday is Tuesday 00:30 in Arusha, so a span's events are read from the shop's midnight starting its first day to the one ending its last. `get_setting` and `closed_days` take the shop's dates, never datetimes, and need the shop open: the book reads through the shop's guard, in the shop the visit opened (ADR-0022), and ATT-7's night job opens each shop in turn. `first_in` and `first_out` are the rule's, minutes with no seconds. `rules_used` keeps money and times as text (rule 3), a copy, never read back as today's rules. A fine is `Decimal`, copied from the rule's answer. The fines in the tests are the demo's made-up 500 and 1,500 (rule 10). No `record_change`: no laptop keeps a copy of the days, as payslips never sit on shared devices (03 §6.1) **(v1.139)**. No route reads the table yet, so the tenancy sweep needs no row of it (`backend/tests/conftest.py` gives rows to tables a route shows); its table checks (a shop, `PROTECT` links, the guard) find it by themselves **(v1.139)**.
**Built** **(v1.139)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the six commits after the card's. *The table:* `AttendanceDay` in `apps/staffpay/models.py`, with two rules in its migration (`0003_attendance_days`): one day per job and date, on `(shop, date, staff)`, which is also the index a month of the shop's days is read by; and a day that fits its status, no fine on an off, absent, on-time or review day, and both taps on an on-time, late or half day. The edit history's trigger, and one refusing a delete (`NoDeleteTrigger`), as closures have; the link code `attendance_day`. The events gain the index `(shop, trusted_time)`. *The rules:* `day_rules` and `rules_as_text` in `apps/staffpay/selectors.py`, the nine rules from one list (`DAY_RULE_SETTINGS`), each copied as its setting's kind writes it. *The book:* `work_out_days` in `apps/staffpay/services.py`, in one transaction; the jobs in the order of their ids, so two runs write in the same order; a day that comes out the same is not written; a changed one is an UPDATE by its id, a new one an upsert, read back after it, as a run that lost the race to another answered an id no row has, which `test_working_out_twice_keeps_one_row` found (until the review: now a plain INSERT, as the jobs' lock lets one run at a time) **(v1.140)**. A closed day with a tap keeps its taps' minutes and the day's rules beside its review, for whoever decides it, so only an off day keeps no rules (Claude's default, said before its step). 02 §12 and 03 §6.6 say how the book works (02 v1.44, 03 v1.81). Proven red: a voided tap counted, the laptop's time read, UTC's date, a closed day's review dropped, a closed day ignored, Sunday worked out, and the switch, the job's days and the today cut each left out, each put in on purpose. The book's services and selectors are covered in full by their tests.
**From the review** **(v1.140)**: nothing broke a rule, and no day was paid wrongly in normal running. Jack chose to fix every finding (A), each in its own commit, but three that touched the same lines and the same test (two runs at once, their comments, and a real test of them). *One run per shop at a time:* two managers opening November at once could write an older answer over a newer one (Asha's Toka came between their reads) or deadlock; `work_out_days` now locks the shop's jobs (`select_for_update(no_key=True)`, in the order of their ids), so the second run waits and reads the taps afresh, and a new day is a plain INSERT, the one-day rule its last guard; a tap pushed meanwhile still saves, its link taking only a KEY SHARE lock. Each read sees what was committed when it began (READ COMMITTED), as the comments now say. *Written as nobody:* inside a manager's visit the edit history would have named the manager for the book's change; the book writes as the system, from no device (`acting_as`, ADR-0036). *One read for a span:* `shop_settings_between` (`apps/shops/selectors.py`) answers each of a list of the shop's rules on each day of a span as `get_setting` would, in one read; `day_rules_between` builds on it, and the book reads the switch the same way: a month takes 11 queries, not some 300. Each kept day comes with its worker (`select_related`). *Only an off day keeps no rules*, a database rule (`staffpay_attendanceday_rules_kept_unless_off`, in `0003`, not yet on main). *Comments:* a day changes for a late tap, a void or a moved first day, never a rule, as none starts on a day gone by (`setting.date_past`); a suspended worker's taps count until ATT-11. *Who is in the book* (Jack: A): every job, the owner's too, absent every working day as he never clocks in; ATT-5 and WAGE-2 show and pay only workers with a daily pay rate, as their rows say.
**Done when:**
- [x] Each worker has one kept day per date, worked out by ATT-4's rule from that date's taps and rules, the rules copied.
- [x] Sunday and a closed day are off; a tap on a closed day makes it need review.
- [x] No day is kept before attendance is on, outside the job's first and last day, or for today.
- [x] A voided tap counts for nothing; a tap is read by its trusted time, on the shop's date; an unverified tap makes its day need review.
- [x] Worked out again, a day follows a changed rule or a late tap, and one that comes out the same isn't written again.
- [x] The tenancy sweep and the edit history's check pass with the new table; services and calculations keep at least 90% coverage.
- [x] ATT-11's row holds every note ATT-10's row had for the flagged taps.
**Tests:** in `apps/staffpay/tests/test_attendance_book.py` (new): `test_a_day_is_kept_with_its_rules` · `test_one_row_per_worker_per_day` · `test_sunday_is_off` · `test_a_sunday_tap_is_still_off` · `test_a_closed_day_is_off` · `test_a_tap_on_a_closed_day_needs_review` · `test_no_day_before_attendance_is_on` · `test_no_day_before_the_first_day_or_after_the_last` · `test_today_is_not_kept` · `test_a_voided_tap_counts_for_nothing` · `test_a_day_reads_the_trusted_time` · `test_a_tap_falls_on_the_shops_date` · `test_an_unverified_tap_needs_review` · `test_an_unverified_tap_falls_on_its_laptops_date` · `test_a_day_follows_a_rule_changed_later` · `test_a_day_that_comes_out_the_same_is_not_written_again` · `test_working_out_twice_keeps_one_row` · `test_two_jobs_on_one_day_read_their_own_taps` · `test_a_day_no_longer_counted_is_not_answered`; in `apps/staffpay/tests/test_day_rules.py` (new): `test_day_rules_read_each_rule_on_its_date`. Built too **(v1.139)**: `test_a_day_fits_its_status` · `test_each_status_saves_as_it_should` · `test_database_refuses_to_delete_a_day` · `test_a_day_with_no_taps_is_absent` · `test_a_day_follows_a_tap_that_came_late` · `test_work_out_days_refuses_a_moment_or_another_shop`; in `test_day_rules.py`: `test_day_rules_read_only_the_shops_own` · `test_a_days_rules_are_kept_as_text`. From the review **(v1.140)**: `test_a_second_run_waits_for_the_first` (two connections) · `test_the_book_writes_as_nobody` · `test_the_nights_job_keeps_another_shops_workers_out` · `test_today_is_the_shops_today_not_utcs` · `test_a_month_is_read_in_a_few_queries` · `test_each_day_comes_with_its_worker` · `test_only_an_off_day_keeps_no_rules`; in `apps/shops/tests/test_get_setting.py`: `test_settings_between_answers_as_get_setting_on_each_day` · `test_settings_between_refuses_wrong_arguments`.
**Commits:** `docs: the att-10 card` → `feat(staffpay): the book of days keeps one row per worker per day` → `feat(staffpay): the day's rules as they stood on its date` → `feat(staffpay): the book works out each day from its taps` → `feat(staffpay): sundays and closed days are off` → `feat(staffpay): days count only while attendance is on and the job lasts` → `docs(plan): att-10 says what it built`, then `main` brought in, the review's fixes, and `docs: the att-10 card follows the review` **(v1.140)**

### ATT-11 · Attendance days: the flagged taps
`M` · Claude-led · after ATT-10 · branch `feat/att-11-flagged-taps` **(v1.142)**
**Learn:** reading another app's table without importing it: a link code names the table at run time (`table_of`, `apps/core/links.py`), as `staffpay` sits below `sync` (ADR-0043); a subquery (`EXISTS`) that asks, for each tap of a month, whether a flag sits on it, in the same one read; a fact kept at the moment it is known, on the new row, as a ledger's old row never changes.
**Build:** the taps the book of days (ATT-10) can't take as they are. Each sends its day to review (`needs_review`), for a manager or the owner to decide (ATT-6). In `apps/staffpay`, server only. No route and no screen: ATT-5 shows the days, ATT-6 decides them.
- *A suspended or gone worker's tap:* Baba suspends Juma on Monday at 11:00; the Dawa laptop, offline, doesn't know, and on Tuesday Juma clocks in at 07:20. The push flags it on the tap's line in the answer log (`suspended_staff_activity`, about the `pushed_document`, X2), not on the event, whose own check can be `server` or `device_ok`, so the book counts his Tuesday as any other. Now the book reads those flags too: the line found by its `document_id`, the event's own id, through its link code (`table_of("pushed_document")`), as `staffpay` may not import `sync` (ADR-0043), in the same read as the taps.
- *The tap a later one disagrees with:* the Dawa laptop's clock goes wrong. Juma's Toka says 18:45, then Asha's, the record after it, says 18:30. One of the two times is wrong, and nobody knows which: Asha's tap is `unverified`, and its `unverified_time` flag names Juma too (ATT-1's review), but Juma's tap keeps its trusted time and its day counts as normal. Now Juma's day needs review too (Jack: A). A flag names people, not records, so the clock-in keeps the record its time disagrees with as it is saved (`disagrees_with`, that record's document id), only when that sign is the reason (`Sign.RECORD_BEFORE_IS_LATER`); the push hands the id in beside `made_before_by` (`_record_before`, `apps/sync/push.py`). A tap whose id another tap keeps sends its own day to review, whoever made either.
- *A tap outside the job's days:* Asha is added with a first day of 10 Nov and clocks in on the 10th and the 11th; a manager then moves her first day to 12 Nov (PPL-10). Her two taps now fall before her first day, so those two days need review (Jack: A): the manager puts her first day back, or decides whether they are paid. The same for a tap after a job's last day. A day outside the job with no tap stays as ATT-10 left it: not answered, and its row kept if it was kept before (rule 7).
- *A day sent to review by a tap in doubt* keeps its rule's minutes and the day's rules, for whoever decides it, and takes no fine (`NO_FINE`), as a closed day with a tap does (ATT-10) *(the row's default)*.

**Choices by Jack (19 Nov 2026):** *the tap a later one disagrees with* sends its day to review too, as its time can be the wrong one (A). *A tap before a job's first day,* or after its last, sends its day to review, rather than counting for nothing (A). 01 §8.1 and 02 §12 say so (01 v1.8, 02 v1.46).
**Claude's defaults:** *A flag counts whatever its status:* a manager who closes Juma's flag has looked at the tap, not decided the day, which is ATT-6's (`override`). *Only the suspension's flag on a line counts:* a `device_gap` flag on the same line, a missing number, says nothing about whether the worker came. *A voided tap sends nothing,* as it counts for nothing (T2): the manager voids Juma's tap, and his Tuesday is absent. *Sunday stays `off`,* even with a tap in doubt (01 §8.1); a day before attendance was on, or today, is still not kept (ATT-10). *A carried tap* (SYNC-12) is `unverified` already, so its day needs review whatever its loader's flag says; its time is checked against no record before, so it keeps none. *The record kept, not found again:* the book could look for the record before through the answer log, but the flag names only people, so Juma's own two taps that disagree would look like one person's flag, and the search would copy `_record_before`'s rules into `staffpay`; the push already has the record, so the tap keeps it. *No reason on the day,* as ATT-4 gives none for dharura: ATT-5 shows each tap beside its day, with its flag, the tap it disagrees with, or the job's first and last day. Server only (03 §6.6).
**Watch out:** the record before can be any document, a sale once SALE-2 comes, so `disagrees_with` keeps its id with no foreign key, and only a tap's id sends a day. The tap it names may be another day's, or another month's: the book asks, for each tap of its span, whether any tap names it, never only the taps in the span. `AttendanceEvent` is a ledger (rule 2): the new column is written once, by the clock-in's saving code, never afterwards, so taps saved before it keep none; a database rule allows it only on an `unverified` tap, with its migration read line by line (rule 9). The flags and the answer log are read through the shop's guard, as every read is (ADR-0022), and a month stays a few queries (`test_a_month_is_read_in_a_few_queries`). Two comments in `apps/staffpay/services.py` wait for this card: that a suspended worker's taps count until ATT-11, and that ATT-11 decides what a day before a moved first day shows.
**Built** **(v1.143)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the four commits after the card's. *The suspension's flag:* `_taps` (`apps/staffpay/services.py`) asks it of each tap in the taps' own query (`EXISTS`), the answer log named by its code (`ANSWER_LOG`) and read through `_answer_log()`, typed `Any`, as the type checker can't know the columns of a table it may not import, so its `document_id` is proven by the tests instead; the shop's flags of that kind are read once per run. *The record before:* `disagrees_with` on `AttendanceEvent`, with the rule `staffpay_attendanceevent_disagrees_only_unverified` (`0004_disagrees_with`); the void-stamp trigger compares every column, so it freezes the new one with no change. `_record_before` (`apps/sync/push.py`) gives the record's own id too, the push hands it in (`Document.record_before`), and `post_clock` keeps it only for that sign. The book asks of each tap whether any tap of the shop keeps its id, wherever that tap fell, through a partial index of the taps that keep one (`staffpay_event_by_disagreement`, `0005_disagreement_index`), which the planner uses (an index-only scan). One change from the card, found while writing its tests: only a tap timed by the laptop's clock (`device_ok`) is in doubt for a disagreement, as the row's own words had it: a tap sent at once has the server's time, which no laptop's clock can make wrong. And the tap that disagrees counts voided or not: a void says a tap counts for nothing, not that the laptop's clock was right. *Outside the job's days:* each date reads every job (`_ran_on`), one outside its days only where it tapped; on a Sunday such a job keeps no day, as off pays nothing. No laptop gets the new column: the pull lists no taps. 02 §12 and 03 §6.6 say how (02 v1.47, 03 v1.83). Proven red: each behaviour's tests failed before its code; the guards (another flag, a voided tap, the server's time, Sunday) passed before and after.
**From the review** **(v1.144)**: nothing broke a rule, and no day was paid wrongly. Jack chose to fix all ten findings (A), each code fix in its own commit and the docs in this one. *Another shop's taps:* shop B's laptop can send a document with the id of one of shop A's taps, set aside (`id.taken`) but kept as a line in B's log, and B's next clock-in can keep that id as the record it disagrees with; under the night's job (`system_context`) only the book's own `shop=` keeps them from A's days, which a test now pins. *Comments:* three kinds of day go to review, not two; every flag of the shop is read once per run, as no index has the kind, cheap at a shop's size; a sentence of the push's cut by an edit is whole again. *A rescued tap* keeps no record, though its laptop's record before has a later time: its time came from a file, checked against no record. *From the push to the book:* the two halves the book reads, built by hand in its own tests, are proven to join through the real push, a suspended worker's taps and two taps that disagree. *Docs:* 01 §8.1 says a tap the server timed is never in doubt; 02 §12 that a day before a moved first day is read again when the job tapped on it; 03 §6.6 which two of `sync`'s facts the book reads by name, and which tests guard them; ATT-5's and ATT-6's rows that the review list is the book's answer (`work_out_days`), never a row's `status` in the table, as a kept row outside the job's days stays as it was once its tap is voided.
**Done when:**
- [x] A tap whose line carries a `suspended_staff_activity` flag sends its day to review, whatever the flag's status; a voided one sends nothing.
- [x] A tap timed by the laptop's clock that a later tap's time disagrees with sends its day to review, whoever made either, on whatever day it fell; one with the server's time doesn't **(v1.143)**.
- [x] A tap before a job's first day, or after its last, sends that day to review; a day outside the job with no tap is not answered.
- [x] A day sent to review this way keeps its minutes and its rules, and takes no fine; Sunday stays off.
- [x] Services and calculations keep at least 90% coverage; a month is still read in a few queries.
- [x] ATT-5's, ATT-6's and WAGE-2's rows hold what they inherit.
**Tests:** in `apps/staffpay/tests/test_attendance_book.py`: `test_a_suspended_workers_tap_needs_review` · `test_a_gone_workers_tap_needs_review` · `test_a_closed_suspension_flag_still_needs_review` · `test_another_flag_on_the_line_sends_nothing` · `test_a_voided_flagged_tap_sends_nothing` · `test_the_tap_a_later_one_disagrees_with_needs_review` · `test_a_disagreement_reaches_another_day` · `test_a_tap_before_the_first_day_needs_review` · `test_a_tap_after_the_last_day_needs_review` · `test_a_kept_day_before_a_moved_first_day_turns_to_review` · `test_a_sunday_tap_in_doubt_is_still_off` · `test_a_day_in_doubt_keeps_its_minutes_and_rules`; in `apps/sync/tests/test_clock_in.py`: `test_a_clock_in_keeps_the_record_it_disagrees_with` · `test_another_sign_keeps_no_record`; in `apps/staffpay/tests/test_attendance_event.py`: `test_only_an_unverified_tap_disagrees`. Built too **(v1.143)**: `test_a_tap_timed_by_the_server_is_not_in_doubt` · `test_a_voided_tap_disagreed_with_sends_nothing`. From the review **(v1.144)**: `test_another_shops_taps_never_send_this_shops_day`; in `apps/sync/tests/test_carried.py`: `test_a_carried_clock_in_keeps_no_record_it_disagrees_with`; in `apps/sync/tests/test_clock_in.py`: `test_a_suspended_workers_pushed_tap_sends_its_day_to_review` · `test_two_pushed_taps_that_disagree_send_both_days_to_review`.
**Commits:** `docs: the att-11 card` → `feat(staffpay): a suspended or gone worker's tap sends its day to review` → `feat(staffpay): a clock-in keeps the record its time disagrees with` → `feat(staffpay): the tap a later one disagrees with sends its day to review` → `feat(staffpay): a tap outside the job's days sends its day to review` → `docs(plan): att-11 says what it built`, then the review's fixes, and `docs: the att-11 card follows the review` **(v1.144)**

### WAGE-1 · Pay rates
`M` · Claude-led · after PPL-6 · branch `feat/wage-1-pay-rates` **(v1.149)**
**Build:** each worker's daily pay rate, dated, on the worker's page (PPL-6): `PayRate` (02 §12) in `apps/staffpay`, the service that sets one, the rates a span of days reads, an office route, and the page's part, *Malipo kwa siku*. Nothing is paid yet: WAGE-2 does the sums.
- *The row:* `PayRate(AppendOnly)`: the job (`staff`, PROTECT), `daily_rate` (money, `numeric(14,2)`, above 0, in whole shillings, as the money rules are, Jack, 16 Nov 2026), `shares_meals` (yes; the cook's no, for WAGE-7), `effective_from` and `note`; who set it is the row's `created_by`. A dated rule, so a ledger (rule 2), as `Setting` is: written once, never changed or deleted, the migration's trigger refusing both. A mistake is mended by a new row for the same day. Never on a laptop: a worker's pay never sits on a shared device (03 §6.1), so it writes no line in the change log.
- *In force on a day:* the latest row with `effective_from` on or before it; of two on the same day, the one written last (`created_at`, then `id`), as for settings (02 §4). `rates_between(shop, first, last)` in `apps/staffpay/selectors.py` answers each job's rate on each day of a span, in one read: a raise from 15 Nov splits November at the 15th, for WAGE-2; a job with no rate on a day has none that day, which ATT-5 reads to list only workers with a rate.
- *The service:* `set_pay_rate(staff, *, daily_rate, effective_from, shares_meals, note, by)` in `apps/staffpay/services.py`. Who may is the PIN's rule (02 §5), through people's own lock and check (`_locked_for_a_change`, made public, never copied): `staff.manage` on the whole shop, holding every key the worker was given; never one's own rate (`staff.own`); an owner's only by the superuser; not a worker who left (`staff.left`) *(default)*. And `settings.manage` on the whole shop, the key that changes "the shop's rules and rates", so a manager handed `staff.manage` to reset PINs can't change anyone's pay *(default)*. From any day of the job, gone by or still to come: Juma started on 10 Nov, and Baba types his rate on 19 Nov from the 10th, so those days are paid. Never before the job's first day (`pay_rate.before_first_day`). WAGE-3 adds the refusal of a day in an approved month.
- *The route:* `/api/v1/staffpay/pay-rates/`: a job's rates (`?staff=`), newest first, the one in force on the shop's today marked, and a new one (`CreateOnceMixin`); both behind `settings.manage` on the whole shop, as a route of the whole shop's rows (PPL-4). Money as strings.
- *The page:* *Malipo kwa siku* on the worker's page, under the roles, as Jack pictured it (19 Nov 2026), for whoever holds both keys: *Sasa: TSh 10,000 kwa siku · anachangia chakula*; then *Historia*, each rate from its day, newest first, with its note. A worker with none: *Bado hana malipo kwa siku.* and *Weka malipo*; with one, *Badilisha malipo*. The form: *Kiasi kwa siku*, *Kuanzia* (the job's first day for a first rate, else today *(default)*), *Anachangia chakula?* (*Ndiyo*, *Hapana, ni mpishi*), *Sababu (si lazima)*, *Hifadhi* and *Ghairi*.
- *The demo:* the seed gives Meneja, Asha and Baraka made-up rates from their first day, which stay as made (`demo.people_fixed`, as their details do); a worker a visitor adds can be given one, with no note, as a stranger's words would show to every visitor after (`demo.no_note`, SHOP-3).
**Choices by Jack (19 Nov 2026):** the page's part as he pictured it; WAGE-1 before ATT-5, so the month page lists only workers with a rate from the start (A). His picture of the worker's page goes on to the month so far, line by line, to the total to pay today (WAGE-2's row), and an advance is one thing, money given before payday, typed on the day it is given (WAGE-3's row).
**Built** **(v1.150)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the six commits it named. *The row* (`f84fecf`): `PayRate(AppendOnly)` (`apps/staffpay/models.py`) and staffpay's 0006, with its append-only trigger and two rules, above 0 and in whole shillings (`FLOOR`), each with the code `pay_rate.amount_invalid`, and an index by shop, job and day; `rates_between(shop, first, last)` (`apps/staffpay/selectors.py`): one read, each job's rates newest first (`NEWEST_FIRST`: the day, then the row written last, then the id), walked day by day; only with the shop open, and only for the shop's dates, never moments, as for the book. *Setting one* (`06931f5`): `set_pay_rate` (`apps/staffpay/services.py`) asks who may through `lock_worker_for_a_change` (`apps/people/services.py`), a public door to the lock and check people already had, then `settings.manage` on the whole shop, read after the lock; a worker who left is refused, for the system too; the amount and the first day are refused together, each beside its box; on the demo, a person's note (`demo.no_note`); no line in the change log. *The route* (`8994d78`): `/api/v1/staffpay/pay-rates/` (`apps/staffpay/api/`), `PayRateViewSet`, both actions behind `settings.manage`; the list needs `?staff=`, and another shop's worker is answered as an id no shop has; a create must say `shares_meals`, so a form that forgot never makes the cook pay; the sweep's rows (`sweep_pay_rates`) and its query (`sweep_queries`); the bootstrap's check of secret names knows rate, wage, salary and payslip; two new codes with their sentences, and the three who-may sentences name pay (*malipo*). *The page* (`f014ad9`): `worker-pay.tsx`, `pay-rate-form.tsx` and `pay-rates.ts` (`src/features/workers/`): the card under the roles, for whoever holds `settings.manage`, its read asked by the page and handed down; one form at a time on the page; the form's own checks in the server's sentences (`typedAmount`: whole shillings above 0); the same id for a try of the same rate; one region for the whole card, so its button is named by it. The list's answer is one object (`many=False`), which the route had described as a list: fixed in this commit, the first to read its type. Jack checked the Swahili of every new sentence. *The demo* (`669373e`): Meneja 15,000, Asha and Baraka 10,000 a day, made up, from their first day, set by the system; today's demo is made again when it lacks a rate the seed gives (`_holds_its_rates`). Proven red: the selector's order and its first day; the service's second key, its check of one who left, and its first day; the page's first day, own page, retry id and who sees the card; the seed's rate and its check. Honest limits: the page's code was written before its tests, then each of four lines proven by breaking it; nothing refuses a rate in an approved month yet (WAGE-3).
**From the review** **(v1.151)**: nothing must be fixed, and no rule was broken: another shop's workers and rates are never reached nor shown, no two locks can wait on each other in a circle (the book and a rate both take the jobs' light lock in id order), and who may can't be skipped, a create sent again included. Jack chose to fix all (A), each in its own commit, after bringing main in with PPL-12 (`286f4a1`): its *Kuingia* card sits between the status buttons and the roles, *Malipo kwa siku* under the roles, one form open at a time across the four. *Seeing pay* (Jack: A): a manager handed `settings.manage` only to change the late fine could read every worker's pay through the route; the route now asks `staff.manage` on the whole shop too, for every action, a create sent again included (`initial()`, `apps/staffpay/api/views.py`), and the page asks the rates, and shows the card, only for whoever holds both (`maySeePay`). *After a last day:* `rates_between` reads no job's first or last day, as a tap after the last day can go to review and be decided paid, which needs its rate; its docstring says so and a test pins it, so ATT-5 and WAGE-2 keep to the days the book answers. *Tests:* the shop's own rates under a system job, which only the selector's `shop=` keeps apart; a suspended manager sets no rate; a suspended worker can still be given one; *Weka malipo* hides while another card's form is open; a saved rate read back as *Sasa* and in *Historia*. *Fixed:* a second rate for a worker who hasn't started yet starts on their first day, as today would be refused; the history's line comes whole from the books, its " · " too; `demo.people_fixed` says why in words that fit pay too (*ili kila mgeni awakute kama walivyo*), and the cook's English line reads "(the cook)"; Jack checked the Swahili. Left as it is: the page's commit also carries the route's one-object mark (`many=False`), as main keeps one squash commit.
**Watch out:** `_locked_for_a_change` locks the job, as the book's run does (ATT-10), so a rate set during a run waits a moment for it. The days keep no rate: WAGE-2 reads the rates and copies each one onto the payslip (T4). The three who-may sentences (`staff.own`, `staff.superuser_only`, `staff.more_keys`) speak of a worker's details and status: check that they read right for a rate too. Each new code gets its two sentences and a line in `errors.test.ts` (UI-2); Jack checks the Swahili. `pnpm gen:api` after the route. A new table: its row for the tenancy sweep's two shops (`backend/tests/conftest.py`); a ledger, so no edit history.
**Checked** **(v1.152)**: merged as #316 on 20 Nov 2026, its every check green at the first run, `image` and `e2e` among them. On the live demo, the same morning, Jack, as Meneja: Asha's *Malipo kwa siku* said *Sasa: TSh 10,000 kwa siku*; *Badilisha malipo* on her was refused with the demo's lock; a worker he added, Zawadi, was given 9,000 with *Weka malipo*, shown on her card (his comment on #316). Each box ticked by its tests too: #1 `test_baba_sets_a_rate_from_the_first_day` and the page's save read back; #2 `test_a_raise_mid_month_splits_the_days` and `test_a_raise_to_come_leaves_this_month_as_it_is`; #3 `test_of_two_rates_on_one_day_the_newest_counts` and `test_a_rate_is_never_changed_or_deleted`; #4 `test_the_cooks_rate_shares_no_meals`; #5 `test_set_pay_rate.py`'s who-may tests and `test_route_permissions.py`'s; #6 `test_setting_a_rate_tells_no_laptop` and the planted `daily_rate` in `test_bootstrap_secrets.py`; #7 the seed's tests and the live demo; #8 `errors.test.ts`, `pnpm check:api`, and Jack's yes to each step's Swahili.

- [x] A raise from 1 Dec shows in the history; `rates_between` gives November the old rate and December the new, and a raise on the 15th splits a month there.

### ATT-5 · Attendance month view
`M` · Claude-led · after ATT-10, WAGE-1 · branch `feat/att-5-attendance-month` **(v1.153)**
**Build:** *Mahudhurio ya mwezi*, the month's attendance on one office page, as Jack pictured it (19 Nov 2026): the days a person must decide at the top, then one line per worker with the month's counts. It only shows: ATT-6 decides a day, and ATT-12 opens a worker's month and a day's taps.
- *Why a day needs a decision:* the book (ATT-10) keeps it beside the day, a new column, `review_reason`, written with the day's other answers (`WORKED_OUT`), so this page, ATT-12 and ATT-6 read one answer. From the day's rule (ATT-4, `work_out_day`): `time_unknown`, a tap whose time can't be trusted; `no_in`, a Toka with no Ingia; `came_late`, in from `review_from` (08:30) and before `afternoon_from` (12:00), or after `midday` (13:00); `no_out`, an Ingia with no Toka after it; `left_early`, out before closing outside the half day's windows. From the book: `outside_job`, a tap before the job's first day or after its last (ATT-11); `closed_day`, a tap on a closed day (ATT-10); `not_working`, a suspended or gone worker's tap (ATT-11); `clock_disagrees`, the tap a later one's time disagrees with (ATT-11). One reason a day, the first that holds in that order: the book's before the rule's, the rule's in its own order. Every other day keeps none.
- *The route:* `GET /api/v1/staffpay/attendance-month/?month=2026-11`, behind `attendance.manage` on the whole shop, as attendance belongs to the whole shop. It works the month out through the book (`work_out_days`, in the visit, writing as nobody), and answers the shop's `today`; each listed worker with their counts, `on_time`, `late`, `half`, `absent` and `needs_review` (off days aren't counted); and the days to decide (`to_decide`), each with its worker, its date, its reason, and the first Ingia and Toka the rule read, for "alifika saa 09:10".
- *Who is listed:* a worker with a daily pay rate (`rates_between`) on a day of the month the book answers (ATT-10's review, Jack: A; WAGE-1's review). So not the owner, absent every working day as he never clocks in, nor a worker gone before the month. Learned on the server, never through the rates route, so a manager with `attendance.manage` alone sees who is listed and no amount (WAGE-1's review, Jack: A). The days to decide are the listed workers', from the book's answer, never a row's `status` in the table (ATT-11's review).
- *The page:* `/attendance-month/?month=2026-11`, its button on the office home for whoever holds `attendance.manage` on the whole shop (Owner, Manager). `PageHeader`, then `‹ Novemba 2026 ›`, whose › stops at this month. This month's counts reach yesterday, and the page says so (*Hadi jana, 19/11/2026*), as the book keeps no day until it is over. *Zinahitaji uamuzi (N)*: a line per day, *Juma · Alhamisi 12/11 · hakuna Toka*. Then a line per worker: ✓ on time, *Ch* late, ½ half, *H* absent, ! to decide, with a key under them. With nobody listed, one line says so.

**Choices by Jack (20 Nov 2026):** the row held two pages' work, so it splits (A): ATT-5 is the month page; ATT-12, a new row after it, a worker's month and a day's taps. The demo is made again every morning with no day before today, so this page shows nobody there: DATA-8, a new small row right after this card, gives it three weeks of made-up taps (A).
**Claude's defaults (in the plan Jack approved):** this month's counts reach yesterday, and today's taps show in ATT-12; the days to decide oldest first, the workers by name; no browser test until the demo has days to show, which DATA-8 adds.
**Watch out:** the route writes, though it is a GET, as the book keeps the days it works out; it is safe to send again, as a month asked for twice writes nothing the second time (ATT-10). Each day comes with its worker (`select_related`, ATT-10's review), so the answer stays a few queries; a test counts them. A month to come, or one before attendance was switched on, lists nobody. `month` is `YYYY-MM`; anything else is refused under `month`. The page keeps the month in the address, replaced, not pushed, as `Tabs` keeps its tab, so the back arrow leaves the page, and ATT-12's arrow comes back to the same month. Times of day through the books' `attendance.timeOfDay` with `dayPart` (SYNC-11); the names of the weekdays and the months from the word books, so both languages say them. `review_reason`'s migration is read line by line (rule 9); its rules: only a known reason, and only on a day to decide. Not "every day to decide has one": a day kept before this card has none until the book works it out again. A new route: `pnpm gen:api`; its row in the tenancy sweep's two shops (`backend/tests/conftest.py`) and its `month` (`sweep_queries`); its key pinned in a test, and a refusal without it.
**Built** **(v1.154)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the four commits after the card's. *Why* (`6c7f1df`): `ReviewReason` (`apps/staffpay/models.py`), nine reasons in the order they are tried; `AttendanceDay.review_reason`, with the rule `staffpay_attendanceday_reason_only_to_decide` (migration staffpay 0007, the column empty on every day already kept); the rule's five reasons on its answer (`DayResult.reason`, `apps/staffpay/calculations.py`), which refuses a day to decide with none and a reason on any other day; the book's four before them (`work_out_days`), its taps in doubt now each with its reason, a suspension's before a disagreement's; `review_reason` one of the book's answers (`WORKED_OUT`), so a day kept before gets its reason at its next working out. Every test of the rule's dharura now names its reason. *The route* (`2aa043b`): `attendance_month(shop, month)` (`apps/staffpay/services.py`), any day naming its month, answering `AttendanceMonth` with a `WorkerMonth` per listed job; `AttendanceMonthViewSet` at `attendance-month/` (`apps/staffpay/api/`), its answer one object (`many=False`), times as `HH:MM`; the sweep's rows (`sweep_attendance`: the switch, a worker paid by the day since 40 days ago, and their days worked out, in both shops) and its month, yesterday's (`sweep_queries`), as on the 1st this month lists nobody. *The page* (`bbda297`): `/attendance-month/` (`src/app/(office)/attendance-month/page.tsx`), `month-screen.tsx` and `attendance-month.ts` (`src/features/attendance/`): a table, a row per worker and a column per kind, each column's mark for the eye and its kind in words for a screen reader, and the marks' key under it; the month's name between ‹ and ›, the names from the word books; the month in the address, replaced; this month read once, as the page opens (`useState(() => Date.now())`, as `register-this-device.tsx` does), as React's rules forbid reading the clock while drawing, so a page left open into the next month moves on at its next opening; *Hadi jana* by the server's today, and on a month's 1st *Bado hakuna siku iliyokwisha mwezi huu.* *The days to decide* (`181ea1b`): `to-decide.tsx`, a heading with the count, *Zinahitaji uamuzi (0)* and its line when none waits, a weekday worked out at noon UTC so no phone's zone moves it, and the two reasons with a time said with its part of the day (*alifika saa 09:10 asubuhi*). Jack checked the Swahili of every new sentence. Proven red: a reason dropped from the book's answers, two reasons in the wrong order, a month to come opened, the month not kept in the address, and › shown on this month, each put in on purpose and each caught; the sweep failed before shop A had days in the book.
**From the review** **(v1.155)**: nothing broke a rule, and nothing of another shop or of anyone's pay showed. Jack chose to fix all thirteen findings (A), each in its own commit, and to leave one as it is. *The first days of a month:* on 1 December, or on Monday 2 March after a Sunday the 1st, no working day is over, so nobody is listed, and the page said *Hakuna mfanyakazi anayelipwa kwa siku*, untrue; the answer now says whether a working day of the month is over (`working_day_over`), and with none the page says only *Bado hakuna siku ya kazi iliyokwisha mwezi huu.* (Jack checked it). *A page left open:* opened on 30 November and looked at again on 2 December, November's full table said *Hadi jana, 01/12/2026*; whether the month shown is this month is now the server's to say, from its own month and today, and the page reads the clock again each time it comes back into view (`usePagesClock`), so › reaches December, while the month on show stays the one it opened on until the person moves, as Baba is checking November before payday. *The month itself:* a strict field (`MonthField`, `apps/staffpay/api/serializers.py`), `YYYY-MM` from 2000-01 on, described as such in the API, where a date field took "2026-1" and said a whole date; "0001-01", whose midnight in Arusha falls before UTC's first day, was a server error, and so was "9999-12", whose last day was worked out by a sum running past the calendar's end, now `calendar.monthrange`. *Who is listed:* by working days alone, so Neema, whose last day was Sunday 1 March, has no line of zeros in March. *The page:* › greys out on this month and keeps its focus (`focusableWhenDisabled`), where it went away with the keyboard's place; no ‹ › for someone the month is refused to; the days-to-decide list named once, by its own heading (`useId`), not by a region around it too; a month asked again only once a minute old (`STALE_AFTER`), as each ask works the month out and locks every job's row a moment; `dayBefore`, `formatDayMonth` and `weekdayOf` in `src/lib/format.ts`, where dates are written. *Tests:* the two orders of reasons the card fixes and no test held, outside the job's days before a closed day and a suspension before a disagreement; the month's query count taken on a second visit, as the first can't see a lost `select_related` (proven: 104 queries without it). *Comments:* the services' header names the month and its route. *Left as it is* (Jack: A): a person hired again within the month (PPL-11) has two lines of one name, each a job's; ATT-12's calendar tells them apart.
**Checked** **(v1.156)**: merged as #319 on 20 Nov 2026, its every check green at the first run, `image` and `e2e` among them. On the live demo, the same afternoon, Jack, as Meneja: *Mahudhurio ya mwezi* on the office home; the page on *Novemba 2026*, › greyed out, saying *Bado hakuna siku ya kazi iliyokwisha mwezi huu.*, as the demo has no past day until DATA-8; ‹ to *Oktoba 2026*, saying *Hakuna mfanyakazi anayelipwa kwa siku katika mwezi huu.* (his comment on #319). Each box ticked by its tests too: #1 `office-home.test.tsx` and `test_the_attendance_month_needs_attendance_manage`; #2 `month-screen.test.tsx`; #3 `test_attendance_month.py`; #4 `to-decide.test.tsx` and `test_the_days_to_decide_oldest_first_with_why`; #5 `test_attendance_day.py` and `test_attendance_book.py`; #6 the tenancy sweep with `sweep_attendance`; #7 `messages.test.ts`, `pnpm check:api`, and Jack's yes to each step's Swahili.
