### DATA-8 · Demo seed: three weeks of taps
`M` · Claude-led · after ATT-5 · branch `feat/data-8-demo-taps` **(v1.157)**
**Build:** the demo's morning reset (`seed_demo`, DATA-1) also gives the shop the 21 days before today, as if Asha, Baraka and Meneja had clocked in and out on a demo laptop: most days on time, a few late with their fine, a half day, an absent day, and one day to decide. So *Mahudhurio ya mwezi* (ATT-5) has days to show a visitor. Only the demo's data: no screen changes.
- *From three weeks back:* the shop's rules (`DEMO_RULES`: attendance on, its two fines), each person's first day (`add_staff(started_on=…)`) and their rate from it (`set_pay_rate`) all start 21 days before today, so the book (ATT-10) counts those days. `set_setting` refuses a day gone by (`setting.date_past`, Jack, 15 Nov 2026), so on the demo the seed alone may start a rule on one (`as_the_demo_seed`, as the system, with the demo's sign, `DEMO_SEED`; DATA-8's review): the seed makes a new shop, where no day has been worked out yet. A person still can't, on the demo or anywhere, and the real server never has the sign.
- *The laptop:* *Laptop ya Dawa*, on Dawa, paired as any laptop is (`start_pairing`, then `confirm_pairing` by Meneja), as the one-tap demo pairs a browser (OPS-3). Visitors see it among the shop's devices; no browser holds its key.
- *The taps:* each a clock document, `no_photo` `demo`, as the demo keeps no photo (ATT-3 **(v1.122)**), sent through the push (`push`, SYNC-4) at the reset, in pushes of at most 100 as the route takes them, as a laptop that was offline sends later: the server keeps each tap at the laptop's time (`device_ok`, ATT-1), as no sign says its clock moved. Meneja taps too: he has a daily rate (WAGE-1), so the month lists him, and he logs in on a laptop with his password (OPS-13).
- *The days:* every working day (not Sunday), Ingia between 07:10 and 07:34 and Toka between 18:40 and 19:05, but for these. Asha: late at 07:50 (the first fine, 500), late at 08:10 (the second, 1,500), a half day (out at 13:10), and the day to decide, her last working day before today, an Ingia with no Toka (`no_out`). Baraka: late at 07:45 (500), and absent one day. Meneja: late once. Each of these days is counted among the working days back from yesterday, so none falls on a Sunday, and the day to decide is always the latest. On 20 Nov 2026, *Novemba 2026* shows Asha 12 ✓, 2 Ch, 1 ½, 1 !; Baraka 14 ✓, 1 Ch, 1 H; Meneja 15 ✓, 1 Ch; and *Zinahitaji uamuzi (1)*: *Asha · Alhamisi 19/11 · hakuna Toka*. The minutes come from a fixed random seed, so the shop is the same at every reset (DATA-1 **(v1.51)**, moved here from DATA-2).
- *Kept or made again:* a start keeps a demo made today only if it holds the taps too, as for its rules, keys and rates: a deploy's day would otherwise keep a demo the older seed made that morning, with nobody on the month page until the next one (#275).
**Choices (Jack's yes to the card, 20 Nov 2026):** the system may start a rule on a past day on the demo only; one demo laptop sends all the taps; Meneja taps too. Size `M`, not the row's `S`, with the browser test.
**Watch out:** the push never raises for a tap it refuses: it answers `invalid` and goes on. So the seed reads every answer and stops on any but `accepted`, inside its one transaction, so a refused tap undoes the whole reset and yesterday's demo stays. The new laptop shows on the devices page and on the laptops' health (SYNC-14), so a browser test that counts devices must find its own by name. On the 1st of a month, or a Monday the 2nd, the day to decide falls in the month before: the browser test opens that month.
**Done when:**
- [x] On a fresh demo, the month shows Asha, Baraka and Meneja with their counts, and one day to decide: Asha's, with no Toka.
- [x] Every tap reaches the server through the push, at the laptop's time (`device_ok`), with `no_photo` `demo`, and raises no flag.
- [x] The seed makes the same shop, its taps included, at every reset (`test_seed_demo_is_repeatable`).
- [x] On the demo, a person still can't start a rule on a past day; only the system can, as the demo's seed, by name (`as_the_demo_seed`) **(v1.159)**.
- [x] A demo made this morning without the taps is made again.
- [x] A browser test: Meneja opens *Mahudhurio ya mwezi* and sees Asha's line and her day to decide.
**Tests:** in `apps/shops/tests/`, the system starting a rule on a past day on the demo, a person refused there, and the system refused without the sign; in `apps/demo/tests/test_seed_demo.py`, `test_seed_demo_gives_three_weeks_of_taps`, `test_seed_demo_taps_are_the_laptops_time`, `test_seed_demo_makes_todays_shop_again_without_its_taps`, and `test_seed_demo_is_repeatable` comparing the taps; `frontend/e2e/attendance-month.spec.ts` (new).
**Built** **(v1.158)**: in the plan Jack approved, in the four commits after the card's. *The past day* (`a36e21d`): `set_setting` skips `setting.date_past` for `by` None while `DEMO_SEED` is set (`seeding_the_demo`); from the review, only when the seed also says so (`as_the_demo_seed`). *The weeks* (`60629db`): in `apps/demo/services.py`, `HISTORY_DAYS` (21), `OddDay` and each `DemoPerson`'s `odd_days`, counted back among the working days from yesterday (`_working_days_back`, Sunday left out); the ordinary minutes from `random.Random(TAPS_SEED)`, Ingia 07:10–07:34, Toka 18:40–19:05; `_demo_laptop` (`start_pairing`, `confirm_pairing` by Meneja, its key dropped, `@sensitive_variables`); `_send_the_taps`, clock documents version 2, numbered from 1, through `push` in batches of `MOST_DOCUMENTS` (100), its `last_server_time` 06:50 on the first day, any answer but `accepted` raising, which takes the wipe back. The shop's own block ends first, so its lines are numbered first, all at once (SYNC-15); the push then numbers each tap in a block of its own, inside a transaction of its own. *Kept or made again* (`938a514`): `_holds_its_taps`, the seed's people's taps timed before today, at least as many as `_demo_taps` gives, now worked out from the date and the zone alone. *The browser test* (`2ce0def`): `attendance-month.spec.ts`, going back a month when the page says no working day is over yet. Found on the way: the taps cost each reset some 3.3 seconds, and the demo's tests make the shop some 65 times, so `apps/demo/tests/conftest.py` makes it with no day before today unless a test takes `three_weeks` (from 4 m 37 s to about 2 minutes); the browser tests always run it whole. The one-tap tests leave the seed's laptop out of their counts (`SEEDS_LAPTOP`), and `shop-settings.spec.ts` finds the seed's 500 from 21 days back.
**Checked** **(v1.160)**: merged as #322 on 20 Nov 2026, every check green at the first run, `image` and `e2e` among them, and the demo's deploy green (`/api/health/` answered `1c4b11f`). On the live demo, the same afternoon, Jack, as Meneja: *Mahudhurio ya mwezi* on *Novemba 2026*, *Hadi jana, 19/11/2026.*; *Zinahitaji uamuzi (1)*: *Asha · Alhamisi 19/11 · hakuna Toka*; Asha 12 ✓ 2 Ch 1 ½ 0 H 1 !, Baraka 14 ✓ 1 Ch 0 ½ 1 H, Meneja 15 ✓ 1 Ch, as the card says (his comment on #322). The demo had been made that morning by the older seed, so the deploy's start made it again with its weeks (`_holds_its_taps`). Each box ticked by its tests too: #1 `test_seed_demo_fills_the_month_page` and `test_seed_demo_gives_three_weeks_of_taps`; #2 `test_seed_demo_taps_are_the_laptops_time` and `test_seed_demo_stops_when_a_tap_is_flagged`; #3 `test_seed_demo_is_repeatable`; #4 the four tests of the seed's word in `test_set_setting.py`; #5 `test_seed_demo_makes_todays_shop_again_without_its_taps` and `test_seed_demo_keeps_todays_shop_with_its_taps`; #6 `attendance-month.spec.ts`. Issue #321 made by `github_sync.py issues --stage 4`; its board fields came out empty (the known gap) and were filled by hand: Done, Hours 2, stage 4, M, Claude-led.
**From the review** **(v1.159)**: no never-broken rule broken; the tap pattern, the card's counts and every tap's `device_ok` checked by hand. Jack chose to fix all but three (A), each in its own commit. *Comments:* four said what the code no longer does, among them that a block may not sit inside an `atomic()` part, which `apps/core/changes.py` allows: the shop's block ends first so its lines are numbered first, all at once. *Midnight:* the seed asked the shop's day three times, so a reset across midnight in Arusha made the taps and the laptop's last word from two days, and flagged the first day's taps; it now asks once, and stops on a flagged tap as on a refused one (`test_seed_demo_reads_its_day_once`, `test_seed_demo_stops_when_a_tap_is_flagged`). *The seed by name:* `set_setting`'s exception is asked by `as_the_demo_seed`, never guessed from `by` None, which is also the superuser and ATT-7's nightly book. *Tests:* the past-day refusal with the sign off; a wake-up keeping a visitor's tap of today; the month page's own answer (`test_seed_demo_fills_the_month_page`); every odd day inside the 18 working days; Asha's line by her exact name. Left as they are (Jack: A): the taps check counts, as the rules' check reads keys only; the commit that moved the fine before the browser test followed it, as `main` keeps one squash commit; `MOST_DOCUMENTS` imported from the route's serializers, which the layers allow, as it is the route's own limit.
**Commits:** `docs(plan): data-8's card` → `feat(shops): on the demo, the system may start a rule on a day gone by` → `feat(data): the demo has three weeks of clock-ins from its own laptop` → `feat(data): a demo made today without its taps is made again` → `test(e2e): Meneja sees the month's attendance on the demo` → `docs(plan): data-8 says what it built`, then the review's fixes, one commit each, and `docs: the data-8 card follows the review` **(v1.159)**

### ATT-12 · Attendance: a worker's month and a day
`M` · Claude-led · after ATT-5 · branch `feat/att-12-worker-month` **(v1.161)**
**Build:** a worker's month and one day of it, as Jack pictured it (19 Nov 2026) and approved as screens (20 Nov 2026). On *Mahudhurio ya mwezi* (ATT-5) Baba taps *Juma*: Juma's month opens as a calendar, and a day tapped opens under it, with its taps and their photos. It only shows: ATT-6 decides a day.
- *The route:* `GET /api/v1/staffpay/attendance-worker/{id}/?month=2026-11` **(v1.162)**, the worker's job in the address, behind `attendance.manage` on the whole shop, as the month's is. It works the month out through the book (`work_out_days`, as `attendance_month` does), and answers the shop's `today`; the job's line as on the month page (`worker`: `staff`, `display_name` and its counts, `on_time`, `late`, `half`, `absent`, `needs_review`) **(v1.163)**; its first and last day (`started_on`, `left_on`); each day the book answers for the job (`date`, `status`, `reason`, the day's `review_reason` or null **(v1.163)**, `fine`, and **(v1.162)** `first_in` and `first_out`, the two taps the rule read, for "alifika saa 09:10"); and each of the job's taps of the month, today's included, as the book keeps no day until it is over (ATT-10): its `id`, its `date` and minute on the shop's clock (`time`, by the time the server trusts; none for one it doesn't trust, with the laptop's own beside it, `laptop_time`), `kind`, the laptop's `name`, its photo (`photo`: its id and whether it is `kept`, `waiting` or `removed`) or why it has none (`no_photo`), and whether it is in doubt (`doubt`: `not_working`, `clock_disagrees`, or none). A worker who is no job of the shop, another shop's or an id no shop has, is "not found" alike (404, 03 §5.4) **(v1.162)**.
- *A tap in doubt:* the question is asked once, by one helper the book and the route share (`apps/staffpay/services.py`): the push's flag on a suspended or gone worker's tap, on its line in the answer log, found by the tap's id (`not_working`, ATT-11 **(v1.143)**); the tap a later one's time disagrees with, named by that tap's `disagrees_with`, only by the laptop's clock (`clock_disagrees`). So the calendar's ! and the tap's own line never disagree. A tap outside the job's days says so by the day's reason, `outside_job`, with the job's first and last day beside it.
- *The calendar:* `/attendance-month/worker/?staff=…&month=2026-11`, opened from a worker's name on the month page. `PageHeader` with the worker's name, the month, the counts, then the month Monday first (*Jt Jn Jtn Al Ij Jm Jp*), each day its mark, as the month page draws them (✓ *Ch* ½ *H* !), – for a Sunday or a closed day (`off`), • for today, and empty for a day the book doesn't answer: a day to come, before the job's first day or after its last, or before attendance was switched on. A day outside the job's days that it tapped on shows ! (ATT-11). A key of the marks under it. No ‹ ›: the month is the one the page came from (Jack's yes, 20 Nov 2026).
- *The day:* a day tapped opens under the calendar, on the same page, and the address keeps it (`&day=2026-11-12`). Its weekday and date; its status in words; *Kwa nini:* and its reason on a day to decide; its fine on a day with one; then each tap: *Ingia* or *Toka*, its time with its part of the day (*07:20 asubuhi*, `clockPart`, SYNC-11), the laptop, and the photo, or *Hakuna picha:* and its reason (`no_camera`, `blocked`, `failed`, `old_app`, `demo`, ATT-3 **(v1.120)**), or that the photo hasn't arrived yet, or was removed; a tap in doubt says why on its line. A day with no Ingia or no Toka says which is missing. Today says it isn't over yet, and shows its taps as they come.
- *From the month page:* each worker's name is a link to their calendar, on the month shown; each line of *Zinahitaji uamuzi* opens its day directly. The back arrow comes back to the same month (ATT-5).
**Choices (Jack's yes to the screens, 20 Nov 2026):** the day opens under the calendar, not on a page of its own; a line of *Zinahitaji uamuzi* opens its day; no ‹ › on a worker's calendar.
**Claude's defaults (in the plan Jack approved):** one answer for the whole month, the days and the taps together, some 50 taps, so a day opens with no wait; the photos through CTRL-3's route, with no new server code for them; a voided tap isn't shown, as the book doesn't count one and nothing voids one until ATT-6, which shows them crossed out (A2).
**Watch out:** the route writes, though it is a GET, as the month's does (ATT-5): safe to send again. The taps come in one read, with their laptops and their doubt, and the photos in one more; a test counts the queries. The fine is the shop's own rule, which anyone working there may read on the rules page, never the worker's rate or the day's pay, so no pay key is asked (WAGE-1 **(v1.151)**). The photo opens from `/api/v1/control/attachments/{id}/file/`, which asks `attendance.manage` again and keeps no copy (`no-store`, CTRL-3); the page shows a photo only when the answer says it is kept. A tap's date and minute come from the server, on the shop's clock: the page never moves a time by the phone's zone. A person hired again within a month (PPL-11) has two lines of one name on the month page, each a job's: each opens its job's own calendar (ATT-5's review, Jack: A). The address keeps the worker, the month and the day, replaced, not pushed, as the month page keeps its month; a page opened from a typed address goes back to the month page on its month (`fallback`). Dates through `formatDayMonth`, `weekdayOf` and `dayBefore` (`src/lib/format.ts`); the weekdays and the months from the word books. A new route: `pnpm gen:api`; its row in the tenancy sweep (`sweep_queries`: yesterday's month; its worker in the address, each of shop A's in turn **(v1.162)**); its key pinned in a test, and a refusal without it.

### ATT-6 · Attendance: decide a day
`M` · Claude-led · after ATT-5, ATT-12 · branch `feat/att-6-decide-a-day` **(v1.165)**
**Build:** a manager or the owner decides a day, from the day under a worker's calendar (ATT-12), in a sheet that slides up over it, as Jack pictured it and approved as screens (20 Nov 2026). Juma's Thursday waits on *Zinahitaji uamuzi*, *hakuna Toka*: Baba opens it, taps *Amua siku hii*, picks *Siku kamili*, types *Alisahau kubonyeza Toka*, and taps *Hifadhi uamuzi*. The day leaves the list, counts as a full day, and still shows what the system worked out (A2). Fixing the taps themselves, adding a forgotten one or crossing out a wrong one, is ATT-13's.
- *The decision:* new columns on `AttendanceDay` (02 §12's `override` and `absence_type`), beside what the book worked out, which they never touch: the book writes only `WORKED_OUT`. `decided_status` (`on_time`, `late`, `half` or `absent`), `absence_type` (`office_duty`, `permission` or `no_show`, and only on an absent decision, where it is needed), `decided_fine`, `decided_by` (the job that decided), `decided_at` and `decision_reason`. Whole or none, a rule in the migration: every part filled, or all empty; a reason with at least one character that isn't a space. Not a ledger, as the day isn't: deciding again writes over it, and the day's edit history keeps the decision before (ADR-0036).
- *Six choices, one sheet:* *Siku kamili* (on time), *Amechelewa* with its fine (late), *Nusu siku* with its fine (half), *Kazi ya ofisi* (absent, office duty: paid as a full day), *Ruhusa* (absent, permission: unpaid), *Hakuja bila taarifa* (absent, no-show: unpaid). The same six for a day to decide and for any other day that is over: an absent day marked office duty, or an on-time day the owner makes a half day, each with its reason.
- *The fine* **(v1.130)**: a day sent to review carries none (`NO_FINE`), so a decided late or half day reads its fine again, from the first clock-in and the rules the day used (`rules_used`, T4), never today's rules. A pure function (`apps/staffpay/calculations.py`): *Amechelewa* after `on_time_until` and before `second_fine_from` takes the first fine, from `second_fine_from` on the second; with no trusted Ingia, or an Ingia on time, *Amechelewa* isn't offered. *Nusu siku* keeps a late morning arrival's fine, as the rule's morning half does (the owner, ATT-4); an afternoon arrival, or none known, has none. The other four take none. The fine is kept with the decision, so a tap that arrives later doesn't change what Baba decided.
- *Who:* `attendance.manage` on the whole shop, as the month's routes ask. Never one's own day (Jack: A, 20 Nov 2026): the manager's days are the owner's to decide, as a person never approves their own. A day of the shop only (T1); a day that is over and kept by the book; never `off`, a Sunday or a closed day nobody tapped on.
- *The route:* `POST /api/v1/staffpay/attendance-days/{id}/decide/` with the choice, the reason, and the decision the page saw (`replaces`, its `decided_at`, or none), so a second manager deciding the same day at the same moment is told, not written over (409). Its answer is the decision, as the day shows it **(v1.167)**.
- *The answers:* the month (`attendance_month`) and a worker's month (`worker_attendance`) read the decision first: a decided day leaves *Zinahitaji uamuzi* and counts as decided. A worker's month answers each day's decision (who, when, why, its fine), the choices the day may take with each one's fine (none on an off day, nor on the viewer's own), and whether the job is the viewer's own.
- *The sheet:* shadcn's `sheet` part, on Base UI's dialog (no new package), worn by our `Sheet` (`components/design/`, ADR-0039): a title, the form, and two buttons at the bottom, *Ghairi* and the main one. Jack chose sheets for short actions (20 Nov 2026), so ATT-13's fixes open the same way.
- *The day:* under the reason, *Amua siku hii*; once decided, the decision (*Siku kamili · uamuzi wa Baba, 20/11*), *Sababu:*, *Mfumo ulisema:* and what the book worked out, and *Badilisha uamuzi*. The viewer's own day has no button and says the owner decides it. The calendar marks a decided day by its decision (✓ *Ch* ½), *O* for office duty, *R* for permission, *H* for no-show, with the key under it.
**Choices (Jack, 20 Nov 2026):** split from the old row (A): this card decides a day, ATT-13 fixes the taps; sheets with *Ghairi* and a main button; the screens as pictured; never one's own day (A).
**Claude's defaults (in the plan Jack approved):** the fines as above, from 08:00 on the second fine, 09:10 too; the month line counts office duty with ✓ (a paid full day), permission and no-show with *H*, and the calendar shows each day's O, R or H; no way to take a decision back to none: a manager decides again; a decision on a day the book no longer answers (ATT-11, a first day moved later) is kept and read by nothing.
**Watch out:** the decision is a write by a person on a row the book also writes: the service locks the day (`select_for_update(no_key=True)`), and the book's `bulk_update` names only `WORKED_OUT`, so neither undoes the other. `decided_by` is checked to be of the day's shop (T1). The book of days lives on the server only (03 §6.6): no laptop sees a decision, so no change line. Office duty is paid as a full day: WAGE-2 pays the decision, not the book's status, and WAGE-7 decides whether office duty shares the meals. Once WAGE-3 locks an approved month, a decision on its days is refused.
**Done when:**
- [x] On a day that is over, *Amua siku hii* opens a sheet with the six choices, a reason, *Ghairi* and *Hifadhi uamuzi*.
- [x] A decided day shows the decision, who decided and when, its reason, and what the system worked out; it leaves *Zinahitaji uamuzi*.
- [x] A decided late or half day takes the fine its own day's rules give its first Ingia; office duty is paid, permission and no-show are not.
- [x] Nobody decides their own day; a seller is refused; another shop's day is "not found" (the tenancy sweep).
- [x] A decision can be changed, and the one before stays in the day's edit history.
- [x] A Sunday, a closed day and today have no button.
- [x] Every new sentence is in `sw.json` and `en.json`, and Jack has checked the Swahili; the schema and the types are regenerated.
**Checked** **(v1.168)**: merged as #329 on 21 Nov 2026, every check green at the first run, `image` and `e2e` among them, and the demo's deploy green (`/api/health/` answered `f89f5e5`). On the live demo, the same morning, Jack, as Meneja on the phone: *Mahudhurio ya mwezi* listed *Asha · Ijumaa 20/11 · hakuna Toka*; her day opened under her calendar; *Amua siku hii* slid the sheet up with its choices; *Siku kamili* with the ready reason *Alisahau kubonyeza Toka*, then *Hifadhi uamuzi*: the sheet closed, the day showed its decision, *Sababu:* and *Mfumo ulisema:*, with *Badilisha uamuzi*, and no day was left to decide (his comment on #329, posted by Claude with his yes). That morning's demo was made by the older seed, with one day to decide; the next reset gives three. Each box ticked by its tests too: #1 `offers Amua siku hii where the server offers choices, and opens the sheet over the day` and `names the worker and the day, and lists the server's choices, each with its fine`; #2 `shows a decision first, who made it and when, its reason, then what the system said (A2)` and `test_a_decided_day_waits_no_more_and_counts_as_decided`; #3 `test_decided_late_0750_takes_the_first_fine` (and 0800, 0910), `test_a_day_decided_late_takes_its_fine_from_its_own_rules` and `test_an_absence_counts_by_its_kind`; #4 `test_nobody_decides_their_own_day`, `test_deciding_a_day_needs_attendance_manage` and `test_another_shops_day_is_not_found_whatever_is_sent`, with the tenancy sweep; #5 `test_a_decision_changed_keeps_the_one_before`; #6 `offers nothing on a day the shop was shut`, the today test and `test_an_off_day_is_not_decided`; #7 Jack's yes to each step's Swahili, and `check:api`.
**Tests:** `test_attendance_book.py` (the decision's two rules in the database, every choice saved beside the book's answer, and the book never writing over a decision), `test_attendance_day.py` (`test_decided_late_0750_takes_the_first_fine`, `…_0800_…`, `…_0910_…`, late not offered on time or with no Ingia, a half day's fine, an afternoon half, the sheet's order, a shop with no fine) **(v1.166)**, `test_day_rules.py` (a day's copy read back as its rules), `test_decide_day.py` (new: each refusal, two managers at once, a decision changed and the one before in the history, the demo's ready reasons), `test_decide_day_route.py` (new: the request and answer as the sheet sends and reads them, the refusals beside their boxes, another shop's day "not found"), the month's and a worker's month's tests, and in `backend/tests/test_route_permissions.py`, `test_deciding_a_day_needs_attendance_manage` and `test_attendance_day_route_names_its_permission`; `sheet.test.tsx`, `decide-day.test.tsx` (new), the day's and the calendar's, and `/design`'s; `attendance-worker.spec.ts`: Meneja decides Asha's day on the demo.
**Built** **(v1.166)**: in the plan Jack approved, in ten commits after the card's, one more than planned. *The decision* (`ee8c75b`): six columns on `AttendanceDay` and two rules in staffpay's 0008, `staffpay_attendanceday_decision_whole_or_none` and `staffpay_attendanceday_decision_fits_its_status`; `AbsenceType`, `DECIDED`, the sheet's `DayChoice` and how each is kept (`DECISION_OF`), in `apps/staffpay/models.py`; the edit history keeps each decision with no change to its trigger. *The fine* (`e2d097f`): `day_choices` (`apps/staffpay/calculations.py`), the choices a day may take with each one's fine, and `rules_from_text` (`apps/staffpay/selectors.py`), a day's copy read back. *The service* (`7bb19f2`): `decide_day`, which locks the day's job then the day, as the book does, asks `attendance.manage` again, and writes only `DECISION`, the book's `WORKED_OUT` untouched; someone deciding meanwhile is `VersionConflict` (409), by the decision the page saw (`replaces`), never a version of the row, which the book's own writes would move. *The answers* (`69b022b`): a decided day leaves the list and counts as decided (`_counted_as`); a worker's month answers each day's `decision` and `choices`, `yours` and `ready_reasons`. *The route* (`0e40f88`): `AttendanceDayViewSet`, five new codes with their sentences. *The sheet* (`bf65183`): shadcn's `sheet`, downloaded with Jack's yes (no package of its own), worn by `Sheet`, on `/design` too; `actionDisabled` came with the page, for no network. *The id* (`b9b9604`): found while building the page, one commit more than the plan: the sheet sends to the day's own address, so a worker's month names each day's `id`. *The page* (`5d98760`, `d6cc2a8`): `decide-day.tsx`, the day's decision beside *Mfumo ulisema*, and the calendar's O, R and H (P and A in English, whose marks are its own letters). *The e2e* (`6762492`): a decision is never taken back and the browser tests share one database, so it runs last of those that read Asha's day, after `attendance-month.spec.ts`, as files run in their names' order. Jack checked the Swahili of every step. **One choice by Jack while building:** on the demo, a reason is one of three ready ones to tap, *Alisahau kubonyeza Toka*, *Alikuwa kazini nje*, *Ameruhusiwa na mwenye duka* (A), as a typed one would show to every visitor after them. `make check-e2e`: 13 passed, at the second run, the first meeting the login's double move (#327) on the month page, before any decision.
**From the review** **(v1.167)**: nothing broke a never-broken rule, and the book and a decision neither block nor undo each other; one bug and fourteen smaller findings. Jack chose to fix all (A), each in its own commit, and answered the two that were his. *The bug* (`475778d`): the sheet read the decision it replaces at the save, and the month is asked again each minute while the page is open, so a decision someone made meanwhile was sent as the one the person saw, and written over with no word; the sheet now keeps the decision on the screen as it opened. *The server:* the route finds the day before it reads the body, so the tenancy sweep's empty request meets the guard (`ad99a36`); the same decision sent again by its maker, its answer lost, is done, not refused as someone else's (`aaf0484`); a day is decided only as the book answers it, asked for afresh under the book's own locks, every job then the day, so a row it no longer reads, its job's first day moved past it, is refused (`78455f1`). *Tests:* two decisions at once, and a decision beside the book, each on a connection of its own, proven to fail with the locks taken away (`22d23e7`); a suspended manager, a key on one side alone, and a person hired again on their old job's day (`bc5cc03`); today offers no button (`17d991a`); the tests' days are made through real taps and the book (`apps/staffpay/tests/days.py`). **Jack's two choices (A, A):** a day whose time is in doubt, `time_unknown` or `clock_disagrees`, is decided with no fine, as no arrival is known (`91b6e80`); and the demo gives three days to decide, Asha out at 15:00, Baraka in at 09:10 and Asha's Ingia with no Toka, on the three latest working days, so a visitor who decides one leaves others for the next (`cfdcf54`): the seed's counts move (Asha 13 ✓ and 2 to decide, Baraka 15 ✓ and 1), and the two browser specs check what holds in one month or two. *The rest:* `aboutTheWholeRequest` lives in `src/api/refusal.ts`, with tests of its own (`fcfabdd`); one's own days say they are not one's to decide, true on the owner's own month (`8ae9d9a`); comments made true, and a note on `rules_from_text` for WAGE-3 (`8356b6c`). Touched tests and the browser tests (13 passed) after the fixes; CI runs the rest.

### ATT-13 · Attendance: fix the taps
`M` · Claude-led · after ATT-6 · branch `feat/att-13-fix-taps` **(v1.169)**
**Build:** a manager or the owner fixes a worker's taps from the day under their calendar (ATT-12), each fix in ATT-6's `Sheet`, and the laptop offers no *Ingia kazini* after a *Toka*, as Jack pictured it and approved as screens (21 Nov 2026). Juma's Thursday: *Ingia* 07:20, then *Toka* 10:00 by mistake. At 10:05 the laptop offers no button and says *Umeshatoka leo. Mwambie meneja.*; he works on to 18:45 and leaves with no tap. That evening Baba opens the day, *Kwa nini: aliondoka saa 10:00*: he crosses out the 10:00 Toka, *Alibonyeza kwa makosa*, and adds *Toka 18:45*, *Alisahau kubonyeza Toka*. The day is worked out again: a full day, as the shop closes at 18:40 (18:30 in the card's first draft, a day that would still wait, "left early" **(v1.170)**). The crossed-out tap stays on the day, struck through, with who, when and why, and counts for nothing (A2).
- *The laptop* (from ATT-4 **(v1.130)**): Toka ends the day (Jack, ATT-4's review), so once the person's last tap today on this laptop is a Toka, *Mahudhurio* shows no button, and under *Ulitoka kazini saa 10:00 asubuhi.* says *Umeshatoka leo. Mwambie meneja.* (Jack: A). Today it offers *Ingia kazini* again, as it was built for a lunch break (`attendance.tsx`), and a second Ingia counts for nothing. A tap the server refused still doesn't count for the button (SYNC-11). The laptop never hears of a fix: the book of days lives on the server only (03 §6.6), so Juma, his Toka crossed out, still can't tap that day, and Baba adds his Toka.
- *A tap entered in the office* (from ATT-1, ATT-3 and ATT-12's review **(v1.163)**): an `AttendanceEvent` like a laptop's, in the same table, so the book reads one table. It has no laptop: `device`, `device_seq`, `client_time`, `last_server_time` and `clock_error`, which `CounterRecord` gives every counter record, are empty, on this table alone (its fields written over in the model). `created_by` is who entered it, as on every row, so no `entered_by` column (one fact lives once; 02 §12 follows); `staff` is whose tap it is; `received_at` when it was entered; and a new `entry_reason`. Its time is the minute the manager typed, on the shop's clock, and trusted (`TimeCheck.ENTERED`, new): an Ingia added at 07:50 takes the late fine, as a real tap would. It has no photo, and says why: `entered`, new in `NoPhoto` (*Hakuna picha: imeongezwa ofisini*). The migration adds one rule and rebuilds two: a tap is either the laptop's (every counter part filled, no entry reason) or the office's (every counter part empty, `created_by` filled, a reason with a character that isn't a space, `entered` as its check and its photo reason); the trusted time's rule takes `entered` with a time; the photo reason's rule takes `entered`.
- *Adding a tap* (`add_tap`, `apps/staffpay/services.py`): *Ingia* or *Toka*, a day and a minute, and a reason. On any of the job's days, from its first to its last, up to today, never a Sunday, and never a minute still to come. The id comes from the client (UUIDv7), so a request sent twice adds one tap.
- *Crossing out a tap* (`void_tap`, from ATT-1): the void stamp filled with one guarded `update()` (`filter(pk=…, voided_at__isnull=True)`), never `save()`, as CORE-3's *Watch out* says. 0 rows updated means someone crossed it out first, or the request came twice: the stored void is answered. `voided_by` is checked to be of the tap's own shop (T1), as the database holds only a complete stamp **(v1.58)**. A crossed-out tap is never brought back: a mistake is mended by adding a tap. An added tap is crossed out like any other. `void_approval` comes with the Approval table (R1).
- *Who:* `attendance.manage` on the whole shop, as the month's routes ask; never one's own taps, by the person, so a person hired again (PPL-11) fixes neither job's, as nobody decides their own day (ATT-6). Each fix locks the tap's job, the book's own lock (`work_out_days`), so a fix, a run of the book and a decision take turns.
- *A decided day* (Jack: A, 21 Nov 2026; the question from ATT-6 **(v1.165)**): a fix never changes a decision, which the book never writes over (ATT-6). The day says *Ingia au Toka zimebadilika baada ya uamuzi.* under the decision, so whoever looks can tap *Badilisha uamuzi*, when one of its taps was added, arrived (`received_at`) or was crossed out after the decision was made.
- *The routes:* `POST /api/v1/staffpay/attendance-taps/` (`id`, `staff`, `kind`, `date`, `time`, `reason`) and `POST /api/v1/staffpay/attendance-taps/{id}/void/` (`reason`), each answering the tap as the day shows it. A worker's month (`attendance-worker/{id}/`) answers the crossed-out taps too, each with `voided` (by, at, reason), and each added tap with `entered` (by, at, reason) and no `laptop` or `laptop_time`; and each day's `taps_changed`.
- *The page:* under each tap, *Batilisha*, which opens its sheet: *Batilisha Toka ya Juma*, the day and the tap (*Alhamisi 19/11 · Toka 10:00 asubuhi*), *Sababu:*, *Ghairi* and *Batilisha*. Under the taps, *Ongeza Ingia au Toka*, which opens its sheet: *Ongeza Ingia au Toka ya Juma*, the day, *Ingia* or *Toka*, *Saa:*, *Sababu:*, *Ghairi* and *Ongeza*. A crossed-out tap struck through, with *Imebatilishwa na Baba, 19/11 · Sababu: Alibonyeza kwa makosa*; an added one *Toka 18:45 jioni · imeongezwa na Baba, 19/11/2026* **(v1.170)**, *Sababu: Alisahau kubonyeza Toka* and *Hakuna picha: imeongezwa ofisini*. No buttons on the viewer's own month, a Sunday, a day outside the job's days, or a day to come.
- *On the demo* (as ATT-6's ready reasons, Jack: A): a reason is a ready one, *Alisahau kubonyeza* to add, *Alibonyeza kwa makosa* to cross out, as a typed one would show to every visitor after them.
**Choices (Jack, 21 Nov 2026):** the screens as pictured: the laptop after a Toka, the day with *Batilisha* and *Ongeza Ingia au Toka*, the two sheets, and the day after both fixes; a fix keeps a decided day's decision, and the day says its taps changed (A).
**Claude's defaults (Jack's yes, 21 Nov 2026):** who may, as above; any of the job's days up to today but a Sunday, never a time to come; an added time trusted, fine and all; a crossed-out tap never brought back; the laptop never hears of a fix; the demo's two ready reasons.
**Watch out:** a clock-in the server refused (SYNC-11) shows on *Mahudhurio* with "Mwambie meneja" and is never sent again, so the manager adds it as a missed one: that mends the book, not the laptop, whose warning stays until a way to clear refused records comes (§20, item 23) **(v1.64)**. Crossing out the only tap of a day outside its job's days leaves that day's row as it was, `needs_review`, which the book no longer answers, so nothing reads its own status (from ATT-11's review **(v1.144)**): a test proves it leaves *Zinahitaji uamuzi*. The migration changes a ledger's columns and rules, never its rows: the append-only and void triggers act on rows, and stay as they are; read it line by line (rule 9). The minute typed is on the shop's clock, made a moment with the shop's zone on the server, never the phone's. The clock document's saving code (`documents.py`) is unchanged: a laptop's tap always has its laptop. Once WAGE-3 locks an approved month, a fix on its days is refused. New routes: `pnpm gen:api`; their rows in the tenancy sweep; their key pinned in a test, with a refusal without it; new codes with their sentences (`errors.test.ts`). The browser tests share one database, and ATT-6's decides one of Asha's days: the new one fixes a tap where the other specs' counts still hold.
**Done when:**
- [ ] After a Toka, the laptop shows no *Ingia kazini* that day and says *Umeshatoka leo. Mwambie meneja.*
- [ ] *Batilisha* under a tap opens a sheet with a reason, *Ghairi* and *Batilisha*; the tap then shows struck through, with who, when and why, and counts for nothing.
- [ ] *Ongeza Ingia au Toka* opens a sheet with *Ingia* or *Toka*, the time and a reason; the tap then shows who added it and why, with *Hakuna picha: imeongezwa ofisini*, and counts as a tap at that time, its fine too.
- [ ] The day is worked out again at once; a decided day keeps its decision and says *Ingia au Toka zimebadilika baada ya uamuzi.*
- [ ] Nobody fixes their own taps; a seller is refused; a Sunday, a day outside the job's days and a time to come are refused; another shop's tap is "not found" (the tenancy sweep).
- [ ] A crossed-out tap is never brought back; the database keeps a tap with no laptop only with who entered it and why.
- [ ] Every new sentence is in `sw.json` and `en.json`, and Jack has checked the Swahili; the schema and the types are regenerated.
**Tests:** `test_attendance_event.py` (the two kinds of tap and their rules in the database, an office tap with no reason refused, the void stamp complete); `test_fix_taps.py` (new: a tap added counts at its minute and takes its fine, a Toka crossed out leaves the day as the taps say, the same request twice adds or crosses out once, one's own taps refused, a Sunday, a day outside the job and a minute to come refused, a crossed-out tap stays crossed out, another shop's person refused, the demo's ready reasons, the only tap outside a job's days crossed out leaves nothing to decide, a fix and a decision at once take turns); `test_fix_taps_route.py` (new: the requests and answers as the sheets send and read them, the refusals beside their boxes, another shop's tap "not found"); `test_worker_month.py` (crossed-out and added taps answered, a decided day's `taps_changed`); in `backend/tests/test_route_permissions.py`, `test_fixing_taps_needs_attendance_manage` and `test_attendance_tap_route_names_its_permission`; `attendance.test.tsx` (no Ingia after a Toka, *Umeshatoka leo*); `void-tap.test.tsx` and `add-tap.test.tsx` (new), and the day's in `worker-day.test.tsx`; `attendance.test.tsx`'s two tests that offered Ingia after a Toka, now refused, and Juma still offered his; `attendance-worker.spec.ts`'s third test, Meneja fixes Asha's day out at 15:00 on the demo **(v1.170)**.
**Built** **(v1.170)**: in the plan Jack approved, in ten commits after the card's, as the card's *Commits* line names them. *The shape* (`067552c`): staffpay's 0009, `device`, `device_seq` and `client_time` written over from `CounterRecord` to be empty, `last_server_time` and `clock_error` too, `entry_reason`, `TimeCheck.ENTERED`, `NoPhoto.ENTERED`, the new rule `staffpay_attendanceevent_from_a_laptop_or_the_office`, the trusted time's and the photo reason's rebuilt; no line written, so the ledger's triggers never fire. The type checker found the month's answer must take a tap with no laptop: so `laptop` and `laptop_time` may be null from this commit, and the day shows such a tap without one. *The services* (`a94bc44`, `173a86c`): `add_tap` and `void_tap` (`apps/staffpay/services.py`), each in `recording_changes`, as the push saves a tap, each starting with `_ready_to_fix`: the key asked afresh, the tap's job locked as the book locks it, never one's own; the void one guarded `update()`, its 0 rows answered with the void kept; `DEMO_ADD_REASONS` and `DEMO_VOID_REASONS`. *The answer* (`743ae49`): a worker's month answers every tap, the crossed-out ones too, each with `voided` and each added one with `entered` (`TapStamp`: by, at, reason), and each day's `taps_changed`, from when its taps were kept or crossed out after its decision. *The routes* (`05e8380`): `AttendanceTapViewSet` at `attendance-taps/`, a create (`CreateOnceMixin`) and a `void` action, each answering the tap as the day shows it (`TapAnswer`, through `tap_line`, which shares `_tap_lines` with the month); its `initial()` asks the key for every action before anything is read, as a create sent again hands back the tap it made, so a seller sending a tap's id her laptop knows is refused, not handed it (as the pay rates' route, WAGE-1's review); the month answers the demo's `ready_add_reasons` and `ready_void_reasons`; four codes with their sentences; a tap per shop in the tenancy sweep; `WorkerField` says its type to the schema, a uuid, as a serializer with no model can't. *The laptop* (`7210e1e`): `outForTheDay` in `attendance.tsx`, *Umeshatoka leo. Mwambie meneja.* (`attendance.doneToday`). *The sheets* (`55d05f6`, `53fc473`): `void-tap.tsx` and `add-tap.tsx`, on ATT-6's `Sheet`; the add sheet picks Toka on a day with an Ingia, makes the tap's id once as it opens, and takes the minute in the phone's own time box; the day draws a crossed-out tap struck through (`<s>`) with who, when and why, an added one with who added it, and *Ingia au Toka zimebadilika baada ya uamuzi.* under a decision its taps moved past; *Ongeza Ingia au Toka* only on the job's days up to today but a Sunday (`mayAddOn`), never on one's own month. *The browser test* (`2e3609c`): `make check-e2e`, 14 passed, the image from the cache. Jack checked the Swahili of every step. **Found on the way:** the shop closes at 18:40, so the card's story had Juma leave at 18:30, a day that would still wait, "left early": 18:45, as the card now says. The whole frontend suite met timeouts in files this card never touched (workers, settings) while another project on the laptop ran its own server and browsers; the attendance files passed each time, and the suite runs again at `/finish-task`.
**Commits:** `docs(plan): att-13's card` → `feat(staffpay): a tap a manager enters in the office has no laptop` → `feat(staffpay): a manager adds a tap a worker forgot` → `feat(staffpay): a manager crosses out a wrong tap` → `feat(staffpay): a worker's month shows crossed-out and added taps` → `feat(staffpay): the routes that add and cross out a tap` → `feat(frontend): the laptop offers no Ingia after a Toka` → `feat(frontend): a manager crosses out a tap in a sheet` → `feat(frontend): a manager adds a tap in a sheet` → `test(e2e): Meneja fixes a tap on the demo` → `docs(plan): att-13 says what it built` **(v1.170)**: as built

## 10. Stage 5 — Catalog and the sale skeleton

**Goal:** products and prices are managed online and found instantly offline; one cash sale travels from the device to the ledgers and back (03 §16, step 7).
**Done when:** the Playwright test *offline sale → online → stock reduced on the server → pending effect dropped on the device* passes, and the same sale made online; the demo shows it.
**You'll learn:** search indexes; ledgers with running totals; property-based tests; posting several ledgers in one transaction.
**Order:** top to bottom.

The budget check weighs the screen (`scripts/counter-budget.mjs`): `/attendance/` weighed 234.6 KB of 300 at SYNC-11, nearly all of it shared, so about 65 KB is left: a search library or a big import may pass the line; load it when needed, or raise the budget in 03 §14 with a reason; from SYNC-13 **(v1.69)**: while a sale has lines, the screen calls `useCounterBusy(true)` (`src/offline/busy.ts`), so no update reloads it mid-sale, in any tab |

After a lost answer the push may wait while the pull runs, so an effect the server has posted is counted twice until the next push stamps it, up to 5 minutes: decide whether the screens can tell |

## 11. Stage 6 — Drugs side live (R1)

**Goal:** the Drugs side sells, takes every kind of payment, gives credit, receives deliveries and closes the drawer on the system; SmartMauzo and the paper books stop after the parallel run.
**Done when:** two weeks of real use with paper alongside, the drawer difference explained every day, and the owner agrees to stop the paper.
**You'll learn:** money flows; approvals; allocation algorithms; imports; reports.
**Needs:** OWN-9 (product sheet), OWN-10 (madeni book transcribed), OWN-11 (tolerances, credit and discount rules), OWN-12 (SmartMauzo), OWN-13 (backup phone and power bank).

## 13. R2 — Foods side (outline)

Cards are written at the stage 7 review, with their own epic codes. Epics in a likely order:

- **FLOW — Writer → cashier → loader:** sales waiting for payment; the writer's screen; the cashier's queue (pull every 3 s while open); slips printed only after payment (M6), with a QR code if the shop wants one (§20, item 14) **(v1.11)**; handover per line and *collect later*; *collected by* name and phone; cashier-only selling when the network is down (03 AD16).
- **BILL — One customer, both sides:** bills grouping one sale per side; a notification to the other side; the inter-side amount and `side_transfer`; `test_e6_mixed_bill_foods_holds_15000_for_drugs`.
- **PHONE — Phone orders:** the caller's number brings up debts, the last sale and formulas.
- **SVC, FORM, EXCH — Services, jobs, formulas, exchange:** services priced per kg; the job list and its statuses; the customer's goods kept apart from shop stock; formulas with versions and "same as last time"; `test_e7_exchange_maize`.
- **PROD, BAG, XFER — Production, open bags, transfers:** `test_e8_production_loss_within_tolerance`; `test_e9_open_bag_difference_flagged`; transfers between sides with difference flags.
- **PART — Partners and supplier credit:** a partner as debt holder; `supplier_credit` and `supplier_payment`; supplier balances.
- **RPT — Weekly and monthly reports:** best and slowest sellers; monthly profit with unknown costs shown as unknown; attendance and pay.
- **PRAC — Practice shop:** a copy of the products with fake customers and money (02 D14).
- **Price suggestions** when a delivery's cost goes up.
- **Decision — a local hub or not**, from the outage numbers collected since stage 2 (03 AD16).
- **OPS:** resize the server to 4 GB; continuous WAL archiving; **LIVE-3** Foods side go-live.

## 14. R3 and Later (outline)

**R3 — Customer side**, in this order (spec §7.10): a price list with in-stock status, by animal and problem → order now, collect later → my debts and payments (customer login) → a message when my job is ready → debt reminders. Pilot with 5–10 regular customers. PostgreSQL row-level security is switched on before a second real shop joins (03 AD8). So is **AUTH-7 · "Which shop?" at login** **(v1.29)**: a person with current jobs at two shops picks one after logging in, and the session keeps it until they switch; until then AUTH-3 refuses them safely (`staff.choose_shop`). From AUTH-6 **(v1.32)**: a person with jobs at two shops has one password, so when they can work, a manager at one shop must not be able to reset it, and the mark must reach them: `job_of_person` refuses `staff.choose_shop` before the mark, so UI-5's form never opens for them. That check must not read the other shop's rows during a visit, which ADR-0037 keeps for a login before any shop is open: `test_a_job_at_another_shop_is_not_looked_at_until_auth_7` says what to change. From SYNC-15 **(v1.54)**: a change to a person writes a staff line in every shop where they work, the shops read by the person's door (`shops_of_person`, every shop, by id; ADR-0042). Once a person may work at two shops, a job added at one in the moment between that read and the person's commit misses that one change, until the next. A fix to weigh (from SYNC-15's review): lock the person's row with a full `select_for_update()`, not `no_key`, before the door's read, so a new job's link check at its commit waits for the person's save; no circle can form, as that save never takes the new job's shop's counter. From PPL-10's review **(v1.112)**: a fix on the workers' page changes the person's full name and phone, which are theirs at every shop, but who may is asked of the visit's shop's job alone (`_check_may_act_as`), and only that shop's audit trail gets the line (ADR-0044): once a person holds current jobs at two shops, Meneja of shop A, where Baba of shop B also works as a plain worker, could change Baba's phone, his login name at B too, and B would see nothing. Before that, ask who may of the person's job at every shop (as for a password), and write the audit line in each.

**Later** (spec §10): online payments, automatic WhatsApp and SMS, VFD receipts, barcode scanning, proforma and invoice PDFs, sign-up and billing for other shops, counts per store room, two-factor login for the owner and managers.

## 16. Going live

**Every go-live** follows the same checklist:

*Two weeks before*
- [ ] The stage's features are done and shown to the owner.
- [ ] The dress rehearsal (OPS-12) has passed; a restore test passed in the last seven days; alerts are on.
- [ ] Devices are paired and installed as apps, with persistent storage granted; every shop device has a screen lock and disk encryption (03 §6.7).
- [ ] Staff have practised on the demo (the practice shop comes in R2).
- [ ] Data is imported and checked; baselines are recorded (OWN-16).

*Day one and the first weeks*
- [ ] Paper runs alongside the whole time — so stopping is always possible.
- [ ] Jack checks *Hali ya vifaa* and the flags at midday and at closing.
- [ ] Problems become `fix` tasks at the top of the board.

*Stopping the paper*
- [ ] When the agreed period has run, every difference is explained, and the owner agrees.

**LIVE-1 · Attendance (R1b)**
- Start on the **1st of a month**, so the first pay run covers a whole month.
- Before that day, on the server: MPC opened, and Baba made its Owner with his PIN, by `docs/runbooks/open-a-shop.md` (PPL-13) **(v1.127)**.
- Before that day, on *Mipangilio ya duka*: switch `attendance.enabled` on **from the 1st**, so *Mahudhurio* shows on every laptop that morning; and the owner types **both fines**, which start at 0, or the whole month is paid with no fine and nothing warns (ATT-2) **(v1.88)**.
- Each worker logs in once, online, on each clock-in device; that creates the device's offline PIN check (03 §5.9).
- 10 minutes of training per worker.
- The notebook runs alongside for the first month. At month end, the system's payslips are compared with the hand-calculated ones — that comparison is the real acceptance test.

**LIVE-2 · Drugs side (R1)**
- Products imported from the product sheet (DATA-4); the opening count done on paper on a quiet evening or a Sunday, imported (DATA-6), then approved.
- Open debts imported (DEBT-8); the biggest ones confirmed with the customers.
- The laptop and the backup phone paired; a power bank charged; the paper receipt book numbered (03 §6.11).
- Managers trained by Jack (30–60 minutes each).
- Paper books alongside for two weeks; SmartMauzo kept for reference only, and not renewed.

## 17. Portfolio track

The project is also the portfolio, so showing it is part of the plan.

| When | What | Why it matters to a recruiter |
|---|---|---|
| Stage 0 | README with the problem, stack, docs and a CI badge (FOLIO-1) | A clear first page; proof of a working pipeline |
| Stage 1 | Write-up: *Append-only ledgers enforced by PostgreSQL triggers* | Shows database depth |
| Stage 2 | GIF of the offline clock-in; write-up on offline sync (FOLIO-2); the one-tap demo (OPS-3) | The hardest part, visible in a minute |
| Stage 3 | Write-up: *A Django app on a VPS, with backups that are tested* | Shows operations, not only code |
| Stage 4 | The pay baseline: hours by hand vs minutes now (FOLIO-3) | Real impact, real numbers |
| Stage 5 | GIF of an offline sale; write-up: *Documents in, ledgers out* (FOLIO-4) | Domain design |
| Stage 6 | Case study, first version (FOLIO-5) | A real shop running on your code |
| Any time | Write-up: *The same numbers in Python and TypeScript* | Careful engineering |

- Every PR has a **What I learned** note: the raw material for the write-ups, and practice for explaining your work in interviews.
- Write-ups are drafted in `docs/writeups/` and posted where recruiters look (LinkedIn, dev.to), linked from the README.
- Real shop data never goes public; numbers in the case study need the owner's permission (OWN-14).

## 18. Risks

| Risk | Early signal | What we do | Retired by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Offline storage is unreliable on the shop's devices | SPIKE-1 fails on a device | Installed Chrome apps; persistent storage; a different device | SPIKE-1, SYNC-12 |
| Chrome can't print on the shop printer | SPIKE-2 fails | WhatsApp receipts move into R1 (SALE-14); a fix or a new printer before R2 | SPIKE-2 |
| Sync bugs corrupt the ledgers | Flags like `balance_mismatch` or `device_gap` | Idempotent push, commit-order numbering, append-only tables, nightly checks, property tests | SYNC-1, SYNC-4, STK-9 |
| Device and server disagree on money | `total_mismatch` flags | Shared test vectors; one rounding rule | CORE-5, UI-1, SALE-1 |
| Data preparation takes longer than the code | The product sheet fills slowly | Start in stage 1; siblings help; Drugs side first | OWN-9 |
| Long power cuts | Outages on *Hali ya vifaa* | Backup phones, power banks, the paper receipt book; outage numbers decide the R2 hub | SYNC-14, OWN-13 |
| Workers resist clock-in | Missing clock-ins, complaints | The owner's backing; a two-tap screen; workers see their own records (spec principle 7) | LIVE-1 |
| Jack's time | Velocity falls | Small tasks; a weekly rhythm; cut scope, never tests | §19 |
| Scope creep | Ideas arrive mid-stage | Parking lot, R1+ and Later; changes only at a stage review | §19 |
| A fast-moving library breaks something | Dependabot PRs fail | Pinned versions; weekly Dependabot; upgrades as their own tasks | REPO-5 |
| The VPS provider is unreliable | Uptime alerts | Tested backups off the server (USB); Docker Compose moves to another provider unchanged | OPS-8, OPS-9 |

## 19. Tracking and re-planning

- **Hours:** every PR notes the time spent. After ten tasks, the hours in §1 and the calendar are recalculated from real numbers.
- **Weekly:** update the board; look at the next three cards.
- **End of each stage:** a 20-minute review — what went well, what to change — then the next stage's cards are written and its issues created (BP2, BP12).
- **Changes:** a new idea goes to the parking lot, R1+ or Later; it enters a stage only at a review. A changed decision is a new ADR that supersedes the old one, plus the change to 02, 03 or this plan, in a `docs:` commit **(v1.1)**.
- **Version:** this file moves to 1.0 when Jack approves it, and to 1.x at each stage review.

## 20. Parking lot

1. Real hours a week, checked after the first ten tasks (§19).
2. The day the shop starts using each part — decided at the review before each go-live (BP7).
3. SmartMauzo's next payment date and export options (OWN-12).
4. Who fills in the product sheet, and by when (OWN-9).
5. Which devices are clock-in devices: one per side, or one at the entrance. At SYNC-11, every laptop registered to the shop (Jack, 12 Nov 2026), as registering is the owner's permission; a switch per device, set by the owner, would be a card of its own **(v1.64)**.
6. Two-factor login for the owner and managers: R1 or Later *(default: Later)*.
7. When the demo moves to an always-on server (03 §18 #15).
8. Licence review before R3 or a first outside contributor (BP16).
9. A short demo video for each release.
10. Practice mode before R2, if new Drugs staff need it (the demo covers it until then).
11. Four web addresses, one server: `admin.<domain>` (Jack), `owner.<domain>` (the owner), `staff.<domain>` (managers and workers), `<domain>` (customers, R3) — Jack, 23 Oct. Written as an ADR with OWN-3, before OPS-5's Caddyfile **(v1.6)**.
12. Workers' own iPhones: does the app need them, or only the shop's devices? On an iPhone every browser is Safari inside: it may delete a site's data after some days without use, hides installing in the Share menu, and has no Background Sync (03 §7.3, SPIKE-1's report). Until Jack decides, iPhones are for the owner's office screens, online **(v1.10)**.
13. Not tested by SPIKE-1: a second laptop, and Edge offline. Both are checked when the shop's devices are paired and installed before go-live (§16) **(v1.10)**.
14. QR codes on the Foods side slips (03 §7.5 ✚): does the shop want workers to scan a paid slip instead of reading its number? The shop doesn't use QR codes today, so SPIKE-2 left the QR code out (Jack, 26 Oct). Decided when R2 is planned; if yes, check first that the shop's printer prints one a phone can read **(v1.11)**.
15. An admin page for staff members, as its own task (Jack, 27 Oct). The first admin page of a shop table lists its rows through `all_shops`, but its add and change pages fill and check their form through `objects` (drop-downs, unique checks), so they raise `NoShopSelected` (CORE-2). It needs one admin base in `core` that runs those pages inside the row's own `shop_context`, with the shop picked first on an add page **(v1.14)**.
16. The admin's *Add shop* page through `create_shop` (Jack, 27 Oct). Today it saves the Shop row alone: no sides, stock locations or cash drawers, so a shop added there can't work (SHOP-2). It needs the sides on the add form, or a management command instead. No shop exists yet, so none is missing its sides **(v1.15)**. Taken by PPL-13: a command, `open_shop`, and the add page goes (Jack: A) **(v1.127)**. Settled (PPL-13, 19 Nov 2026): `manage.py open_shop` opens a shop through `set_up_shop`, and the admin has no add page **(v1.132)**.
17. A cash drawer serves exactly one side (ADR-0001), while a Lipa Namba may serve several. The database can't hold this: the link table has no `kind`. `create_shop` makes one drawer per side; the service that adds accounts (MONEY-2) checks it **(v1.15)**.
18. Each shop's own colours: today every shop has the one look (ADR-0039). The tokens make a second look possible *(default: Later)* **(v1.40)**.
19. Which side gets the striped band: `SideBand` draws both, but a side has no field that says which. Decide with the first screen that shows a side **(v1.40)**. UI-3 shows a side in words only, with no band, and SHOP-4 gives a side its band (Jack, 7 Nov 2026) **(v1.45)**. Settled (SHOP-4, 16 Nov 2026): a field of the side, `band`, blue unless told otherwise, which the owner picks on the settings page; Dawa blue and Vyakula striped on the demo **(v1.84)**.
20. Dark mode: off for now (ADR-0039). Every colour would need a second shade, and every screen a second check *(default: Later)* **(v1.40)**.
21. The two roundings agree on every amount the shop makes today, but not on an amount with dozens of places: `round_money` keeps 40 digits in all, and `roundMoney` 40 places after the point, so 458.4999… with 38 places rounds to 459 on the server and 458 on a device (found in UI-1's review). Every amount comes from text with two or three places, and a price × kg has five, so none comes near. If a sum ever divides before it rounds, add a vector for it, and decide then: both sides refuse an amount with too many places, or both cut at the same place **(v1.43)**.
22. A suspended shop's laptops keep syncing (SYNC-9): the device check's 401 `shop.suspended` is none of the answers the app acts on (`device-check.tsx`), so the saved copy stands, and each moment's pull is refused, quietly, every 30 s. Decide with the first card that shows a shop's suspension on a device: lock the app, as a block does, or say so and stop the moments **(v1.62)**.
23. Clearing a refused record off a laptop (SYNC-11): the push sets it aside for good, and *Mahudhurio* shows its warning, dated once it isn't today's, to everyone who logs in there. The manager mends a refused clock-in in the attendance book (ATT-13 **(v1.165)**), but nothing removes the record or its warning from the laptop. Decide who may clear it, and how: the sync health page (SYNC-14), or a manager's PIN at the counter; and whether the export of unsent records (SYNC-12) carries it **(v1.64)**. SYNC-14 left it: the health page is an office screen, which never reaches a laptop's own database (rule 5), so clearing a record from there needs a note from the server that travels to the laptop; a card of its own, or the counter's PIN way **(v1.71)**.
24. An edited copy of a record the server holds already (SYNC-12's second review): a rescued record whose file was edited by hand after the first copy reached the server is a `duplicate`, answered as the first, and its new content is dropped with no flag. Decide whether a duplicate whose content differs from the stored copy should raise a flag for a manager **(v1.67)**.
25. A money rule with cents (SHOP-5): the server keeps two places for a money rule, and the settings page takes them, showing them with `formatMoneyExact`. But `formatMoney` refuses cents everywhere else, so a rounding step like 2.50 (`money.rounding_step`) would stop every screen that writes a rounded amount. Decide before the sale screens (stage 5) whether a money rule may have cents, or its kind says whole shillings **(v1.79)**. Settled (Jack, 16 Nov 2026): whole shillings. The server's Money kind refuses cents (`setting.value_invalid`), and the page's box takes digits only **(v1.82)**.
26. Editing a role's keys: 02 §5 lets the owner edit a shop's roles, but no card does yet (PPL-4's split, 17 Nov 2026). When one does: an edit that adds keys to a role must ask `_check_no_open_temporary_password` for each person holding it, as giving one does, and the Owner role must not pass that check just because an edit cut it down to keys the setter holds **(v1.32)**; an edit of a role's codes names every holder with `record_change`, as their keys travel on their staff rows (SYNC-15) **(v1.54)** **(v1.100)**; from PPL-7: the push checks a record made offline against the keys its maker held when it was made, reading each role's codes as they are now, so an edit of a role's codes must keep what the role gave before it, or an old sale reads a key it never had **(v1.119)**.
27. Who the superuser was, in a shop's history (PPL-13, Jack: A): his commands act as `by=None`, as the system does, so the edit history and the Owner's role row name nobody, as for the demo seed. With one superuser, that is Jack; a second one, or an owner asking "who made him Owner?", needs a sign of its own: the superuser named as such, or each run of a command kept in the audit trail **(v1.132)**.
28. A shop outside Tanzania (PPL-13, Jack: A): `create_shop`, and so `open_shop`, take no currency, timezone or language, so every shop opens in TZS, `Africa/Dar_es_Salaam` and Swahili; and a phone typed without its country's code is read as Tanzania's (`normalise_phone`, region TZ). The admin can change the timezone and the language later, but never the currency, fixed once the shop exists. A first shop in Kenya or Uganda needs `open_shop` to ask them, `create_shop` to take them, and its own region for phones **(v1.132)**.
29. A side renamed or added on a screen (PPL-13's review, Jack: A): `rename_side` and `add_side` have their routes (`apps/shops/api/views.py`), but *Mipangilio ya duka* → *Pande* shows only each side's switch and band. So a typo in a side's name made by `open_shop`, or a side the shop adds later, waits for a card that puts both on that tab; until then the runbook says to check every side before the yes. A side's letter never changes (D7), so a wrong one is left switched off, and the right one added once that screen exists **(v1.133)**.

## Appendix A — Changes to the other docs

Applied in portion 5 (18 Oct 2026) in spec v1.1, domain model v1.2 (where T2 now lists attendance events among the records that can only be voided) and architecture v1.1 **(v1.1)**:

1. **Spec §10 and §11:** attendance (R1b) is built before the Drugs side (R1); every part is in the one website and is switched on when the shop starts using it (BP7).
2. **03 §16:** the custom `User` moves into stage 0, before the demo's first deploy (BP5); the walking skeleton is proven first with the clock-in, then with the sale (BP6).
3. **03 §5.6:** attendance events also allow the one-time void stamp, as 02 §12 describes (`void`: by, reason).
4. **03 §5.8 and §9:** role templates live in code and are copied into each new shop by `create_shop` (roles belong to a shop), instead of being seeded by a data migration.
5. **03 §12:** release-please writes the changelog and the version tags (BP11); the production deploy runs as a job of the release workflow (OPS-6).

## Appendix B — Data sheets to start filling now

One spreadsheet per sheet. The imports (DATA-4, DATA-5, DEBT-8) must accept these columns as they are.

**Products** — one row per selling unit; repeat the product name on each row.

| Column | Example | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Side | D | D, F or both |
| Product | Ivermectin 1% | |
| Other names | ivomec, dawa ya minyoo | Words customers use, separated by commas |
| Tags | ng'ombe, mbuzi, minyoo | Animal, use, brand, type |
| Base unit | L | kg, L, pcs or m |
| Batches | yes | yes for drugs |
| Prescription only | no | |
| Selling unit | Chupa 100 ml | |
| Factor | 0.1 | Base units in one selling unit |
| Weighed | no | yes for loose kg |
| Retail | 12,000 | Made-up example |
| Jumla | 11,000 | Empty if none |
| Floor | 10,500 | Empty if no bargaining |
| Buying price | 9,000 | Per selling unit; empty if unknown |
| Shelf | A3 | |
| Reorder level | 5 | In selling units |

**Customers:** name · phone(s) · place · person, business or institution · customer / supplier · retail or jumla · notes.

**Debts (madeni book):** customer name · phone · side · date of the debt · amount · items · notes.

**Staff:** name · phone · job title · side(s) · role(s) · daily rate · started on. Entered by hand in the staff screen (PPL-6; the phone, PPL-10) **(v1.100)**; the sheet only collects the details.

The opening count sheet is printed by the system once products are in (STK-7), so it isn't needed yet.
